# Resolved & Archived Troubleshooting Entries

Entries moved out of the thematic guides because nothing in them is still actionable: the pipeline defect is fixed on `main`, the script, flag or gate they describe no longer exists, or the one-off workaround is no longer needed. They are kept verbatim as history (with the archive date and reason as their first bullet), so `git grep` on an old error message still finds them.

Open and current entries live in the thematic guides listed in [`./README.md`](./README.md). No entry here carries an open marker: `git grep -n -i "open pipeline" docs/troubleshooting/` lists only what is still to do. If a symptom recorded here comes back, move the entry back to its thematic guide and mark it open (see [`./AGENTS.md`](./AGENTS.md)).

## From [`./toolchain-upgrades.md`](./toolchain-upgrades.md): Toolchain & Frama-C Upgrade Notes

### Symptom: the pinned `framac/frama-c:dev` image had none of the Frama-C 34 Eva changes
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. The dev base image was replaced by a source build of 34.0~beta, so the symptom cannot recur; the 'uncommitted' note is stale.
- **Seen on:** `framac-analysis:latest` built on the `framac/frama-c:dev` digest `sha256:2296220f…` (2026-11-04, the newest `dev` push), checked against the Frama-C 34.0~beta release notes on 2026-11-13.
- **Cause:** the image reports `35.0~~dev (Bromine)` and its `/frama-c/Changelog` already holds the kernel's 34.0 section, but its Eva predates the 34 work. `frama-c -eva-help-options` listed `-eva-partition-history` and had no `-eva-partition-path` or `-eva-auto-partition-path`. The Eva soundness fixes of 34.0 were therefore missing too: octagon on `x = *p + 1` when `p` points to `x`, casts from or to floating-point values, right shifts of negative values by an imprecise amount, and `memmove` with overlapping locations and an imprecise size. Docker Hub has no 34.0 tag of `framac/frama-c`; its newest numbered tag is `33.0`. This is not a pipeline defect: the base image was older than its version string suggested.
- **Fix (on main, e06d2407):** the analysis image is built from the 34.0~beta source tarball as above. Eva Valid statuses from the earlier image may be wrong on code that hits one of the four fixed cases.
- **Benchmark A/B (2026-11-13):** `benchmark.py run` with the same scripts on both images, runs `ab-fc35dev` and `ab-fc34`, all 10 targets. Every module's Eva `alarms.csv` is byte-identical (25 modules), so are the Valid, Unknown, Invalid and Dead counts and the functions with an Unknown; recall is 7/7 on both, and `benchmark.py compare` finds nothing but the toolchain change. None of the four soundness fixes changes a status on these targets. Wall time 4,720 s against 4,992 s, CPU 7,669 s against 7,684 s. Both runs carry the fuzz `ZeroDivisionError` of [`./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md`](./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md), so their fuzz results compare like for like but are not campaign results.

### Symptom: `-eva-partition-history` renamed `-eva-partition-path`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Allowlist enforces the new name; committed.
- **Seen on:** Frama-C 34.0~beta. The old name still works and prints `[kernel] Warning: -eva-partition-history is a deprecated alias for option -eva-partition-path.`
- **Fix (on main):** [`../../scripts/eva_options.py`](../../scripts/eva_options.py) allows `partition-path` and `partition-path-function` and refuses the old names, with a message naming the new one, so a module configuration shows what Eva actually runs. The `history` ladder step uses `partition-path` with the same values, so `LADDER_VERSION` stays 2. `auto-partition-path` and `auto-partition-path-function` are allowlisted but not in the ladder until a benchmark compares them with `history`. Tests: `tests/test_eva_tuning.py`.

## From [`./phase0-build-and-model.md`](./phase0-build-and-model.md): Phase 0: Build Capture, Frama-C Model & Threat Model

### Symptom: A Generated Header (`config.h`) Is Missing in Eva, WP or the E-ACSL Build
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Generated headers are exported to the snapshot since 47d7c89; only a pre-fix capture needs a rerun.
- **Cause:** generated headers stay in the build tree on the scratch volume, and the later analyses read `workspace/work`, which holds only the target's source tree.
- **Fix:** **fixed** (`47d7c89`). The capture exports the headers the build generated to `workspace/snapshot/generated/` (`generated` in `build_capture.json`, `generated_dirs` in the snapshot manifest), and `target_env.with_include_flags` adds `-idirafter` for them to every Frama-C parse, the E-ACSL build, the harness probe and the sanitizer builds. If one is still missing, check that the run's capture predates `47d7c89` (rerun it) or that the header is listed under `generated`. Only as a stop-gap, copy it into `workspace/harnesses/include/`.

### Symptom: `no_threat_model` Although `THREAT_MODEL.md` Has a `framac-threat-model` Block
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The CommonMark fence scanner is in scripts/trust.py on main (the 'uncommitted' note is outdated).
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (no target run). `trust.extract_block` returned `(None, [])` for a block whose fence is indented by two spaces.
- **Cause:** `_FENCE_RE` in [`../../scripts/trust.py`](../../scripts/trust.py) wanted the fence at column 0, a closing fence identical to the opening, and the info word bare. An indented fence, a longer closing fence or `{framac-threat-model}` gave "no block", the same status as an absent file, so trust fell back to the default rules.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/trust.py`](../../scripts/trust.py)):** the regex scanner was replaced with linear CommonMark line scanner `_scan_code_fences` in `extract_block`, eliminating quadratic backtracking on unclosed fences. The fence may be indented by up to three spaces, the closing fence may be longer, and the info word may be in braces. A fence that names the block but cannot be read (inside a list item or a quote) is now `invalid_threat_model` with an error. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py).

### Symptom: A THREAT_MODEL.md With CRLF Line Endings Is Reported as `no_threat_model` ("has no structured block")
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The closing fence accepts a trailing CR.
- **Seen on:** `test_threat_model_schema_takes_a_sink_format` on Windows (2026-11-10, while adding M5): `Path.write_text` wrote CRLF, and `trust.load_threat_model` found no block.
- **Cause:** `_FENCE_RE` in [`../../scripts/trust.py`](../../scripts/trust.py) accepted only spaces and tabs between the closing fence and the end of the line, so the `\r` of a CRLF file left the fence unclosed. A target checked out on Windows (or a threat model edited there) silently lost its threat model: only L3 was generated.
- **Fixed (worktree commit of M5):** the closing fence accepts a trailing `\r` (`[ \t\r]*$`); the test now reads the same block with LF and with CRLF line endings. A one-line hunk in `trust.py`, which the libc track also edits (its syscall readers), in another function.

### Symptom: A Product Unit With `check` in Its Name Is Marked `test` and Left Out of the Analysis (`test_reason: "name"` or `"test_sources"`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. `check`/`checks` are no longer test words; the 'uncommitted' note is outdated.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (no target run). `build_capture.test_reason("plugins/check_http", ...)` returned `"name"`; `libbounds.a` built from `bounds_check.c` and `sanity_check.c` returned `"test_sources"`.
- **Cause:** `TEST_WORDS` in [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py) held `check` and `checks`, and any `_`, `.` or `-` separated word of a unit or file name was compared with it. Test units are dropped by `build_callgraph_dag.py`, `phase0_model.py` and `trust.py`, with only `test_reason` in the report.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py)):** `check` and `checks` are no longer test words in a name; a `check/` directory (`TEST_DIRS`) still marks a test. **Behaviour change:** a libcheck test built from `check_<x>.c` outside a test directory is now analysed as product code (`tests/test_build_capture.py::test_test_units_are_marked` changed accordingly). Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening.py).

### Symptom: A Unit-Test Driver Without a Test Name Is Analysed as Product Code, and Its Static Library Has No Entry Point
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both rules and the run-3 recurrence fix are merged.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-17, `mpack` `run-1` and `run-2` (`run-2-42659b61e061`), supervision report [`../runs/2026-11-17-mpack-run1-supervision.md`](../runs/2026-11-17-mpack-run1-supervision.md). `build_capture`: `".build/unit/everything-debug/runner": ["executable", 16, false]` (`test_reason` None), and `.build/mpack-lib/libmpack.a` had `entry_points: []` with public headers `outside_source_dirs` = `examples/sax-example.h`, `test/fuzz/fuzz-config.h`. `phase0_model.json` entry points were `['.build/unit/flagtest.c#main', 'test/unit/src/test.c#main']`. After 1,032,144 passing checks, `seed_harvest` `calls.jsonl` held only `{"fn":"test_system"}` and `{"fn":"test_common"}`. The agent abandoned `run-1` and restarted with `--scope src/mpack` to keep the test suite out of Phases 1-3; the user then stopped `run-2` to wait for the fix.
- **Cause 1:** `test_reason()` in [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py) marked tests only by a name word, a test directory, all-test sources or a `test(s)` suffix. The runner compiles `src/mpack/*.c` again next to `test/unit/src/test*.c` (its `main` is in `test.c`) and matched none of them. As a product unit, its embedded API (`test_*` and `mpack_*`) was wrapped by the seed recorder, which records only outermost calls.
- **Cause 2:** with no install target, a static library's public headers are the project headers outside the source directories. For mpack those are an example header and a fuzz config header that declare no `mpack_*` function, so `libmpack.a` got no entry point. The runner's embedded API had hidden this. With the runner classified as test alone, the recorder planned 0 functions (391 on the `run-2` data before).
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-17, merged into `phase1-remediation` as `c9b29a4` from `sup/mpack-test-runner-classification`):**
  - `c2ddddb` adds the `TEST_RULE` case `main_in_test_source` (`mark_test_drivers`): an executable whose `main` is in a test source, and whose other sources all belong to a non-test library, is a test unit.
  - `339919b` adds `PUBLIC_FALLBACK_RULE` (`static_lib_entries`): public headers that declare none of a static library's functions are replaced by its consumers' headers, or by every project header when nothing links the library, and a warning names the library.
  - Tests are in [`../../tests/test_build_capture.py`](../../tests/test_build_capture.py).
  - Checked on the real mpack tree: the runner is `test` (`main_in_test_source`) and `libmpack.a` gets 319 entry points (0 before).
  - This is separate from the header-defined entry points of plan H1 ([`../plans/header-entry-points-plan.md`](../plans/header-entry-points-plan.md)).
- **Recurrence on `mpack` `run-3`, 2026-11-17; **fixed** by `c57d2ea` from `sup/mpack-test-driver-any-holder`, merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-17 (merge `aa2f82b`):**
  - **Seen:** with the recipe `make -C examples sax-example && python3 test/unit/configure.py && ninja -f .build/unit/build.ninja`, no library unit exists. The product sources `src/mpack/mpack-*.c` were held only by `examples/sax-example`, a non-test executable. `.build/unit/everything-debug/runner` stayed `test=False`, with `test/unit/src/test.c#main` as an entry point and `warnings []`.
  - **Cause:** `mark_test_drivers` accepted only a non-test static or shared library as the holder of a driver's product sources.
  - **Fix on the branch:** the holder may be any other non-test unit (library, executable or `unlinked:<dir>`, all analysed as product code) that is not itself a driver candidate (an executable whose `main` is in a test source). Two drivers over product code that no product unit holds therefore do not mark each other. Tests: `test_a_test_driver_whose_product_sources_an_example_program_holds_is_a_test_unit` and `test_a_driver_is_marked_only_when_another_product_unit_holds_its_sources` (6 cases).
  - **Checked** on the real mpack tree with that recipe (tag `sup-mpack-real2`): the runner is `test`, and `examples/sax-example` stays product with 342 `mpack_*` embedded-API functions and no `test_*`.
  - **Workaround in the run:** the agent added `build/libmpack.a` to the recipe, which the merged rule already handles.

### Symptom: Product Unit `unlinked:/tmp/cgXXXXXX` Fails in `phase0_model` With `[kernel] User Error: source file '/tmp/cg7zzCGG/dummy.c' does not exist` (Autotools Targets)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. is_temp_probe drops out-of-tree temp probes; fix merged.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, 2026-11-18 (recipe `./autogen.sh && ./configure && make`). `build_capture.json` listed a product unit `unlinked:/tmp/cg7zzCGG` (kind `unlinked`, files `["/tmp/cg7zzCGG/dummy.c"]`); `phase0_model.py run` then failed on it with `[kernel] User Error: source file '/tmp/cg7zzCGG/dummy.c' does not exist` and counted it as a `gcc_fallback` product unit.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. The bear events show `cc -c -o /tmp/cg7zzCGG/dummy.o /tmp/cg7zzCGG/dummy.c` (cwd `/framac-scratch/tree`), parent `/bin/bash ./build-aux/config.guess`, parent `./configure`: GNU config.guess makes `$TMPDIR/cgXXXXXX` and compiles `dummy.c` there to tell glibc from musl, then deletes the dir. `is_probe_path()` in [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py) only knew CMake try-compiles / `CMakeFiles`, `meson-private` and autoconf `conftest*`, so the compile was kept and its object, linked into nothing, became an `unlinked:<dir>` unit. Every autotools target whose config.guess runs this probe hits it.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-18, commit `0b3b43b` on branch `sup/libmodbus-config-guess-probe`, merged live into `phase1-remediation` at the user's request):** new `is_temp_probe()` in [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py); `classify_executions` drops (and counts in `probes_dropped`) a compile whose source or output lies outside the tree under `/tmp`, `/var/tmp` or `$TMPDIR`. An out-of-tree source elsewhere is still kept. Test: `tests/test_build_capture.py::TestRecordedEvents::test_config_guess_temp_dir_probe_is_dropped`. A run captured before the fix needs `build_capture.py run` again (or the stray unit ignored) to clear the `gcc_fallback` count.

### Symptom: Unrecognized Compiler Variants Dropped Silently During Build Capture
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Compiler regexes extended and skipped_compiler_like warnings emitted; 'uncommitted' note is outdated.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M2). Intercepted build commands using `tcc`, `icx`, `afl-clang-fast`, or posix-suffixed compilers like `gcc-13-posix` were not recognized as compilers, and skipped compiler-like executions were dropped without warnings.
- **Cause:** `_COMPILER_RE` and `_TRIPLE_COMPILER_RE` in [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py) lacked pattern alternatives for `tcc`, `icx`, `-fast`, and `-posix`. Skipped executions were dropped without recording.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py)):** expanded compiler regexes to recognize `tcc`, `icx`, `clang(?:-fast)?`, and `(?:-posix)?`. In `classify_executions`, tracked skipped compiler-like commands in `skipped_compiler_like` and surfaced them in `report["warnings"]`. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py).

### Symptom: Function Calls in Inline Bodies or Comments Falsely Identified as Declarations
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. declared_names uses C tokenization; header_declared_functions exists.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M2). `build_capture.declared_names` matched function call sites inside inline function bodies, comments, and string literals.
- **Cause:** `declared_names` in [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py) used regex search `rf"\b{re.escape(n)}\s*\("` over raw or preprocessed header text.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py), [`../../scripts/phase0_model.py`](../../scripts/phase0_model.py)):** updated `declared_names` to use C tokenization (`tokenize_c_acsl`), checking zero brace and parenthesis depth to ignore function bodies, comments, and strings. Added `phase0_model.header_declared_functions` to query model declarations directly from Phase 0 AST facts. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py).

### Symptom: Every Module Including `<pthread.h>` Is Fuzzed With the Sanitizers ("includes setjmp.h ... E-ACSL cannot build it")
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. includes_setjmp_h ignores bits/setjmp.h; fix merged.
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, 2026-11-16 ([`../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md`](../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md)).
  - `build_callgraph_dag.py` warned, for `libcares_a`, `adig` and `ahost`: `module <m>: includes setjmp.h (src/lib/util/ares_threads.c), whose setjmp declaration Frama-C rejects (CERT MSC38-C): E-ACSL cannot build it; fuzzed with the sanitizers`.
  - Every unit got `fuzz_engine: sanitizers`, which removed E-ACSL runtime checking from Phase 1 for the whole target.
  - `ares_threads.c` never includes `<setjmp.h>`: on Linux it includes `<pthread.h>`, `<time.h>` and `<sys/time.h>`. `clang -M` lists only `/usr/include/x86_64-linux-gnu/bits/setjmp.h`.
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py) set `compiles[].includes_setjmp_h` whenever a system dependency's basename is `setjmp.h`.
  - That also matches glibc's private `bits/setjmp.h`, which `<pthread.h>` includes for `__jmp_buf`.
  - E-ACSL builds that code. Measured with `run_eacsl_fuzz.py build --engine eacsl` in `framac-analysis:latest`: a source that only includes `<pthread.h>` gives `status: built`, `engine_used: eacsl`, with or without c-ares's feature macros.
  - Only the libc's `<setjmp.h>` fails: `[kernel:CERT:MSC:38] /usr/include/setjmp.h:36: User Error: Attempt to declare setjmp as external identifier outside of the stdlib`.
- **Fix:** `build_capture.includes_setjmp_h()` counts a `setjmp.h` only when its directory is not `bits/`. The setjmp/longjmp call detection is unchanged.
  - Tests: `tests/test_build_capture.py::test_only_the_top_level_setjmp_h_counts`, the `lock.c` case of `::test_compiles_record_setjmp_h_and_the_functions_the_build_defines`, and `::test_eacsl_builds_pthread_h_and_rejects_setjmp_h` (Docker).
  - Commit `625b74b`, from branch `sup/c-ares-setjmp-basename`, fixed and merged into `phase1-remediation` (2026-11-16).
- **Recovering a run captured before the fix:** rerunning `build_callgraph_dag.py` alone is not enough. The per-compile system dependencies are not stored, so the flag cannot be recomputed from the report. Instead:
  1. Rerun `build_capture.py run` with the fixed scripts.
  2. Rerun `build_callgraph_dag.py` and the Phase 0 steps after it.
  3. Rebuild the modules (`build-<mod>`), so the harness gate reads the new `engine_used`.

  Untested shortcut: `pipeline_make.py params set --module <m> --fuzz-engine auto`.

### Symptom: `source file 'objs/autotest.c' does not exist` and a `gcc_fallback` unit for nginx's `configure` probe executable
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. drop_vanished_probes drops vanished probes; fix merged.
- **Seen on:** nginx-1.31.9 run-1 (`nginx-1.31.9-3e44199e7906 / run-1-73bbe362401c`, 2026-11-19), Phase 0 Step 1c, with the user's recipe `./configure ... && make -j"$(nproc)"`. `build_capture.json` lists `objs/autotest` as `kind: executable`, `test: false`, `files: ["objs/autotest.c"]`; `phase0_model.json` records `objs/autotest` as `gcc_fallback` with the exact error `[kernel] User Error: source file 'objs/autotest.c' does not exist [kernel] Frama-C aborted: invalid user input.`
- **Cause:** nginx's `configure` writes a temporary C program to `objs/autotest.c`, compiles it to `objs/autotest`, runs it as a feature probe and deletes the source. `bear` still records the compile, and [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py) keeps the in-tree probe as a product unit because `is_temp_probe()` only drops sources or outputs under `/tmp`, `/var/tmp` or `$TMPDIR` (the libmodbus `unlinked:/tmp/...` gap). Frama-C cannot parse a source that no longer exists, so the empty unit lands in the model and the DAG as a fallback.
- **Fix:** none in the run directory; `build_capture.json` is a generated report and must not be hand-edited. This unit alone did not stop the run; the model stopped on `objs/nginx` (see [`./kernel-and-stubs.md`](./kernel-and-stubs.md), the `cpu_set_t` entry).
- **Status (2026-11-19):** **fixed** in `f85de2c`, merged into `wip/main-checkout-2026-11-19` (fast-forward to `4e6ac27`). run-1's `events.jsonl` shows nginx's configure links 9 distinct probe command lines as `cc ... -o objs/autotest objs/autotest.c` and then runs `rm -rf objs/autotest objs/autotest.c`.
  - New `drop_vanished_probes()` in [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py), which `capture()` calls right after `classify_executions`. It applies no name rule. A compile counts as a probe when its source and its output are both gone after the build. A link or archive counts as a probe when its output is gone and every object in it is such a compile.
  - A compile whose object a surviving artifact still uses is kept, as is a generated source still in the tree (`objs/ngx_modules.c`).
  - The dropped probes are listed in `build.vanished_probes`, counted in `build.probes_dropped`, and named in a warning.
  - Tests: `tests/test_build_capture.py::TestRecordedEvents::test_a_probe_whose_source_and_output_are_gone_after_the_build_is_dropped`, `::test_a_generated_source_the_build_deleted_is_kept_when_its_output_survives` and `::test_the_capture_drops_vanished_probes_before_the_analysis`.
  - A run captured before the fix keeps the unit until `build_capture.py run` is run again. Not yet checked on the nginx tree.

## From [`./harnesses-and-seeds.md`](./harnesses-and-seeds.md): Harnesses, Reach Gates & Seeds

### Symptom: `lib/getopt.c:45: fatal error: gettext.h: No such file or directory` in a Harness Build (nettle getopt)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed pipeline bug in build_callgraph_dag.py, nothing open in the entry.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (`scripts/build_callgraph_dag.py`, commit `d71b255`): 9 executable modules of nettle run-3 (`base16dec`, `base16enc`, `base64dec`, `base64enc` and the tools) failed `build-check` with this error.
- **Cause:** Frama-C parses with its own libc, which has no `__GLIBC__`; the build and every harness build use glibc. nettle's root `getopt.c` includes `lib/getopt.c` only when `ELIDE_CODE` is unset, and sets it under `__GLIBC__ >= 2`. The glibc build therefore compiled `getopt.c` to an empty object: in `build_capture.json` it has `headers: []` and `defined_functions: []`, and `lib/getopt.c` is no compiled source. The model took the other branch and put `_getopt_internal`, `getopt_long`, and the rest in `lib/getopt.c`. The DAG took `required_sources` from the model's defining files, so the argv harnesses compiled `lib/getopt.c` on its own.
- **Fix:** a module's `required_sources` now hold only compiled sources (`compiles[].source`). Any other file becomes the compiled source whose `cc -M` list (`compiles[].headers`) includes it, or is dropped when none does. A function the real build does not define carries `model_only` with the reason, and the DAG lists these per file under `model_only_functions` with a warning. A function is marked when no compile compiles or includes its file, or when it is non-static and no object of its units defines it (statics are never marked: the compiler may inline them). Marked functions are never harness sources or reach-gate entry points (`harness_reach.built_entries`). Eva and WP may still analyse them; the ledger shows `bookkeeping.model_only` and REPORT.md lists them. `build_capture.py` now records `compiles[].probe_errors`, so an empty `headers` or `defined_functions` list is no longer confused with a failed probe. A capture that predates it trusts only non-empty symbol lists. `build_only_functions` no longer reports the functions of an included `.c` (nettle's `eccdata.c` including `mini-gmp.c`).
- **Check:** after `build_callgraph_dag.py`, no `required_sources` names a file outside `compiles[].source`. Each `model_only_functions` warning names code only Frama-C's configuration compiles. Report it to the user: Eva and WP results there describe code the shipped binary does not contain.

### Symptom: A Harness Misses a Build Flag (`-include config.h`, a Quoted `-D`, `-std=`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both gaps (quoted -D, pre-C99 -std) are fixed; the -std part is closed by commit 2f22b2c recorded in the fd_set entry.
- **Cause:** harnesses take the unit's flags from the DAG. The unit's `-include` files are kept apart (`force_includes`) and applied by `generate_harness.py`. Quoted `-D` values (`-DLOG_PATH="log.txt"`) and the build's `-std=` are not copied. Later stages split and re-join flag strings, and a pre-C99 `-std` rejects both the C99 harness and E-ACSL's printed code.
- **Fix:** since `784401d` (sudo run-1, 2026-11-21), the build's string-valued `-D` macros are copied with their quotes; see the `LOCALEDIR` entry below. State only a `-D` value with whitespace yourself, and only through a force-included header.
  - A `-std=` of C99 or later given there is kept.
  - A pre-C99 one got through until the fix described in the next entry, which drops it.

### Symptom: `generate_harness.py --api` Fails With `fatal error: 'workspace/work/config.h' file not found` When the Build Force-Includes a Generated `config.h`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in build_callgraph_dag.py with a regression test, no open part.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18, `standard` profile), module `libmodbus_so_5_1_0`. The command
  `generate_harness.py --module libmodbus_so_5_1_0 --api --header workspace/work/src/modbus.h --extra-include workspace/work/src/modbus-rtu.h --extra-include workspace/work/src/modbus-tcp.h --max-size 1500 --out-dir workspace/harnesses`
  exited 1 with `{"ok": false, "error": "module 'libmodbus_so_5_1_0': the harness does not compile outside its cases: <built-in>:1:10: fatal error: 'workspace/work/config.h' file not found"}`.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. The build runs with `-include config.h`, and `config.h` is generated by `./configure`, so it exists only in the build tree and in `workspace/snapshot/generated/config.h`; it is not part of the shadow overlay (`workspace/work/` holds only the target's sources). `build_callgraph_dag.py` reads the unit's `-include`/`-imacros` tokens and roots every one at the work tree (`unit_cppflags` -> `_rooted(nxt, root)`), so the unit's `force_includes` is `workspace/work/config.h`. `generate_harness.py` applies that path with `-include` before its clang probe, ahead of the snapshot `-idirafter` that supplies generated headers. The earlier generated-header fix (`47d7c89`, [`./phase0-build-and-model.md`](./phase0-build-and-model.md), "A Generated Header (`config.h`) Is Missing in Eva, WP or the E-ACSL Build") covers the `-idirafter` path but not this explicit force include.
- **Fix:** **fixed** in [`../../scripts/build_callgraph_dag.py`](../../scripts/build_callgraph_dag.py), commit `9f7b123` on branch `sup/libmodbus-generated-force-include`, merged live into `phase1-remediation` as `222d1d3` (2026-11-18, while the run was paused). A force-include listed in the capture's `snapshot.generated.headers` now resolves to `workspace/snapshot/generated/<rel>` (new `generated_root`, and a `generated=` parameter on `unit_cppflags` filled by `unit_view`); in-tree force-includes keep `workspace/work/<rel>`. The `-idirafter workspace/snapshot/generated` alone could not rescue it, because a relative `-include` is looked up from the cwd before the include chain. Regression test: `tests/test_unit_view.py::test_generated_force_include_resolves_to_its_snapshot_copy`. A run that built its DAG before the fix reruns `build_callgraph_dag.py`, then `generate_harness.py`. No workaround was applied before the fix: copying `config.h` into `workspace/work/` would fail `workspace_overlay.py check-acsl-only` (`new source file created outside baseline`), and `generate_harness.py` has no flag to skip the DAG's force includes.

### Symptom: `seed_harvest.py record` Reports `status: "not_relinked"` and 0 Calls Because Every Test Unit Is `not_built` (Post-Build Move of the Test Executables)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 4c88db5 with tests; nothing open.
- **Seen on:** CryptoLib benchmark target (`5b98c8b`), Phase 0 only run `cl-p0-20261123` (2026-11-23), and in every stored baseline since `phase0-i9` (`build_capture.seeds.status: "not_relinked"`, `calls: 0`). `workspace/reports/seed_harvest.json` listed 192 planned API functions, and `relink.<exe>.status` was `"not_built"` for all 16 test units (`build/internal/test/ut_tc_process`, `ut_tm_apply`, ...), although the capture's `build.log` showed each one linked. `build_capture.json` also warned `the architecture of 16 of 72 build output(s) was not checked`.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap. CryptoLib's `test/CMakeLists.txt` adds a `POST_BUILD` command to every unit-test target that runs `cmake -E copy $<TARGET_FILE:...> ${PROJECT_BINARY_DIR}/bin/<name>` and then `cmake -E remove $<TARGET_FILE:...>` (`build.log` prints `Created /framac-scratch/tree/build/internal/bin/ut_tc_process`). `build_capture.py` recorded only the linker's output path (`build/internal/test/<name>`). By the time `check_architecture` and `seed_harvest.record` inspected `(tree / unit_name).is_file()`, that file no longer existed, so every test unit was `not_built` and nothing was relinked. The `ctest` recipe runs the copy at `build/internal/bin/<name>`. This is a target build pattern, not a target defect.
- **Fix:** **fixed** (commit `4c88db5`) in [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py), [`../../scripts/seed_harvest.py`](../../scripts/seed_harvest.py), [`../../schemas/build_capture.schema.json`](../../schemas/build_capture.schema.json), and [`../../schemas/seed_harvest.schema.json`](../../schemas/seed_harvest.schema.json).
  - `build_capture.parse_file_action` and `classify_executions` follow in-tree `cp`, `mv`, `install`/`ginstall`, `cmake -E copy|copy_if_different|rename`, `rm`/`unlink`/`cmake -E remove|rm`, and `mkdir`/`install -d`/`cmake -E make_directory` executions in `events.jsonl` (including chained transfers and directory destinations) and record surviving in-tree destinations in `units.<name>.installed_as`.
  - `drop_vanished_probes`, `check_architecture`, `derive_units` (linking against a copied library), `nm -D` on moved shared libraries, and `build_snapshot` (`shared_closure`) follow `installed_as` when the original link output was moved or removed.
  - `seed_harvest.record` reads `installed_as` from `build_capture.json` (falling back to `events.jsonl` for captures that predate the field), probes symbols from the existing `installed_as` binary when the original link output is gone, relinks the unit, atomically copies the wrapped binary to every existing in-tree `installed_as` destination (restoring the post-build removal of the link output when it did not exist beforehand), records `relink.<unit>.installed_as`, includes moved shared-library directories in `shared_library_dirs`, and runs moved executables in `_run_executables` when there is no `test_command`.
  - Verified on CryptoLib (`5b98c8b`) in `framac-build:CryptoLib-f1f14f143ef7`: `build_capture.py` records `installed_as` on all 17 units (`libcryptolib.so` + 16 unit tests) with `architecture.unknown: 0`; `seed_harvest.py record` relinks 16/16 test units into `build/internal/bin/`, `ctest` exits 0, and records 1,031 calls (221 unique) across 36 API functions and 43 traces (including 16 unique calls to `Crypto_TC_ProcessSecurity`, 22 to `Crypto_TC_ApplySecurity`, 20 to `Crypto_TM_ProcessSecurity`, and 24 to `Crypto_AOS_ProcessSecurity`).
  - Tests: [`../../tests/test_seed_harvest_post_build_move.py`](../../tests/test_seed_harvest_post_build_move.py) (10 unit tests plus the Docker CMake `POST_BUILD` copy-and-remove integration test `test_cmake_post_build_copy_and_remove_captured_and_recorded_in_build_image`).

### Symptom: ASan stack-buffer-overflow in `modbus_get_float_*` / `modbus_set_float_*` (One `uint16_t` Passed, Two Read)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by f59649e with a regression test; nothing open.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, 2026-11-18, Phase 1 fuzz. Eva reports the same eight functions Invalid (`mem_access`, modbus-data.c:89-238).
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. An unsized pointer to a non-byte scalar became a single object (`unsigned short c0a0; f(&c0a0)`), but `modbus_get_float_abcd(const uint16_t *src)` reads `src[0]` and `src[1]`. The overflow comes from the harness, not the library.
- **Fix:** fixed by `f59649e`, merged into `phase1-remediation` as `5a9ff81` during the run.
  - Such a pointer gets `ARRAY_ELEMENTS` zeroed elements (`_scalar_array`), with element 0 read from the input as before, so the layout does not move.
  - In a call that also takes a count or an index, it gets a spare buffer's worth of elements.
  - Test: `tests/test_api_harness_array_count.py::test_a_pointer_to_a_scalar_with_no_count_is_a_small_array`.

### Symptom: ASan SEGV at modbus-tcp.c:684 in `modbus_tcp_pi_listen` (Single Case and Lifecycle Method)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by d12b16f (backend-matched constructors), with a test; the legacy rollback is documented in the entry only as an opt-out.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, 2026-11-18, Phase 1 fuzz. It still occurs after the `sup/libmodbus-harness-contracts` fixes, in a replay of the run's crash inputs.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug (harness-induced type confusion).
  - The harness builds every `modbus_t` with `modbus_new_tcp`, the constructor with the fewest parameters.
  - `modbus_tcp_pi_listen` reads `backend_data` as a `modbus_tcp_pi_t`. Its `char *node` at offset 8 overlays `modbus_tcp_t.ip`, so the bytes of the IP string are dereferenced as a pointer.
  - The API's precondition (a context from `modbus_new_tcp_pi`) is broken by the harness. libmodbus cannot tell TCP from TCP-PI by `backend_type`, so this is at most a hardening note upstream, not a finding.
- **Fix:** **Fixed under `FRAMAC_API_TRUST_SCHEMA=on` (default `on`; `=off` restores legacy rollback; commit `d12b16f`, [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py)):** handle constructor selection now scores constructors by shared backend words with the method (`tcp_pi` prefers `modbus_new_tcp_pi`, `rtu` prefers `modbus_new_rtu`). Regression test: `tests/test_p2a_schema_trust_harness.py`. With `FRAMAC_API_TRUST_SCHEMA=off`, the legacy single-constructor rule still applies and the crash verification gate must classify this crash as `harness_induced`.

### Symptom: Coverage and Sanitizer Builds Fail With `clang: error: cannot specify -o when generating multiple output files` (Headers in the Sidecar `sources`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 7dc1be4, merged; nothing open.
- **Seen on:** mpack run-3 (2026-11-17), every module. `workspace/reports/coverage/<mod>.json` has `status: compile_error`, `compile.stderr` = `clang: error: cannot specify -o when generating multiple output files`; every campaign report has `dual_engine_skipped: sanitizer build failed` for the same reason, and every `cross_engine` entry of `workspace/reports/replay/index.json` is `build_failed`. `modules.mk` shows the cause directly, e.g. `SOURCES_libmpack_a__mpack_common := ... mpack-common.h ... mpack-reader.h ...`.
- **Cause:** `scripts/api_harness.py` builds the sidecar's `sources` from the entry functions' `file` field plus the unit's `required_sources`. When an entry point is a header `MPACK_INLINE` function (mpack's tag/load/store accessors, `mpack_reader_ensure`), its file is a `.h`, so a header lands in `sources`. `pipeline.mk` then passes it to every engine: E-ACSL/Frama-C accepts a header as a translation unit, but clang does not (it builds a precompiled header per `.h`, which conflicts with `-o`), so the source-coverage build and the ASan+UBSan build fail while the E-ACSL build succeeds.
- **Fix:** **fixed** by `sup/mpack-harness-sources-and-fd-leak` (`7dc1be4`), merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-17 (merge `7cb53a7`); see the supervisor's entry for the same defect in [`./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md`](./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md). The run-local workaround used before the merge: run `run_eacsl_fuzz.py coverage` by hand per module with the `.h` entries dropped from `--sources` and the same flags as the Make recipe (the reports are identical in shape), then `harness_reach.py dynamic --module <mod>` by hand, since `reach-dynamic-%` depends on `coverage-%`. Consequence to report: the sanitizer engine is unavailable for the whole run, so Phase 1 has E-ACSL evidence only, and header-inline functions show `executions: 0` in the coverage report even when their dispatch case runs (they are compiled into the `.c` files, and the report lists the 140 `.h` functions it does attribute — so check the report before calling such a gap a measurement artifact).

### Symptom: `--api` Harness Fails With `unresolved identifiers after automatic prelude resolution: jmp_buf, FILE`, or Leaves Partial `harness_<mod>.{c,json}` After Exit 1
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. All parts fixed in generate_harness.py/api_harness.py and merged (e36ef39); only a target re-run on libpng is missing, which is not a pipeline defect.
- **Cause:** the auto-prelude knew no header for standard-library names the target's headers use without including them; a failed generation left its half-written files, which `gen` and the reach gate can pick up; the case probe stopped at clang's 20-error cap, so more failing cases needed several rounds (libpng `pngwutil`, `pngset`, `pngget*`; defect E and "partial files" of `docs/progress/2026-11-09-libpng-harness-generation-bugs.md`).
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `scripts/generate_harness.py` and `scripts/api_harness.py`: the prelude includes the standard header of such names (`_STD_HEADER_OF`); generation restores the previous harness files on any failure (`harness_files_kept_on_failure`) and fails on a harness that still does not compile after the prelude (`fail_on_compile_error`); the probe lifts the error cap and fails on errors outside the cases instead of dropping cases for them. The missing header default is **fixed** in `e36ef39` (branch `ot/o4-api-headers`, O4 in [`../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md`](../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md)): without `--header`, `--api` takes the build capture's headers for the unit (see "`--api` Skips Cases With `invalid application of 'sizeof'`" below). Unit-tested only; not yet re-run on libpng (O6).

### Symptom: Auto-Prelude Reports Identifiers Unresolved That a Manually Provided Prelude Header Already Defines
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in generate_harness.py on main with a test; the manual-prelude workaround is a documented option, not a defect.
- **Seen on:** ptf-original run-2 (2026-11-10), `libXmlHandler_a__XmlUtil`: with `--extra-include xmlhandler_prelude.h` (which includes `<libxml/tree.h>`, `XmlParser.h`, ...) on the `-I` path, `resolve_prelude_headers` still reported `xmlNodePtr, xmlXPathParserContextPtr, xmlXPathContextPtr, XML_ERROR, xmlDocPtr` as unresolved, although a manual `clang -fsyntax-only` of a file including only that prelude compiles cleanly with the same flags.
- **Cause (from the code, reproduced with a modelled clang, not confirmed on ptf):** the harness includes the module header first and the `--extra-include` headers after it, so the module header is compiled before the header that defines its types; and the definition search reads only the `*.h` files directly in each `-I` directory, so a definition reached through `<libxml/tree.h>` is never found. The manual check compiled the prelude alone, which does not show the order.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/generate_harness.py` `resolve_prelude_headers`):** before reporting `unresolved`, each `--extra-include` header is moved in turn before the module header and clang runs again; the move is kept only when identifiers are resolved, none is new and fewer errors remain. The moved headers are recorded as `extra_includes_first` in the sidecar, and their line carries `/* auto-prelude: moved before the header that needs it */`. Harnesses with nothing unresolved, and `--no-auto-prelude`, are unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_harness_prelude_extra_include.py` (clang modelled by a fake runner, plus a real-clang test that is skipped without clang and passes in `framac-analysis:latest`, 2026-11-11). Not run on ptf.
- **Workaround (still valid):** the documented manual form — `--no-auto-prelude` with an explicit prelude header that includes every definer in dependency order (`PtfUtil.h` before `ExtCommonDefs.h`, `XmlParser.h` before `XmlUtil.h`, `Logger.h` before `LogFiles.h`, ...). The skeleton compile still fails loudly if something is genuinely missing.

### Symptom: `--api` Skips Cases With `invalid application of 'sizeof' to an incomplete type` or `call to undeclared function`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Capture-header default (e36ef39) and the never-fail fallback (92ed5e4) are merged with tests; the 'not merged yet' text is stale and the target re-run (O6) is not a pipeline defect.
- **Cause:** the harness was generated with `--header none`, so it includes no project header. The auto-prelude only searches for headers that define identifiers the module header uses, and there is none. Internal headers (`aes-internal.h`, `ghash-internal.h`...) are not found either. Found on nettle: 22 of 35 libnettle clusters failed outright and libhogweed skipped 109 cases.
- **Fix:** pass the headers explicitly, one `--extra-include <hdr>.h` each (the public headers of the unit and the internal ones its API uses), and regenerate. On nettle this took the gaps from 245 to 70. Since `e36ef39` this is the default when `--header` is not given (below).
- **Seen again:** c-ares run-1, 2026-11-16 ([`../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md`](../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md)). An `--api` run without `--header` made 19 cases, and the compile probe dropped 18 with `call to undeclared function`. Without a header, the harness declares only its first entry point, and the skill's `--api` text did not say a header is needed.
  - Commit `2424b9b` (branch `sup/c-ares-api-outparam-ctor`, fixed and merged into `phase1-remediation` (2026-11-16)) makes `--api` without `--header` print `api_harness: warning: --api without --header: ...` and add `api_warnings` to the result, naming the build capture's public headers.
  - It also makes `skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md` say to pass `--header <public header>` plus `--extra-include` for the rest.
  - The public headers were not used as a default then, because their recorded paths (`/framac-scratch/install/include/...`) do not map reliably to an include path.
- **Fixed** in `e36ef39` (branch `ot/o4-api-headers`, step O4 of [`../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md`](../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md); not merged yet). Without `--header`, `api_harness.capture_headers` reads `workspace/reports/build_capture.json` and takes:
  - the public headers (`public_headers.files`) that the compiles of the module's link unit include (`compiles[].headers`). An installed path is mapped back to its tree header the way `phase0_model.public_header_files` maps it, by the longest shared trailing path;
  - the other headers those compiles include that declare an entry point of the module that no public header declares. The text is read without the preprocessor and follows renaming macros (`#define _aes_set_key _nettle_aes_set_key` in `aes-internal.h`).
  - The header declaring the first entry point becomes `--header`, and the others become extra includes, placed before any `--extra-include` given. Paths are rooted like the DAG's `cppflags` (`workspace/work/<path>`). A second header with the same file name is left out.
  - The sidecar's `api_headers` records the choice and the reason: `given`, `none`, `capture` or `not_found`. `--header none` still means none. A unit without capture data, or one whose capture gives no header, keeps the warning above.
  - Tests: `tests/test_api_harness_capture_headers.py`.
  - **Never fails a module for its own headers** (`92ed5e4`): the automatic choice degrades in steps (`api_harness.header_attempts`). It tries every chosen header, then the public ones alone (only when internal headers were chosen), then no capture header. The last step is exactly the behaviour without a capture: no header, the given `--extra-include`, and the warning above. A step is abandoned when the probe reports `the harness does not compile outside its cases` (errors the callback-stub re-plan does not explain) or no case compiles. Each step starts from the header-free plan with its own layout probe, and leaves the harness files as they were. `api_headers` keeps `source: capture` when the public-only step works, says `capture_failed` for the last step, and lists each abandoned step in `fallbacks` (the headers dropped, the first three errors). `public` and `internal` list what the final harness includes. A given `--header` that does not compile still fails the module.
  - **Not verified on a target:** the plan's acceptance (nettle's libnettle clusters reach the 70-gap result without `--header` or `--extra-include`) waits for O6.

### Symptom: `--api` Skips Exported Entry Points No Header Declares (`call to undeclared function`), and `reach_gaps_accepted` Takes One Reason for Every Gap
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 8ef9c98 with tests; nothing open.
- **Seen on:** CryptoLib benchmark target (`5b98c8b`), runs `cl-p0-api-20261123`, `cl-p0-api-eb-20261123`, and `cl-p02-api-20261123` (2026-11-23), module `libcryptolib_so` (`{"generate": "api", "header": "workspace/work/include/crypto.h"}`). The reach gate failed with 29 gaps out of 193 entry points (164 reached directly, 227 of 232 functions reached):
  - **26 exported functions** that no `.h` file anywhere in the checkout declares (`Crypto_AOS_Calculate_Padding`, `Crypto_TM_FECF_Validate`, `Crypto_TM_Check_IV_ARSN`, `Crypto_Get_Error_Code_String`, `sa_populate`, `sa_verify_data`, `update_sa_from_ptr`, `key_validation`, `cryptography_gcry_setup`, `crypto_free_config_structs`, etc.), dropped by the compile probe with `error: call to undeclared function '<fn>'; ISO C99 and later do not support implicit function declarations`;
  - **2 functions taking a file-system path** (`Crypto_Config_Cam`, `Crypto_Config_Kmc_Crypto_Service`, deliberately skipped);
  - **1 function taking an anonymous struct by value** (`Crytpo_TC_Validate_TC_Temp_Header`).
  On `cl-p02-api-20261123`, exempting the 29 gaps via `reach_gaps_accepted` required a single module-level string that applied one reason to all 29 gaps and would silently exempt any new regression gap added later.
- **Cause:**
  1. [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py) declared entry points only via included headers; when a library defines non-`static` functions with no header prototype, neither `--header` nor automatic capture-header selection (`e36ef39`) can declare them, even though `phase0_model.json` already has their exact signature and `const_pointer` formals.
  2. `apply_reach_exemptions` in [`../../scripts/benchmark.py`](../../scripts/benchmark.py) accepted only a single string for `reach_gaps_accepted`, exempting every gap reported for the module indiscriminately.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline gaps (`8ef9c98`):**
  - **Reactive `extern` prototype synthesis ([`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py), [`../../schemas/harness_sidecar.schema.json`](../../schemas/harness_sidecar.schema.json)):** when `probe_cases()` reports `call to undeclared function` or `implicit declaration of function` for an exported (non-`static`, non-variadic) function, `synthesize_extern_prototype()` emits an `extern` prototype from the Phase 0 model signature (restoring `const` on `const_pointer` formals, resolving anonymous typedef struct/union tags via an `anon_record_tags` fallback against `visible.anon_records`, and emitting forward `struct`/`union` tag declarations for opaque pointers). Any individual synthesized prototype whose line errors in `probe_cases()` is rolled back via `_synth_proto_errors()` without failing the harness or valid prototypes. Surviving prototypes are recorded in sidecar `api_headers.synthesized_prototypes`.
  - **Per-function `reach_gaps_accepted` mapping ([`../../scripts/benchmark.py`](../../scripts/benchmark.py)):** `validate_manifest` and `apply_reach_exemptions` now accept either the legacy string or a `{function_name: reason}` map (matching bare names or `file#fn` IDs). Only listed gaps are exempted with their specific reasons; unlisted gaps stay unexempted so regressions fail the reach gate, and `unlisted_gaps` / `unused_exemptions` are reported.
  - **Regression tests:** [`../../tests/test_api_harness_synth_protos.py`](../../tests/test_api_harness_synth_protos.py), [`../../tests/test_api_harness_global_setup.py`](../../tests/test_api_harness_global_setup.py) (Docker Clang ASan + Frama-C parse), and [`../../tests/test_benchmark.py`](../../tests/test_benchmark.py).

### Symptom: Trace-Derived `--api` Sequence Cases Drop the Library's Global Setup (`Crypto_Config_*`, `Crypto_Init`) and Replay Only Repeated Calls
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by f7784ce with tests; nothing open.
- **Seen on:** CryptoLib benchmark target (`5b98c8b`), Phase 0 only run `cl-p0-api-seeds-20261123` (2026-11-23), the first run with test seeds after `4c88db5`. `seed_harvest.py record` relinked 16 of 16 test units and recorded 1,031 calls (223 unique) over 36 API functions in 43 trace segments. The segments keep the tests' setup order, for example `Crypto_Config_CryptoLib > Crypto_Config_TC > Crypto_Config_Add_TC_Gvcid_Managed_Parameters > Crypto_Init > Crypto_TC_ApplySecurity` (segment 14). The generated harness went from 13 to 29 `sequence` cases (177 to 193 cases), yet every new one repeated a single function without setting CryptoLib up first (for example `Crypto_TC_ProcessSecurity` 2 to 8 times in a row), so `Crypto_TC_Process_Sanity_Check` (`crypto_tc.c:1661`) immediately returned `CRYPTO_LIB_ERR_NO_CONFIG`.
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py) `chains()` linked calls only when they passed the same pointer address, so process-global setup calls (`Crypto_Config_*`, `Crypto_Init`) never joined chains for `Crypto_{TC,TM,AOS}_{Process,Apply}Security`. In addition, pointer-free packed/bit-field structs passed by value to configurators (such as `struct _TCGvcidManagedParameters_t`) were dropped from `record_layouts()`, and unconfigured pointer-chain repeats consumed all 16 `MAX_SEQUENCE_CASES` slots.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-23, `f7784ce`, [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py)):**
  - Added transitive global-state flow extraction (`_global_state_flow`) from the Phase 0 model (`writes.globals`, `global_refs`, and `call_sites`) and trace-based setup/teardown planning (`_global_setup_prefix`, `_global_setup_plan`). For each non-setup target function called in recorded traces whose transitive global reads intersect globals written by preceding setup calls (`_GLOBAL_SETUP_WORDS` / `_GLOBAL_INIT_WORDS`) since the last teardown (`_GLOBAL_TEARDOWN_WORDS`), the generator retains the minimal concrete setup prefix (collapsing duplicate setup calls before capping at `GLOBAL_SETUP_MAX_PREFIX = 6`) and appends a matching zero-argument teardown (such as `Crypto_Shutdown`) when present in the trace.
  - Setup and teardown calls in sequence cases (`setup_positions` in `sequence_case`) are bound from the exact recorded trace values via `_bind_recorded_setup_call` (scalar literals, finite floats, and `static const unsigned char ...` + `sizeof`-guarded `memcpy` for pointer-free value structs tracked in `Declared.pointer_free_records`), consuming zero fuzz slots and leaving `case.rest` for the target function's attacker-controlled input buffer.
  - In `plan()` (gated on `effective_trust_schema` so `FRAMAC_API_TRUST_SCHEMA=off` preserves the legacy rollback path), configured global-setup cases are emitted before pointer chains, pointer chains for global-setup targets are wrapped with their recorded in-trace setup/teardown prefix, and unconfigured pointer-chain repeats of global-setup targets are skipped. Non-global-state targets keep their exact legacy ordering.
  - **Verification:** unit and Docker integration tests in [`../../tests/test_api_harness_global_setup.py`](../../tests/test_api_harness_global_setup.py) (ASan+LSan+UBSan + `frama-c -machdep gcc_x86_64` in `framac-analysis:latest`), plus an end-to-end check on real CryptoLib (`cl-p0-api-seeds-20261123`) in `framac-analysis:latest`: `generate_harness.py --api` emitted configured sequence cases for `Crypto_{TC,TM,AOS}_{Process,Apply}Security` (plus `Crypto_Shutdown`) with 0 sidecar schema errors, `seed_harvest.py write` produced 15 sequence seeds, `clang -fsanitize=address,undefined` and `frama-c -main eva_main` both succeeded, and running the sequence cases cleared `CRYPTO_LIB_ERR_NO_CONFIG` (`Crypto_TC_ApplySecurity` reached `Crypto_TC_ApplySecurity_Cam -> Crypto_TC_Finalize_Frame_Setup -> Crypto_TC_Accio_Buffer`; the other five frame entry points still received zeroed non-`const` buffers until the next entry below).

### Symptom: `--api` Zeroes a Non-`const` Frame Buffer the Function Reads, so a Configured Case Stops at the First Header Check (`Crypto_TM_ProcessSecurity`, `Crypto_AOS_ProcessSecurity`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by a52178b with tests; nothing open.
- **Seen on:** CryptoLib benchmark target (`5b98c8b`), Phase 0 only run `cl-p0-api-gs-20261123` (2026-11-23, `main` at `a0b0124`), the first run after `f7784ce`. Of the six configured frame entry points, only `Crypto_TC_ApplySecurity` (whose frame parameter is `const uint8_t *`) read its frame from the fuzz input. The other five received a zeroed spare buffer (`static _Alignas(16) unsigned char c4a0[9216]; memset(c4a0, 0, sizeof(c4a0));`), and `Crypto_TC_ProcessSecurity` also received a zeroed `int c4a1[4]` for `int *len_ingest`. Each therefore stopped at the first check after configuration:
  - `Crypto_TC_ProcessSecurity`: `crypto_tc.c:1670`, `*len_ingest < TC_FRAME_HEADER_SIZE`.
  - `Crypto_TM_ProcessSecurity`, `Crypto_TM_ApplySecurity`: GVCID lookup at `crypto_tm.c:1225` and `:906` (zeroed header gives `scid = 0` vs configured `44`).
  - `Crypto_AOS_ProcessSecurity`, `Crypto_AOS_ApplySecurity`: GVCID lookup at `crypto_aos.c:1058` and `:177` (zeroed header gives `tfvn = 0` vs configured `1`).
  - The single cases of the same five functions zeroed the frame the same way (hidden behind `CRYPTO_LIB_ERR_NO_CONFIG` before `f7784ce`).
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py) `_is_input()` treated every non-`const` pointer (`kind: "pointer"`) as an output, so non-`const` byte buffers (both read-only missing `const` and in-place read/write buffers like `Crypto_{TM,AOS}_ApplySecurity`) stayed zeroed. In addition, `_is_pair()` and [`../../scripts/seed_harvest.py`](../../scripts/seed_harvest.py) `plan_function()` only paired `(byte *, int)` and not `(byte *, int *)` (`Crypto_TC_ProcessSecurity(uint8_t *ingest, int *len_ingest, ...)`). Once real frames reached `Crypto_{AOS,TM}_ApplySecurity`, `Crypto_check_buffer_size()` (`src/core/crypto.c:1675`) also called `malloc_usable_size(pTfBuffer)`, causing ASan `bad-malloc_usable_size` on a `static` global array.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-23, `a52178b`, [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py) and [`../../scripts/seed_harvest.py`](../../scripts/seed_harvest.py)):**
  - Added `recorded_input_buffers()` (extracting non-empty, non-zero `'b'` byte buffers from seed traces while ignoring pointee-only `'p'` records) and `_is_nonconst_input_buf()` in [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py). A non-`const` byte buffer paired with a length or length-pointer is promoted to a writable fuzz input copied from `case.rest` into a writable zeroed buffer (never mutating `const uint8_t *fuzz_buf`) when positive evidence exists: (1) threat model explicitly names it in `attacker_params`, (2) recorded traces passed a non-zero `'b'` buffer on entry, or (3) buffer/length formal names contain an input word (`_IN_BUF_WORDS`), while rejecting threat-model `output`/`caller_storage`/`trusted` roles, explicit output names (`_OUT_BUF_WORDS`), and non-length scalars (`port`, `byte_idx`).
  - Added `(byte *, len *)` pair support in [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py) (`_is_buf_len_ptr_pair`, `_param_roles`, `_target_claims_rest`, `_bind_call` for both unshared and shared sequence buffers) and safe pointer-to-length recording in [`../../scripts/seed_harvest.py`](../../scripts/seed_harvest.py) (`is_length_ptr`, record kind `"bp"`, and `fc_copy`-guarded `fc_mem_lenptr`). Pure outputs such as `Crypto_Get_Sdls_Ep_Reply(uint8_t *buffer, uint16_t *length)` and `pp_processed_frame`/`p_decrypted_length` remain zeroed.
  - Added `_HEAP_SIZE_PROBERS` (`malloc_usable_size`, `malloc_size`, `_msize`) reachability on `ApiInfo.heap_buffer_fns`: writable byte buffers for callers transitively querying heap allocation size (`Crypto_{AOS,TM}_ApplySecurity`, `Crypto_check_buffer_size`) use `calloc`/`free` instead of `static` arrays.
  - **Verification:** unit and Docker integration tests in [`../../tests/test_api_harness_nonconst_input.py`](../../tests/test_api_harness_nonconst_input.py) (verifying `fc_mem_lenptr` recording, writable in-place + `malloc_usable_size` execution under ASan/LSan/UBSan, and `frama-c -machdep gcc_x86_64 -main eva_main` parse), plus end-to-end build and execution on real CryptoLib (`cl-p0-api-seeds-20261123` with `--max-size 4096`) under `gcc -fsanitize=address,undefined -DTC_DEBUG -DTM_DEBUG -DAOS_DEBUG` (`BUILD_RC=0`, `RUN_RC=0`, zero ASan/UBSan errors): `seed_harvest.py write` produced 134 seeds including 1,786-byte TM/AOS frames, `Crypto_TM_ProcessSecurity` parsed `tvfn=0 scid=44 vcid=0` past `crypto_tm.c:1225` into SA lookup, `Crypto_AOS_ProcessSecurity` parsed `tvfn=1 scid=0 vcid=48` past `crypto_aos.c:1058` into SA lookup, `Crypto_TC_ProcessSecurity` passed `crypto_tc.c:1670` into TC header parsing and GVCID lookup, `Crypto_AOS_ApplySecurity` found operational SA index 10 and passed `Crypto_check_buffer_size` to FECF location `1784`, and `Crypto_TM_ApplySecurity` parsed `scid=0x002C (44)` into SA lookup.

### Symptom: `validate_artifacts.py` Fails With `$.api_cases: one layout per dispatch case`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 7b2a7ef; the probe loop now runs until no case is refused.
- **Cause (pipeline bug, fixed):** `api_harness.generate` drops the cases clang refuses, one probe round at a time, for at most `MAX_PROBE_ROUNDS` rounds. Clang stops after 20 errors by default, so a module with more refused cases than a few rounds can clear (nettle libhogweed: 88; `libnettle_a__sha256`: 5 more) ends with `api_cases` already filtered but `harness_<mod>.c` and `dispatch_cases` still holding a case that does not compile. The harness then fails to build, and the reach gate counted the broken case as reaching its function.
- **Fix:** `api_harness.probe_cases` lifts the error cap (`-ferror-limit=0` for clang, `-fmax-errors=0` for gcc), so one round finds every refused case, and the probe loop now runs until no case is refused (`MAX_PROBE_ROUNDS` is gone); the harness is rewritten after every drop, so the harness on disk and the sidecar always list the same cases (phase1-remediation `7b2a7ef`). If a run predates the fix, regenerate every sidecar the validator names. Expect the reach gate to report new gaps afterwards: the dropped cases never really reached their functions.

### Symptom: Harness Linking Fails With `undefined reference to '<internal_helper>'` (e.g. `syscall_iterate`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix present in api_harness.py (unions the module's required_sources); nothing open.
- **Cause:** `scripts/api_harness.py` collected source files defining only the public entry points called by the harness case, omitting internal helper translation units (e.g. `src/syscalls.perf.c` generated by gperf or auxiliary module sources) required by those API functions.
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `scripts/api_harness.py`. When building `--api` harness sources, the generator unions `dag.get("modules", {}).get(module, {}).get("required_sources")` into the harness compilation units so all internal helper sources of the module are linked into the harness binary.

### Symptom: A Dispatch Harness's Corpus Fills With Inputs That Run No Case (Empty Inputs, Selectors of Skipped Cases, Oversized Inputs)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. H3 fixed in the template and generator with a test.
- **Seen on:** H3 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), read from `templates/harness_dual.c.template` and `generate_harness._build_dispatch_invocation` (2026-11-10); not measured on a target run.
- **Cause:** `LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput` returned 0 for every input, also one longer than `MAX_INPUT_SIZE`, one without a selector byte, and one whose case a keep-going round skips (`-DFRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES`). LibFuzzer counts such an input as executed and may keep it, so later rounds mutate inputs of a case that is no longer compiled in.
- **Fix (fixed, H3):** the template returns `-1` for those inputs, which libFuzzer never adds to the corpus. Checked in `framac-analysis:latest` (Debian clang 19.1.7): a harness that rejects all but one prefix keeps only inputs with it. The dispatch code calls `FRAMAC_FUZZ_REJECT()` on an empty input and on a skipped case; a hand-written case whose own length guard turns the input away before any call can call it too. In `eva_main` the macro is empty. The generated `--api` cases call with zeroed arguments when the input is short, so they never reject. `tests/test_harness_reject.py` builds a dispatch harness with case 0 skipped and checks that no kept input selects it, is empty or is oversized.

### Symptom: Functions Named `Initialize`, `Freeze`, or `Destroyer` Falsely Selected as API Constructors/Destructors
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Word-boundary regexes are in api_harness.py on main (the 'uncommitted' note is stale).
- **Cause:** `_INIT_NAME_RE` and `_DTOR_NAME_RE` in `scripts/api_harness.py` matched bare `Init` and `Free|Destroy|Release` as unbounded substrings.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/api_harness.py`):** Bound CamelCase/PascalCase matches to C identifier word boundaries `(?:^|(?<=[a-z0-9_]))` and `(?=[A-Z0-9_]|$)`.

### Symptom: Multi-Dimensional and Array-Pointer Parameters Generate Broken Harness C Code
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix present in generate_harness.py on main.
- **Cause:** `_PARAM_DECL_RE` in `scripts/generate_harness.py` allowed at most one array dimension bracket pair, corrupting parameter names and declarations for multi-dimensional arrays (`int matrix[3][4]`) and array pointers (`int (*ptr)[10]`).
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/generate_harness.py`):** Allowed multiple bracket pairs in `_PARAM_DECL_RE`, added `_ARRAY_PTR_PARAM_RE`, decayed array parameters to pointers according to C99 rules, and added `_format_c_decl` helper for variable formatting.

### Symptom: String Literals Containing `main(...) {` Falsely Trigger `-Dmain` Redefinition
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix present: _source_defines_main takes the capture and masks comments and literals.
- **Cause:** `_source_defines_main` in `scripts/pipeline_make.py` and `scripts/generate_harness.py` scanned source files with a regex without masking string literals or checking build capture symbols.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/pipeline_make.py`, `scripts/generate_harness.py`):** Checked `defined_functions` from `build_capture.json` when available, and masked comments and string literals with `mask_comments_and_literals`.

### Symptom: Harness Dispatch Counting and Seed Binding Depend on Re-Parsing C Code
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. dispatch_case_count is in the sidecar schema and read by the consumers.
- **Cause:** `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py` and `scripts/seed_harvest.py` parsed C switch statements with regex rather than consuming structured sidecar metadata.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `schemas/harness_sidecar.schema.json`, `scripts/generate_harness.py`, `scripts/api_harness.py`, `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`, `scripts/seed_harvest.py`):** Added `dispatch_case_count` to sidecar schema and generator outputs, and updated consumers to read sidecar metadata first.

### Symptom: The Static Reach Gate Passes for an Entry Point the Harness Never Calls
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Token-based _used_identifiers is on main with tests, and the later direct-call fix (fd139c4) went further.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (no target run). `harness_reach.harness_roots` returned `stat`, `hash` and `rec_next` for a harness holding `struct stat st;`, `int hash = 0;` and the string `"rec_next failed"`.
- **Cause:** every identifier of the harness text outside comments counted as a root: variable and member names, string contents, code under `#if 0`, and bare prototypes. The `dynamic` gate caught such an entry point later, the static gate did not.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/harness_reach.py` `_used_identifiers`):** the harness is tokenized (`acsl_ast.tokenize_c_acsl`); literals, `#if 0` bodies, members, tags, declared names and file-scope prototypes are not roots. A function used as a value (`cb = on_msg;`, `register_cb(on_msg)`) and the body of a harness-local `#define` still count. Text the tokenizer rejects falls back to the old rule. **Behaviour change:** a harness that passed only through such a name now fails `reach-gate`; add the call or an exemption. Not run on a target. Tests: `tests/test_regex_hardening.py`.

### Symptom: Harness Root Extraction Dependent on Tokenizer Heuristics Instead of Clang AST
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Clang AST callee extraction is on main with the token fallback.
- **Cause:** `scripts/harness_reach.py` only used token heuristic filtering in `_used_identifiers`, which can misidentify non-callee identifier tokens or miss complex call contexts.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/harness_reach.py`):** Implemented `_callees_from_clang_ast` and `_callees_from_clang` to extract callees from Clang JSON AST when Clang is available, falling back to `_used_identifiers`.

### Symptom: After Exempting Dynamic-Reach Gaps, Every `coverage-<mod>` Still Fails With `reach gate failed ... dynamic: entry points never executed by the corpus`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix (regate_after_exempt, digest without reach_exempt) is on main with tests; the 'uncommitted' note is stale.
- **Seen on:** ptf-original run-2 (2026-11-10): after exempting ~300 dynamic gaps, `pipeline_make.py run coverage reach-dynamic` still failed every affected `coverage-<mod>` at the `$(GATE) --module <mod> --need reach` check, and `harness_reach_dynamic.json` kept showing the same gaps although the sidecars held the exemptions.
- **Cause (two effects compounding):**
  1. `phase0_gates.py check --need reach` reads the **recorded** dynamic rows in `phase0_gates.json`, not a fresh computation. The rows were recorded when the gaps were found; exempting functions does not re-record them.
  2. The make target that re-records them, `reach-dynamic-%`, depends on `coverage-%`, which the stale gate now blocks — a circular block. `make -j` also leaves **stale rows** in `harness_reach_dynamic.json` for modules whose `reach-dynamic-%` never ran (the report merges per-module rows across invocations), so the file can show gaps that are already exempted.
  A second trap: the corpus evolves between coverage measures, so a later measure can list a **new** never-executed entry point that the exemption batch did not cover (run-2: `dateAndtime`, then `WriteOptionalNodes`, each appearing one measure later).
- **Workaround used on run-2:** `harness_reach.py dynamic --module <mod>` run directly (not through Make) for every affected module, then `pipeline_make.py run coverage reach-dynamic` again. The documented step, `pipeline_make.py run reach-gate` after the exemptions, was skipped; it re-records every row and would have unblocked the run in one command.
- **Fixed pipeline bug (2026-11-10, uncommitted; `scripts/harness_reach.py`, `scripts/phase0_gates.py`):**
  1. `harness_reach.py exempt` re-records the gate rows the exemption can change (`regate_after_exempt`: the module, its unit and sub-modules, and every row the gate holds as failed) and prints the result under `gate` (`rechecked`, `still_failing`). No `reach-gate` run is needed after an exemption, so the circular block cannot form. `--function` can be repeated. Before the gate's first run nothing is recorded (`note`).
  2. The gate's message ends with the next step: `reach gate failed for <mod>: ...; repair the harness and run python3 $S/harness_reach.py check again, or record why with python3 $S/harness_reach.py exempt --module <mod> --function <fn> --reason <why> (which re-checks the gate)`.
  3. Found while testing 1: the sidecar is part of the harness digest, so **one** exemption made the next reach check treat the harness as repaired and drop **every** dynamic gap of the module, also those not exempted (`apply_dynamic`). The gaps came back only with the next coverage measure, which may be part of run-2's "second trap". The dynamic rows now record a digest without `reach_exempt` and `warnings` (`phase0_gates.harness_digest(..., ignore_exempt=True)`); a row written before the change still matches while its sidecar is unchanged.
  Tests: `tests/test_harness_reach.py` (`test_exempting_a_dynamic_gap_reopens_the_gate_without_a_reach_gate_run` and the three after it).

### Symptom: Seed Recorder Segfaults in Tests That Call a Variadic API Function (`Received signal 11` in `seed_harvest.json` `log_tail`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by branch merged as 4d214f5; the next entry holds the details.

- **Seen on:** minmea run-1, 2026-11-14 (found after the run, while diagnosing the CVE-2026-29974 miss above). `workspace/reports/seed_harvest.json` `log_tail`: `86%: Checks: 38, Failures: 0, Errors: 5`, with `tests.c:115:E:minmea_scan:test_minmea_scan_c:0: (after this point) Received signal 11 (Segmentation fault)` and the same for `test_minmea_scan_i` (`tests.c:258`), `test_minmea_scan_complex1` (`:357`), `complex2` (`:404`) and `complex3` (`:443`). Each of these calls `minmea_scan` with more than one vararg. The step still reported success, because a failing test suite is not an error for seed harvest.
- **Cause:** Frama-C translates `bool minmea_scan(const char *sentence, const char *format, ...)` into the model signature `_Bool(char *,char *,void **)` with a last formal named `__va_params`. [`../../scripts/build_callgraph_dag.py`](../../scripts/build_callgraph_dag.py) recognises that (`VA_PARAMS`; the DAG has `is_variadic: true`), but `split_signature` in [`../../scripts/seed_harvest.py`](../../scripts/seed_harvest.py) only treats a trailing `...` as variadic. So `plan_function` generated `__wrap_minmea_scan` with three fixed arguments; linked with `-Wl,--wrap`, it forwards only the first vararg to the real function, and a call with two or more varargs reads garbage pointers. The 63 `minmea_scan` records in `calls.jsonl` are the calls that survived (one vararg, or the first of several). `plan_params` in [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py) shares `split_signature`, so an `--api` harness would not skip such a function either: it would fuzz the format string and store input bytes where the vararg pointers go.
- **Fixed:** branch `fix/variadic-va-params-detection` (`72f1658`), merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-16 (`4d214f5`); the next entry describes the change. A workspace harvested before the merge keeps its unreliable `minmea_scan` records until seeds are harvested again.

### Symptom: `llvm-cov` Fails With `truncated coverage data` in `workspace/reports/coverage/<mod>.json`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The pipeline handles it automatically; the entry says no action is needed.
- **Cause:** a source includes a header inside a function body (CryptoLib `crypto.c:1674`, `#include <malloc.h>`). LLVM 19 then writes a coverage mapping `llvm-cov` cannot read.
- **Fix:** none needed. `run_eacsl_fuzz.py coverage` rebuilds once with those headers included first (`-include`), so the in-body include expands to nothing behind its include guard, and lists them in the report's `pre_included`. If the report still carries the error, no such header was found or one does not preprocess on its own with the unit's flags.

### Symptom: `reach-dynamic-<mod>` Fails With `dynamic: entry points never executed by the corpus` on Crashing API Cases, and `make coverage-<mod>` Deadlocks on `phase0_gates.py check`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both coupled bugs fixed and merged; the --allow-dynamic-gap flag was later replaced by S5 and is gone.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR run-6, 2026-11-14, module `libqcbor_a`. Dispatch case `129` (`QCBORDecode_Private_ExitBoundedLevel`) crashed immediately inside its callee `DecodeNesting_LevelUpCurrent` (`SEGV` / E-ACSL `mem_access`) during Phase 1 Round 1 (`workspace/crashes/libqcbor_a/crash-*`), was skipped from `workspace/corpus/libqcbor_a/` in rounds 2+ by `FRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES`, and showed `0` `.profraw` executions in `coverage-libqcbor_a`. Adding repair seeds to `workspace/corpus/libqcbor_a/` without modifying `harness_libqcbor_a.c` then failed at `coverage-libqcbor_a` (`phase0 gate: reach gate failed for libqcbor_a: dynamic: entry points never executed by the corpus`, `make: *** [pipeline.mk:163: coverage-libqcbor_a] Error 1`).
- **Cause:** two coupled pipeline bugs:
  1. Crashing inputs are saved under `workspace/crashes/<mod>/` rather than `workspace/corpus/<mod>/`, `FRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES` skips crashed dispatch cases in Round 2+, and raw `SIGSEGV` terminates before flushing `.profraw`. Because [`../../scripts/harness_reach.py`](../../scripts/harness_reach.py) (`check_dynamic`) checked only `.profraw` function execution counts in `workspace/reports/coverage/<mod>.json`, entry points that crashed on entry or inside a callee appeared unexecuted and reopened `workspace/reports/phase0_gates.json` with `DYNAMIC_GAP_ERROR`. Appending a new dispatch case to repair the gap also changed `dispatch_case_count` (`181 -> 182`), shifting `byte0 % N` across existing corpus inputs.
  2. In [`../../templates/pipeline.mk`](../../templates/pipeline.mk), `coverage-%` ran `$(GATE) --module $* --need reach`. Once `reach-dynamic-<mod>` wrote `DYNAMIC_GAP_ERROR` into `workspace/reports/phase0_gates.json`, adding seed files left `harness_digest` unchanged, so `coverage-<mod>` aborted before `coverage-<mod>` and `reach-dynamic-<mod>` could re-measure the new seeds.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline bug (`d5ae38a`, merged into `phase1-remediation` as `1cfef2d` on 2026-11-15):** first fixed and verified on the held branch `sup/QCBOR-reach-dynamic-gate`. Both items changed again afterwards: the crash credit of item 1 was tightened (`a923d42`, `f2492a1`, `ffca52b`; see "Dynamic Reach Credits Entry Points From Crash Files Recorded Under an Older Dispatch Case Count" below), and the `--allow-dynamic-gap` flag of item 2 was replaced by S5 (`c6cc73e`): a dynamic gap no longer fails `phase0_gates.check` (`_row_reach_ok`), and the flag is gone from `scripts/` and `templates/`. As first fixed:
  1. [`../../scripts/harness_reach.py`](../../scripts/harness_reach.py) (`check_dynamic`, `run_dynamic`, `--fuzz-dir`) inspects reproducible crash/leak artifacts in `workspace/reports/fuzz/<mod>.json` for unexempted, statically reached entry points with `0` `.profraw` executions and credits `crashed_entry_points` ([`../../schemas/harness_reach_dynamic.schema.json`](../../schemas/harness_reach_dynamic.schema.json)) when `failure.function` or a stack frame names the entry point directly, or when a dispatch `single` case (`first_byte % dispatch_case_count`) targets the entry point and `failure.function` lies in its transitive DAG callee closure (or is unattributed with empty `inits`, preventing setup crashes in `inits` helpers from crediting the entry point).
  2. [`../../scripts/phase0_gates.py`](../../scripts/phase0_gates.py) (`--allow-dynamic-gap`) and [`../../templates/pipeline.mk`](../../templates/pipeline.mk) (`coverage-%`) allow `coverage-<mod>` to run across an existing `DYNAMIC_GAP_ERROR` when `harness_digest` and `dag_digest` match, while keeping `fuzz-%` and `eva` blocked until `reach-dynamic-<mod>` passes. Covered by 5 new tests in [`../../tests/test_harness_reach.py`](../../tests/test_harness_reach.py) and [`../../tests/test_phase0_gates.py`](../../tests/test_phase0_gates.py).

### Symptom: `generate_harness.py --record-repair <sidecar> --reason ...` Combined With `--api` or `--entry-fn` Exits `0` Without Regenerating `harness_<mod>.c`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR run-6, 2026-11-14 (`19:46:48Z`), module `libqcbor_a`. Running `generate_harness.py --module libqcbor_a --header ... --api --dispatch-case '...' --record-repair workspace/harnesses/harness_libqcbor_a.json --reason '...'` incremented `repair_attempts` and exited `0` without updating `harness_libqcbor_a.c`, requiring a second CLI call.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in [`../../scripts/generate_harness.py`](../../scripts/generate_harness.py): the `if args.record_repair is not None:` handler printed the repair receipt and returned `0` unconditionally, ignoring `--api`, `--dispatch-case`, and all other generation flags passed on the same command line.
