- **Fix:** **Fixed** (branch `sup/QCBOR-fuzz-runtime-skip-cases`, commit `aeac81d`, merged into `phase1-remediation` as `436691f` on 2026-11-15 after run-6 ended): [`../../scripts/generate_harness.py`](../../scripts/generate_harness.py) keeps standalone `--record-repair` unchanged, and when generation flags accompany `--record-repair` it validates the sidecar path, module, CLI flags, and repair cap (`repair_attempts < max_repair_attempts`) upfront, records the repair, runs the requested regeneration (restoring the sidecar if regeneration fails), and includes `repair_recorded`, `repair_attempts`, and `exhausted` in the output JSON. Covered by `tests/test_generate_harness.py` on `sup/QCBOR-fuzz-runtime-skip-cases`.

### Symptom: `build-check` Fails with `use of undeclared identifier 'LOCALEDIR'` on Every Module That Uses a String-Valued `-D` Macro (sudo: 12 Modules)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged (784401d); the first former open-marker bug marker describes the run-directory workaround that the fix replaced.
- **Seen on:** sudo 1.9.18 run-1 (`run-1-e7bc881eee3c`), 2026-11-21, first `build-check`. `fuzz_<mod>.sanitizer_compile.log`: `workspace/work/plugins/sudoers/audit.c:160:31: error: use of undeclared identifier 'LOCALEDIR'`; the E-ACSL log of the same module says `Cannot resolve variable LOCALEDIR`. Target fuzzers made with `generate_harness.py --cppflags=<flags joined by spaces>` failed worse: `expected expression`, `use of undeclared identifier 'usr'/'local'/'share'` (the quotes of `-DLOCALEDIR="/usr/local/share/locale"` were lost in the join, so the macro became a bare path).
- **Cause:** `_merge_dag_module_cppflags` ([`../../scripts/generate_harness.py`](../../scripts/generate_harness.py), the `norm_flag.startswith("-D") and any(c in norm_flag for c in "\"' \\")` skip) deliberately leaves every `-D` macro whose value has a quote, a space or a backslash out of the harness's cppflags, so `harness_<mod>.json` has `-DSUDOERS_UID=0` but not `-DLIBDIR`, `-DLOCALEDIR`, `-D_PATH_SUDOERS`, `-D_PATH_CVTSUDOERS_CONF`, `-D_PATH_SUDO_CONF`, `-D_PATH_SUDO_LOGSRVD_CONF`, `-DDEFAULT_TEXT_DOMAIN`. sudo passes all of these on the compile line (callgraph_dag.json has them), and its sources use them (`bindtextdomain("sudoers", LOCALEDIR)`), so every module that compiles one of those files fails to build, with E-ACSL and with the sanitizers. Nothing in Phase 0 says a macro was dropped.
- **Fix (first, run-directory workaround only; fixed later, see below):** Worked around inside the run directory only: the seven macros were appended (under `#ifndef`) to `workspace/snapshot/generated/config.h`, and the `tf_*` harnesses were regenerated (`repair/gtf.py`) with the string-valued flags left out of `--cppflags`. The snapshot manifest digest was not recomputed. A pipeline fix would carry these macros into the harness (for example as a generated `#define` prelude next to the sidecar, one per module, not as shell-split flags) and report the ones it cannot carry.
- **Cause, in full (supervisor fixer):** three hops lost the macro.
  1. **The harness merge dropped it.** `generate_harness._merge_dag_module_cppflags` skipped every DAG `-D` that had a quote, added in `bdba3fa` as a workaround for hop 3.
  2. **A typed flag lost its quotes.** For a target fuzzer, `--cppflags='<the unit's cppflags>'` (as `skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md` says) joins the DAG list with spaces, and shlex splits that into `-DLOCALEDIR=/usr/local/share/locale`.
  3. **The E-ACSL frontend re-joined the flags bare.** `eacsl_toolchain.normalize_passthrough` did this. e-acsl-gcc passes `-E` to Frama-C's `-cpp-extra-args`, and Frama-C runs cpp through `/bin/sh`, which strips the quotes. This is CryptoLib's earlier `fopen(log.txt)`.
  - **What was not at fault:** Make, the sanitizer argv and Eva/WP all kept the quotes.
- **Fixed (supervisor, merged live into `main` as `784401d`, branch `sup/sudo-string-defines` commit `bbb8ec1`):**
  - **Harness merge:** it now carries string-valued `-D` flags, shell-quoted. `_restore_unquoted_macro` puts the quotes back on an unquoted twin the caller typed.
  - **E-ACSL frontend:** its flags are now shell words (`normalize_passthrough(..., shell_words=True)`). The `-e` and `-l` values stay bare.
  - **Not copied:** a `-D` value with whitespace, and a pre-C99 `-std=`.
  - **`skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md`:** updated, and its mirrors were updated in the same merge.
  - **Tests:** `tests/test_string_macro_flags.py` (`test_docker_fuzz_builds_with_string_macros` runs under `FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1`).
  - **Run-1:** its `#ifndef` appends to `config.h` are compatible with the fix.

### Symptom: `[kernel] User Error: failed to run: gcc -E -C ...` / `fatal error: parse_json.h: No such file or directory` for Another Unit's Source in a Target Fuzz Harness
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged; the remaining note (other unit's -D flags not carried) is tracked in the open sudo fifth-attempt entry.
- **Seen on:**
  - sudo run-1 (2026-11-21), modules `tf_fuzz_iolog_json` and `tf_fuzz_iolog_legacy` (unit `libsudo_iolog_a`).
  - The real error is the line above "failed to run" in the compile log's STDERR: `/framac-run/workspace/work/lib/eventlog/parse_json.c:42:10: fatal error: parse_json.h: No such file or directory`.
- **Cause:**
  - The harness's `sources` included `lib/eventlog/*.c`, which is not in the unit's `required_sources`, but its cppflags were only the unit's.
  - Every sudo unit harness that compiles `lib/util/*.c` also misses `libsudo_util`'s own `-DDEFAULT_TEXT_DOMAIN` and `-D_PATH_SUDO_CONF`. That is harmless on sudo: the first is behind `#ifdef`, and `pathnames.h` gives the second a default.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/sudo-harness-gaps` (`750fb00`), merged live into `main` as `126b489` during sudo run-1, at the user's request.
  - `include_context.source_include_dirs` returns the captured `-I` directories of each source's own unit; `generate_harness._capture_source_include_dirs` adds them to the harness, and `fuzz_build.with_capture_include_dirs` adds them to the fuzz compile for a source link repair brought in. Recorded as `compile.source_include_dirs`.
  - The other unit's `-D` flags are still not carried (harmless on sudo, see above).
  - Tests: `tests/test_sudo_harness_gaps.py`. The earlier run-dir workaround (`-Iworkspace/work/lib/eventlog` in the sidecar) is no longer needed after the harness is regenerated.

### Symptom: `build-check` Still Fails on Most sudo Modules After the Parse, Libc and String-Macro Fixes (run-1, third attempt, 2026-11-21 18:13)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** stale (what it describes no longer exists). A snapshot of build-check #3 whose six causes are each recorded and fixed in other entries; build-check #6 passed (ok: true, 24 modules on E-ACSL).
- **Seen on:** sudo 1.9.18 run-1 after the Phase 0 redo on `main` at `f97dd72` (all 25 units parse with Frama-C, string `-D` macros carried). `build_check.json` `ok: false`: `libprotobuf_c_a`, `libsudo_eventlog_a`, `sudo_noexec_so` build with E-ACSL; 7 modules `eacsl_fallback` (E-ACSL failed, sanitizers built); 13 modules `build_failed` (both failed). Distinct causes, each from the module's `fuzz_<mod>.compile.log` / `.sanitizer_compile.log`:
  - **`link_globals.c` includes a header without the build's `config.h`** (`cvtsudoers`, `visudo`, `libparsesudoers_a`, `tf_fuzz_sudo_conf`). `workspace/harnesses/fuzz_cvtsudoers_link_globals/link_globals.c` starts with `#include "../../work/plugins/sudoers/log_client.h"` and nothing before it, so `TAILQ_ENTRY`, `bool`, `sudo_dso_public`, `GETGROUPS_T` are unknown: Frama-C `log_client.h:52: syntax error before token: TAILQ_ENTRY` (included from link_globals.c line 4), clang `unknown type name 'bool'` / `'sudo_dso_public'` (`include/compat/stdbool.h:23: expected ';' after enum`). **Cause:** `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` link repair writes the defining header into `link_globals.c` ahead of any force-included or `config.h` header. Fixed later (`ef2f6fb`, see the `link_globals.c` entry below).
  - **A repaired global has a different initializer than the one in the module's own sources** (`liblogsrv_a`: `global var protobuf_c_empty_string at fuzz_liblogsrv_a_link_globals/link_globals.c:6 has different initializer than workspace/work/lib/protobuf-c/protobuf-c.c:103`; `tf_fuzz_policy`: `audit_msg ... fuzz_policy.c:50 has different initializer than audit.c:43`; `tf_fuzz_sudoers` `sudo_conv`, `tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif` `sudo_printf`). The Frama-C link of the E-ACSL build then aborts (`error encountered during linking`). **Cause:** link repair defines a global another unit defines although the module's sources, or the target fuzzer that the `tf_` harness compiles, already define it. Fixed for link repair (`ef2f6fb`) and partly for the target fuzzers (`126b489`, `7979a9f`); the rest is tracked in the `link_globals.c` entry below.
  - **E-ACSL output does not compile when an OpenSSL opaque type is tracked** (`sudo`, `sudoers_so`, `libsudo_util_so_0_0_0`, `sudo_intercept_so`, `sudo_sendlog`, `sudoreplay`, `visudo`, `tf_fuzz_iolog_*`, `tf_fuzz_sudo_conf`, `tf_fuzz_logsrvd_conf`): clang `fuzz_sudo.instrumented.c:354148: error: invalid application of 'alignof' to an incomplete type 'const EVP_MD'` (also `SSL`), from `__e_acsl_aligned((void *)sudo_debug_ret, _Alignof(EVP_MD const))`. **Cause (probable, not confirmed):** E-ACSL's alignment check on a pointer to a struct the headers only declare. Fixed later (`9217c47`, `tests/test_eacsl_incomplete_alignof.py`).
  - **E-ACSL output with a missing declaration** (`testsudoers`, `tsdump`): `fuzz_testsudoers.instrumented.c:3474: use of undeclared identifier 'sudoers_subsystem_names'` in `unsigned int sudoers_subsystem_ids[sizeof(sudoers_subsystem_names)...]`. Fixed later (`126b489`, `eacsl_toolchain.rewrite_forward_array_sizes`).
  - **`sudo_logsrvd`:** Frama-C `Incompatible declaration for iolog_open_all: Definitions of struct iolog_file are not isomorphic ... different number of fields in union`. Two different `struct iolog_file` definitions are compiled into one link. Fixed later (`126b489`, see the `iolog_open_all` entry below).
  - **`libsudo_iolog_a`, `tf_fuzz_iolog_*`:** `gcc -E` fails with `'parse_json.h' file not found with <angled> include` (the unit's cppflags lack `-Ilib/eventlog`, which `lib/eventlog/parse_json.c` needs). The earlier run-dir workaround is not in place any more (the clean regeneration dropped it).
  - **`libsudo_eventlog_a` E-ACSL** reported 193 `mangled line number section (bad file number)` lines in one attempt and then built.
- **Not worked around:** none of these is fixable from the run directory with the pipeline's own commands (the files are regenerated by the build, and `--fuzz-engine sanitizers` goes through the same link repair).

### Symptom: `Incompatible declaration for iolog_open_all: Definitions of struct iolog_file are not isomorphic ... different number of fields in union` (sudo `sudo_logsrvd`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged, verified on run-1 build-check #4.
- **Seen on:** sudo run-1 (2026-11-21), Phase 0 `build-check` #3, module `sudo_logsrvd`, Frama-C link of the E-ACSL build.
- **Cause:** the harness's automatic prelude included `sudo_iolog.h` above `config.h`. `struct iolog_file` has a union member that `config.h` macros (`HAVE_ZLIB_H`) decide, so the harness's translation unit saw a different struct than the unit's own sources, which include `config.h` first.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/sudo-harness-gaps` (`750fb00`), merged live into `main` as `126b489` during sudo run-1, at the user's request. `generate_harness.py` places the prelude following clang's `In file included from` chain for the header, so `config.h` and the includer's own headers come first. Test: `tests/test_sudo_harness_gaps.py`. Regenerating the harness after the Phase 0 rerun picks it up.
- **Verified** on sudo run-1's build-check #4 (2026-11-21): `sudo_logsrvd` builds with E-ACSL.

### Symptom: A Target Fuzzer Module of a Library Pulls Another Program's Sources and Ends `compile_error`: `use of undeclared identifier 'LOCALEDIR'`, `undefined reference to 'gc_add'`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed, merged and verified on run-1 build-check #5.
- **Seen on:** sudo run-1, Phase 0 build-check #4, 2026-11-21.
  - `tf_fuzz_sudo_conf` (driver `lib/util/regress/fuzz/fuzz_sudo_conf.c`, unit `libsudo_util_so_0_0_0`) went from 51 unit sources to 165.
  - `tf_fuzz_iolog_json`, `tf_fuzz_iolog_legacy`, `tf_fuzz_iolog_timing` (unit `libsudo_iolog_a`) and `tf_fuzz_logsrvd_conf` (unit `sudo_logsrvd`) pulled in sudo's front end (`src/*.c`) the same way.
  - Link repair then needed `src/sudo.c` and `src/exec_ptrace.c` (`undefined reference to 'gc_add'`, `exec_ptrace_intercept_supported`) and compiled them with the library's cppflags: `workspace/work/src/sudo.c:161:34: error: use of undeclared identifier 'LOCALEDIR'`. Five `tf_` modules ended `compile_error`.
- **Cause (pipeline bug):** for `--target-fuzzer`, `scripts/generate_harness.py` passes the whole driver text to `_merge_dag_harness_sources`, which took every identifier naming a DAG function and appended its lexer-module closure or module `required_sources`. Three names did the damage:
  - `disable_coredump`, only in the string `"Set disable_coredump %s"`, resolved to `src/limits.c` (module `sudo`) and added 19 files, `src/exec*.c` among them.
  - `output`, only in the comment `/* no output for fuzzers */`, resolved to `plugins/sudoers/display.c`.
  - `getenv`, a real call to libc's `getenv`, resolved to the interposer sudo's own program defines in `src/env_hooks.c`, a link unit the library never links.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/sudo-tf-snippet-sources` (`2007534`, `8c2edce`, `5c91c5f`, `1699aff`), merged live into `main` as `7979a9f` during sudo run-1, at the user's request.
  - `_merge_dag_harness_sources` masks comments and literals (`acsl_ast.mask_comments_and_literals`) before scanning identifiers.
  - With the harness's link unit known, a system function resolves only to a definition that unit's link can hold (`_link_module_sets`: the unit, what it links, and for a library the project libraries a program links next to it). A system function is one the driver's system headers declare (`gcc -E` line markers) or one a program or shared library of the target calls as an external function while linking none of the target's own definitions of it. Names left out are said on stderr and recorded in the sidecar's new `names_outside_link`.
  - Any other name still resolves wherever it is defined, because a fuzzer's own link can include another program's objects (sudo's `fuzz_sudoers` links visudo's `check_aliases.o`, `fuzz_sudoers_ldif` cvtsudoers' `parse_ldif.o`).
  - Replay on run-1's DAG (in the analysis image): `tf_fuzz_sudo_conf` 52 sources (was 166), `tf_fuzz_iolog_json` 82 (was 153), no `src/` file in any `tf_` module, non-`tf_` harnesses unchanged; nanopb and nginx harnesses unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_tf_snippet_sources.py`.
  - Verified in run-1's build-check #5 (2026-11-22): `tf_fuzz_sudo_conf`, `tf_fuzz_iolog_json`, `tf_fuzz_iolog_legacy`, `tf_fuzz_iolog_timing` and `tf_fuzz_logsrvd_conf` build with E-ACSL.
  - Limits: a real call to another program's project function still pulls that program in; without a link unit, or when `gcc -E` of the driver fails, only the masking and the external-callee signal apply; literals inside preprocessor directives are not masked.

### Symptom: Link Repair Adds a File That Defines What the Build Already Defines: `multiple definition of 'open_sudoers'` (sudo `tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed, merged and verified on build-check #6.
- **Seen on:** sudo run-1, Phase 0 build-check #5 (2026-11-22), `tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif`, `build_failed` in both engines. `workspace/harnesses/fuzz_tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif.sanitizer_compile.log`: `/framac-run/workspace/work/plugins/sudoers/cvtsudoers.c:786: multiple definition of 'open_sudoers'; /tmp/fuzz_sudoers_ldif-8ff175.o:/framac-run/workspace/work/plugins/sudoers/regress/fuzz/fuzz_sudoers_ldif.c:39: first defined here`.
- **Cause:** link repair round 0 added `plugins/sudoers/cvtsudoers.c` because it is the only definer of `log_warnx` (a function) and `filters` (a global), which `cvtsudoers_merge.c` and its siblings need. A linked file brings every name it defines, and `cvtsudoers.c` also defines `open_sudoers`, which the target fuzzer defines itself.
  - The sidecar's `target_fuzzer.link.excluded_sources` (`a0ecb75`) only lists files that were in the initial sources.
  - The DAG has no record of the driver, because the build does not capture it. So nothing saw the clash.
- **Fix:** fixed in `scripts/fuzz_build.py` (commits `0ecf165` and `d158912`, branch `sup/sudo-link-redef-fwdtags`, merged live to `main` as `d158912`).
  - Before adding a file, `repair_sources_for_link` compares what the file defines with what the build already defines:
    - what the file defines comes from `_file_external_definitions`: DAG functions, build-only functions and model globals;
    - what the build defines is the linked files' names, grown through the round, plus the target fuzzer's own names (`_target_fuzzer_own_definitions`: the sidecar's excluded symbols, plus `generate_harness.target_fuzzer_definitions` run on the driver).
  - A clashing file is treated like an excluded one. The symbol is stubbed in `link_stubs.c` / `link_globals.c`, with a `reason` naming each clash and its current definer. Without a build directory it is recorded unresolved.
  - `main` counts too, because `-Dmain=` gives every unit's `main` the same name.
  - A global that another linked file also defines is not a clash. Both builds use `-fcommon`, and the model has no initializer flag. **Limit:** two linked files that both *initialize* one global are still not caught.
  - In this module, `log_warnx` becomes an aborting stub, so an input that reaches it gives a `link_stub_aborts` entry, not a finding.
  - Replay of round 0 on a copy of run-1: `cvtsudoers.c` is no longer added, and no name is defined twice. Over all the link repairs that `bcheck5.out` records (11 modules), only this module's round changes.
  - Tests: `tests/test_sudo_link_redef_fwdtags.py` (Part A).
  - Verified in run-1's build-check #6 (2026-11-22, `ok: true`): `tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif` builds with E-ACSL. Its sources no longer include `cvtsudoers.c`, and its build record stubs `log_warnx` and `filters` with the reason `linking plugins/sudoers/cvtsudoers.c would define a second time what the build already defines: open_sudoers (defined in the target fuzzer plugins/sudoers/regress/fuzz/fuzz_sudoers_ldif.c)`.

### Symptom: `seed_harvest.py record` Returns `no_calls_recorded` With Every Relinked Executable Exiting 127 (`error while loading shared libraries: libsudo_util.so.0`) and 167 API Functions Skipped With `return type 'int )'`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both bugs fixed, merged and verified on the Phase 0 redo.
- **Seen on:** sudo run-1 (2026-11-21, quick profile, no test command). `seed_harvest.json`: `status: no_calls_recorded`, `source: executables`, 8 relinked executables (`src/.libs/sudo`, `plugins/sudoers/.libs/visudo`, ...). Every run: `returncode 127`, `log_tail` `/framac-scratch/tree/src/.libs/sudo: error while loading shared libraries: libsudo_util.so.0: cannot open shared object file: No such file or directory`. `functions.skipped`: 190 of 609 planned, of which 167 are `return type 'int )'`, `'void )'`, `'_Bool )'`, `'unsigned int )'`, `'unsigned long )'`, `'long )'`, `'long long )'`. Same result on the Phase 0 redo.
- **Cause (two pipeline bugs, both in `scripts/seed_harvest.py`):**
  - `_run_executables` and the test-command run started libtool's uninstalled `.libs/<prog>` binaries with no `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` covering the tree's own shared libraries (`lib/util/.libs/libsudo_util.so.0`). libtool's real binaries carry only the install `rpath`, so the dynamic loader cannot find the tree's libraries and nothing runs.
  - `_ATTR_RE = r"\b__attribute__\s*\(\(.*?\)\)"` was non-greedy, so on the model signature `unsigned long __attribute__((__visibility__("default")))(char *,...)` (sudo's `sudo_dso_public`) it stopped at the first `))` and left a stray `)`. `split_signature` then returned the return type `unsigned long )`, and `classify_type` rejected it. Every exported function of a library that marks its API with a visibility attribute was skipped.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/sudo-seed-harvest` (`6dfe3c0`), merged live into `main` as `63caeeb` during sudo run-1. The merge went around another session's uncommitted `seed_harvest.py` hunk (the recorder's `fc_tok` frame check), which stays uncommitted.
  - `_strip_attributes` replaces `_ATTR_RE`. It matches each `__attribute__((...))` to its balanced `)`, including nested parentheses, string arguments, several attributes in a row and attributes on parameter types. An unbalanced group is left as it is, so the signature is not understood rather than misread. `_norm_type` and `split_signature` use it; `api_harness.py` and `variadic_calls.py` import them. On the run-1 model the plan goes from 609 to 776 functions; only 17 variadic functions and 6 empty signatures stay skipped (see "Functions of a Flex-Generated Source Missing From the Phase 0 Model" in [`./phase0-build-and-model.md`](./phase0-build-and-model.md)).
  - New `shared_library_dirs` / `library_env`. The executable runs and the test command get `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` set to the directories of the capture's `shared_lib` link outputs (or, when the capture saw none, the tree's directories with a `*.so` / `*.so.*`), before the recipe's own value. The report records them as `library_path` (new optional schema property). The relink keeps the build's environment.
  - Not verified: whether `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` on a test command that rebuilds or runs tools (`make check` on a target that builds its own `libz.so.1`) shadows a toolchain library. libtool's own test wrappers set the same path.
  - Tests: `tests/test_seed_harvest_sudo.py`, including a Docker test that records a libtool-layout executable (install-only rpath) end to end. The same commit fixes `security_props._split_params` (see [`./security-properties.md`](./security-properties.md)).
  - Verified on sudo run-1's Phase 0 redo (2026-11-21 20:15): `status: ok`, 160 calls recorded (98 unique) across 8 library directories, 17 of 782 planned functions skipped (was 190 of 609).
  - Not re-run on sudo itself: run-1 recorded its seeds before the merge.

### Symptom: `seed_harvest.py record` Returns `no_test_units` Although the Recipe Has a Test Command
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in seed_harvest.record (every executable relinked when a test command exists).
- **Cause:** only executables the name rule calls tests were relinked with recorders. libpng's `ctest` runs `pngtest`, `pngvalid`, `pngstest`, ..., none of which the rule recognizes (libpng `a70313dbb`, run-3; `docs/progress/2026-11-09-libpng-harness-generation-bugs.md`, defect D).
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `scripts/seed_harvest.py` (`record`): with a test command every executable is relinked and the test command decides which ones run; `test_units` still lists the ones named as tests, and `executables` lists all of them.

### Symptom: Seed Recording Records Only a Handful of Calls (mpack: 3 Calls) Although the Test Suite Runs Thousands of API Calls
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged, verified on mpack.
- **Seen on:** mpack run-3 (2026-11-17). The test command `python3 test/unit/configure.py && ninja -f .build/unit/build.ninja more` ran the whole unit suite, but `seed_harvest.json` recorded 3 calls.
- **Cause:** the recorder's `fc_depth` counter was not longjmp-safe. mpack's unit tests longjmp out of a wrapped API call: the test framework's assert handler leaves through an expected `mpack_assert` failure. When a test longjmps out of a wrapped call, that wrapper's `fc_leave` never runs, so `fc_depth` stays above 0 for the rest of the process. From then on, every later call counts as nested and is not recorded.
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `84f76b6`, merged into `phase1-remediation` live during mpack run-3, in `scripts/seed_harvest.py` (`RECORDER_RUNTIME`, `wrapper_source`):
  - Each wrapper writes a unique token into its own frame.
  - The runtime keeps a per-thread stack of live wrappers (`FC_STACK` 256).
  - On entry, it drops every entry whose frame is not strictly above the current one, or whose token was overwritten.
  - A call is outermost when the stack is empty.
  - Test: `tests/test_seed_harvest_longjmp.py::test_recorder_records_outermost_calls_after_a_longjmp`, gcc and clang, Linux only, skipped on Windows.
  - On real mpack the recording went from 3 calls to 55,714 (9,641 unique), covering 189 of 342 functions. The rest are `MPACK_INLINE` header functions, which the recorder cannot wrap.
  - A run that recorded before the fix must rerun `seed_harvest.py record`, then regenerate its `--api` harnesses so the seeds bind to cases.

### Symptom: A Module's Fuzz Campaign Starts From an Empty Corpus Although the Target Has Tests or Shipped Corpora, and No Log Says Why
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed (K1) and merged.
- **Cause:** in `templates/pipeline.mk`, the `seeds` recipe and the two seed lines of `fuzz-%` (`seed_harvest.py write`, `fuzz_assets.py write`) start with `-`, and the `seeds` loop ends in `|| true`. Seeds are optional, so the recipes are non-blocking on purpose, but a failed write was invisible. Both writes stopped at the first exception with a traceback and exit 1, after deleting the module's old inputs, so `workspace/seeds/<mod>/` or `workspace/imported_corpus/<mod>/` could hold part of the new inputs while their per-module reports kept their old content. `fuzz_assets.py write` also raised on a malformed `workspace/reports/fuzz_assets.json`; `seed_harvest.py write` silently skipped a requested module with no sidecar. The fuzz report said nothing about seeds.
- **Fix (fixed, K1 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), commit 93446b9):** `scripts/seed_harvest.py` and `scripts/fuzz_assets.py` `write` record each module's outcome (`ok`, `skipped` with a reason, `failed` with the exception) in `workspace/reports/seed_status/<writer>/<mod>.json`. An exception fails only that module, and the write exits 1; the Make lines keep `-`, so a seed failure never fails the target. `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py campaign` copies both statuses into `workspace/reports/fuzz/<mod>.json` as `seeds: {status, reason, writers}`; a writer that recorded no status while the campaign reads `--extra-corpus` counts as `failed`. Read `seeds.status` before tuning a module whose corpus is empty. A status file left over from an older run is shown as is, with its `generated_at`.

### Symptom: A Crash Artifact No Longer Reproduces After the Harness Gains a Dispatch Case (`not_reproduced`, although the target defect is real)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by the identity selector.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15. Candidate `src/UsefulBuf.c#UsefulOutBuf_Compare::e_acsl_rte::0e34c586b3` (crash captured with a 181-case harness) replayed as `not_reproduced` after the harness was regenerated with one extra `--dispatch-case` (182 cases): the artifact's selector byte 191 met `191 % 181 = 10` at capture time and `191 % 182 = 9` afterwards, so it drove a different case and never called `UsefulOutBuf_Compare`. The same shift explains the earlier execution dropout of `case 74` after repair case `181` was appended during the `reach-dynamic` repair. Verifier `verify-usefuloutbuf_compare_b939358da2-4` also hit it. (Two entries recorded this bug, one by the run agent and one by the first supervisor. They were merged here.)
- **Cause:** `generate_harness.py` selects the dispatch case as `fuzz_buf[0] % <case count>` ([`../../templates/harness_dual.c.template`](../../templates/harness_dual.c.template)). Adding a case appends it at the end, but the count changes, so every artifact whose selector byte is at or above the old count (and every seed) silently addresses another case. The harness sidecar records the cases but not the count the corpus was captured under, and the campaign report does not record it either, so nothing detects the mismatch.
- **Fix:** **Pipeline gap, fixed (`45c7a5f`, see Status below).** Suggested at the time: decode a fixed-width selector (`uint8_t` up to 256 cases, `uint16_t` above) and reject out-of-range values (`if (sel >= N) return 0;`) instead of `sel % N`, so that appended cases never remap old inputs; or record the case count (and a harness digest) in `workspace/reports/fuzz/<mod>.json` and refuse or flag a replay whose digest differs; or map the selector to a stable case id (a per-case key written into the artifact at capture time); or require a re-run of `fuzz-<mod>` after any harness regeneration and say so in `wp_scheduler.py`'s repair actions. Workaround used in run-6: after the harness repair, the module's campaign was re-run on the repaired harness (`run build fuzz coverage reach-dynamic ingest`), so the corpus and the artifacts in `workspace/reports/fuzz/libqcbor_a.json` address the current 182-case harness; verifiers of the older artifacts rebuilt witnesses from the harness source and listed them in `record.reproducers`, which `ingest-verifier` replays.
- **Status (2026-11-17): fixed** in `45c7a5f` (2026-11-15), [`../../scripts/generate_harness.py`](../../scripts/generate_harness.py) `_build_dispatch_invocation`. The first byte is the case index (`dispatch selector: identity`), and a byte at or above the case count runs no case, so an appended case no longer remaps stored inputs. The sidecar records `dispatch_selector`; a harness without it is still decoded modulo its case count. Tests: `tests/test_dispatch_selector.py`.

### Symptom: A Dynamic Reach Gap Can Be Fixed in the Corpus but Not Re-Measured (`phase0 gate: reach gate failed for <mod>: dynamic: entry points never executed by the corpus`, then `coverage-<mod>` refuses to run)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by S5; a dynamic gap no longer fails the gate.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15. `reach-dynamic` failed on `QCBORDecode_Private_ExitBoundedLevel` (0 executions: the generated case calls it on a context with no bounded level, so it returns early). After adding a dispatch case plus corpus inputs selecting it, `pipeline_make.py run coverage` stopped at the same gate message and never re-measured.
- **Cause:** `coverage-<mod>` (and `fuzz-<mod>`, `eva-<mod>`) run `phase0_gates.py check --need reach`, and a recorded dynamic gap is kept failing until the harness digest changes (`phase0_gates.harness_digest`: the sidecar without `reach_exempt`/`warnings`, plus `harness_<mod>.c`) or the entry point is exempted. A change to `workspace/corpus/<mod>/` or `workspace/seeds/<mod>/` does not change that digest, so a legitimate corpus/seed repair can never replace the failing row.
- **Fix:** **Fixed in S5 (D2, D18), on `p1/s5-dynamic-gate`.** A dynamic gap no longer fails `phase0_gates.check` (`_row_reach_ok`, the row's `origin`); `--allow-dynamic-gap` is gone from `scripts/phase0_gates.py` and `templates/pipeline.mk`; `ledger.py gate-reach` holds the module's planned units out of WP instead, and `wp-replay` and `wp_scheduler.py init` call it. Fuzz, coverage and Eva of the module run on. (The earlier suggestion was: let `coverage-<mod>` run when the only failing reason is `dynamic:`, which the fix now does for every target.) Crash-attributed dynamic reach was already on `phase1-remediation` (`d5ae38a`). Workaround used in run-6: re-express the repair as a harness change (a `--dispatch-case` that peeks the item type, then enters and exits a real map or array, which reaches `QCBORDecode_Private_ExitBoundedLevel` through its documented precondition), so the digest changed and `coverage`/`reach-dynamic` could run; two seed inputs were added under `workspace/corpus/libqcbor_a/` (selectors 74 and 181) so the new case and a case that only seeds reached are selected.

### Symptom: A Recorded Sequence Case the Compile Probe Refuses Vanishes Without a Trace (`array type 'unsigned char[16]' is not assignable`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed on main with regression tests.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR run-1 inputs (2026-11-22, `libqcbor_a`), reproduced by the supervisor's fixer on a scratch copy. The 48-call recorded encoder chain was refused with `harness_libqcbor_a.c:1114: error: array type 'unsigned char[16]' is not assignable` on `s1 = UsefulBuf_FromSZ((char *)c58a0); (void)QCBOREncode_Finish(..., (struct q_useful_buf_c *)s1)`.
- **Cause:** `_sequence_case` assigned a by-value builder's result (`api.byval_inits`) to a shared byte buffer `s1`. `generate()` then dropped the case. A sequence case names no entry point, so nothing reached `api_skipped` or the result.
- **Fix:** **Fixed on `main`:** `sup/QCBOR-stateful-sequence-cases` (`5743663`) was merged through `feat/phase1-qcbor-lessons` in `ecc274b` (2026-11-23).
  - The builder now assigns through its type (`*(T *)s1 = builder(...)`).
  - `generate()` lists every sequence or lifecycle case the probe drops in `api_dropped_cases` (kind, calls, entry if any, first error), with a warning on stderr.
  - Tests: `tests/test_p2a_schema_trust_harness.py::test_a_by_value_builder_of_a_shared_buffer_assigns_through_its_type` and `::test_generate_reports_a_dropped_sequence_case_and_keeps_both_copies_equal`.
  - On the QCBOR reproduction the encoder chain now compiles and no case is dropped.

### Symptom: Dynamic Reach Credits Entry Points From Crash Files Recorded Under an Older Dispatch Case Count, or From Crashes That Never Reached the Entry
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged with tests; the only open items were in the held-branch version.
- **Seen on:** post-run review of the held branch `sup/QCBOR-reach-dynamic-gate` (`d5ae38a`, not merged), 2026-11-15, replayed on a copy of QCBOR run-6 data. With the right case count (182) the attribution is correct: of 42 reproducible artifacts, 25 entries would be credited and all are named in the artifacts' `replay_log_tail`. With `dispatch_case_count` 181 the same crash files credit `QCBORDecode_GetInt64ConvertAllInMapN`, `QCBORDecode_GetTBigFloatBigMantissaInMapN` and `QCBOREncode_Private_CloseMapOrArray`, which those inputs never executed (first bytes 239, 250, 255), and drop three correct entries. Run-6 itself went from 181 to 182 cases and then ran `coverage` and `reach-dynamic` without re-fuzzing.
- **Cause:** **Fixed pipeline bug (`ec1924f`, `a923d42`, `f2492a1`, `ffca52b`; see Status below; found on the then held branch plus the fuzz report).** As found: `workspace/reports/fuzz/<mod>.json` records no harness digest or case count per artifact, and `_crashed_entry_points` in `scripts/harness_reach.py` on the branch decodes the first byte with the current sidecar's count. The same function has four more attribution gaps, each shown by a scenario test during the review:
  - when the failure function is known, the case's `inits` are not consulted, so a crash in an init helper's callee that is also in the entry's callee closure credits the entry (98 of QCBOR's 156 single cases with inits have overlapping closures, for example `QCBORDecode_Finish` / `QCBORDecode_Init` through `UsefulInputBuf_Init`);
  - a UBSan crash in the harness body is recorded as `LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput`, treated as unknown, and credited for a single case without inits;
  - a `__gen_e_acsl_<entry>` failure (a precondition check before the body runs) credits `<entry>`;
  - `sequence` and `custom` cases and non-dispatch harnesses are never credited, so the original deadlock remains for them. `custom` is what a `--dispatch-case` repair adds (run-6 case 181).
  - The branch's "stack frame" test uses a `frames` field that `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` never writes; real reports carry `replay_log_tail`.
- **Fix:** none yet. The `coverage-%` half of the branch (`--allow-dynamic-gap`) is sound and independent. Proposed for the crash half: record the harness digest and case count in the fuzz report and per artifact and refuse artifacts from another harness; consult `inits` when the failure function is known; do not credit harness-body or `__gen_e_acsl_` failures.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`ec1924f`, `a923d42`, `f2492a1`, gate message `769619e`).
  - `run_eacsl_fuzz.py campaign` stamps `harness_digest` and `dispatch_case_count` on the report and on each crash artifact it found (an artifact carried over from an earlier campaign keeps its own values), and records the replay stack as `frames`.
  - `harness_reach._crashed_entry_points` uses an artifact only when both values equal the current harness's (an unstamped report only when it is newer than `harness_<m>.c`). It consults the case's init helpers also when the failure function is known, never credits a failure in the harness body, and does not credit `<fn>` for an E-ACSL wrapper failure that precedes the body (a Precondition, a failure named `__gen_e_acsl_<fn>`, or a failure under `<fn>`'s name whose stack shows only the wrapper; only a Postcondition proves the body ran). `custom` and `sequence` cases are credited only on direct evidence (the failure function or a stack frame is the entry). Frames are matched by exact name. A missing `reproducible` does not count.
  - A crash that did not count is explained in the row's `crash_notes`, and the gate's reason quotes up to three of them.
  - Replay on a trimmed copy of run-6 (`tests/fixtures/qcbor_run6_reach/`): the 25 correct credits are kept at 182 cases; nothing is credited at 181. Tests: `tests/test_harness_reach_crash_credit.py`, `tests/test_fuzz_harness_identity.py`, `tests/test_phase0_gates.py::test_the_reach_gate_reason_says_why_crash_inputs_did_not_count`.
  - The two weak paths of the first version are closed (`ffca52b`): a crash at an unknown location credits nothing, and an E-ACSL failure under the entry's own name without any stack frame credits nothing (sanitizer kinds and postconditions keep the credit). Both leave a note. On the run-6 fixture all 32 credits rest on a stack frame (31) or a direct failure with a stack (1), so nothing is lost there. Kept: a failure in the entry's callee closure but in no init helper's, for a `single` case, as the fallback when a tail call removed the entry's frame.
  - The dispatch selector is now the case index itself (`45c7a5f`): `switch (harness_selector)`, a first byte at or above the case count runs no case, and the sidecar, the fuzz report and each artifact record `dispatch_selector: "identity"`. Appending a case no longer changes the case an existing input selects. A harness without the field decodes modulo the case count, and an artifact found under the other encoding is not used. `generate_harness.py` refuses more than 256 dispatch cases (split with `--scope-file`; `--api` keeps 255 and lists the rest in `api_skipped`). Test: `tests/test_dispatch_selector.py`.
  - Checked in `framac-analysis:latest` on a 3-case module in a run-directory layout, through the real writers: only first bytes 0 to 2 reach a target function; the fuzz and Eva reports carry the digest `phase0_gates.harness_digest` computes; keep-going rounds crash in case 0, then 1, then run clean; the two crashing entries are credited and the third is not; after a harness edit nothing is credited; Eva reaches all three cases.
  - **For a resumed run:** regenerating the harness switches it to the identity selector, and an input whose first byte is at or above the case count then selects no case. That holds for corpus inputs and for recorded crash files: 5 of run-6's 42 reproducible crash files (first bytes 239, 239, 250, 250, 255, against 182 cases) would stop reproducing. Either keep the existing harness (the modulo selector is still decoded) or regenerate, harvest seeds and fuzz again, and get new reproducers for the findings that rest on those files. There is no remapping tool.

### Symptom: `--api` Skips `ares_dns_class_fromstr` With `pointer to an anonymous struct`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both fixes (anonymous enum, nested const) merged with tests; no open marker.
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, 2026-11-16, `libcares_a__dnsrec`. After the out-parameter constructor fix, `ares_dns_class_fromstr`, `ares_dns_rec_type_fromstr` and `ares_dns_record_query_get` are still in `api_skipped`.
- **Cause:** the parameter is a pointer to an anonymous enum typedef: `ares_dns_class_t *`, which the model shows as `enum __anonenum_ares_dns_class_t_7 *` (a Frama-C tag that no header defines).
  - `_spell_params` in [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py) rejected any pointer whose pointee contains `__anon`, and its reason said "anonymous struct" even for an enum.
- **Fix:** fixed and merged into `phase1-remediation` (2026-11-16), from branch `sup/c-ares-api-anon-enum` (`1a7799f`). Verified by the supervisor: `test_api_harness_anon_enum.py` and `test_api_harness_outparam.py` gave 15 passed, 2 skipped; the fixer also ran the Docker test, 7 of 7 passed.
  - The layout probe records each parameter that points to an enum through a typedef name, as the declaration writes it (`enum_pointer_spellings`, carried as `Declared.enum_pointers` into `ApiInfo.enum_ptrs`).
  - `_bind_call` declares the object and casts it through that typedef (`ares_dns_class_t c0a0[1]`, `(ares_dns_class_t *)c0a0`).
  - With no typedef name, the function stays skipped, with `pointer to an anonymous enum <tag>: ...`. There is no `int` stand-in, because an enum's size need not be `int`'s, and a write through it could become an overflow made by the harness.
  - Pointers to anonymous structs keep their reason. A pointer to an anonymous union now says union.
  - On a copy of run-1's inputs, dnsrec goes from 59 entry points with a case and 3 skipped to 62 with none skipped. `ares_dns_record_query_get` binds all five arguments and joins the record's lifecycle case.
  - Tests: `tests/test_api_harness_anon_enum.py`, including a Docker test that takes the AST from real clang, compiles and fuzzes 100k runs under ASan and UBSan.
- **Workaround on an older checkout without the fix:** the run's hand-written `--dispatch-case` (`record_case_1` in `run-1/gen_harnesses.py`). It can be dropped once the run's scripts have the fix.
- **Related pipeline bug (nested `const` dropped):** fixed and merged into `phase1-remediation` (2026-11-16), from branch `sup/c-ares-api-nested-const` (`95cab11`, `1265378`). Verified by the supervisor: the three `test_api_harness_*` files gave 22 passed, 3 skipped; the fixer ran the Docker tests, 105 passed.
  - **Symptom:** the model drops `const` inside nested pointers, so the cases cast to `char **` or `unsigned char **` where the header declares `const char **` or `const unsigned char **`.
  - **Affected:** `ares_dns_record_query_get` (its single case and the record's lifecycle case), `ares_dns_rr_get_opt` and `ares_dns_rr_get_opt_byid`. `ares_dns_write` takes `unsigned char **buf` and was not affected.
  - **Impact:** clang only warns (`-Wincompatible-pointer-types-discards-qualifiers`), and the compile probe's `-w` hid it. gcc 14 in the analysis image refuses all four calls even under `-w`, so a gcc build of the whole harness failed.
  - **Fix, spelling:** the layout probe records each parameter that points to a qualified pointer as the header writes it (`qualified_pointer_spellings` → `Declared.qualified_pointers` → `ApiInfo.qual_ptrs`). `_bind_call` casts to that type and declares the object with its element type (`const char * c0a2[1]`, `(const char **)c0a2`). The model's pointee still decides how the object is filled, so no input layout changes. Anonymous types are never spelled.
  - **Fix, compile probe (clang only):** `-w` is replaced with `-Wno-error -Werror=incompatible-pointer-types-discards-qualifiers`, and the target's own `-Werror*` and `-pedantic-errors` are dropped. That error counts only on case lines, so a target header's own inline code cannot fail the harness. Without a layout probe, the model's `(char **)` stays and the probe skips the case with its error. A hand-written `--dispatch-case` that drops a nested `const` is now refused too.
  - **On run-1's inputs:** still 64 dispatch cases for 62 entry points, none skipped, and identical `api_cases`. Before the fix the harness had 4 clang diagnostics and 4 gcc 14 errors; after it, none.
  - **Tests:** `tests/test_api_harness_nested_const.py` (8 tests, including a Docker test: real clang AST, a `-Werror=` compile, a gcc compile, `probe_cases` refusing the old casts, and 100k LibFuzzer runs under ASan and UBSan). `test_api_harness_anon_enum.py` now expects `(const char **)`.

---

## From [`./kernel-and-stubs.md`](./kernel-and-stubs.md): Frama-C Kernel Parsing, Preprocessing & Stubs

### Symptom: `User Error: syntax error` on `fd_set`, `sizeof on incomplete type 'struct ip_mreq'`, or `Cannot find field sin_zero in type struct sockaddr_in` in the Phase 0 Model Parse
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** stale (what it describes no longer exists). The repairs it names (fc_compat_types.h includes, a Dockerfile RUN step) no longer exist: the gap is closed by opam patch 26-libc-socket-header-gaps.patch (upstream MR !45) carried by the image.
- **Seen on:** ptf-original run-1, unit `src/LibPtfCom/libPtfCom.a` (`LitePing.c:111`, `LowLevelPortHandler.c:126` and `:237`), 2026-11-10. The whole unit fell back to GCC, which would have blocked Eva and WP for the run's only module.
- **Cause:** three `__fc_libc` gaps. Frama-C's `<sys/socket.h>` pulls neither `<sys/types.h>` nor `<sys/select.h>`, so `fd_set` is undeclared (glibc reaches it through `sys/types.h -> sys/select.h`). `__fc_inet.h` has `ip_mreqn` and `ipv6_mreq` but not glibc's `__USE_MISC` `struct ip_mreq`. And no `__fc_libc` header defines `sockaddr_in`'s `sin_zero[8]` padding field. All three are still present on upstream `master` `3dff90fc70`: drafted with a validated libc patch as Issue 26, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-libc-socket-header-gaps.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-libc-socket-header-gaps.md), filed as [pub/frama-c#2814](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/work_items/2814) with merge request [!45](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/merge_requests/45). The repairs below can go once a release carries it.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted):** `fd_set` and `struct ip_mreq` are repaired in [`../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h`](../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h) (`#include <sys/select.h>` and `#include <netinet/in.h>` before the missing definitions, `__fc_libc` branch only). `sin_zero` cannot live there (a force-included redefinition of `sockaddr_in` collides with `__fc_inet.h`), so the analysis image patches the installed `__fc_inet.h` in place: a `RUN` step in the `framac-analysis` stage of the `Dockerfile` appends `unsigned char sin_zero[8];` to `struct sockaddr_in` (asserts on the upstream shape first). Rebuild the image after pulling.

### Symptom: `User Error: Unable to compute offset .arr[i] in type struct s: Index is not constant`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The pipeline wrapper (fc_cpp_wrapper.py offsetof rewrite) is on main; the upstream request is tracked under docs/upstream and needs no pipeline action.
- **Cause:** Frama-C's `<stddef.h>` (and gcc's, in E-ACSL host-header builds) defines `offsetof` as `__builtin_offsetof`, which the kernel folds to a constant at parse time. GCC also accepts a non-constant array index in the member designator (`offsetof(type, member[i])`); Frama-C aborts on it. Found on `libseccomp` (`src/arch.c:54`, `default_arg_offset(x)` is `offsetof(struct seccomp_data, args[x])`, used at `src/arch.c:267`). Frama-C limitation (C17 requires a constant designator; GCC and Clang accept this as an extension): drafted as a feature request with a validated kernel patch, Issue 25, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-kernel-offsetof-non-constant-index.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-kernel-offsetof-non-constant-index.md), filed as [pub/frama-c#2813](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/work_items/2813) with merge request [!44](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/merge_requests/44). The wrapper below can go once a release carries it.
- **Rejected workaround:** the libseccomp run redefined `offsetof` for every target as `((size_t)&(((type *)0)->member))` in `fc_compat_types.h` (and in an untracked `templates/fc_stubs/eacsl/stddef.h`). Reproduced on the analysis image (Frama-C 35.0~~dev), 2026-11-09: the kernel no longer folds it (`(size_t)(& ((struct s *)0)->arr)` instead of `4UL`); Eva with `-warn-invalid-pointer` reports `\object_pointer(&((struct s *)0)->arr)` **invalid** and the function becomes `NON TERMINATING`; WP can no longer prove `\result == 4`; `_Static_assert` on an offset stops the parse. Both overrides are removed; `test_no_fc_stub_redefines_offsetof` keeps them out.
- **Fix (pipeline, commit `7e15154`):** new `scripts/fc_cpp_wrapper.py`, a gcc `-wrapper` that `augment_framac_cppflags` (model, Eva, WP, kernel AST) passes to the preprocessor. `build_eacsl_flag_sets` (E-ACSL frontend only) passes `scripts/fc_cpp_wrapper_eacsl.py`, which does the same repair and also rewrites `<==>` (see [`./wp-and-guardrail.md`](./wp-and-guardrail.md), `labels pre-analysis`). After `cc1 -E` succeeds, it rewrites in the `.i` file only the `__builtin_offsetof(T, D)` whose designator has an index that is not an integer literal: `__builtin_offsetof(T, m[i])` becomes `(__builtin_offsetof(T, m) + (__typeof__(sizeof(0)))(i) * sizeof((((T *)0)->m)[0]))` (nested designators through `__typeof__`). Constant offsets stay folded, comments, literals and directives are untouched, the line count is kept, line markers gcc puts inside a system-header macro expansion are skipped, and each rewrite is logged as `[fc-cpp] <file>:<line>: offsetof with a non-constant index rewritten: ...`. Any failure leaves the output alone; `FRAMAC_CPP_REWRITE=off` disables it. Verified on the analysis image: Eva 0 alarms with `-warn-invalid-pointer`, WP 11/11 including `\result == 16 + 8*a`, the E-ACSL build and run pass (`test_frama_c_and_eacsl_accept_a_non_constant_offsetof_without_losing_precision`, `FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1`).
- **Caveats:** the wrapper is offered only on POSIX hosts where it is executable and its path has no space or comma (gcc and Frama-C split on them). Both wrapper scripts are committed with mode 100755, and a test checks it. The cache key covers them (keys hash all of `scripts/`).

### Symptom: `User Error: syntax error` on ioctl Invocations Passing Struct Types
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. _IOC family defined in fc_compat_types.h by 7e15154; nothing open.
- **Cause (pipeline gap, fixed):** Frama-C's `__fc_libc` `<sys/ioctl.h>` does not define the Linux ioctl encoding macros (`_IO`, `_IOR`, `_IOW`, `_IOWR`). `_IOWR(magic, nr, struct foo)` was then a call to an undeclared function with a type-name argument: a syntax error. Found on `libseccomp` (`src/system.c:522`).
- **Fix (commit `7e15154`):** `templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h`, in the `__fc_libc` branch only (E-ACSL builds take glibc's own `<asm-generic/ioctl.h>`, so no redefinition warnings): the generic-layout `_IOC*` constants, `_IOC`, `_IOC_TYPECHECK`, `_IO`/`_IOR`/`_IOW`/`_IOWR` and the `_IOC_DIR`/`_IOC_TYPE`/`_IOC_NR`/`_IOC_SIZE` decoders, guarded by `#ifndef _IOC`. Listed under "Libc contract overrides" in `templates/fc_stubs/README.md`. A `-I` override of `<sys/ioctl.h>` with `#include_next` is not an alternative: Frama-C puts its own libc `-I` before `-cpp-extra-args`, so the force-include is the only place. Values are x86_64's (generic layout); architectures with their own layout (powerpc, mips, sparc) would need theirs.

### Symptom: `User Error: Cannot resolve variable TIOCM_RTS` (then `TCGETS2`) in the Phase 0 Model Parse; Model Status `no_unit_parsed`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in f0bea19 (merged as 34f8747); upstream filed as #2830/!58 with no pipeline action left.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, 2026-11-18 (Frama-C `34.0~beta`, image `framac-analysis:latest`). `phase0_model.py run` sent the only product unit, `src/.libs/libmodbus.so.5.1.0` (`src/modbus-rtu.c`, `modbus.c`, `modbus-tcp.c`, `modbus-data.c`), to `gcc_fallback` with `[kernel] src/modbus-rtu.c:254: User Error: Cannot resolve variable TIOCM_RTS`, so the model status was `no_unit_parsed`.
- **Cause (pipeline gap, upstream `__fc_libc` gap):** `src/modbus-rtu.c` does `ioctl(fd, TIOCMGET, &flags); flags |= TIOCM_RTS;`. Frama-C's `<sys/ioctl.h>` defines `TIOCMGET`/`TIOCMBIS`/`TIOCMBIC`/`TIOCMSET` but none of the modem-line bits, which glibc's `<sys/ioctl.h>` defines in `<bits/ioctl-types.h>` (and Linux in `<asm-generic/termios.h>`). Under `-nostdinc` glibc's chain is never read, and the snapshot's libc-gap export cannot help: `__fc_libc`'s header shadows glibc's and includes no `asm/` header. With the bits added, the same unit stopped next at `src/modbus-rtu.c:695: Cannot resolve variable TCGETS2` when `HAVE_STRUCT_TERMIOS2` is set: libmodbus's configure defines it when `<asm/termbits.h>` has the type, as the build image `framac-build:libmodbus-d4227309de07` does (the run's `config.h` was not read). `__fc_libc`'s `<sys/ioctl.h>` stops at `TCGETS`/`TCSETS` (34.0~beta and master `95408044f7`). `struct termios2` and `BOTHER` themselves come from `<asm/termbits.h>` through `-idirafter`. `TIOCGRS485`/`TIOCSRS485` are in `__fc_libc`, and `struct serial_rs485`/`SER_RS485_*` come from `<linux/serial.h>` (in the check below from the image's `/usr/include` through `-idirafter`; a run takes it from the snapshot's libc-gap export), so the RS485 code needed nothing.
- **Fix (pipeline, fixed: commit `f0bea19` on branch `sup/libmodbus-tiocm-bits`, merged live into `phase1-remediation` as `34f8747` on 2026-11-18 at the user's request, so the paused run could resume):** [`../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h`](../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h), in the `__fc_libc` branch only, next to the `_IOC` encoding: `TIOCM_LE` to `TIOCM_LOOP` with the Linux values, and `TCGETS2`/`TCSETS2`/`TCSETSW2`/`TCSETSF2` as `_IOR`/`_IOW('T', 0x2A..0x2D, struct termios2)`, each `#ifndef`-guarded. Verified in `framac-analysis:latest` with the pipeline's flags (`augment_framac_cppflags`) and a hand-written stand-in `config.h` (`-include`, as libmodbus's `Makefile.am` does), not through `phase0_model.py` or the run's captured flags: before, the four-file unit stops at `modbus-rtu.c:254`; after, it parses (rc 0), with and without `HAVE_STRUCT_TERMIOS2`. The parse also prints 156 cpp lines about macros such as `"VTDLY" redefined` between `<asm/termbits.h>` and `__fc_libc`'s `<termios.h>`; the count is the same before and after the fix, and the parse succeeds with them. Tests: `test_modem_line_bits_and_termios2_requests_are_defined_for_frama_c_libc_only` (unit) and `test_modem_line_bits_and_termios2_requests_parse_with_frama_c_libc_as_gcc_reads_them` (`FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1`: the bits match the image's `<asm-generic/termios.h>`, the requests match gcc with glibc) in [`../../tests/test_fc_compat_contracts.py`](../../tests/test_fc_compat_contracts.py).
- **Upstream:** the `TIOCM_*` gap is still on `master` `b80fcac74f` (the head since 2026-11-17; same output as `95408044f7` and `34.0~beta`). Filed on 2026-11-18 as pub/frama-c#2830 with merge request !58 (Issue 41, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-libc-ioctl-modem-bits.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-libc-ioctl-modem-bits.md)): a validated patch that adds the eleven names glibc's `<sys/ioctl.h>` defines (`TIOCM_LE` to `TIOCM_RI`). `TIOCM_OUT1`, `TIOCM_OUT2` and `TIOCM_LOOP` are Linux-only (`<asm-generic/termios.h>`; gcc with glibc rejects them after `<sys/ioctl.h>`), so the upstream patch leaves them out and the pipeline header keeps defining them. The termios2 requests are noted there but not requested (they need the Linux UAPI `struct termios2`).

### Symptom: `syntax error` or `no member named 'ip_hl'` on `icp->icmp_ip.ip_hl`, or `ICMP_UNREACH` / `ICMP_TIMXCEED` / `ICMP_MASKREQ` Undeclared (`<netinet/ip_icmp.h>`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Stub rewritten to follow glibc (S4) with a layout and constant comparison test.
- **Cause:** the shipped `templates/fc_stubs/netinet/ip_icmp.h` declared `id_ip` as raw bytes and only the Linux names (`ICMP_DEST_UNREACH`, ...), and its `struct icmphdr` had a `reserved[4]` member glibc does not have. Code written against glibc's BSD names (`struct icmp`, `icmp_ip.ip_hl`, `ICMP_ADVLEN(p)`, `ICMP_UNREACH_*`) did not parse with Frama-C although gcc built it.
- **Fix (fixed, S4 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md)):** the stub now follows glibc: `id_ip` holds a `struct ip` from `<netinet/ip.h>`, all Linux and BSD type and code names are defined, and `struct icmphdr` matches. `tests/test_fc_stub_repairs.py::test_icmp_stub_matches_glibc_in_gcc_and_in_frama_c` compares 28 layouts (`sizeof`, `offsetof`) and every constant with glibc's header in `framac-analysis:latest`, with gcc and with Frama-C's own parse.

### Symptom: `frama-c` (Eva / WP) Cannot Find `<bsd/string.h>`, `<json-c/json.h>`, `<netinet/ip_icmp.h>`, or `__int32_t`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Force-include and stubs in place; the json-c contract gap was fixed (4b0cd64) and the lint allowlist is empty.
- **Cause:** `frama-c` runs with `-frama-c-stdlib` (`-nostdinc`), searching only `__fc_libc` and explicit `-I` flags so host `/usr/include` does not shadow `__fc_libc`.
- **Fix:** `run_eva_slice.py` and `run_wp_function.py` automatically append `-I/opt/framac-pipeline/templates/fc_stubs -include fc_compat_types.h`, providing bodyless prototypes for `bsd/string.h`, `json-c/json.h`, `netinet/ip_icmp.h`, and glibc `__int8_t`..`__uint64_t` typedefs while keeping external specifications `Considered valid` (`STATUS_ASSUMED`) in the ledger. **Fixed pipeline bug (`4b0cd64`, on `phase1-remediation`):** not every stub had a contract. `bsd/string.h` had contracts; `json-c/json.h` had none, so Eva and WP used default `assigns` for its functions and a WP proof through them was reported `unsound_proof`. S4 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md) gave every prototype of `templates/fc_stubs/json-c/json.h` a contract, and the stub contract lint (S1, `tests/test_fc_stub_contracts.py`) has an empty `MISSING_CONTRACT_ALLOWLIST`, so a shipped prototype without an `assigns` contract now fails the suite.

### Symptom: `strlcpy` / `strlcat` Calls Get the `__fc_libc` Preconditions on Top of the Stub's (Eva / WP Through `<bsd/string.h>`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in S4 (rename around string.h, fc_strlcpy.h); nothing open.
- **Seen on:** S4 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md) (2026-11-10), `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 35.0~~dev). Frama-C 35's `<string.h>` declares `strlcpy` and `strlcat` with its own contract; the kernel merges a second declaration's contract into it instead of replacing it, so the stub's precise contract was conjoined with the libc one, which requires the whole `src` and `dst` ranges differently.
- **Cause:** the same contract merge as `getsockopt` and `getopt`: a stub can only replace a libc contract by renaming the libc declaration before `<string.h>` is first included.
- **Fix (fixed, S4):** `templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h` (force-included first, `__fc_libc` branch only) defines `strlcpy`/`strlcat` to `__fc_libc_strlcpy`/`__fc_libc_strlcat` around `#include <string.h>` and then includes `templates/fc_stubs/fc_strlcpy.h`, which `bsd/string.h` also includes. Strict destination range for Eva and WP (`\valid(dst + (0 .. size - 1))`), exact under E-ACSL. `FC_STUBS_KEEP_LIBC_STRLCPY` keeps the libc contract. Checked by `tests/test_fc_stub_repairs.py::test_bsd_string_in_eva_wp_eacsl_and_against_glibc` (Eva, WP 100%, E-ACSL, ABI against glibc's declarations).

### Symptom: Undeclared Function Diagnostics Missed Due to GCC Curly Quotes or Backticks
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The fix is in scripts/framac_common.py on main (the 'uncommitted' note is outdated).
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M2). `framac_common.scan_undeclared_functions` failed to extract function names when diagnostics used GCC backticks `` `foo' `` or curly quotes `'foo'` in `Calling undeclared function` logs.
- **Cause:** `_QUOTE_OPEN` in [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py) omitted backticks, and the `Calling undeclared function` pattern only supported optional ASCII apostrophe `'?`.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py)):** expanded `_QUOTE_OPEN` to `['`\u2018]` and applied `{_QUOTE_OPEN}?` and `{_QUOTE_CLOSE}?` to `Calling undeclared function`. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py).

### Symptom: `stub_check.py shipped` Fails a Header With `structs: with the real header: layout.c:10:89: error: invalid application of 'sizeof' to incomplete type 'char[]'`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Flexible array members are probed without sizeof; the limit noted is by design.
- **Seen on:** `templates/fc_stubs/sys/inotify.h` in `framac-analysis:latest`, 2026-11-18, once the header declared `struct inotify_event` (`char name[];`). No pipeline run. `tests/test_stub_check.py::test_shipped_stubs_match_the_real_headers_of_the_image` would have failed.
- **Cause (pipeline bug):** `layout_probe_source` in [`../../scripts/stub_check.py`](../../scripts/stub_check.py) printed `sizeof(((struct T *)0)->m)` for every member, and a flexible array member has no size. No shipped stub had one before.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-18 on `phase1-remediation`):** `struct_members` lists a flexible array member as `name[]` (`FLEXIBLE_SUFFIX`), and the probe prints its offset and the type class of one element instead of a size. Test: `tests/test_stub_check.py::test_a_flexible_array_member_is_probed_without_sizeof`; the image test above passes again.
- **Limit (by design):** for a header whose Eva / WP text sits under `__FRAMAC__` (the epoll and inotify headers), the shipped check compiles with gcc, which reads the real header on both sides, so it compares the real header with itself. The layout of such a text is checked under Frama-C's own parse (`_Static_assert`s generated from glibc's values, in `tests/test_fc_stub_repairs.py`).

### Symptom: `stub-check` Blocks Every Module With `eva: error`, Reason `[eva:assigns:missing] .../workspace/stubs/sys/epoll.h:49: User Error:`, and No Log to Read
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. All fixes are on main, and the one 'Still open' item (Phase 2 hiding such a frame) is itself fixed: run_eva_slice reports stub_assigns_without_from.
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, 2026-11-16, 02:20-02:22 UTC, `pipeline_make.py run stubs stub-check` ([`../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md`](../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md)).
  - The stub authors had filled `epoll_create1`, `epoll_ctl` and `epoll_wait` with `assigns errno, \result;`, and the inotify frames the same way.
  - The dynamic check reported `build_failed`.
- **Cause:** three defects.
  1. **Pipeline:** `stub_check.check_eva` wrote `eva.log` into a `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory` that was then deleted, and `_first_error` returned only the first line containing "error". Frama-C prints the message on the next, indented line, so the agent saw neither the text nor a log.
  2. **Brief:** rule 4 of [`../../skills/framac-slice/stub_author_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-slice/stub_author_brief.md) made `\from` optional. Eva treats `assigns:missing` as a deferred error and aborts on any location other than `\result` that has no `\from`: `no \from part for clause 'assigns __fc_errno;'` (or `'assigns *__event;'`), then `[kernel] Plug-in eva aborted: invalid user input.` Phase 2 Eva (`run_eva_slice.py`) aborts the same way.
  3. **Brief:** it prescribed `__fc_errno`, which is unbound in the E-ACSL build: `[kernel:annot-error] ...: Failure: unbound logic variable __fc_errno`, then `e-acsl-gcc: fatal error: aborted by Frama-C`.
  - **Ground truth in `framac-analysis:latest`:**
    - `errno` and `__fc_errno` fail Eva identically, with or without `<errno.h>`. Frama-C's `<time.h>` already brings in errno as `__fc_errno`.
    - Only `assigns errno \from ...;` with `<errno.h>` passes abi, eva and dynamic. The shipped `templates/fc_stubs/errno.h` maps `errno` to `__fc_eacsl_errno` under E-ACSL.
  - **What the run did without logs:** its agent concluded "Eva treats the errno location in the frame as fatal" and dropped `errno` from the three epoll frames and the two inotify frames. Those five frames are now unsound for errno: callers that read errno after these calls are analysed with a stale value.
- **Fix (fixed):** commits `d8e6d7c` and `778f2ae` (branch `sup/c-ares-stubcheck-eva-log`), merged: both are ancestors of `phase1-remediation` (checked 2026-11-18).
  - **Logs kept:** [`../../scripts/stub_check.py`](../../scripts/stub_check.py) keeps every header's generated programs and logs under `workspace/reports/stub_check/<include with / as __>/`, for example `sys__epoll.h/`. It names them in `stub_check.json` (`artifacts`, plus a `log` per check) and in the gate text.
  - **Reason:** the reason is now the most fatal message with its continuation lines, followed by `(see <log>)`.
  - **E-ACSL errors block:** a header whose E-ACSL build stops on an `annot-error` located in the header now fails. Before, a dynamic `build_failed` never blocked.
  - **Header preamble:** [`../../scripts/stub_gen.py`](../../scripts/stub_gen.py) writes `#include <errno.h>` after `#include_next`. Headers written earlier keep their text.
  - **Brief:** it requires a `\from` on every location other than `\result`, and the spelling `errno`.
  - **Tests:**
    - `tests/test_stub_check.py::test_the_reason_is_the_fatal_message_with_its_text`
    - `::test_run_keeps_each_headers_logs_and_names_them`
    - `::test_an_eacsl_annotation_error_in_the_header_blocks_it`
    - `::test_errno_in_a_stub_contract_against_eva_and_eacsl` (Docker)
- **Recovering a run:**
  - Restore `assigns errno \from \nothing;` (or `\from indirect:<args>`) in each frame.
  - Add `#include <errno.h>` after `#include_next` in any older header.
  - Give every pointer location a `\from`, then rerun `pipeline_make.py run stubs stub-check`.
- **Follow-up fix (fixed):** commit `7da578d` (branch `sup/c-ares-stub-skeleton-from`, cut from `778f2ae`), merged: an ancestor of `phase1-remediation` (checked 2026-11-18).
  - **Skeleton:** `stub_gen.skeleton_contract` gives each frame one `\from` that lists every named parameter, for example `assigns *ctx, out[0 .. 31], \result \from ctx, data, len, out;`. A `// TODO(stub-author):` note asks the author to add what the function reads through those pointers and to mark as `indirect:` a pointer that only says where it writes. Until then, Eva reports `garbled-mix:assigns`.
    - The old skeleton aborted Eva in the image: `no \from part for clause 'assigns *ctx;'`.
    - The new skeleton reaches `VALUES COMPUTED`.
    - `assigns \result;` alone only warns (`assigns:missing-result`).
  - **Static check:** `stub_check.assigns_without_from` reads every active contract of the header, without running anything. It skips drafts under `#ifdef FC_STUB_DRAFT` and includes named behaviors and `//@` lines. It fails the header whatever the checks or reachability: `<header>:<line>: assigns clause of <fn> lacks \from for <loc>: Eva aborts on it ([eva:assigns:missing])`.
    - This closes the reachability gap: on run-1, `epoll_wait` passed `eva: ok` because its valid call failed `positive_maxevents` first. The check now flags `sys/epoll.h:91` (`__events[0 .. __maxevents - 1]`).
  - **Brief:** the stub-author brief shows the new skeleton and the new reason.
  - **Tests:**
    - `tests/test_stub_gen.py::test_every_skeleton_location_but_result_has_a_from`
    - `tests/test_stub_gen.py::test_a_skeleton_filled_as_written_passes_eva_in_the_image` (Docker)
    - `tests/test_stub_check.py::test_assigns_without_from_is_found_in_the_header`
    - `tests/test_stub_check.py::test_a_header_whose_frame_lacks_from_fails_without_running_eva`
  - **Also checked:** nothing under `templates/` and no other generator in `scripts/` writes a function `assigns` without `\from`. `frame_skeleton.py` always emits one, and `loop_seeds` writes only `loop assigns`.
- **Phase 2 (fixed):** `run_eva_slice.py` now fails such a frame with reason `stub_assigns_without_from` instead of downgrading it. See "A Stub Frame Without `\from` Is Downgraded in Phase 2 Under a Recursion Label" in [`./eva.md`](./eva.md).

### Symptom: `stub_check.py` Reports a Stub `passed` Although Eva Raised a Precondition Alarm on the Valid Call
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The CSV-report based check is in scripts/stub_check.py on main (the 'uncommitted' note is outdated).
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (no target run). With the location written `fc_stub_smoke.c:12:3:`, `_WARNING_RE` read file `12` and line `3`, and `check_eva` dropped the alarm as not from the smoke file. Whether Eva prints a column in this message was not established.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/stub_check.py`](../../scripts/stub_check.py)):** `check_eva` appends `"-then", "-report", "-report-csv", str(csv_path)` to the Frama-C invocation, fails closed if the CSV report is not produced, and reads precondition rows directly via [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py) `read_report_csv`. Preconditions for `ok` calls are flagged as alarms unless status is `Valid` or `Valid under hyp`; preconditions for `short` calls require a status other than `Valid` or `Dead` to count as caught. `_WARNING_RE` log parsing is retained only as a fallback for alarms uncarried by the report CSV, tagged with `(Eva log only)`. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py).

### Symptom: `stub-check` Fails Correct, Documented OpenSSL Contracts as `violated` (`postcondition failed against the real library`) and Blocks Every nginx Module
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. classify_run fix merged into main (6217968); the listed limits are by design and the run-4 overlay note concerns a stopped run only.
- **Seen on:** nginx 1.31.9 run-4, 2026-11-19, `pipeline_make.py run stubs stub-check`.
  - `stub_check.json` failed eight OpenSSL overlay headers (`crypto`, `err`, `evp`, `objects`, `ocsp`, `ssl`, `stack`, `x509`), with nine functions `violated`. `blocked_modules` listed every nginx module.
  - The reasons look like `OpenSSL_version: postcondition failed against the real library (see workspace/reports/stub_check/openssl__crypto.h/run6.log)`.
  - The brief told the authors that `violated` means the postcondition is wrong, so the run sent correct contracts back to be weakened.
  - By the time of the fix, those `run<N>.log` files had been overwritten by later stub-checks (they exit 0), and the eight headers no longer appear in the run's `stub_check.json`. The messages below are quoted from the supervisor's diagnosis made during the run, not re-read from the logs.
- **Cause (pipeline bug):** `check_dynamic` in [`../../scripts/stub_check.py`](../../scripts/stub_check.py) mapped every E-ACSL `Postcondition failed` to `violated`. Two classes of failure are artifacts of the checker, not evidence against the contract.
  - **A. A fabricated object behind a pointer.** `call_lines` gives every pointer parameter 64 patterned bytes (`v[i] = i*31+seed`) cast to the parameter's type, whatever the pointee. The library then reads garbage from an opaque or structured pointee (`EVP_CIPHER_CTX *`, `SSL *`, `OPENSSL_STACK *`).
    - `evp.h` `EVP_CIPHER_CTX_is_encrypting`: `Postcondition failed: bool_result: \result == 0 || \result == 1 ... \result: 1294864368`. The contract is right per the OpenSSL documentation.
    - `stack.h` `OPENSSL_sk_num`: `count_or_null: \result >= -1`, `\result: -2141109725`.
    - `ssl.h` `SSL_get_version`: `non_null: \result != \null`, `\result: (nil)`.
    - A pointer-to-pointer out parameter is prefilled with the pattern too. `ERR_peek_error_data`'s `*\old(data)` is the driver's `0xd9ba9b7c5d3e1f00` when the library does not write it.
    - The same fabricated handles also crash the library (exit -11 or -7). Those runs were already `inconclusive` and did not block.
  - **B. E-ACSL cannot see memory the uninstrumented library owns.** `valid_read_string(\result)` and `\valid_read(...)` evaluate to 0 on a pointer to one of libcrypto's static strings (addresses `0x7...`), because E-ACSL tracks only the memory instrumented code registers. This is the open limitation in "E-ACSL Rejects a Pointer the Program Got From an Uninstrumented Library" in [`./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md`](./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md).
    - Affected: `OpenSSL_version` (int argument), `OBJ_nid2sn` (int), `OCSP_cert_status_str` (long), `OCSP_response_status_str` (long) and `X509_verify_cert_error_string` (long).
    - For example `valid_str: valid_read_string(\result) ... valid_read_string(\result): 0 ... \result: 0x78675abc321d`.
  - The [`../../skills/framac-slice/stub_author_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-slice/stub_author_brief.md) said `violated` "means the library broke one of your `ensures` ...: the postcondition is wrong, fix it".
- **Fix (fixed, branch `sup/nginx-stub-check-dynamic-artifacts`, merged into main in `6217968` on 2026-11-19):** commits `65bff62` (tests), `ba2cba0` (fix) and `facc5d0` (nested applications, guarded Docker case). `classify_run` in `stub_check.py` decides each function's dynamic record.
  - **`inconclusive`, `reason: fabricated_argument`:** a postcondition failed and `fabricated_params` names a pointer parameter whose pointee is not a plain buffer. A plain buffer is one `*` over the words `void char short int long float double signed unsigned _Bool bool size_t ssize_t ptrdiff_t intptr_t uintptr_t wchar_t char16_t char32_t int8_t..uint64_t u_char u_short u_int u_long`, qualifiers aside. Anything else is fabricated: a struct, union, typedef'd or incomplete pointee, a pointer to pointer, or a `void *` that no length bounds. When in doubt the parameter counts as fabricated. It is read from the type text, so nothing takes `sizeof` of an incomplete type.
  - **`inconclusive`, `reason: untracked_library_memory`:** every `Postcondition failed` block of the run is explained by memory predicates alone.
    - The failing predicate is read from E-ACSL's report, whose Unicode operators (`≡`, `∨`, `∧`, `⇒`) are read as ASCII.
    - An implication fails on its consequent. A disjunction needs one explained disjunct. A conjunction needs every conjunct explained, or a `T != \null` whose printed value is not NULL.
    - An explained atom has the shape of one or more outermost applications of `\valid`, `\valid_read`, `\initialized`, `\block_length`, `\base_addr`, `\offset`, `\separated`, `\freeable`, `\allocable`, `\object_pointer` and `valid_[read_][n|w]string`, compared at most with constants.
    - Each of those applies to memory the library returned or wrote: its argument names `\result` or a dereference.
    - No printed term of the argument is NULL.
    - E-ACSL printed at least one memory-predicate line as `0` (`- valid_read_string(\result): 0`, label prefix dropped).
  - **Still `violated` (blocking):** a value failure on genuine arguments (`\result == 0`, `\result: 42`), a memory predicate on NULL, on a buffer the driver passed, negated, or beside a value comparison.
  - **What the report keeps:** each `inconclusive` record keeps E-ACSL's `report`, a `detail` sentence and, for class A, `fabricated_params`. The header verdict is unchanged: only `violated` adds a reason.
  - **Tests** (in `tests/test_stub_check.py`; each new test failed on `2c6c699`, most because the functions did not exist yet; the verdict and S9 tests failed on behaviour):
    - `::test_a_postcondition_failure_is_violated_only_on_evidence_against_the_contract`, parametrized over the run-4 functions above, plus genuine, negative and nested cases (`\block_length(\base_addr(\result)) >= 16` explained, `== t` not). Before the fix: `AttributeError: module 'stub_check' has no attribute 'classify_run'`.
    - `::test_postcondition_reports_are_read_from_the_images_output`, on `tests/fixtures/stub_check/eacsl_post_{value,fabricated,untracked,guarded}.log`. These are the image's own E-ACSL reports for `tests/fixtures/stub_check/oplib.c`. The `guarded` one prints `\result ≢ \null ⇒ valid_read_string(\result)`.
    - `::test_fabricated_params_are_read_from_the_parameter_types`.
    - `::test_the_header_verdict_follows_the_dynamic_classification` (through `stub_check.py run --checks dynamic`). Before the fix: `AssertionError: assert {'op_answer':...lated', None)} == {'op_answer':...rary_memory')}`, every oplib function `violated`.
    - `::test_dynamic_artifacts_of_the_checker_do_not_block_but_a_wrong_ensures_does` (Docker, `framac-analysis:latest`). Before the fix it reported `violated` for `op_is_on` (`\result: 1564352256`), `op_version` (`valid_read_string(\result): 0`, `\result: 0x78007e6e6000`) and `op_answer`. After the fix `op_is_on`, `op_version` and the guarded `op_name` are `inconclusive`. `op_answer` stays `violated` and still blocks its module.
    - `::test_stub_check_against_the_real_library[bad]` now expects `inconclusive`/`fabricated_argument`: `ml_hash` takes `ml_ctx *`, an incomplete type.
  - **Docs updated to match:** the brief, [`../../skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md`](../../skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md) (mirrors refreshed with `tools/sync_skill_mirrors.py`), [`../guides/slicing-and-onboarding.md`](../guides/slicing-and-onboarding.md) and the `stub_check.py` docstring. Each describes the two reasons, and the brief tells authors never to weaken a documented `ensures` because of an `inconclusive` result.
- **Limits (by design):**
  - A wrong `ensures` on a function that takes a handle is never caught dynamically: any postcondition failure with a fabricated argument is `inconclusive`. `ml_hash`'s wrong `\result == 1` is an example.
  - A typedef'd pointer passed by value (`typedef struct x *X_HANDLE;`) has no `*` in its text. It gets a zeroed local (NULL) and stays a value parameter.
  - A memory predicate on `\result` counts as untracked even when the library returns a pointer into a buffer the driver passed, which E-ACSL does track (a `strchr`-like function). A real out-of-bounds result there reads `inconclusive`.
  - The `stub_check.py run` gate CLI is no longer exercised on a `violated` header in the mylib Docker scenario. The oplib Docker test checks `blocked_modules`, but not `phase0_gates.py check`.
  - The [`./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md`](./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md) entry has no back-reference to this one: the main checkout had uncommitted edits to that file.
- **What run-4 did with it:** the run sent the eight headers back to their authors, and each weakened a documented contract to pass, naming the artifact in its own diagnosis. The user stopped run-4 in Phase 0 for this reason (`run_status` `incomplete`). Its overlay keeps the weakened contracts, so a later run must start from a fresh overlay, not from run-4's:
