  - `crypto.h` `OpenSSL_version`, `ocsp.h` `OCSP_cert_status_str` and `OCSP_response_status_str`, `x509.h` `X509_verify_cert_error_string`: `valid_read_string(\result)` became `\result != \null`.
  - `evp.h` `EVP_CIPHER_CTX_is_encrypting`: `\result == 0 || \result == 1` removed.
  - `ssl.h` `SSL_get_version`: `\result != \null` removed.
  - `stack.h` `OPENSSL_sk_num`: `\result >= -1` removed.
  - `objects.h` `OBJ_nid2sn`: its `ensures` removed.
  - `err.h` `ERR_peek_error_data`: now `(\result != 0 && data != \null) ==> \initialized(data)`. The `\result != 0` guard is a genuine correction; dropping the validity of `*data` was the artifact.

### Symptom: `syntax error: ... before or at token: p` on `char* __restrict__ p` When Frama-C Parses With Host Headers (`-no-frama-c-stdlib`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by f2772cf (merged 87841fb); the upstream lexer fix is a separate drafted package.
- **Seen on:** mpack run-3 (2026-11-17), every E-ACSL harness build (`build-check`, `pipeline_make.py run build-<mod>`), module sources `src/mpack/mpack-writer.c`. Exact text: `[kernel] workspace/work/src/mpack/mpack-writer.c:1274: User Error: syntax error: Location: "workspace/work/src/mpack/mpack-writer.c", line 1274, before or at token: p`, with the preprocessed line `char* __restrict__ p = writer->position;`.
- **Cause:** MPack defines `MPACK_RESTRICT` as `__restrict__` whenever `__GNUC__` is defined (target mpack, `src/mpack/mpack-platform.h:1327-1332`). Frama-C's own libc accepts that GNU keyword (the `phase0_model.py` parse of the same file is `ok`), but the E-ACSL frontend runs against the host glibc headers (`-no-frama-c-stdlib`), and in that configuration Frama-C's parser rejects `__restrict__` in a block-scope declaration. Reproduced by `frama-c -machdep gcc_x86_64 -typecheck -no-frama-c-stdlib ... src/mpack/mpack-writer.c` (fails) against the same command with the kernel libc (passes). **Pipeline gap (fixed, see "Fixed" below):** `fc_cpp_wrapper_eacsl.py` did not normalize the GNU restrict spellings for the host-header frontend.
- **Fix:** workaround in the run: add `-D__restrict__=restrict` to the module cppflags (`generate_harness.py --cppflags '-D__restrict__=restrict ...'`), which maps the GNU spelling to the C99 keyword the kernel accepts. The pipeline defect was open at that time; see "Fixed" below.
- **Fixed:** `f2772cf` from `sup/mpack-eacsl-restrict`, merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-17 (merge `87841fb`). The analysis image was rebuilt the same day.
  - The `__FRAMAC_EACSL__` block of `templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h`, which `build_eacsl_flag_sets()` force-includes on the frontend, defines `__restrict__` as `__restrict` under `#ifndef`, before its includes.
  - Clang compiles never see it, because the header is empty without `__FRAMAC__`.
  - Tests: `tests/test_fc_compat_contracts.py::test_eacsl_host_headers_map_the_gcc_restrict_spelling_before_any_include` and `::test_gcc_restrict_spelling_builds_under_eacsl_and_clang` (Docker, `FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1`).
  - The analysis image copies in `templates/` (`Dockerfile:83,166`), so after merging, rebuild it for runs that do not bind-mount the checkout.
- **Upstream cause, corrected (2026-11-17):** the position of the declaration does not matter. Frama-C's lexer (`clexer.mll`) knows `restrict` and `__restrict` but not `__restrict__`, which it reads as an identifier, in every position. Frama-C's libc only hides this: `share/libc/features.h:94` defines `__restrict__` as `__restrict`. Drafted as package 35 with a validated one-line kernel patch on `master` `95408044f7`, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-kernel-restrict-gnu-spelling.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-kernel-restrict-gnu-spelling.md). Not filed.

### Symptom: Complex C Declarators Dropped or Void Functions Misclassified in Stubs and Layout Checks
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The declarator and void-return fix is in scripts on main (the 'uncommitted' note is outdated).
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M1).
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/stub_check.py`](../../scripts/stub_check.py) used regex patterns to parse function prototypes and struct members, misclassifying functions returning void with qualifiers/attributes (`__inline static void quiet(int *p)`, `__attribute__((noreturn)) void die(int code)`) as returning values, missing complex struct declarators (arrays, function pointer tables, aligned/packed members), and failing open on unparseable headers.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/stub_check.py`](../../scripts/stub_check.py), [`../../scripts/c_definitions.py`](../../scripts/c_definitions.py)):** standardized declarator analysis and void-return detection via [`../../scripts/c_definitions.py`](../../scripts/c_definitions.py) `returns_void` and AST declarator resolution; struct member extraction fails closed with `AcslSyntaxError` on headers that fail tokenization. In [`../../scripts/stub_check.py`](../../scripts/stub_check.py), `_without_attributes` is applied to tokens in `struct_members` so struct headers with attributes like `struct __attribute__((packed)) s` are parsed and not silently dropped from layout checks. In [`../../scripts/c_definitions.py`](../../scripts/c_definitions.py), `_resolve_declarator` unwraps redundant outer parentheses `((f))` and verifies name presence before parameter list recursion. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py), [`../../tests/test_stub_check.py`](../../tests/test_stub_check.py), [`../../tests/test_challenger_m1_2_empirical.py`](../../tests/test_challenger_m1_2_empirical.py).

---

## From [`./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md`](./eacsl-and-fuzzing.md): E-ACSL + LibFuzzer Build & Runtime Issues

### Symptom: Fuzz Stage Fails With `ZeroDivisionError: integer modulo by zero` in `artifact_dispatch_case`; Campaigns `not_run`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. dispatch_case_count fix is in the code with its regression test.
- **Seen on:** benchmark runs `ab-fc35dev` and `ab-fc34` (2026-11-13), on both analysis images: `fuzz: rc=1` on cryptolib, minmea, oscs-qlz, oscs-semver, oscs-stmr, qcbor-dev and qcbor-master, and `benchmark.py compare` against `phase0-r1` reports `campaign no longer runs: not_run` for their modules. The fuzz log ends `File ".../run_eacsl_fuzz.py", line 1429, in artifact_dispatch_case: return first[0] % n_cases ... ZeroDivisionError: integer modulo by zero`, then `make: *** [.../pipeline.mk:148: fuzz-minmea] Error 1`. Recall stayed 7/7: the replays do not go through the campaign.
- **Cause:** a pipeline bug from `f23757c` (M4 hardening). `dispatch_case_count` started reading the harness sidecar first and returned its `dispatch_case_count` as is. Every sidecar records that field, 0 for a harness that is not a dispatch harness, so a plain `LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput` harness was taken for a dispatch harness with 0 cases: `run_campaign_rounds` ran dispatch rounds, and the first crash it recorded computed `first[0] % 0`. The sidecar shortcut also skipped the old check that the source holds `FRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES`, without which a build cannot skip cases.
- **Fixed (uncommitted, written in a worktree while the benchmark ran, then applied to the checkout):** [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) `dispatch_case_count` reads the sidecar only for a source holding the skip macro and returns None for a count of 0 or an empty case list; `artifact_dispatch_case` returns None for `n_cases <= 0`; `run_campaign_rounds` runs fork mode when `n_cases` is 0. Test: `tests/test_run_eacsl_fuzz.py::test_a_harness_that_is_not_a_dispatch_harness_runs_in_fork_mode`. The two A/B runs both carry the bug, so their fuzz results compare like for like but are not campaign results.

### Symptom: `e-acsl-gcc` Fails on `__fc_builtin.h`, `__fc_libc/stdlib.h` (`\old(endptr)`), or Unknown Legacy Flag `--rte=`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Harness template guard and -D__FRAMAC_EACSL__ flags are in place.
- **Cause:**
  1. `e-acsl-gcc` runs `frama-c -no-frama-c-stdlib` so its second pass can compile `instrumented.c` with host `clang` against host glibc. If a harness guards `#include "__fc_builtin.h"` only with `#ifdef __FRAMAC__`, `e-acsl-gcc` enters that block and fails because `__fc_builtin.h` is not on the host include path, and host `<math.h>`/`<stdlib.h>` expose `_Float128` prototypes on glibc >= 2.26.
  2. Frama-C 33+ installs `e-acsl-gcc` (no `.sh`). Its `-h` lists only the short flags (`-F`, `-G`, `-E`, `-e`, `-l`, `-n`, `-C`, `-M`), though long options such as `--rte=all` are still accepted. Running `-rte` across all functions without `-rte-select` instruments unused inline helpers.
- **Fix:** `harness_dual.c.template` guards `#include "__fc_builtin.h"` and `eva_main()` with `#if defined(__FRAMAC__) && !defined(__FRAMAC_EACSL__)`, and `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` automatically passes `-D__FRAMAC_EACSL__ -D_Float128=double -U__SIZEOF_INT128__` (host `<linux/types.h>` declares `__int128` typedefs, which Frama-C rejects, only when that macro is defined) along with `-rte-select` and `-e-acsl-functions` scoped to the functions defined in the target slice `.c` files. Full compiler diagnostics are saved to `<out-bin>.compile.log`. If `e-acsl-gcc` still fails in `campaign` mode, `--engine auto` falls back to native `clang -fsanitize=fuzzer,address,undefined`.

### Symptom: Selftest `compiled: false` With `undefined reference to 'main'` / `fail to compile/link un-instrumented code`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Stage 1 already passes -n.
- **Cause:** by default `e-acsl-gcc` instruments **and** links the original sources. A libFuzzer harness has no `main()`, so that link fails and `e-acsl-gcc` exits 1, even though the instrumented file was written.
- **Fix:** `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` passes `-n` (instrument only) in stage 1.

### Symptom: Every Input Crashes in `shadow_alloca` (`e_acsl_segment_tracking.c`), or Valid Stack Accesses Fail `\valid`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Runtime init is automatic via fuzz_init.c; guard exists.
- **Cause:** E-ACSL initializes its runtime at the top of `main()`, and a harness has none. Without full memory tracking, its memory analysis leaves a caller's stack buffer untracked, so a callee's valid write fails `\valid`. With full tracking but no runtime initialization, the runtime segfaults on the first tracked stack block.
- **Fix:** automatic. Fuzz builds use `-e-acsl-full-mtracking` and link [`../../templates/eacsl_runtime/fuzz_init.c`](../../templates/eacsl_runtime/fuzz_init.c), whose `LLVMFuzzerInitialize` calls `__e_acsl_memory_init` and `__e_acsl_globals_init`. Do not define `LLVMFuzzerInitialize` in a harness (`reason: eacsl_init_hook_conflict`). The selftest's `benign_input_clean` check catches either regression.

### Symptom: Every `va_arg` Fails `\initialized(&ap)` (`rte: initialization` in a Variadic Function)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 7be8bd1 with skip_va_list_initialized_checks.
- **Seen on:** minmea (benchmark revision `c43c9e7`), the manifest's MINMEA-01 and MINMEA-02 reproducers replayed through `run_eacsl_fuzz.py replay` with `-rte-initialized` (2026-11-14). Both stopped at `minmea.c:253` in `minmea_scan` with `\initialized(&ap)`, on `va_arg(ap, union minmea_type *)`. Until S9 the `strtol` `endptr` false alarm at `minmea.c:224` stopped both earlier and hid this one. The benchmark manifest built minmea with `fuzz_rte_initialized: false` because of these alarms; that opt-out was dropped on 2026-11-14.
- **Cause:** only `va_start` and `va_copy` initialize a `va_list`, and both are compiler builtins (`__builtin_va_start(ap,format)`) that E-ACSL does not instrument. So the `\initialized(&ap)` check `-rte-initialized` puts before each `__builtin_va_arg` is false on every call. Marking `ap` initialized after `va_start` does not work: the instrumented code registers it with Frama-C's machdep size (`__e_acsl_store_block((void *)(& ap),8UL)` on `gcc_x86_64`), while the check uses clang's `sizeof(va_list)`, 24 bytes.
- **Fix (fixed, commit `7be8bd1`, `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py` `skip_va_list_initialized_checks`, called from `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`):** with `-rte-initialized`, the build replaces each `__e_acsl_initialized(&<lv>, sizeof(va_list))` in the instrumented file by `1` and records the count as `va_list_init_checks_skipped`. A `va_list` read before `va_start` is no longer reported; compilers warn about that one.

### Symptom: Every Input Fails `\initialized(&__retres)` in a `__gen_e_acsl_<fn>` Wrapper
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Result-init pass is automatic.
- **Cause:** an E-ACSL gap in Frama-C 33. The wrapper that checks a function contract registers its `__retres` with the runtime but never marks it initialized after `__retres = <fn>(...)`. Under `-rte-initialized`, any `ensures` that reads `\result` then fails on the first input.
- **Fix:** automatic. Between instrumentation and compilation, `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` inserts the missing `__e_acsl_initialize` after each such assignment; the build report counts them in `compile.wrapper_result_inits`. This is sound, because the callee's own return value is still checked for uninitialized reads. A report of this failure means the build ran without that step: rebuild it, and never weaken a contract to get past it.

### Symptom: The E-ACSL Selftest Passes Although the Result-Init Pass, the `isnan` Shim or the errno Shim Is Broken
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Canary extended (C1) and its test exists.
- **Seen on:** C1 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 35.0~~dev), 2026-11-10.
- **Cause:** the canary called no function with a contract on its result, used no `<math.h>` classification macro and never wrote `errno`, so a regression of `mark_wrapper_results_initialized`, of `-D__SUPPORT_SNAN__` with the `fc_stubs/eacsl` `math.h` shim, or of the errno stub and shim (S5) still passed the selftest and showed only on a target. Frama-C 35 checks `\initialized` on a contract wrapper's `__retres` only where the `ensures` reads a field of a struct `\result` (`\initialized(&\result.n)`); a scalar `\result` (`ensures \result <= len`, `\result >= 0`, `\result == \old(x) + 1`) or a pointer `\result` gets no such check, so a canary with a scalar result would not catch the regression.
- **Fix (fixed, C1):** `templates/eacsl_canary/canary.c` runs three more calls on every input, before its input-dependent bugs: `canary_clamp` (`ensures \result.len <= span.len` on a struct result), `canary_classify` (`isnan`) and `canary_errno` (`errno = 0`, `strtol` of an out-of-range number, then `/*@ assert canary_errno_synced: synced != 0; */` on `errno == ERANGE`). `tests/test_eacsl_canary.py` (Docker) builds the canary four times: production passes, and without the result-init pass (`\initialized(&\result.len)`) or without the errno shim (`canary_errno_synced`) `benign_input_clean` fails; without `-D__SUPPORT_SNAN__` the build fails.

### Symptom: Every Call Fails `\initialized` on a Formal Parameter (`\initialized(&s.len)`, often at a Function's First Statement)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. formal_inits pass is automatic; upstream MR !28 also queued as image patch 13.
- **Cause:** an E-ACSL gap, reproduced on Frama-C 35. Each function registers its formals with `__e_acsl_store_block` but never marks them initialized, although the caller has just written them. Under `-rte-initialized`, the first read of a by-value struct field, or of a scalar formal through its address, fails whatever the caller passed. The assertion is blocking, so nothing after it in that function is ever checked. In QCBOR run-5, 13 of 29 crash candidates were this artifact, and it hid a real signed overflow a few lines below one of them.
- **Fix:** automatic. Between the two stages, `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` inserts `__e_acsl_full_init` after each formal's registration; the build report counts them in `compile.formal_inits`, and the selftest canary reads a by-value struct and an address-taken scalar formal on every input. Locals are untouched, so uninitialized locals are still reported. The cost: E-ACSL no longer reports a struct passed by value with uninitialized members. A report of this failure means the build ran without that step: rebuild it rather than using `--no-rte-initialized`. Upstream report (filed as [pub/frama-c#2797](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/work_items/2797), merge request [!28](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/merge_requests/28)): [`frama-c-eacsl-formal-params-initialized.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-formal-params-initialized.md).

### Symptom: E-ACSL Formals Uninitialized or Scalar Const Global Aborts on Non-`UL` Integer Suffixes
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Regex fix is in the code, not uncommitted any more.
- **Cause:** `_STORE_BLOCK_LINE_RE` and `_READONLY_SCALAR_RE` in `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py` required `\d+UL`. Size constants emitted with `U`, `L`, `ULL`, or bare digits failed to match, leaving formals uninitialized or const scalars without address-of.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`):** Replaced `\d+UL` with `\d+[uUlL]*`. Added a warning when store_block lines exist in text but none match the regex.

### Symptom: `implicit cast: cannot cast from __e_acsl_mpz_t to size_t` (E-ACSL silently in GMP-only mode)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Option normalisation and the selftest check are in the code.
- **Cause:** `e-acsl-gcc` splits the values of `-e`/`-E`/`-F`/`-l` on spaces and re-parses the tokens. A standalone `-g` inside `-e` is read as its own `--gmp` option (`-e-acsl-gmp-only` up to Frama-C 33, `-e-acsl-O-gmp-only` from 34).
- **Fix:** `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` normalizes every passthrough value: `-g` becomes `-gline-tables-only`, `-I dir` becomes `-Idir`, and other standalone single-letter tokens or paths with spaces are refused (`reason: eacsl_argv_unsafe`). `run_eacsl_fuzz.py selftest` checks that the compile log contains neither spelling of the GMP-only option.
- **Pipeline bug, fixed 2026-11-19 (committed to `main` 2026-11-20; found by the option audit on `framac-analysis:latest`, Frama-C 34.0~beta):** the selftest check `no_gmp_only` could not fail on Frama-C 34. `e-acsl-gcc -g` now writes `-e-acsl-O-gmp-only` on the Frama-C command line (`OPTION_GMP` in the wrapper), and `-e-acsl-gmp-only` is only a deprecated alias (`[kernel] Warning: -e-acsl-gmp-only is a deprecated alias for option -e-acsl-O-gmp-only.`). Measured: the log of `e-acsl-gcc -n -g` holds `-e-acsl-O-gmp-only` once and `-e-acsl-gmp-only` never, so `"-e-acsl-gmp-only" not in log_text` ([`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py), `checks["no_gmp_only"]`) was true whether or not GMP-only mode was triggered. `normalize_passthrough` kept a standalone `-g` away from the wrapper, so no build is known to be affected; only the selftest's second line of defence was gone. The check now uses `_GMP_ONLY_FLAG_RE` (`-e-acsl-(?:O-)?gmp-only\b`), which matches both spellings and not the opposite options (`-e-acsl-O-no-gmp-only`). Test: `test_selftest_sees_gmp_only_in_both_spellings` in `tests/test_eacsl_toolchain.py`. Not rerun on the image: the real selftest was not started, because a pipeline run was live.

### Symptom: Campaign Reports `engine_used: eacsl` but E-ACSL Never Fires, or the Guardrail Always Passes
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Binary selection and refusal exist.
- **Cause:** `e-acsl-gcc -O <out>` writes the instrumented executable to `<out>.e-acsl`; `<out>` is a plain build.
- **Fix:** the script selects `<out>.e-acsl` and refuses any binary without E-ACSL instrumentation (`reason: eacsl_binary_not_instrumented`). The guardrail refuses uninstrumented binaries (`guardrail_error`) instead of reporting `passed_dynamic_oracle`.

### Symptom: `redefinition of 'timeval'` / `'timespec'` or Conflicting `fd_set` Types When Compiling the Instrumented File
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Two-stage build is the implemented design.
- **Cause:** the instrumented file printed by Frama-C is self-contained (it has no `#include` lines and already contains every typedef). Force-including headers that pull in host system headers into its compile duplicates those typedefs.
- **Fix:** `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` builds in two stages. All preprocessor flags (`-I`, `-D`, `-include`) go to instrumentation (`-E`) only; the instrumented file is compiled with codegen flags only (`e-acsl-gcc -C -e ...`). `fc_compat_types.h` is empty whenever `__FRAMAC__` is undefined. Boundary stub headers need no E-ACSL-specific branches.

### Symptom: `syntax error` on `__int32_t`, `u_short` (E-ACSL Host-Header Mode)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. fc_compat_types.h includes glibc sys/types.h in host-header mode.
- **Cause:** E-ACSL runs Frama-C with `-no-frama-c-stdlib`, so the glibc-internal and BSD aliases must come from host headers.
- **Fix:** in host-header mode `fc_compat_types.h` includes glibc `<sys/types.h>`, so the aliases are identical to glibc's by construction. Do not hand-write `__intN_t` typedefs: `__int64_t` is `long` on LP64 and `long long` on ILP32.

### Symptom: `undefined reference to 'open_fallback_N'` at Link Time
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fallback generation is automatic.
- **Cause:** in host-header mode, the Variadic translation emits `<fn>_fallback_<N>` for variadic calls without a libc specification (`open`, `fcntl`, `ioctl`, ...), and the E-ACSL RTL does not define them.
- **Fix:** automatic. The script generates `<out-bin>_fallbacks/eacsl_fallbacks.c`, which forwards to the real function (or fails with `ENOSYS` when the signature or header is unknown, recorded as `mode: enosys`), compiles it to a link-only object, and retries the link once. Never add these definitions to the instrumented sources: each definition shifts the fallback numbering. `--no-variadic-translation` passes `-no-variadic-translation` to Frama-C instead (no fallbacks are emitted).

### Symptom: Link Repair and Fallback Function Generation Fail on LLVM lld `undefined symbol:` Diagnostics
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. lld 'undefined symbol:' regexes are in the code.
- **Cause:** `_UNDEFINED_REF_RE` in `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py` and `_FALLBACK_UNDEFINED_RE` in `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py` only parsed GNU ld's `undefined reference to 'X'`, ignoring LLVM lld's `undefined symbol: X`.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`, `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`):** Expanded regexes to match both `undefined reference to` and `undefined symbol:`.

### Symptom: The Build Report Gives Only the Last Attempt's Failure After Link Repair (an OOM-Killed First E-ACSL Attempt Is Hidden)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Attempts reporting is merged.
- **Seen on:** nginx 1.31.9 run-3, 2026-11-19, `run_eacsl_fuzz.py build` for `nginx__ngx_event_openssl`.
  - The first E-ACSL attempt was OOM-killed: a docker `oom` event at 15:01:48, with Frama-C at 8.7 GB.
  - Link repair then added files and built again.
  - `workspace/build/nginx__ngx_event_openssl.build.json` says only `engine_fallback_reason: eacsl_instrumentation_failed`, the post-repair attempt's reason (a missing generated source, see [`./phase0-build-and-model.md`](./phase0-build-and-model.md)).
  - `fuzz_nginx__ngx_event_openssl.compile.log` holds only that last attempt.
- **Cause (pipeline bug):** in [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py), the repair loop of `build_module_binary` reassigns `compile`, `eacsl_compile_attempt` and `engine_fallback_reason` on each attempt. Each compile also rewrites the same `<out>.compile.log` and `<out>.sanitizer_compile.log`. `link_repair.rounds` records only what was added, not why each attempt failed.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/nginx-generated-sources`, commits `c97e28c` and `79f49ec` (not merged).
  - After a repair, the build result has `attempts`, which the report copies as `build_attempts`. Each attempt gives the engine, the return code, the reason, the end of stderr, and the E-ACSL attempt that fell back.
  - The logs of every earlier attempt are kept as `<log stem>.attempt<N>.log`.
  - `diagnostic_hints` says when an earlier attempt was killed or looks killed.
  - **How a kill shows:** checked in `framac-analysis`, `e-acsl-gcc`'s `error()` exits 1 whatever Frama-C's status. Bash prints no `Killed` for a Frama-C killed inside its `$(...)`: a test script got return code 137 inside and printed nothing. So `killed` (return code 137 or -9, or a `Killed` line) fires only for a direct kill. For E-ACSL, `killed_suspected` marks an attempt that stopped with `aborted by Frama-C` and no Frama-C diagnostic of its own (`[kernel] User Error`, `Failure`, `Fatal error`, `Out of memory`). That is a heuristic; confirm it with `docker events` (`oom`).
  - Tests: `tests/test_generated_sources.py::test_the_build_keeps_every_attempts_failure` and `::test_a_killed_attempt_is_told_from_a_frama_c_error`.
  - **Not verified on run-3:** its first attempt's log was overwritten, so there is no way to check that it had no Frama-C diagnostic.

### Symptom: The E-ACSL Fuzz Binary Never Sees an `errno` Set by libc (`errno == ERANGE` After `strtol`, `EINTR`/`EBADF` After `read` Take the Wrong Branch)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Errno shim fixed in 7a23e9e.
- **Seen on:** S5 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), a minimal repro in `framac-analysis:latest` built with `run_eacsl_fuzz.compile_eacsl_binary` (2026-11-10).
- **Cause:** `templates/fc_stubs/errno.h` maps `errno` to the tracked global `__fc_eacsl_errno` in E-ACSL builds, and libc never writes it: `ERRNO_AFTER_STRTOL=0 ERANGE=34 v=9223372036854775807`. The mapping cannot simply go away. glibc's errno lives in libc.so's TLS block, outside the TLS segment the E-ACSL runtime shadows (only the executable's block; probe: `errno_location=0x7f6007f480c0 tls_segment=[0x7f6007f48140,0x7f6007f48440)`). Without the stub every input aborts with `e_acsl_segment_tracking.c:1018: Use of invalid address ... in unsafe_initialize`. Registering the address from `fuzz_init.c` (`__e_acsl_store_block(__errno_location(), sizeof(int))` and `__e_acsl_full_init`) crashes instead with `UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer: SEGV on unknown address 0x000000000000 ... e_acsl_segment_tracking.c:303:22 in shadow_alloca`.
- **Fix (fixed, commit 7a23e9e):** `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` links a generated, link-only errno shim (`<out>_errno_shim/eacsl_errno_shim.o`, `-Wl,--wrap=<fn>` for the functions of `ERRNO_SHIM_FUNCTIONS` in `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py` that the slice calls). Each wrapper copies the global into libc's errno before the call and libc's errno back after it, and the variadic fallbacks sync errno the same way. The build result records `errno_shim` (the wrapped functions, or `status: failed` with `unsynced`) as a coverage caveat. Remaining gap, by design: after a libc call outside the table, or a call through a function pointer, the target still reads the last errno it wrote, and the global is shared across threads. Add a function to the table when a target branches on its errno.

### Symptom: Libc Calls in Complex Statements Lack Errno Shims
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. _CALL_SITE_RE extension is in the code.
- **Cause:** `_CALL_SITE_RE` in `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py` expected calls only at line starts or after `=;{}`. Calls in `if (f) fclose(f);`, `return close(fd);`, and ternary expressions were missed, leaving `__fc_eacsl_errno` unsynced after those calls.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`):** Extended `_CALL_SITE_RE` delimiters to include `(`, `)`, `:`, `!`, `?`, `&`, `|`, `,`, and keywords `return`, `else`, while excluding control keywords with lookahead. Added a warning when a libc call gets no errno shim.

### Symptom: An E-ACSL `\exists` Passes Although One of Its Conjuncts Is False (`\exists integer i; 0 <= i < n && P && Q` Checks Only `Q`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Upstream defect fixed by queued image patch 24; S4 stub fixes and their test are in place; the open item was a lint that the image patch makes moot.
- **Seen on:** S4 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md) (2026-11-10), `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 35.0~~dev, E-ACSL). In a minimal repro (`requires` clauses, called with `size == 5`, `char buf[8] = "abc"`), `\exists integer n; 0 <= n < size && n == 1000 && n >= 0` passed, and so did `\exists integer n; 0 <= n < size && buf[n] == 'z' && \valid(buf + (0 .. n))`; with the two conjuncts swapped, both fail as they should. In the S4 stubs, the conjuncts moved into one predicate call (`0 <= i < n && P(s, i)`) are all checked: the E-ACSL cases of `tests/test_fc_stub_repairs.py` stop the overflowing and unterminated calls.
- **Cause:** an E-ACSL translation bug of guarded quantifiers: the conjunct right after the guard is dropped (`bound_variables.ml`, `Pand` case, loses the goal found in its left operand). Drafted with a validated patch as Issue 24, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-guarded-exists-drops-conjunct.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-guarded-exists-drops-conjunct.md), and filed upstream as [pub/frama-c#2793](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/work_items/2793) on 2026-11-15, with the fix in merge request [pub/frama-c!25](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/merge_requests/25) (reproduces on `master` `3dff90fc70` and 34.0~beta).
- **Fix in the S4 stubs (fixed):** every E-ACSL quantifier in `fc_strlcpy.h`, `bsd/string.h` and `json-c/json.h` has exactly one predicate call after its guard (`__fc_bsd_cpy_fits(...)`, `__fc_json_nul(...)`); `tests/test_fc_stub_repairs.py::test_eacsl_quantifiers_have_one_conjunct_after_the_guard` enforces it.
- **Fixed for the vocabulary (integration of S4 and S8, branch `templates-stubs-integration`):** S8 replaced `valid_nstring` and gave `valid_read_nstring` libc's semantics without a quantifier; the E-ACSL `valid_read_string` and `valid_string` in `templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h` still had the dropped form (`\exists integer i; 0 <= i <= 65536 && \valid_read(s + (0 .. i)) && s[i] == '\0'`) and now call one predicate after the guard (`__fc_eacsl_read_nul_at`, `__fc_eacsl_nul_at`), checked by `tests/test_fc_stub_repairs.py::test_compat_eacsl_string_predicates_have_one_conjunct_after_the_guard`. **Resolved by image patch 24 (was an Pipeline bug (closed, see the archive note)):** contracts written by provers and breakers could hit it: a guardrail can pass a clause it did not check in full. Not yet done: a lint over guardrail sources, and an upstream report.

### Symptom: E-ACSL Crash With `failure: null` or a Location Inside the E-ACSL Runtime (`unsafe_initialize`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Sanitizer replay attribution is automatic.
- **Cause:** heap corruption made the E-ACSL RTL crash before it printed the failing assertion.
- **Fix:** automatic. The crash input is replayed on a sanitizer build of the same slice; `attribution` and `failure_source: sanitizer_replay` give the precise file:line. The crash is still classified from the E-ACSL run.

### Symptom: `UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer: SEGV on unknown address` Is Not Parsed as a Failure
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. parse_sanitizer_failure UBSan SEGV fix is in with its test.
- **Fixed pipeline bug (2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/failure_identity.py`](../../scripts/failure_identity.py))** (found 2026-11-10 while diagnosing the guardrail entry in [`./wp-and-guardrail.md`](./wp-and-guardrail.md), on the output of a libmnl guardrail binary built by E-ACSL on Frama-C 35). E-ACSL binaries print a deadly signal as `UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer:DEADLYSIGNAL`, then `==N==ERROR: UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer: SEGV on unknown address ...`, a stack and a `SUMMARY:` line. Previously `parse_sanitizer_failure` expected `runtime error:` for UBSan or `AddressSanitizer` for ASan and returned `None`.
- **Fix:** `parse_sanitizer_failure` in [`../../scripts/failure_identity.py`](../../scripts/failure_identity.py) now parses `ERROR: UndefinedBehaviorSanitizer: <kind>` (e.g. `SEGV`, `BUS`, `FPE`, `ILL`, `ABRT`, `stack-overflow`) and its `SUMMARY:` fallback, skips runtime/harness/bare-libc frames (`__ubsan_`, `__sanitizer_`, `fuzzer::`), sets `kind: "ubsan_<slug>"` and `is_rte: True`, and marks `.instrumented.c` frames for sanitizer-replay attribution (`needs_attribution`). Test: `test_parse_sanitizer_failure_recognizes_ubsan_segv_and_deadly_signals` in [`../../tests/test_failure_identity.py`](../../tests/test_failure_identity.py).

### Symptom: E-ACSL and Sanitizer Parser Misses Locations in Non-`.c`/`.h` Source Files (`.inc`, `.y`, `.cpp`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. File patterns generalised (now in failure_identity.py).
- **Cause:** `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py` regexes `_FN_RE`, `_ERR_RE`, `_OLD_RE`, and `_UBSAN_RE` required files to have `.c`, `.h`, `.i`, or `.acsl` extensions. Errors in included template headers (`.inc`), grammar specifications (`.y`), or other extensions were rejected and returned `None`.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`):** Changed file patterns to `(?:[A-Za-z]:)?[^\s:'"]+`, anchored by `:(?P<line>\d+)(?::\d+)?: Error:` and `: runtime error:`.

### Symptom: Sanitizer Crash Fingerprinted as `unknown#unknown_function` Despite Stack Trace
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Frame regex decoupled from file/line.
- **Cause:** `_STACK_FRAME_RE` in `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py` coupled function extraction to `.c`/`.h` file and line presence. When symbols were in non-`.c` files or had binary offset addresses (`(/bin+0x123)`), `_STACK_FRAME_RE` skipped the frame, resulting in `candidates = []` and fingerprinting as `unknown#unknown_function`.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`):** Decoupled function extraction from file and line in `_STACK_FRAME_RE`, with defensive guards against `None` file paths in `_parse_segment_failure` and `parse_sanitizer_failure`.

### Symptom: Multi-Word AddressSanitizer Errors Misclassified as `mem_access`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Multi-word ASan kinds handled.
- **Cause:** `_ASAN_ERR_RE` in `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py` matched `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`, truncating `attempting double-free` and `attempting free on address which was not malloc()-ed` to `"attempting"`. `_map_sanitizer_alarm_kind` failed to match `"bad-free"` or `"attempting free"` and defaulted to `mem_access`.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`):** Added explicit multi-word kinds to `_ASAN_ERR_RE`, added `"double-free" in low` to `_map_sanitizer_alarm_kind`, and normalized kind slug names.

### Symptom: Access Size Variations Fragment ASan Crash Fingerprints
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. ASan op normalised to direction only.
- **Cause:** `_ASAN_OP_RE` in `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py` preserved `of size <N>` in the fingerprinted predicate, causing identical bugs with different read/write byte widths (e.g. 1 vs 4 bytes) to yield distinct fingerprints.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`):** Normalized operation capture in `_ASAN_OP_RE` to direction only (`READ` / `WRITE`), dropping the byte size from the fingerprinted predicate.

### Symptom: Predicate Normalizer Strips Expression Keywords `check`, `admit`, `assert`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Label regex requires a colon; _ALARM_KIND_RE precompiled.
- **Cause:** `_LABEL_RE` in `scripts/fingerprint.py` matched `(?:assert|check|admit)\s+` without requiring a trailing colon, erroneously stripping variable names in expressions like `check > 0`.
- **Fix (fixed, uncommitted, `scripts/fingerprint.py`):** Required colon `:(?!:)` after label keywords in `_LABEL_RE`. Precompiled `_ALARM_KIND_RE` once at module scope to eliminate per-call regex compilation.

### Symptom: One overflow becomes two candidates, `minmea.c#minmea_scan::signed_overflow::525b51da66` and `minmea.c#minmea_scan::signed_overflow::d8e58750af`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Site merge landed in 4de97ca with its test; the remaining fingerprint difference is tracked in the later entry at line 290.
- **Seen on:** minmea run-1, 2026-11-14, modules `minmea` and `tf_fuzzer`, engine `eacsl+sanitizers`. Two sinks (`minmea.c:186` in `minmea_scan`, `minmea.c:656` in `minmea_getdatetime`) gave four fingerprints; `ingest` kept four candidates and `wp_scheduler.py step` dispatched four Crash Verifier tasks.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. `make_fingerprint` in [`../../scripts/fingerprint.py`](../../scripts/fingerprint.py) hashes the normalized predicate, and the two engines describe the same failure differently. The E-ACSL assertion gives the RTE predicate (`scale * 10 ≤ 2147483647`, failure kind `assertion`); UBSan gives its own message turned into a predicate (`rte: signed_overflow: signed integer overflow: N * N cannot be represented in type 'int'`, failure kind `ubsan_runtime_error`). File, function and alarm kind are equal, so only the hash differs. This contradicts the module's own statement that one root cause keeps one identity across tools.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline bug (Phase 1 step S4, merge `4de97ca`, on `phase1-remediation`):** `ledger.upsert_candidate` records a detection at the statement site (`failure_identity.site_key`) of an unverified candidate on that candidate, so the two engines' reports of one statement are one candidate with two `observations` and one verifier task (`tests/test_candidate_sites.py::test_asan_eacsl_and_eva_at_one_statement_give_one_candidate_with_three_observations`). Limits: the two fingerprints still differ (`make_fingerprint` is unchanged), a detection whose source line cannot be read keeps its own candidate, and no target run has checked it yet (validation V of the remediation plan has not run). As recorded on run-1: no merge is done at ingest either. Each bug costs a second verifier agent and appears twice in the report unless a verifier links them. No workaround was applied to the run.

### Symptom: `memcmp` With Identical or Overlapping Buffers Aborts Under `--libc-replacements` With `memcpy: overlapping memory areas` (`libc_contract` False Positive)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Upstream memcmp defect fixed by queued image patch 17, which makes the open gap moot.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR run-6, 2026-11-14, module `libqcbor_a`, dispatch case `5` (`UsefulInputBuf_Compare` in `src/UsefulBuf.c:236`), Frama-C `34.0~beta (Selenium)`, engine `eacsl` (`--libc-replacements`). The E-ACSL campaign recorded `unknown#UsefulInputBuf_Compare::libc_contract::6f7b1f0708` (`memcpy: overlapping memory areas`, `failure_kind: "eacsl_libc_replacement"`), while the `sanitizers` engine executed case `5` cleanly with zero crashes.
- **Cause:** upstream Frama-C E-ACSL bug ([`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-memcmp-overlapping-buffers.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-memcmp-overlapping-buffers.md), Issue 17 in [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-e-acsl-issues.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-e-acsl-issues.md)). In `share/e-acsl/libc_replacements/e_acsl_string.c:235-242`, `eacsl_builtin_memcmp` invokes `validate_overlapping_spaces((uintptr_t)s1, n, (uintptr_t)s2, n, "memcpy");` — a copy-paste defect from `eacsl_builtin_memcpy`. ISO C (C99/C11/C17 7.24.4.1) places no non-overlap restriction on `memcmp`, which only reads both regions (`const void *s1, const void *s2`); self-comparison (`memcmp(p, p, n)`) and sliding-window prefix checks (`memcmp(buf, buf + k, n)`) are strictly conforming C. In `--api` harnesses where two `q_useful_buf_c` slices share or alias `fuzz_buf`, `UsefulBuf_Compare` calls `memcmp` on overlapping ranges with `n > 0` and triggers a deterministic `abort()`.
- **Fix / workaround:** Crash Verifier subagents replay the artifact on a plain ASan+UBSan build (where `memcmp` succeeds) and inspect `e_acsl_string.c:240` to reject the false positive. When a module hits repeated false stops in `--libc-replacements`, `--no-fuzz-libc-checks` disables E-ACSL's libc replacement wrappers for that module. **Resolved by image patch 17 (was an Pipeline gap (closed, see the archive note)):** [`../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py) does not yet wrap or rewrite `__e_acsl_builtin_memcmp` to check only `\valid_read` without `validate_overlapping_spaces`.

### Symptom: Multi-Round Dispatch Fuzzing Spends Over Half Its Wall-Clock Time in Single-Threaded `frama-c -e-acsl` Stage 1 Rebuilds (`-DFRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES`) and Overwrites `workspace/harnesses/fuzz_<mod>`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Runtime skip merged.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR run-6, 2026-11-14, module `libqcbor_a` (`181`-case `--api` dispatch harness). Phase 1 `fuzz` took `39 min 06 s` wall clock (`19:02:19Z` to `19:41:25Z`) for `17 min 21 s` of actual LibFuzzer execution (`521.6 s` `sanitizers` across 12 rounds + `519.6 s` `eacsl` across 15 rounds); the remaining `21 min 45 s` went into 14 single-threaded `frama-c -rte -e-acsl` Stage 1 rebuilds (~93 s and ~27 MB of `state/cache/eacsl-build` growth each, ~380 MB total).
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) (`run_campaign_rounds` / `main`) and [`../../templates/harness_dual.c.template`](../../templates/harness_dual.c.template). Whenever a round found a new crashing dispatch case, `_rebuild(mask)` appended `-DFRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES=0x...` / `-DFRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES_k=0x...` to `cppflags` and invoked [`../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py) with `out_bin=args.out_bin`. Because `cppflags` is hashed into the Stage 1 `eacsl-build` cache key, every new skip bitmask missed the Stage 1 Frama-C AST/E-ACSL cache, forced a full single-threaded OCaml `frama-c -e-acsl` pass over the module, did not count rebuild time against the campaign budget, and clobbered `workspace/harnesses/fuzz_<mod>` on disk with a skip-masked binary.
- **Fix:** **Fixed** (branch `sup/QCBOR-fuzz-runtime-skip-cases`, commit `aeac81d`, merged into `phase1-remediation` as `436691f` on 2026-11-15 after run-6 ended): [`../../templates/harness_dual.c.template`](../../templates/harness_dual.c.template) and [`../../scripts/generate_harness.py`](../../scripts/generate_harness.py) emit `framac_fuzz_skip_case(unsigned int c)` under `#if !defined(__FRAMAC__) || defined(__FRAMAC_EACSL__)` (`#define FRAMAC_FUZZ_RUNTIME_SKIP_CASES 1`), parsing comma-separated decimal case indices from `FRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES` once into a 4096-bit static BSS bitset while keeping `-DFRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES[_k]` compile-time masks intact. [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) passes `FRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES` through sandbox `extra_env` across rounds with zero Stage 1/2 rebuilds (`keep_going.runtime_skip: true`), and writes legacy fallback rebuilds to `.skip_<bin>/` (`use_cache=False`) so `--out-bin` is never overwritten. Covered by [`../../tests/test_fuzz_keep_going.py`](../../tests/test_fuzz_keep_going.py).

### Symptom: `getenv(...)` Always Returns `NULL` Inside E-ACSL-Instrumented Binaries Compiled With `--full-mtracking` (`e-acsl-gcc -M`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Upstream defect fixed by queued image patch 18; the pipeline shim was the workaround.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR run-6 supervision, 2026-11-14, while verifying runtime `FRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES` under Frama-C `34.0~beta (Selenium)` (`framac-analysis:latest`). Any `getenv(...)` call inside `LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput` or `main` after `__e_acsl_memory_init` returned `NULL` despite the variable being set in the process environment.
- **Cause:** upstream Frama-C E-ACSL runtime bug ([`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-memory-init-clobbers-environ.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-memory-init-clobbers-environ.md), Issue 18 in [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-e-acsl-issues.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-e-acsl-issues.md)). In `share/frama-c/share/e-acsl/observation_model/segment_model/e_acsl_segment_observation_model.c:283-293` and `bittree_model/e_acsl_bittree_observation_model.c:573-578`, `argv_alloca()` registers and marks initialized every string in `environ`, advancing global `extern char **environ` in place (`while (*environ) { ... environ++; }`) instead of iterating a local `char **env = environ;` cursor, leaving global `environ` pointing at its terminating `NULL`.
- **Fix:** worked around in pipeline Stage 2 (branch `sup/QCBOR-fuzz-runtime-skip-cases`, commit `aeac81d`, merged into `phase1-remediation` as `436691f` on 2026-11-15); the upstream E-ACSL defect remains (Issue 18): when `fuzzer_flag` is set and `<out>.instrumented.c` calls `getenv(...)`, [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) (`_build_environ_keep_object`) compiles `<out>_env_keep/eacsl_env_keep.o` and links with `-Wl,--wrap=__e_acsl_memory_init` to save `environ` before `__real___e_acsl_memory_init` and restore it immediately afterward, preserving both `getenv(...)` lookup and E-ACSL shadow-memory validity/initialization on the returned string.

### Symptom: A Keep-Going Campaign Stops After Its First Round With `stopped: time_budget_spent`, `skipped_cases: []` and `fuzz_seconds` Far Above `max_time`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed behind FRAMAC_FUZZ_EXPLORE_JUDGE (default on), merged.
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, module `libcares_a__parsers`, 2026-11-16, found while reviewing [`../plans/fuzz-budget-design.md`](../plans/fuzz-budget-design.md). The fuzz report has `sizing.max_time: 60`, `keep_going.fuzz_seconds: 182.0`, one round with `crashed_cases: [1]` and `stopped: time_budget_spent`, and `skipped_cases: []`. The log tail shows a crash while reading the seed corpus (`ReadAndExecuteSeedCorpora`) after 22 executed units, on the `inet_ntop4` `%u` false positive. The other 17 cases, fed by 690 imported DNS packets and 14 harvested seeds, were never fuzzed.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug: `run_campaign_rounds` in [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) measured `spent` around the whole `campaign()` call. That call is `run_fuzz_campaign`, which after libFuzzer exits also replays the crash artifacts and builds the ASan+UBSan attribution binary (`<out-bin>.sanreplay`). All of that was charged to the fuzz budget. Once the CPU floor is reached (`MIN_ROUND_CPU_S` CPU-seconds, `MIN_ROUND_WALL_S` before the S6a change), the loop stops before it skips the crashed case, so a crash that needs attribution can end a dispatch campaign after one round.
- **Fix:** **Fixed behind `FRAMAC_FUZZ_EXPLORE_JUDGE` (default `on`, set `=off` to roll back; commits `73f3d48` and `bb7ab9e` in [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py) and [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py)):**
  - P1c pure execution timing charges only libFuzzer subprocess execution time (`fuzz_s`) against round budgets in `run_campaign_rounds()`, recording `build_s`, `fuzz_s`, and `replay_s` separately.
  - P7f dual pre-build compiles and caches both `.e-acsl` and `.san` binaries in the `build` stage so fuzz and replay never compile inside a fuzz lease.
  - P1a/P1b per-case blinding stops a crashing dispatch case after `K_case = 16` consecutive 0-pass crashes (and non-dispatch fork mode after 64 crashing jobs with `corp == 0`) instead of burning the whole module budget.
  - Tests: `tests/test_p4_infra_prereqs.py` and `tests/test_p4_explore_judge.py`. With `FRAMAC_FUZZ_EXPLORE_JUDGE=off`, the legacy timing path can be restored.

### Symptom: After `params set --fuzz-engine eacsl` to Undo the DAG's `sanitizers` Choice, Every Campaign Reports `engine_used: eacsl` and the Dual-Engine Split Never Runs
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. params set --fuzz-engine eacsl warns.
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, all 7 modules, 2026-11-16 (`cmd-set-eacsl.sh` in the run directory). The DAG had chosen `sanitizers` for the units because of the setjmp false positive (F2 of the supervision report). The model overrode it with `--fuzz-engine eacsl`, and Phase 1 fuzzed with E-ACSL only.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap: `eacsl` means one engine only, and `auto` is the value that keeps both (ASan+UBSan first, then E-ACSL). A recorded `auto` overrides the DAG's choice as well ([`../../scripts/pipeline_make.py`](../../scripts/pipeline_make.py), `_plan_module`: the DAG's engine applies only when `params.json` has no `fuzz_engine` for the module). `skills/framac-fuzz/SKILL.md` said that `eacsl` fuzzes with one engine only, but `params set --fuzz-engine eacsl` emitted no warning.
- **Fix:** **Fixed (`6382be6`, [`../../scripts/pipeline_make.py`](../../scripts/pipeline_make.py), P2b):** `params set --fuzz-engine eacsl` now emits an explicit warning (both in `warnings` and on stderr) stating that `eacsl` disables dual-engine mode and naming `--fuzz-engine auto` to restore it. Test: `tests/test_p3_p4_pipeline_make.py`. In a run, use `params set --module <mod> --fuzz-engine auto`.

### Symptom: With `-warn-invalid-pointer`, Almost Every Input Fails an E-ACSL Build With `invalid pointer: \null ≡ format ∨ \object_pointer(format)`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Upstream \object_pointer defect fixed by queued image patch 29; pipeline never passed the option.
- **Seen on:** a trial on 2026-11-16 for [`../plans/fuzz-budget-design.md`](../plans/fuzz-budget-design.md) (P9), in copies of minmea run-1 and nettle run-3, Frama-C 34.0~beta in `framac-analysis:latest`. The pipeline does not pass this option to E-ACSL, so no run shows it.
  - `minmea`: 371 of the 372 inputs that pass the normal build fail at `workspace/work/minmea.c:114` (`while (*format)` in `minmea_scan`), with `\object_pointer(format): 0` and `format` pointing to a format string literal.
  - `libnettle_a__sha3`: 15 of 24 fail at `workspace/work/sha3-permute.c:169` on `\null ≡ &nettle_sha3_permute_rc[i] ∨ \object_pointer(...)`, a pointer into a `static const` table.
- **Cause:** not a pipeline defect. E-ACSL's `\object_pointer` is false for a pointer to read-only static data, although the E-ACSL manual lists the predicate as supported. The program below passes for objects 0 and 1 and aborts for 2 and 3, at the object's first byte:

  ```c
  #include <stdio.h>

  static const unsigned char const_table[4] = {1, 2, 3, 0};
  static unsigned char plain_table[4] = {1, 2, 3, 0};

  static int scan(const unsigned char *p) {
    int n = 0;
    while (*p) {
      unsigned char v = *p++;
      n += v;
    }
    return n;
  }

  int main(int argc, char **argv) {
    unsigned char local[4] = {1, 2, 3, 0};
    int which = argc > 1 ? argv[1][0] - '0' : 0;
    if (which == 0) fprintf(stderr, "local array: done %d\n", scan(local));
    if (which == 1) fprintf(stderr, "plain global array: done %d\n", scan(plain_table));
    if (which == 2) fprintf(stderr, "static const array: done %d\n", scan(const_table));
    if (which == 3) fprintf(stderr, "string literal: done %d\n", scan((const unsigned char *)"abc"));
    return 0;
  }
  ```

  - Build: `e-acsl-gcc -c -G clang -F "-rte -warn-invalid-pointer -e-acsl-full-mtracking" -e "-O1 -gline-tables-only" -o five.inst.c -O five five.c`, then `./five.e-acsl <0..3>`.
  - A string literal alone fails the same way with gcc as the back end and without `-e-acsl-full-mtracking`.
  - The check that fails is E-ACSL's own guard named `invalid pointer`, not the `rte/pointer_value` assertion.
  - Why the runtime answers 0: `eacsl_object_pointer` (`share/e-acsl/observation_model/e_acsl_observation_model.c:103-107`) is built on `eacsl_valid`, which is false for a block marked read-only; `eacsl_valid_read` would be right. A direct `//@ assert \object_pointer(p);` fails the same way without `-warn-invalid-pointer`, right after `\valid_read(p)` passes. Same on master (35.0~~dev, `3dff90f`). Tail-merged literals (F11 of [`../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md`](../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md)) are not the cause: one literal alone fails.
- **Fix:** none. Do not add `-warn-invalid-pointer` to the E-ACSL build. Eva's `pointer_value` alarms therefore have no run-time check in Phase 1. Upstream draft, not filed: [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-object-pointer-readonly-static.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-object-pointer-readonly-static.md) (Issue 29). It has the cause, the matrix (gcc/clang, with and without full tracking, 34.0~beta and master) and a two-line runtime change checked on a copy of the runtime.

### Symptom: `\valid_read(charset + j)` Fails at `j: 1` in `ares_buf_consume_until_charset` (`ares_buf.c:980`) on Every Input, Before the Input Is Read
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 078e336; the false negative of the reorder is removed by queued image patch 28; the verifier brief part merged.
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, 2026-11-16, Phase 1 ([`../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md`](../runs/2026-11-16-c-ares-run1-supervision.md)). Fingerprint `src/lib/str/ares_buf.c#ares_buf_consume_until_charset::mem_access::32e8ca0441`.
  - `ahost`, `adig` and `tf_ares-test-fuzz-name` crash on the empty input (`crash-da39a3ee...`), so these three campaigns never ran past init.
  - `libcares_a__channel` hits it on some inputs only.
  - Stack: `ares_buf_split` <- `ares_sconfig_append_fromstr` <- `ares_sysconfig_parse_resolv_line` <- ... <- `ares_init_options`, reading the container's `/etc/resolv.conf`.
- **Cause:** upstream E-ACSL combined with clang. It is not a pipeline function-selection bug: the caller is instrumented, and `" ,"` is registered.
  1. Under `-e-acsl-full-mtracking`, E-ACSL never turns string literals into named arrays. `memory_tracking.ml` does that only in `Varinfos.add`. Each literal is registered as its own block and printed as a literal.
  2. clang ignores `-fno-merge-constants` and emits literals in `.rodata.str1.1` (`AMS`).
  3. GNU ld then stores `","` inside `" ,"`.
  4. `","` is registered after `" ,"` in `__e_acsl_globals_init`, so the shared bytes leave the block of `" ,"`.
  5. `\valid_read(" ," + 1)` then fails the segment model's same-block check.
  - Reproduced with a 15-line fixture on 34.0~beta and on master (35.0~~dev, `3dff90f`). Builds with gcc, and builds without full tracking, are clean.
  - Upstream draft: `docs/upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-full-mtracking-tail-merged-literals.md`, not part of the merge (the branch dropped its copy in `06c4736`); it is kept as an uncommitted draft. It is related to Issue 10 but is not a duplicate.
- **Fix:** commit `078e336`, from branch `sup/c-ares-eacsl-literal-charset`, fixed and merged into `phase1-remediation` (2026-11-16).
  - `order_literal_registrations` in [`../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py) registers literals by ascending block size, so the enclosing literal is the last to write the shared bytes.
  - It is applied in `compile_eacsl_binary` in [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) and recorded as `compile.literal_registrations_reordered`.
  - Tests:
    - `tests/test_eacsl_toolchain.py::test_order_literal_registrations_*`
    - `tests/test_eacsl_toolchain.py::test_tail_merged_string_literals_do_not_fail_valid_read_in_the_analysis_image` (needs `FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1`)
  - Checked on a relink of run-1's `ahost`: the empty input gets past init, then stops at the `inet_ntop4` format false positive (next entry).
  - Run-1's fuzz results for these four modules are stale. Rebuild them with a checkout that has both fixes (this one and the format fix in the next entry, both now merged).
- **Still open:**
  - The reorder accepts a false negative: a read just before a merged suffix, but inside the enclosing literal, is not reported, and the suffix's `\base_addr`, `\offset` and `\block_length` are those of the enclosing literal. The upstream fix (demoting literals under full tracking) would remove it.
  - The Crash Verifier brief never names `toolchain_artifact`. A reproducible E-ACSL tool false positive can then be confirmed, or rejected as `harness_induced`, which triggers useless harness repairs (`wp_scheduler.py` `_ensure_repair`). Fixed and merged into `phase1-remediation` (2026-11-16), from branch `sup/c-ares-verifier-toolchain-artifact` (`fca3aa6`, `66166bd`); see [`./ledger-and-verifier.md`](./ledger-and-verifier.md).

### Symptom: Every Input That Prints With a `*` Width or Precision Aborts With `printf: directive 1 ('%.*s') expects argument of type 'char*' but the corresponding argument has type 'int'`, Then `libFuzzer: deadly signal`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Upstream defect fixed by queued image patch 32 (the entry said no fix).
- **Seen on:** a test fixture, not a target, 2026-11-17: the `read_tool` program of [`../../tests/test_harness_stdin.py`](../../tests/test_harness_stdin.py) printed `printf("RECORD:%.*s\n", (int)n, buf)` in an E-ACSL fuzz build (`fuzz_build.compile_eacsl_binary`, `framac-analysis:latest`). The stack ends in `validate_format_type` (`e_acsl_stdio.c:706`), called from `__e_acsl_builtin_printf`.
- **Cause:** an upstream E-ACSL bug in format-string validation (`--validate-format-strings`), read in the source at Frama-C master `3dff90fc70` (`plugins/e-acsl/share/e-acsl/libc_replacements/e_acsl_stdio.c`).
  - `fetch_format_field_width` and `fetch_format_precision` record a `*` as `-1` and do nothing else with it.
  - Each directive's argument index is its position among the directives (`fetch_format_argno(fmt, dir, prev, i)`), so the `int` a `*` consumes is never skipped.
  - `%.*s`, `%*d` and every directive after them are checked against the wrong argument: a valid program aborts on every call.
- **Also seen on:** sudo run-1, 2026-11-22, in a scratch rerun of `sudo_intercept_so`'s E-ACSL half after fix 14 let it start. The case is `resolve_path`'s `snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "%.*s/%s", dirlen, cp, cmnd)` (`sudo_intercept.c:144`). The fingerprint `unknown#resolve_path::eacsl_format_error::1df4874d0f` is a false positive of this runtime bug.
- **Fix:** none in the pipeline: its patched runtime (`format-runtime-1`, the entry above) does not cover it, and the fixture was changed to avoid `%.*s`.
- **Upstream (2026-11-17):** drafted as package 32 with a validated runtime patch on `master` `95408044f7`, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-format-star-width-precision.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-format-star-width-precision.md). Not filed. The patch does not cover POSIX numbered `*` arguments (`%2$*1$d`).
- **Effect on a run:** every E-ACSL input that reaches such a call aborts there, with a fingerprint ending in `eacsl_format_error`. Treat it as a tool false positive, not a finding. The workaround is the one of the entry above: `pipeline_make.py params set --module <mod> --no-fuzz-libc-checks`, which also drops the libc buffer checks.

### Symptom: `tests/test_failure_fixtures_docker.py` Fails on `asan_global_overflow`, `asan_stack_overflow` (`assert 'ubsan_runtime_error' == 'asan_global_buffer_overflow'`) and `lsan_direct` (`assert None is not None`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixture fix 3579d8e merged.
- **Seen on:** the test suite with `FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1`, `framac-analysis:latest` (Debian clang 19.1.7): 3 failed, 10 passed. First recorded 2026-11-17 on `phase1-remediation` at `9ac8d88`; measured again 2026-11-19 on `76e1f00` (where the test was added), `5cbf8c2`, `c74ae27`, `5daee01`, `22ae459`, `bc9a7fb` and `23e74e6`. A test fixture defect, not a pipeline defect.
- **Cause:** read from the programs' output in the image.
  - **The two ASan fixtures:** the programs indexed a declared array, so UBSan's bounds check reported first (`asan_global_overflow.c:7:12: runtime error: index 8 out of bounds for type 'int[8]'`). The test's recoverable build then also printed the ASan report, and `failure_identity.parse_runtime_failure` returned the UBSan one. A campaign build (`-fno-sanitize-recover=all`, `UBSAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=1`) stops at the UBSan report (`ubsan_reports=1 asan_reports=0`, exit 134), so such a defect is `ubsan_runtime_error` / `index_bound` there and the parser needs no change.
  - **`lsan_direct`:** a copy of the pointer stayed in a dead stack frame, which LeakSanitizer scans conservatively, so the block counted as reachable and the program exited 0 with no report. `LSAN_OPTIONS=log_pointers=1` shows the pointer found in the STACK range, and `use_stacks=0` reports the leak.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-19, `3579d8e`):** `asan_global_overflow.c` and `asan_stack_overflow.c` index through a pointer; `lsan_direct.c` clears the pointer and the dead frames before exit. The test and `expected.json` are unchanged: 13 passed, and the leak was reported in 50 of 50 runs.

### Symptom: A Failure Inside a libc Call Gets the Fingerprint `unknown#<call>::<alarm>::<hash>` (`unknown#free::freeable::...`), Whatever Function Made the Call
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Failure-site attribution fixed in failure_identity.py.
- **Seen on:** `framac-analysis:latest` (Debian clang 19.1.7), 2026-11-19, the programs `asan_double_free.c` and `asan_bad_free.c`, parsed as function `free`, file `unknown`, line 0, where `tests/fixtures/failures/expected.json` names `release_twice` and `release_local`. Also in a real run: libmodbus run-1 recorded `unknown#free::freeable::c2bf36bd0d` (`tests/fixtures/replay_identity/libmodbus_run1/replay/unknown_free_freeable_c2bf36bd0d-2dba217fa2.json`). mpack shows the same shape as `unknown#realloc::freeable::c2bf36bd0d` ([`./harnesses-and-seeds.md`](./harnesses-and-seeds.md)).
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. The image's static ASan runtime prints an intercepted libc call under its plain name and without a source location (`#0 0x... in free (/tmp/prog+0xcbeea)`), at times behind its own helpers (`StrstrCheck`, `StrtolFixAndCheck`, `MemcmpInterceptorCommon`, `printf_common`). `parse_sanitizer_failure` in `scripts/failure_identity.py` skipped only frames starting with `__interceptor_` or `__asan_`, so that frame became the failure's location. Of 42 defects probed in libc calls, only `memcpy`, `memmove` and `memset` (printed as `__asan_*`) were attributed to their caller. Leaks had the same gap for a libc call that allocates (`realpath`). gcc's shared libasan prints `free` with a source path under `libsanitizer`, which was not known as a runtime file. The synthetic fixtures spelled the frame `__interceptor_free`, and the Docker test compared only `kind` and `alarm_kind`, so neither caught it.
- **Effect:** different sites collapsed into one candidate with no source line, so a second defect in the same libc call was never reported on its own.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-19, `scripts/failure_identity.py`):** the site is the first frame of the failure's own stack that has a source location and is neither runtime nor harness code (`_is_target_frame`, `_first_stack`). The `freed by` and `allocated by` stacks are not searched, so a harness that makes the bad call is not blamed on a target function that freed the block earlier. The predicate names the call the target made (` in strstr from parse_name`). A failure with no target frame on its stack keeps the earlier result. ASan's allocation cap was recognized partly by having no location, so `is_sanitizer_allocation_cap` now reads its kind alone and `needs_attribution` keeps refusing it as an artifact's identity. All 42 probed defects now land on their calling line. `asan_double_free.txt` and `asan_bad_free.txt` use the recorded frame shape, and the Docker test also compares the function for ASan and LeakSanitizer.
- **After upgrading:** fingerprints of these failures change. A run started before the fix keeps candidates under the old `unknown#...` fingerprint while a replay reports the new one, so rerun Phase 1 of such a run. How the ledger treats such a mixed run was not measured.

### Symptom: E-ACSL Instrumentation Succeeds but the Generated C Is Rejected With `error: cannot redeclare builtin function '__builtin_add_overflow'`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Overflow builtins defined in fc_compat_types.h.
- **Seen on:** mpack run-3 (2026-11-17), `build-check` of all 13 harness modules. The instrumented file fails to compile (`workspace/harnesses/fuzz_<mod>.instrumented.c:<line>:34: error: cannot redeclare builtin function '__builtin_add_overflow'`), and the same run logs `[kernel:typing:implicit-function-declaration] .../mpack-expect.c:480: Warning: Calling undeclared function __builtin_mul_overflow. Old style K&R code?` plus the matching `__builtin_add_overflow` warnings in `mpack-reader.c`.
- **Cause:** MPack's checked arithmetic expands to `__builtin_add_overflow`/`__builtin_mul_overflow` under GCC (`MPACK_HAS_OVERFLOW_BUILTINS=1`, target mpack `src/mpack/mpack-platform.h:1889-1927`). Frama-C's kernel has no prototype for those builtins, so E-ACSL's printer emits `extern int ( /* missing proto */ __builtin_add_overflow)(...)` into the instrumented C, and clang refuses to redeclare a compiler builtin. The instrumentation step itself finishes; only the compile of the generated file fails. The E-ACSL toolchain did not stub or normalize the GCC overflow builtins (fixed, see below).
- **Run workaround (before the fix):** `-DMPACK_HAS_OVERFLOW_BUILTINS=0` in the module cppflags (`generate_harness.py --cppflags`), which selects the target's portable checked-arithmetic fallback (`result < a`, division-based multiply check: same semantics, no builtins). A report for such a run must state that the analysed configuration uses that fallback.
- **Eva and WP:** with Frama-C's own libc on the pipeline's `gcc_x86_64` machdep, the generated `__fc_machdep.h` includes `FRAMAC_SHARE/libc/__fc_gcc_builtins.h`. It declares the typed forms (`__builtin_sadd_overflow` ... `__builtin_umulll_overflow`) with contracts and defines `__builtin_{add,sub,mul}_overflow` as `_Generic` macros over them, so only the `_p` forms were undeclared (`missing-spec`, any value). Frama-C's `_Generic` picks the typed form from the first operand's type alone, so mixed operand types call the wrong one (`[kernel:typing:incompatible-types-call]`); that is Frama-C's model and the pipeline leaves it alone.
- **Upstream (2026-11-17):** drafted as package 34, issue only, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-kernel-gcc-overflow-builtins.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-kernel-gcc-overflow-builtins.md). Not filed. It reports the wrong Eva result for mixed operand types, the parse error for `char` operands, the missing `_p` forms and the uncompilable implicit declaration.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-17, `a3ec02c` from `sup/mpack-overflow-builtins`, merged into `phase1-remediation` as `f32ba49`; analysis image rebuilt):**
  - `templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h` defines the three `_p` forms for every Frama-C parse, and the generic and typed forms only under `__FRAMAC_EACSL__` (the E-ACSL frontend on host headers, where none is known). They are GNU statement-expression macros with GCC's semantics: each operand is evaluated once, and the computation uses 32-bit halves, so no step raises a downcast or overflow alarm.
  - Tests: `tests/test_fc_compat_contracts.py::test_overflow_builtins_are_defined_only_where_frama_c_lacks_them`, `::test_overflow_builtins_parse_with_frama_c_libc_on_the_pipeline_machdep` (Docker: Eva on `gcc_x86_64` with the compat flags, no syntax error, no undeclared builtin, 0 alarms) and `::test_overflow_builtins_match_gcc_under_eacsl` (Docker: E-ACSL output equal to gcc's builtins case by case, no `missing proto`). mpack's `mpack-{expect,reader,writer,node}.c` typecheck with both flag sets.
  - Not covered: a `_p` form whose third argument is a bit-field or const-qualified.
- **History (pipeline bug in the first fix):** the first version (`4127a28`, merged as `87841fb`) defined all 24 forms for every Frama-C parse. Because `fc_compat_types.h` is force-included before `__fc_gcc_builtins.h`, the typed-form macros expanded inside that header's declarations and every kernel-libc parse failed: the analysis image build stopped at `phase0_model.py selftest` with `parse_failed`, `[kernel] FRAMAC_SHARE/libc/__fc_gcc_builtins.h:37: User Error: syntax error:`. It was reverted the same day (`6632b74`). Its Docker test ran Eva on Frama-C's default machdep (`x86_64`), which never includes that header, so it did not catch this.

### Symptom: Every Sanitizer and Coverage Build of a Module Fails With `clang: error: cannot specify -o when generating multiple output files`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. compile_inputs keeps only translation units.
- **Seen on:** mpack run-3, Phase 1, 2026-11-17.
  - `workspace/harnesses/fuzz_libmpack_a__mpack_writer_3.sanitizer_compile.log` holds `clang ... workspace/work/src/mpack/mpack-writer.c workspace/work/src/mpack/mpack-writer.h ... -o ...` and `Returncode: 1`.
  - Every `libmpack_a__*` fuzz report says `"dual_engine_skipped": "sanitizer build failed"`, so Phase 1 ran E-ACSL only.
  - Every `reports/coverage/<mod>.json` had `compile.returncode` 1 and `the coverage build failed`, until the driving session dropped the `.h` files from `--sources` by hand with a run-local script.
  - `sax_example`, which has no header in its sources, built both engines.
- **Cause:** pipeline defect.
  - mpack defines functions in its headers (`MPACK_INLINE`), so the Phase 0 model gives those functions the header as their file.
  - `api_harness.generate` builds a harness's `sources` from its entries' files plus `dag["modules"][module]["required_sources"]`. For a split cluster, which lives in `dag["submodules"]`, that list is empty, so the header becomes a harness source.
  - Frama-C (E-ACSL stage 1, Eva, WP) parses a header as a file of its own. clang treats it as a precompiled-header output and refuses `-o` when there are several outputs.
  - The mix of `src/...` and `workspace/work/...` paths in the sidecar comes from the overlay resolution, which every consumer normalises; it is not a defect.
- **Fixed:** `6cae079` from `sup/mpack-harness-sources-and-fd-leak`, merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-17 (merge `7cb53a7`).
  - `framac_common.compile_inputs` keeps the translation units of a source list.
  - `fuzz_build._compile_clang_binary` compiles only those. That covers the sanitizer build (fuzz, attribution, crash replay, decision-point replays) and the coverage build.
  - The header stays in the sidecar's `sources`, where E-ACSL's function list (`_discover_slice_functions`) and the module scope read it.
  - Tests are in `tests/test_clang_build_inputs.py`; the supervisor reran them (24 passed together with the S7 tests).
- **Workaround on an older checkout:** pass `--sources` without the `.h` files to the coverage and sanitizer steps. This does not lose header functions from the coverage report, because llvm-cov lists the inline functions the translation units instantiate under the header's name. Run-3's report lists 140 of them.

### Symptom: An E-ACSL Build Stops With `Unexpected error (E_ACSL__Gmp.Q.Not_a_decimal("1e-9"))`, or an Annotation With a Floating-Point Constant Fails Although It Holds (`Assertion failed: f ≡ 0.1f`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. All causes are upstream and fixed by queued image patches 43, 51, 52, 53; the open gap about -float-print hex is moot.
- **Seen on:** the upstream audit of 2026-11-18 (no pipeline run), by hand with `e-acsl-gcc` on `framac-analysis:latest` (34.0~beta) and on `master` `b80fcac74f`, with the same results (cause 1 on plain `master`; causes 2 and 3 on that commit with package 43's patch installed, which changes E-ACSL's `gmp.ml` only). Three upstream E-ACSL and kernel defects around real and floating-point constants. None is a pipeline defect.
- **Cause 1, a constant with an exponent that is not exactly a `double`** (`1e-9`, `1.5e-3`; a tolerance such as `x - y <= 1e-9` is enough): E-ACSL's conversion to a GMP fraction knows plain decimals only, and Frama-C stops with `[kernel] Unexpected error (E_ACSL__Gmp.Q.Not_a_decimal("1e-9"))`, exit code 125. A `long double` constant (`0.1L`) builds, and is 0 at run time without any message. Package 43, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-real-constant-notation.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-real-constant-notation.md), with a validated patch; not filed. With `\abs(x - y) <= 1e-9` the build does not stop: E-ACSL ignores the annotation first, because it does not support `\abs` (`logic functions or predicates with no definition nor reads clause' is not yet supported`).
- **Cause 2, a constant that is exactly a `double` or a `float` and needs more than 12 digits** (`0.1f`, `0x1p-68`; a plain decimal such as `0.1` is not exactly a `double` and goes through GMP, exactly): E-ACSL takes the kernel's value, and the kernel prints a constant without source text with 12 significant digits. The check becomes `(double)f == 0.10000000149`, which is false for `float f = 0.1f`. Only the two equalities were run; an inequality would be tested against a bound that is off in the 13th digit.
- **Cause 3, constant folding:** without `--no-constfold`, `e-acsl-gcc` folds the program's own constant expressions and prints them the same way (`double t = 1.0 / 3.0;` becomes `double t = 0.333333333333;`), so the instrumented program computes other values than the target. The pipeline always passes `--no-constfold` ([`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py), [`../../scripts/stub_check.py`](../../scripts/stub_check.py)), so it does not have this one.
- **What the pipeline's builds are exposed to:** the float-to-integer alarms of `-rte` were checked on two casts on 34.0~beta (`(long long)d`, `(unsigned int)d`) and are not concerned: their bounds come out as GMP strings or as short exact constants such as `4294967296.`. Other alarm kinds were not looked at. Annotations of the target, and the contracts a prover writes and the guardrail replays, are: with cause 1 the build fails, and with cause 2 a clause that compares a floating-point value with a long constant can fail on a correct program, or pass on a wrong one.
- **Fix:** write a tolerance as a plain decimal (`0.000000001`). For cause 2, `-float-print hex` in the Frama-C flags makes the kernel print exact hexadecimal constants; with `e-acsl-gcc --no-constfold -F "-float-print hex"` both examples above hold, on 34.0~beta and on `master`. **Moot after image patches 43, 51, 52 and 53 (was an Pipeline gap (closed, see the archive note)):** `EACSL_FC_EXTRA_FLAGS` ([`../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py)) does not pass `-float-print hex`. It was tried by hand only, not in a pipeline build, and no pipeline code was changed. Causes 2 and 3 are candidate H10 of [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-hunt-2026-11-18-candidates.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-hunt-2026-11-18-candidates.md).
- **Corrections after a second review (2026-11-18, on plain `master` `b80fcac74f` and on 34.0~beta):**
  - **Cause 2:** only `double` values change. A `float` printed with 12 digits reads back as the same `float`; `f == 0.1f` fails because E-ACSL builds the constant as a `double`. An integer literal is concerned too: `double d2 = 123456789012345.0;` with `assert d2 == 123456789012345;` fails (`d2 == 1.23456789012e+14`).
  - **`-float-print hex` is not a complete fix:** an infinite or NaN constant is still printed `inf`, `-inf` or `NaN`, which is not C. With constant folding, `double a = 1.0 / 0.0;` makes the default `e-acsl-gcc` fail (`'inf' undeclared`); with the pipeline's `--no-constfold` it builds and runs. In an annotation, `x < 1e40f` gives `x < inf` with or without the option (candidates H15 and N4).
  - **A fourth cause, a real cast to `double` in an annotation (candidate H11):** E-ACSL converts the GMP fraction with `mpq_get_d`, which truncates. `double e = 0.1; assert e == (double)0.1;` aborts although it holds, and `double e = 0x1.9999999999999p-4; assert e == (double)0.1;` passes although it is false (`e-acsl-gcc --keep-going`, exit 0, no message). No option changes it. A contract that the guardrail replays can therefore pass wrongly on such a clause.

### Symptom: An E-ACSL Build Stops With `[e-acsl] Failure: typing was not performed on construct 1` on a Call to `stpncpy` or `strlcat`, or `construct 0` on a Quantifier Bounded by `strlen`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Upstream typing defect fixed by queued image patches 54 and 44; the open gap is moot.
- **Seen on:** the upstream audit of 2026-11-18 (no pipeline run), by hand on 34.0~beta and on `master` `b80fcac74f`. An upstream E-ACSL defect, not a pipeline defect.
- **Cause:** two paths in E-ACSL's typing. A logic function without a definition (such as `strlen`) in a quantifier's bound raises "not yet supported" while a guard is typed, so the goal is never typed and the translation looks up an untyped constant (`assert \forall integer i; strlen(s) <= i < 3 ==> i >= 0;`). The libc contracts of `stpncpy` and `strlcat` reach a second path, an RTE guard under `\at` (`char d[4]; stpncpy(d, "ab", 4);` is enough). Internal error, exit code 4, no code generated. By reading it is not package 16 (#2806); a build of !37 would confirm. Candidate L4 of [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-hunt-2026-11-18-candidates.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-hunt-2026-11-18-candidates.md); not filed.
- **Fix:** none upstream yet. In a run, a module that calls `stpncpy` or `strlcat` needs an overlay contract for them in `workspace/stubs/` without the clauses on `strlen`, or the call excluded from the E-ACSL build. This was not run through the pipeline, so whether `build-check` already classifies the message is not known. **Moot after image patches 54 and 44 (was an Pipeline gap (closed, see the archive note)):** check that `build-check` triage names this failure.

### Symptom: `fatal error: __fc_string_axiomatic.h: No such file or directory` in Stage 1 of an E-ACSL Build With the Meta File
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by fd13c04.
- **Cause:** the generated meta file includes a Frama-C libc header, which is not on the include path of an `e-acsl-gcc` build (`-no-frama-c-stdlib`).
- **Fix (fixed, commit `fd13c04`, [`../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py)):** `eacsl_meta_file_text` replaces that line with a comment, so the line numbers of the properties are kept.

### Symptom: `[meta] User Error: Error during the typing of a HILARE!` With `What: the HILARE named '<property>'` During a Fuzz Build
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by fd13c04, 8609e21, 959e7a2.
- **Cause:** a property names a global or a term unknown in this module's sources.
- **Fix (fixed, commit `fd13c04`, [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py)):** the named property is dropped and stage 1 retried, three times at most; after that the build is redone without any property, with `security_props.status = "dropped"` and the log kept as `<out>.security_meta.log`.
