- **Fix (fixed for the fuzz reports and the ledger, commit `8609e21`, [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py), [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py)):** the build, campaign, replay and minimize reports carry the build's `security_props` record at their top with `security_props_warnings` (`dropped:`, `error:`, `skipped:`, `rejected:`, `left out:`, `not translated:`, `unplanned instances:`), and each warning is printed on stderr as `security props [<mod>]: ...`, which the `build-<mod>` and `fuzz-<mod>` Make rules show. `ledger.py ingest-fuzz` repeats them under `security_props_warnings` and writes `fuzz_meta_not_checked` evidence on the module's Layer 4-6 units when the build lost the properties (`dropped`, `error`, a toolchain `skipped`).
- **Fix (fixed for `REPORT.md`, commit `959e7a2`, [`../../scripts/generate_report.py`](../../scripts/generate_report.py), `load_fuzz_security_props`, `fuzz_meta_inactive_lines`, `fuzz_meta_note_lines`):** per fuzzed module the report reads `security_props`, `security_props_warnings` and `security_props_harness_induced` from `workspace/reports/fuzz/<mod>.json`, and from `workspace/build/<mod>.build.json` when the module has no campaign report (the replay reports of the fuzz directory are not read). A `dropped` or `error` build is a `> [!WARNING]` block in section 1: `MetAcsl run-time checks: NOT ACTIVE for module <mod> (<reason>). No Layer 3-6 property was checked by fuzzing; see <log>.`, where `<log>` is the record's `log`, or the report that was read when the record has none (an `error`, or `MetAcsl rejected every property`). Any other build gets one line per warning under `### MetAcsl run-time checks in the fuzz builds` (`MetAcsl run-time checks, <mod>: <warning>`), and each harness-induced failure the line `<property> failed in <function>, called by harness function <caller>: not a finding against the target.` Without those fields in any report (engine off) `REPORT.md` keeps its bytes (`test_without_the_engine_the_report_is_todays_byte_for_byte` and the tests after it in `tests/test_metacsl_followups.py`). Unit-tested on synthetic reports only: no report of a real `dropped` build was rendered.

### Symptom: A `requires` Property Fails on Every Input That Makes the Harness Call the Protected Function
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by fd13c04 and 8609e21.
- **Cause:** the harness calls the function without the state its callers establish. Not a target defect.
- **Fix (fixed for the case the build can see, commit `fd13c04`):** a `\precond` property whose target a harness source names is left out of the build and listed under `security_props.left_out`. Any other precondition failure carries `caller` and `harness_induced`.
- **Fix (fixed, commit `8609e21`, [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py), `_fuzz_meta_route` next to `cmd_ingest_fuzz`):** a precondition failure links the unit `file#<caller>::<class>`, where WP and Eva place the obligation, and links nothing without a caller. A failure with `harness_induced: true` makes no candidate and touches no unit: `ingest-fuzz` lists it under `meta_harness_induced`, and the campaign report under `security_props_harness_induced` with a stderr line. A violation in a function the report lists under `security_props.unplanned_instances` is a candidate that links no unit, and a failed guard (`meta_guard`) is a class `rte` candidate at the function. In the image, on `tests/fixtures/metacsl_eacsl_wiring/harness_indirect.c` (the harness calls `admin` through a macro, so the build cannot leave the property out): fingerprint `src/prog.c#admin::meta_l4_auth_required_login::b67973b196`, `caller: LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput`, no candidate, no unit changed.

### Symptom: Replaying a Property Violation With `--binary` From Another Directory Gives `unknown#backdoor::meta_l4_auth_state_login::dd581c3f76` Instead of `src/prog.c#backdoor::...`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 959e7a2.
- **Cause:** the binary names its meta file by the path the build was given, relative to the build's working directory (`workspace/harnesses/fuzz_prog.security_meta.c`). `failure_identity.py` looks for the sidecar next to that path from the replay's working directory, does not find it, and cannot map the function to its file. Seen in the image with `run_eacsl_fuzz.py replay --binary /tmp/run/workspace/harnesses/fuzz_prog` run from `/tmp/elsewhere` (package C2, `tests/test_metacsl_eacsl_wiring.py`). The same replay from the run directory, and a replay that builds in the other directory (`--sources`, `--out-bin`), give the campaign's fingerprint.
- **Fix (fixed, commit `959e7a2`, [`../../scripts/failure_identity.py`](../../scripts/failure_identity.py), [`../../scripts/crash_replay.py`](../../scripts/crash_replay.py)):** `_meta_sidecar` takes the binary that printed the failure and looks for the sidecar next to it first (the name of the printed meta file with `.json`, in the binary's directory: `fuzz_prog.security_meta.json` beside `fuzz_prog.e-acsl`), then at the printed path as before. `parse_runtime_failure` and `parse_eacsl_failure` take it as the keyword `binary`, and `crash_replay.replay_artifact` passes the binary it runs, which covers `replay`, the minimizers and the campaign's own replays; a guard failure (`meta_guard`) is mapped the same way. In the image, `replay --binary /tmp/run/workspace/harnesses/fuzz_prog.e-acsl` from `/tmp/elsewhere` now gives the campaign's `src/prog.c#backdoor::meta_l4_auth_state_login::dd581c3f76` (`test_in_the_image_a_replay_from_another_directory_reproduces` in `tests/test_metacsl_eacsl_wiring.py`; unit tests `test_a_binary_replayed_from_another_directory_keeps_the_campaigns_fingerprint`, `test_a_guard_failure_replayed_from_another_directory_is_still_mapped_to_its_property`, `test_the_sidecar_next_to_the_binary_is_the_one_of_the_binary` in `tests/test_metacsl_followups.py`). A caller that parses a log without naming its binary (the campaign's fallback on libFuzzer's log, the guardrail) still looks at the printed path only; both run in the build's directory. Package H (commit `2c8c723`) removed the old workaround from the `framac-fuzz` skill.

### Symptom: Builds With the Properties Are Never Served From the Build Cache
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Cache key covers the meta file; bypass removed.
- **Cause:** `fuzz_build.py` bypasses the E-ACSL build cache when properties are loaded, because the cache key does not cover the meta file yet.
- **Fix (fixed, commit `8609e21`, [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py)):** the bypass is removed. `_eacsl_cache_material` takes the loaded plan: the hash of the meta file's text, the MetAcsl stage flags (`meta_stage_flags`), and what the sidecar is written from (each property's class and instances, the function-to-file map, the harness's functions, the left-out properties, the rejection limit). The path of `security_props.json` is not in the key, so Make's build, the campaign and a replay share one entry. The meta file and the sidecar are cached outputs, restored on a hit. The key material of a build without loaded properties is unchanged (its key moved once, as after any change under `scripts/`, which the key hashes). In the image, the build report and the output of an engine-off build by `bf3ce08` and by this commit differ in the cache key only. A `dropped` build is never published, so the next build tries the properties again. In the image: first build miss (`290469a0...`), the same build after deleting the meta file and the sidecar a hit with both restored identical, one changed property text a miss (`9e0aa9db...`), the engine off another key (`358dd030...`) shared by `--security-props none` and by a run without the engine.

### Symptom: `tools/check_generated_text.py` Reports a Literal `\n` in a `.sh` File That Contains `\name(`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Checker fix 959e7a2 merged.
- **Cause:** the hook's pattern for a literal backslash-n in shell files also matches the start of the ACSL keyword `\name`.
- **Fix (fixed, commit `959e7a2`, [`../../tools/check_generated_text.py`](../../tools/check_generated_text.py)):** `_SH_ACSL_N_KEYWORD_RE` lists what may follow the backslash-n of an ACSL keyword (`othing`, `ull`, `umof` as whole words, `ame` before an opening parenthesis) and is matched on the line itself. The old test cut six characters after the match, so the word boundary after `othing` never applied: `\nothings` passed, and is reported now. Any other backslash-n on the line is still reported, with `\name(` on the same line too. Tests: `test_the_metacsl_name_keyword_in_a_shell_file_is_no_literal_newline`, `test_the_acsl_keywords_that_start_with_backslash_n_are_fine`, `test_a_literal_backslash_n_in_a_shell_string_is_still_flagged` (`tests/test_metacsl_followups.py`).

### Symptom: `conflicting types for '__sync_synchronize'` or `undefined reference to '__sync_bool_compare_and_swap_uint64_t'` in E-ACSL Stage 2
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed on fix/unit-split-address-flow, merged as f956747.
- **Seen on:** nginx 1.31.9 C7.2 validation on branch `fix/unit-split-address-flow` (2026-11-20), clusters `nginx__ngx_string_2`, `nginx__ngx_files`, and `nginx__ngx_bpf` (`ngx_spinlock` / `ngx_memory_barrier`).
  - Stage 2 Clang compile error: `error: conflicting types for '__sync_synchronize'` on `extern int ( /* missing proto */ __sync_synchronize)(void);` (`note: '__sync_synchronize' is a builtin with type 'void (void)'`).
  - Stage 2 `ld` link error: `undefined reference to '__sync_bool_compare_and_swap_uint64_t'` at `tmp = __sync_bool_compare_and_swap_uint64_t(lock, ...);`.
- **Cause:** two Frama-C 34 builtin gaps when translating GCC `__sync_*` atomic builtins into E-ACSL C output.
  - Neither Frama-C's `__fc_libc` nor host glibc declares `void __sync_synchronize(void);`, so Frama-C synthesizes an implicit `int` prototype that Clang rejects as conflicting with the `void (void)` builtin.
  - Frama-C's `share/machdeps/gcc_builtins.json` lowers GCC's 16 polymorphic `__sync_*` builtins to synthetic typed names (`__sync_<op>_<u?int(8|16|32|64)_t>`) during typing and prints those synthetic names verbatim into `instrumented.c`, where Clang compiles them as external calls and `ld` fails to resolve them.
- **Fix (fixed on `fix/unit-split-address-flow`, commits `e10d660` and `1fbe43a`, merged to `main` in `f956747`):**
  - Declared `/*@ assigns \nothing; */ void __sync_synchronize(void);` inside `#if defined(__FRAMAC__)` in [`../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h`](../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h) (verified across Eva, WP, and E-ACSL in `framac-analysis:latest`).
  - Added `rewrite_sync_builtins` in [`../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py) and wired it between Stage 1 and Stage 2 in [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py) (`result["sync_builtin_rewrites"]`), restoring all 16 typed `__sync_*_<u?int(8|16|32|64)_t>(` calls back to their polymorphic `__sync_*(` spellings while skipping comments and string literals.
  - Tests: `tests/test_eacsl_toolchain.py::test_rewrite_sync_builtins_restores_polymorphic_names_and_skips_comments_and_strings`, `tests/test_fc_stub_contracts.py::test_fc_stub_prototypes_have_contract_with_assigns`; verified on real nginx 1.31.9 (`nginx__ngx_string_2`: 2 rewrites, `nginx__ngx_files`: 13 rewrites, both `engine_used == "eacsl"`, `rc=0`).

### Symptom: Address-Flow Cluster Builds Missed Widened Stubs, Over-Scoped `-e-acsl-functions` to Dead Functions, or Re-Ran E-ACSL on Non-Link Sanitizer Repair Retries
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed on fix/unit-split-address-flow, merged as f956747.
- **Seen on:** nginx 1.31.9 C7.2 validation on branch `fix/unit-split-address-flow` (2026-11-20), clusters `nginx__ngx_files` and `nginx__ngx_bpf`.
  - `_preseed_flow_stubs` read only DAG `sub["link_stubs"]`, missing symbols referenced by harness-widened `flow_build_sources` (80 symbols on `nginx__ngx_files`, 28 on `nginx__ngx_bpf`) and globals referenced through `static inline` header callees (`ngx_crc32_update` -> `ngx_crc32_table256`), forcing `link_repair` rounds and missing the build cache on Attempt 1.
  - `_discover_slice_functions` passed every function defined in `build_sources` (hundreds of unreachable functions) to `-rte-select` / `-e-acsl-functions`, inflating `instrumented.c` to 99.7 MB (`nginx__ngx_files`) and 253 MB (`nginx__ngx_bpf`) and instrumenting dead code (`ngx_open_dir`, `ngx_http_autoindex_handler`, `ngx_http_regex_exec`) that failed Stage 2 on `_Alignof(DIR)` / `_Alignof(ngx_regex_t)`.
  - Passing `header` + `is_static` helpers (`ngx_write_fd`, `ngx_strlchr`) to `-e-acsl-functions` exhausted Stage 1's single missing-function retry.
  - In `engine="auto"`, when E-ACSL failed Stage 2 with a Clang compile error (no `undefined reference`) and the sanitizer fallback hit a link error, `repair_link_rounds` re-invoked E-ACSL on every repair round.
- **Cause:** pipeline bugs in [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py) and [`../../scripts/unit_split.py`](../../scripts/unit_split.py).
- **Fix (fixed on `fix/unit-split-address-flow`, commits `e10d660` and `1fbe43a`, merged to `main` in `f956747`):**
  - Extracted `unit_split.link_stubs_for_files` (traversing `header` callees of compiled `.c` files) and recomputed widened stubs in `_preseed_flow_stubs` and `_flow_sub_stub_info` whenever `flow_files` extends `sub["build_sources"]`.
  - Added `_flow_cluster_functions` to scope `-rte-select` / `-e-acsl-functions` on `address_flow` clusters to reachable cluster + harness-called functions, skipping `header` + `is_static` helpers while keeping explicit `_harness_header_entries`, and widened Stage 1 missing-function drop retries to up to 8 iterations.
  - Locked `build_module_binary` retries to `sanitizers` after a non-link E-ACSL Stage 2 compile error (`eacsl_compile_failed` with no undefined references), `eacsl_build_timeout`, or stub-only repair, preserving `eacsl_compile_attempt` and `engine_fallback_reason`.
  - Tests: `tests/test_link_repair_flow_stubs.py::test_preseed_recomputes_stubs_for_widened_flow_build_sources`, `::test_build_module_binary_does_not_rerun_eacsl_after_non_link_compile_error`, `::test_header_inline_global_refs_are_preseeded_and_static_header_helpers_excluded_from_flow_functions`; verified on nginx 1.31.9 (`attempts=1`, `link_repair=null`, `rte_select_dropped=[]` on all three clusters; `instrumented.c` reduced 99.7 MB -> 18.871 MB on `nginx__ngx_files` and 253 MB -> 77.418 MB on `nginx__ngx_bpf`).

### Symptom: `use of undeclared identifier 'sudoers_subsystem_names'` in E-ACSL Stage 2
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 750fb00, merged as 126b489.
- **Seen on:** sudo run-1 (2026-11-21), Phase 0 `build-check`.
  - Modules `testsudoers` and `tsdump` (`first_error` in `build_check.json`). Also `visudo` and `tf_fuzz_sudo_conf`, where it is the next error once the `_Alignof` errors are rewritten (replayed from run-1's instrumented files).
  - `fuzz_visudo.instrumented.c:530648:43: error: use of undeclared identifier 'sudoers_subsystem_names'` on `unsigned int sudoers_subsystem_ids[sizeof(sudoers_subsystem_names) / sizeof(sudoers_subsystem_names[0]) - (unsigned long)1];`.
- **Cause:** a Frama-C kernel printer bug, not an E-ACSL one: plain `frama-c -print` does the same, on two files or one.
  - `sudoers_debug.h` declares `extern unsigned int sudoers_subsystem_ids[];`, and `sudoers_debug.c` defines `unsigned int sudoers_subsystem_ids[nitems(sudoers_subsystem_names) - 1];` after `static const char *const sudoers_subsystem_names[]`.
  - The kernel completes the array's type and prints the sized definition at the header's declaration, before the table, as `[sizeof(sudoers_subsystem_names) / sizeof(sudoers_subsystem_names[0]) - (unsigned long)1]`.
  - `-constfold` would fold the size to `3UL`; the pipeline passes `--no-constfold` on purpose.
  - No upstream issue exists in [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-e-acsl-issues.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-e-acsl-issues.md); a kernel-printer draft is warranted and not yet written.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/sudo-harness-gaps` (`750fb00`), merged live into `main` as `126b489` during sudo run-1, at the user's request.
  - `eacsl_toolchain.rewrite_forward_array_sizes` runs between E-ACSL stages 1 and 2 (`fuzz_build.py`). It turns each earlier declaration into `extern T x[];` and re-emits the sized declaration right after the `;` that ends the table, on the same line. Recorded as `compile.forward_array_sizes`.
  - Tests: `tests/test_sudo_harness_gaps.py`, including a Docker E-ACSL build where an index past the end is still caught.
  - Replay on copies of run-1's instrumented `testsudoers`, `tsdump`, `visudo` and `tf_fuzz_sudo_conf`: one rewrite each, and `clang -fsyntax-only` goes from 18 errors to 0 (with the `_Alignof` rewrite).
  - Verified in run-1's build-check #4: `testsudoers` records one `forward_array_sizes` rewrite and gets past the error; its E-ACSL build then stops on `fallthrough annotation does not directly precede switch label` (next entry).

### Symptom: `fallthrough annotation does not directly precede switch label` in E-ACSL Stage 2 (sudo `libparsesudoers_a`, `testsudoers`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 931651a, merged as 7979a9f.
- **Seen on:** sudo run-1, Phase 0 build-check #4, 2026-11-21. `workspace/harnesses/fuzz_libparsesudoers_a.compile.log`: `fuzz_libparsesudoers_a.instrumented.c:2911025:6: error: fallthrough annotation does not directly precede switch label`, then `e-acsl-gcc: fatal error: fail to compile/link instrumented code`. `testsudoers` stops the same way (`fuzz_testsudoers.instrumented.c:26896`).
- **Cause:** a Frama-C kernel printer behaviour, not an E-ACSL one.
  - `plugins/sudoers/fmtsudoers.c:113-115` has `FALLTHROUGH; default: print_word:`, and sudo's `config.h` makes `FALLTHROUGH` `__attribute__((__fallthrough__))` once the pipeline's C23 shim is in place.
  - The kernel prints the labels in the other order, `__attribute__((__fallthrough__)); print_word: default:`, so the attribute no longer directly precedes a switch label and clang rejects it.
  - Plain `frama-c -print` (34.0~beta) does the same on a 15-line program (a `switch` with `case 1: ... goto print_word; ... FALLTHROUGH; default: print_word: ...`). An upstream draft is warranted; none is written yet.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/sudo-link-repair-pick` (`931651a`), merged live into `main` as `7979a9f` during sudo run-1, at the user's request.
  - `eacsl_toolchain.rewrite_misplaced_fallthrough` removes each fallthrough attribute that is not directly followed by a `case`/`default:` label, and keeps its `;`. The attribute only silences `-Wimplicit-fallthrough`, so control flow, labels, line numbers and checks are unchanged.
  - `fuzz_build.py` applies it last between the stages and records `compile.misplaced_fallthrough {rewrites, lines}`.
  - On a copy of run-1's instrumented file (2.97 million lines): 1 rewrite, at line 2911025, in 0.26 s; 15 other fallthrough attributes are kept; `clang -fsyntax-only` goes from 1 error to 0.
  - Tests: `tests/test_sudo_link_pick.py`, including a Docker E-ACSL build.
  - Verified in run-1's build-check #5 (2026-11-22): `libparsesudoers_a` and `testsudoers` build with E-ACSL.

### Symptom: `incompatible function pointer types initializing 'int (*)(struct sudoers_context *, const struct sudo_nss *, struct passwd *)' with an expression of type 'int (*)(struct sudoers_context *, const struct sudo_nss *, struct passwd *)'` in E-ACSL Stage 2 (sudo `tf_fuzz_sudoers`, `tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 0ecf165, merged as d158912.
- **Seen on:** sudo run-1, Phase 0 build-check #5, 2026-11-22. `workspace/harnesses/fuzz_tf_fuzz_sudoers.compile.log`: `fuzz_tf_fuzz_sudoers.instrumented.c:256204:13` (`.query = & sudo_file_query,` in a `struct sudo_nss` initializer) and `:3770429:23`. `tf_fuzz_sudoers` falls back to the sanitizers. `tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif` fails the same way, and its sanitizer link fails too (see the link-repair entry in [`./harnesses-and-seeds.md`](./harnesses-and-seeds.md)).
- **Cause:** a Frama-C printer/merge gap, not an E-ACSL one.
  - In the instrumented file, `struct sudo_nss` (line 1373) has the member `int (*query)(struct sudoers_context *ctx, struct sudo_nss const *nss, struct passwd *pw);`. That parameter list is the first mention of `struct passwd`, which is only defined at line 2359. The compile log has the matching `declaration of 'struct passwd' will not be visible outside of this function` warnings.
  - A tag first seen inside a parameter list has prototype scope, so it is a different type from the file-scope `struct passwd`, and clang rejects the initializer although both types print the same.
  - The sources declare it forward (`sudo_nss.h:23`, `sudo_plugin.h:166`: `struct passwd;`). Another file includes `<pwd.h>` first. When Frama-C merges several files, it drops the forward declaration and prints the definition after `struct sudo_nss`.
  - Reproducer (Frama-C 34.0~beta, `framac-analysis:latest`): `common.h` declares `struct passwd;`, a struct `S` with a member `int (*q)(struct passwd *)`, and `f`; `a.c` initializes `struct S s = { .q = &f };`; `b.c` defines `struct passwd { int uid; };` before including `common.h`. `frama-c a.c b.c -print` prints `struct S` before `struct passwd {` with no forward declaration, and clang gives the same error. `b.c a.c` and `a.c` alone are clean. An upstream candidate; no draft written yet.
- **Fix:** fixed in `scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py` and `scripts/fuzz_build.py` (commit `0ecf165`, branch `sup/sudo-link-redef-fwdtags`, merged live to `main` as `d158912`).
  - Between the E-ACSL stages, `rewrite_forward_tag_declarations` adds `struct T;` / `union T;` at the start of line 1 for each tag defined at file scope whose first mention is in a parameter list or a function body. Line numbers do not change, unless the file starts with a `#` directive (then `lines_added` is 1).
  - Recorded as `compile.forward_tag_declarations {count, tags, lines_added}` and noted in the compile log. It runs in about 1.2 s on the 3.86M-line file.
  - Replay with clang `-fsyntax-only` and the stage-2 flags: `fuzz_tf_fuzz_sudoers` goes from 2 errors to 0, `fuzz_tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif` from 1 to 0; `tags` is `["struct passwd"]` on both.
  - Tests: `tests/test_sudo_link_redef_fwdtags.py` (Part B, with a Docker integration test, `test_the_printed_tag_order_builds_under_eacsl`, run with `FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1`).
  - Verified in run-1's build-check #6 (2026-11-22, `ok: true`): both compile logs say `Declared 1 struct/union tag(s) at the start of the file (struct passwd)`, and `tf_fuzz_sudoers` and `tf_fuzz_sudoers_ldif` build with E-ACSL.

### Symptom: `sandbox_exec.py` Fails With `ValueError` / `EPERM` When Setting `RLIMIT_CORE` to `(1, 1)` Inside a Container Whose Hard Core Limit Is `0`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 73f3d48 (_clamped_core_limit).
- **Seen on:** P4 infrastructure hardening (2026-11-22; see the host `core_pattern` pipe-dump background in [`./resources-and-infra.md`](./resources-and-infra.md)).
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in [`../../scripts/sandbox_exec.py`](../../scripts/sandbox_exec.py). `_make_preexec()` unconditionally called `resource.setrlimit(RLIMIT_CORE, (CORE_LIMIT_BYTES, CORE_LIMIT_BYTES))` with `CORE_LIMIT_BYTES = 1`. On container engines or hosts where the process inherits a hard `RLIMIT_CORE` of `0` (for example `--ulimit core=0:0` or a host policy capping core dumps at 0), raising the hard limit from `0` to `1` fails before the fuzz or replay child executes.
- **Fix:** **Fixed (`73f3d48`, P10c, [`../../scripts/sandbox_exec.py`](../../scripts/sandbox_exec.py)):** `_clamped_core_limit()` inspects `resource.getrlimit(RLIMIT_CORE)` and clamps `CORE_LIMIT_BYTES` against the current hard limit (`0 -> (0, 0)`, `RLIM_INFINITY -> (1, 1)`, otherwise `min(1, hard)`). Regression tests: `tests/test_p4_infra_prereqs.py`.

### Symptom: A Broken Harness Produces a Burst of Fuzz Candidates That Spawns Dozens of Redundant Crash Verifiers Before Repair Opens
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Verifier circuit breaker merged.
- **Seen on:** nanopb run-1 (`libprotobuf_nanopb_a`, 25 artifacts / 5+ sequential verifiers all rejected `harness_induced`), libmodbus run-1, and mpack run-3/run-4.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap. [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) dispatched every unverified candidate on a module in parallel without first probing whether the module's harness was systematically broken, and repairing the harness left stale unverified candidates from the old `harness_digest` active until manually closed.
- **Fix:** **Fixed behind `FRAMAC_VERIFIER_BREAKER` (default `on`, set `=off` to roll back; commits `f836ee1`, `323839f`, and `c4aa264` across [`../../scripts/crash_triage.py`](../../scripts/crash_triage.py), [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py), and [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py); see also [`./ledger-and-verifier.md`](./ledger-and-verifier.md)):**
  - `crash_triage.cluster_candidates` groups candidates by `(module, dispatch_case_or_entry, failure_family)`. Modules with `<= 3` candidates pass straight through; modules with `> 3` candidates run a distinct-family probe batch first (`select_probe_batch`, `max_probes = 2`).
  - If `evaluate_circuit_breaker` trips (`>= 2` `harness_induced` verdicts and `>= 2/3` ratio), remaining module verifiers pause (`paused_by_breaker`), in-flight verifiers finish, and `repair:<mod>` opens with fast fuzz-only repair actions when no Eva report exists yet.
  - After harness repair, `wp_scheduler.py refresh` calls `ledger.py supersede-candidates` to mark stale unverified candidates from the old `harness_digest` as `superseded_by_repair` without altering `confirmed` candidates or `findings.json`.
  - Tests: `tests/test_crash_triage.py`, `tests/test_p5_circuit_breaker.py`, and `tests/test_p3_p4_p5_ledger_report.py`.

### Symptom: A Dispatch Campaign Loses a Worker at Each Crash: `-jobs=N -workers=N` Workers Exit and Are Not Replaced, and the Crashed Case Is Never Skipped
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Prompt round-stop on worker crash merged.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR v2.0-beta-1 run-2, 2026-11-23, module `libqcbor_a` (244-case dispatch harness), `run_eacsl_fuzz.py campaign --workers auto --fuzz-budget 21600`: 7 sanitizer workers, `-jobs=7 -workers=7 -max_total_time=3086`. Worker 6 aborted after about 14 minutes and worker 5 after about 31 minutes, both on `qcbor_number_decode.c:1964:65: runtime error: negation of -9223372036854775808 cannot be represented in type 'int64_t'` in `QCBORDecode_ProcessBigNumber` (case 72, known QCBOR bug 09). Each logs `==NNNN== ERROR: libFuzzer: deadly signal` and exits; the other five go on and are equally able to hit case 72.
- **Confirmed by the campaign report** (`workspace/reports/fuzz/libqcbor_a.json`, 09:56): a third worker died the same way (`crash-0cf77316...`, 3 artifacts, one fingerprint), and `keep_going` reads `rounds: 1`, `crashed_cases: [72]`, `skipped_cases: []`, `stopped: time_budget_spent`, `cpu_seconds_spent: 21844.4`, which is 7 workers times the round's 3120.6 s wall time although 3 of the 7 had exited.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap in [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py). In `-jobs/-workers` mode with `jobs == workers`, libFuzzer starts no new job when one exits, so a crashed worker's remaining `-max_total_time` is lost. The dispatch keep-going loop (`run_campaign_rounds`) skips crashed cases only from the next round on, and charged the round its `fuzz_s` wall time until the last worker ended. A round in which only some workers crash therefore used up the whole wall budget: the next round got no time (`time_budget_spent`), the lost worker time was never spent, the crashed case stayed live in the surviving workers for the whole round, and `ExecutionPlateauDetector` waited forever on the exited worker's frozen log. Run 1 of the same target had no crash, so it did not show.
- **Fix:** **fixed (2026-11-23, [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py)):**
  - **Prompt round-stop on worker crash (`FRAMAC_FUZZ_CRASH_STOP`, default `on`, set `=off` to roll back):** `_run_sandboxed_with_plateau_monitor` wraps libFuzzer's parent stderr in `_JobExitStream` (using `read1()` so short `================== Job N exited with exit code RC ============` lines are observed immediately before EOF). When a worker exits with `rc != 0` and `_worker_crash_triggers_stop` confirms it wrote a fatal artifact (`crash-`, `leak-`, `timeout-`, `oom-`; ignoring `slow-unit-` and, for dispatch harnesses, artifacts that select no valid dispatch case such as a 0-byte `oom-`), `_stop_process_group_gracefully` sends `SIGTERM` to the process group and waits for surviving workers to flush final stats and exit (checking non-zombie PIDs in `/proc` before escalating to `SIGKILL`). `run_campaign_rounds` then skips the crashed case (via `FRAMAC_FUZZ_SKIP_CASES` runtime skip or rebuild) and immediately starts the next round with all workers alive and the remaining budget; the round records `crash_stopped: true`. Runs even when `FRAMAC_FUZZ_PLATEAU_STOP=off` (with `detector=None`).
  - **Effective worker-time budget accounting (`effective_worker_fuzz_s`):** when a `-jobs=N -workers=N` round has workers that exited abnormally (`rc != 0`) before the round ended (for example under `FRAMAC_FUZZ_CRASH_STOP=off`, or if a worker died early on a non-dispatch error), `_run_fuzz_campaign_once` records `effective_fuzz_s` as the mean active duration across the `N` worker slots. `run_campaign_rounds` charges `effective_fuzz_s` against the campaign budget (recording it on the round when lower than wall `fuzz_s`) while keeping wall `fuzz_s` in `telemetry.fuzz_s`, so lost worker-seconds return to the next round instead of stopping `time_budget_spent`.
  - **Dead-worker plateau handling:** `ExecutionPlateauDetector.mark_worker_exited` removes exited workers from the active plateau set while keeping them in the observed-worker count, so a dead worker that did not trigger crash-stop cannot permanently disable plateau stopping for the surviving workers.
  - **Tests:** [`../../tests/test_fuzz_worker_crash_stop.py`](../../tests/test_fuzz_worker_crash_stop.py) (9 unit tests including a buffered-pipe `read1` regression guard, plus 1 Docker integration test in `framac-analysis:latest` verifying real Clang LibFuzzer `-jobs=4 -workers=4` under both `FRAMAC_FUZZ_CRASH_STOP=on` and `FRAMAC_FUZZ_CRASH_STOP=off`). Nothing was changed in QCBOR run-2 itself.

### Symptom: The Judge Reports `frontier_total: 0` on a Large Grown Corpus, and Nothing Says Why
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by ba385e7.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR v2.0-beta-1 run-2, 2026-11-23, module `libqcbor_a` (244-case dispatch harness, standard profile). `workspace/reports/judge/libqcbor_a.json` reads `inputs.total` 61,702, mandatory 1235, grown 60,467, `frontier_total: 0`, `frontier_judged: 0`, and 2048 grown inputs selected by the default case-stratified rule; 58,419 inputs were not judged (`not_judged_by_case` lists 186 cases, up to 2283 each, the largest being the stateful map-accessor cases). `judge_s` 110.2, `batch_s` 84.4, `confirm_s` 4.2. `driver-judge.log` has no line about the frontier merge.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap in `discover_judge_frontier` ([`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py), line 4908 at `9077b9b`). It ran `libFuzzer -merge=1` with the `.san` binary directly over `corpus_dir` under a fixed wall limit, `JUDGE_MERGE_TIMEOUT_S = 30`, and returned `None` on a nonzero exit, a timeout, a sandbox error or an empty merge. The caller then left `frontier_total` at 0 and fell back to the cap, and neither the report nor the log kept the merge's exit code, its timed-out flag or its stderr tail. Measured in `framac-analysis:latest` on a 3,000-file synthetic corpus mounted over 9p (`drvfs` from Windows NTFS), direct `-merge=1` on 9p took 6.94 s (750 exec/s on a trivial harness, scaling to ~140 s for 60,467 files), whereas 8-thread parallel staging into container-local `/tmp` took 1.02 s followed by 0.14 s for `-merge=1` on `/tmp` (6x faster overall and 50x faster inside `-merge=1`). The fixed 30 s limit did not scale with the corpus size, so W1-D frontier selection silently disappeared on large corpora.
- **Fix:** **fixed (commit `ba385e7`, [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py), [`../../schemas/judge_report.schema.json`](../../schemas/judge_report.schema.json), [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py)):**
  - **Structured frontier diagnostics (`frontier_status` + `frontier_merge` + `diagnostic_hints` + stderr log):** `discover_judge_frontier_detailed` returns `FrontierMergeResult` (while `discover_judge_frontier` stays a backward-compatible `set[str] | None` wrapper). `run_judge` always records `frontier_status` (`ok`, `provided`, `disabled`, `below_cap`, `no_san_binary`, `skipped`, `timed_out`, `no_files`, `sandbox_unavailable`, or `rc=<N>`), records `frontier_merge` (`returncode`, `timed_out`, `timeout_s`, `elapsed_s`, `stage_s`, `staged_locally`, `corpus_files`, `merged_files`, `matched_files`, `stderr_tail` capped at 2,000 bytes, and optional `reason`) whenever frontier discovery runs or `.san` is missing, appends a fallback entry to `diagnostic_hints` on failure, and logs a one-line `[judge] <module>: frontier merge ...` summary to stderr. [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py) propagates `frontier_status` onto `fuzz_not_judged` evidence when cap overflow withholds E-ACSL `fuzz_clean`.
  - **Corpus-scaled merge timeout (`compute_judge_merge_timeout`):** keeps `JUDGE_MERGE_TIMEOUT_S = 30` as the floor for small corpora (`<= 9,000` files) and scales linearly at `JUDGE_MERGE_FILES_PER_S = 300` files/s (60,467 files -> 202 s), capped at `JUDGE_MERGE_MAX_TIMEOUT_S = 300` s and at a `1 / 3` share of the judge `max_time` budget (`--merge-timeout` overrides explicitly).
  - **Slow-mount detection and parallel local staging (`_is_slow_mount`, `_stage_corpus_for_merge`):** when `/proc/self/mountinfo` places `corpus_dir` on a remote/host-bridged mount (`9p`, `drvfs`, `virtiofs`, `fuse*`, `cifs`, `nfs`, `vboxsf`) and scratch is local, copies regular corpus files in parallel (`JUDGE_MERGE_STAGE_WORKERS = 8`, guarded by a 512 MiB cap and 64 MiB free-space headroom) into container-local scratch before running `-merge=1`, while precomputing `(size, sha256) -> filenames` in memory so matching `floor_*` files needs zero extra reads on the slow mount. Falls back cleanly to reading `corpus_dir` directly if staging hits the cap or fails.
  - **Tests:** [`../../tests/test_judge_frontier_selection.py`](../../tests/test_judge_frontier_selection.py) (13 unit tests + 1 Docker integration test running real Clang LibFuzzer `-merge=1` with local staging and a UBSan-crashing unit in `framac-analysis:latest`) and [`../../tests/test_ledger_judge_frontier.py`](../../tests/test_ledger_judge_frontier.py) (`test_ledger_propagates_frontier_status_when_frontier_merge_failed`). Nothing was changed in QCBOR run-2 itself.

### Symptom: E-ACSL Build Aborts With `Assertion failed` in `plugins/e-acsl/src/libraries/functions.ml:156`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Upstream defect fixed by queued image patch 50; the workaround is no longer needed.
- **Seen on:** Frama-C `34.0~beta (Selenium)` (`framac-analysis:latest`) and upstream `master` `189053d4a2` (`35.0~~dev (Bromine)`), whenever `--validate-format-strings` (`-e-acsl-validate-format-strings`) instruments a slice that passes an `enum` value (or `enum *` pointer) to `printf`, `fprintf`, `sprintf`, `snprintf`, `dprintf`, or `syslog` (2026-11-22). Exact error:
  `[kernel] Unexpected error (File "plugins/e-acsl/src/libraries/functions.ml", line 156, characters 33-39: Assertion failed).`
  For a pointer to an `enum` (such as `&e` passed to `%n`, `%p`, or `%s`), E-ACSL instead aborts with:
  `[e-acsl] Failure: unexpected argument type in printf: type enum <E> of arg <expr>`.
- **Cause:** **Upstream Frama-C E-ACSL bug** (not a pipeline defect). `default_argument_promotion` in `src/kernel/internals/typing/cabs2cil.ml:3847` leaves `TEnum ei` unpromoted, while `Functions.Libc.get_printf_argument_str` in `plugins/e-acsl/src/libraries/functions.ml` groups `TEnum _` into `assert false` (lines 155-156) and omits `TEnum _` from `get_pkind_str` (lines 131-147). Plain enumerator constants are already typed as `TInt ekind`, so only `enum` variables, parameters, `typedef` enums, enum bit-fields, and `enum *` pointers hit the crash.
- **Fix:**
  - **Upstream package ready (not filed, staged as Issue 50):** [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-format-enum-argument.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-format-enum-argument.md), patch [`../upstream/frama-c/patches/50-eacsl-format-enum-argument.patch`](../upstream/frama-c/patches/50-eacsl-format-enum-argument.patch), reproducer [`../upstream/frama-c/repro/50-eacsl-format-enum-argument.sh`](../upstream/frama-c/repro/50-eacsl-format-enum-argument.sh). Maps `TEnum { ekind }` via `get_ikind_str ekind` and recurses in `get_pkind_str` on `Cil_const.mk_tint ekind`; validated on `master` `189053d4a2` and stacks cleanly with `!48`.
  - **Workaround until an upstream release carries the fix:** disable format-string validation (`--no-validate-format-strings` / omit `--validate-format-strings`) for the affected module, or cast the `enum` argument at the call site to its underlying integer type (`(int)e` / `(unsigned)e`) if modifying a harness. Under `--engine auto`, the pipeline falls back to the sanitizer build when E-ACSL Stage 1 aborts.

### Symptom: E-ACSL Aborts in `Crypto_Init` (`memcpy: overlapping memory areas`) Without `sa_save_file.bin`, and `--api` Judge Results Depend on Input Order
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 8ef9c98 (isolated cwd, setup-prefix blinding).
- **Seen on:** CryptoLib `5b98c8b`, run `cl-p02-api-20261123` (`main` at `44ba2dd`, 2026-11-23), module `libcryptolib_so`. Replayed one per process in an empty working directory, `workspace/harnesses/fuzz_libcryptolib_so.e-acsl` aborts on 413 of 1,110 corpus inputs (every input of cases 48-50, 54, 164-169, 172, 173, 175, 176 and 179), including the known `CRYPTOLIB-01` input (`0xA6` + `0003000424` on case 166), with `memcpy: overlapping memory areas` (rc 77) and frames `validate_overlapping_spaces` (`e_acsl_string.c:126`), `__e_acsl_builtin_memcpy`, `update_sa_from_ptr`, `sa_perform_save`, `sa_populate`, `sa_init`, `Crypto_Init`, `LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput`. In a directory holding a `sa_save_file.bin` (either populated or the all-zero file from `workspace/coverage/libcryptolib_so/`), the same `CRYPTOLIB-01` input reaches CryptoLib's own bug at `crypto_tc.c:1949` (`rte/initialization` on `ingest + *byte_idx`, `*byte_idx` is 5 in `Crypto_TC_Set_Segment_Header`). In the judge run (batches of 64 in one shared scratch directory, `workspace/reports/judge/libcryptolib_so.json`: 911 passed, 231 failed, `blinded_cases: []`), only 4 of the 231 failures were this overlap (cases 48, 49, 50 and 54, judged before row 144). Earlier hand-test claims of an abort with a file present and an "about a third blinded" 69-input sample did not reproduce and were withdrawn (a truncated or short file was not tested).
- **Cause:**
  - **Target-side self-copy (C11 UB):** on the `-DSA_FILE` path `Crypto_Init` > `sa_init` > `sa_load_file` fails > `sa_populate` > `sa_perform_save(&sa[0])` (`sa_interface_inmemory.template.c:570`) > `update_sa_from_ptr` (line 189), `location = sa_ptr->spi` is 0, so `memcpy(sa[location].ek_ref, sa_ptr->ek_ref, REF_SIZE)` and `ak_ref` (lines 143-144, 250 bytes) copy memory onto itself (`src == dst`). E-ACSL's `memcpy` replacement strictly rejects `src == dst` (`validate_overlapping_spaces`), while ASan's interceptor skips the overlap check when `to == from` (see [`../bugs-found/cryptolib/README.md`](../bugs-found/cryptolib/README.md), candidate `unknown#update_sa_from_ptr::separation::357c824f07`). In CryptoLib's default CMake configuration (`SA_FILE` off), `sa_init` and `sa_config` both call `sa_populate` unconditionally.
  - **Shared working directory & missing setup-prefix blinding (pipeline bugs):** `run_judge` (and `crash_replay`, `fuzz_coverage`, `harness_smoke`) executed inputs in a shared working directory. From row 144 onward, cases 162 and 192 called `sa_perform_save()` on a zeroed SA and wrote an all-zero `sa_save_file.bin` into the shared directory, making every subsequent `Crypto_Init` load zeros instead of calling `sa_populate`. Conversely, in isolated empty directories, a deterministic setup-prefix abort in `Crypto_Init` hit all 413 inputs without `run_judge` recognizing it as deterministic setup blinding (`blinded_cases` stayed empty until K=16 per case).
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline bugs (`8ef9c98`)** across [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py), [`../../scripts/crash_replay.py`](../../scripts/crash_replay.py), [`../../scripts/fuzz_coverage.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_coverage.py), [`../../scripts/harness_smoke.py`](../../scripts/harness_smoke.py), [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py), [`../../schemas/harness_sidecar.schema.json`](../../schemas/harness_sidecar.schema.json), [`../../schemas/judge_report.schema.json`](../../schemas/judge_report.schema.json), and [`../../schemas/fuzz_report.schema.json`](../../schemas/fuzz_report.schema.json):
  - **Isolated working directories & automatic per-input fallback:** `replay_artifact`, `replay_for_coverage`, `harness_smoke._run_probe_files`, and `run_judge` run each invocation or batch in a fresh empty temporary `cwd` under `scratch` (cleaned up on exit). In `run_judge`, if any batch writes files into `batch_cwd`, the judge records `telemetry.cwd_side_effects` and a diagnostic hint, switches to per-input isolated directories (`telemetry.cwd_isolated_inputs`), and re-queues `batch[1:]` one per empty directory so judge outcomes never depend on input order.
  - **Strict E-ACSL + deterministic setup-prefix blinding:** E-ACSL keeps rejecting `memcpy(p, p, n)` self-copy UB so the defect is still recorded once as a candidate. [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py) now records `setup_calls` on sequence layouts in `harness_<mod>.json`, and `run_judge` (`_deterministic_setup_blinding`) immediately blinds a case on its first confirmed standalone abort (with 0 prior passes) when the outermost frame before `LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput` is inside its deterministic setup prefix (`setup_calls` or legacy `"global setup call:"` notes, or a zero-input single global-setup case), recording `reason: "deterministic_setup_abort"` and `setup_function` in `blinded_cases` and skipping remaining inputs of that case while keeping target-body crashes unblinded.
  - **Regression tests:** [`../../tests/test_fuzz_isolation_and_blinding.py`](../../tests/test_fuzz_isolation_and_blinding.py), [`../../tests/test_api_harness_global_setup.py`](../../tests/test_api_harness_global_setup.py).

### Symptom: 28 CryptoLib Fuzz Candidates Are `harness_libcryptolib_so.c#unknown_function::unparsed_crash::*`, 24 of Them `not_reproduced` on Replay
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 8ef9c98.
- **Seen on:** CryptoLib run `cl-p02-api-20261123`, 2026-11-23. `workspace/reports/replay/index.json` reproduced 35 of 61 fuzz candidates; of the 28 `unparsed_crash` candidates, 4 reproduced under sanitizers (`Crypto_TC_Encrypt::freeable` and `Crypto_TC_Safe_Free_Ptr::freeable`), 13 reproduced only under E-ACSL as `update_sa_from_ptr::separation` (replayed alone in an empty directory), and 11 reproduced under neither engine. The 24 non-reproducing artifacts were 1- to 7-byte inputs (such as single-byte `0x31` / `0xA6`) with `failure: null`, `frames: []`, `reproducible: false`.
- **Cause:** diagnosed from the run's generated harness and campaign/judge logs (two distinct pipeline bugs, not a worker file race on `sa_save_file.bin` as initially suspected):
  1. **24 non-reproducing cross-input crashes (`api_harness.py`):** case 51 (`Crypto_Init_With_Configs`) passed four stack-allocated configuration structs (`&c0a0..&c0a3`) and omitted `Crypto_Shutdown()` because `api_harness.py` suppressed global teardown whenever a setup function took a non-`const` record pointer. `crypto_config.c:288-289` assigned two of those pointers to global pointers (`sa_mariadb_config`, `cryptography_kmc_crypto_config`), leaving dangling stack addresses after case 51 returned. Whichever subsequent dispatch case called `Crypto_Shutdown()` triggered `AddressSanitizer: stack-use-after-scope` inside `crypto_free_config_structs` (`crypto_config.c:985`), blaming an innocent victim input that exited 0 on standalone replay (`reproducible: false`); whenever two workers crashed in the same round, `from_log` (`crash_like == 1`) also dropped the campaign log parse.
  2. **4 reproducible `unparsed_crash` candidates (`failure_identity.py`):** E-ACSL's observation-model runtime aborted with `Not a start of block (0x12b-d190) in free` (via `private_abort_fail`, caller `Crypto_TC_Encrypt` / `Crypto_TC_Safe_Free_Ptr`), a message template missing from `_LIBC_TEMPLATES` in [`../../scripts/failure_identity.py`](../../scripts/failure_identity.py).
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline bugs (`8ef9c98`):**
  - [`../../scripts/api_harness.py`](../../scripts/api_harness.py): added `_single_record_pointee`, `_freeing_global_teardowns`, and `_global_pointer_ownership` driven by `phase0_model.json`'s `global_variables`, `writes.globals`, and `free()` reachability. Single cases for global setup functions now allocate non-`const` record pointers matching freed pointer globals with `calloc(1, sizeof(T))` (guarded by `if (p != 0) { ... (void)teardown(); } else { free(p); }`), give retained non-freed pointer globals `static` storage so they never dangle, keep value/array-copied globals on the stack, and enable global teardown whenever `global_variables` type metadata is known.
  - [`../../scripts/failure_identity.py`](../../scripts/failure_identity.py): added `_LIBC_TEMPLATES` rules for `Not a start of block (...) in free|realloc` and `NULL pointer in free`, mapping them to `kind: "eacsl_libc_error"`, `alarm_kind: "freeable"`, and address-independent fingerprints attributed to the caller after `private_abort_fail`.
  - **Regression tests:** [`../../tests/test_api_harness_global_setup.py`](../../tests/test_api_harness_global_setup.py) (unit + Docker Clang ASan/LSan/UBSan and Frama-C parse) and [`../../tests/test_failure_identity.py`](../../tests/test_failure_identity.py).

### Symptom: The Generated `--api` Harness Reaches `CRYPTOLIB-01` (`crypto_tc.c:1949`) but the 60 CPU-Second Campaign Drops Case 166 After a Non-Reproducing Crash
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 8ef9c98.
- **Seen on:** CryptoLib run `cl-p02-api-20261123`, 2026-11-23. Hand replay of selector `0xA6` + `0003000424` on `fuzz_libcryptolib_so.san` reproduced `AddressSanitizer: heap-buffer-overflow` at `crypto_tc.c:1949` (`Crypto_TC_Set_Segment_Header`), yet none of the 61 fuzz candidates carried it and the recall table printed `CRYPTOLIB-01: not run in this selection` (the recall entry named module `core`). In `workspace/reports/fuzz/libcryptolib_so.json`, `floor_pass.cases[166]` stopped at 3,389 of 25,000 executions (`floor_not_reached`, 483 MB RSS), and dispatch round 5 recorded a non-reproducing 1-byte crash `crash-4df7138b...` (`0xA6`, `failure: null`, `reproducible: false`), after which `keep_going` added case 166 to `skipped_cases` for rounds 6-15.
- **Cause:** two pipeline bugs plus one benchmark gap:
  1. Case 51's dangling stack pointers (see the `unparsed_crash` entry above) caused cross-input ASan aborts inside `Crypto_Shutdown()` that blamed innocent victim cases (including case 166).
  2. `run_campaign_rounds` in [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) added every artifact's dispatch case to `skipped_cases` and blinding without checking `a.get("reproducible") is False`, permanently dropping victim cases on non-reproducing crashes.
  3. [`../../scripts/benchmark.py`](../../scripts/benchmark.py) had no way to resolve and prepend a dispatch selector when replaying a recall reproducer against a `generate: api` harness.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline bugs (`8ef9c98`):**
  - [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) (`run_campaign_rounds`): ignores `reproducible: False` crash artifacts when computing `crashed_cases`, `skipped_cases`, and blinding; records `non_reproducing_cases` per round; retries up to `max_non_reproducing_rounds = 3` rounds before stopping cleanly with `stopped: "non_reproducing_crashes"`. Combined with the `api_harness.py` global-pointer ownership fix, case 166 is neither poisoned by case 51 nor skipped on a flaky crash.
  - [`../../scripts/benchmark.py`](../../scripts/benchmark.py) (`resolve_api_replay_bytes`, `_replay_input`, `inner_replay`, `verifier_result`, `inner_gate`): resolves the matching dispatch selector from `harness_<mod>.json`'s `api_cases` (preferring configured sequences feeding `rest` to `api_entry`/`function`), encodes 1-byte `identity` or 2-byte `identity16` selectors, validates optional per-replay `api_entry`, `case_kind`, and `input_hex`/`input_sha256` overrides, and accepts `generate: api` harness specs without `entry_fn`.
  - **Regression tests:** [`../../tests/test_fuzz_isolation_and_blinding.py`](../../tests/test_fuzz_isolation_and_blinding.py) and [`../../tests/test_benchmark.py`](../../tests/test_benchmark.py).

---

## From [`./eva.md`](./eva.md): Eva Abstract Interpretation Precision & Runtime Issues

### Symptom: an allowlisted Eva option aborts Frama-C 34 (`source file '1' does not exist`, `requires a string as argument`, `option '-eva-no-descending-iteration' is unknown`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. All three wrong option kinds were fixed and the rename is enforced.
- **Seen on:** a probe of every allowlist entry of [`../../scripts/eva_options.py`](../../scripts/eva_options.py) against `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 34.0~beta), during the option review of 2026-11-14 ([`../plans/frama-c-34-option-review.md`](../plans/frama-c-34-option-review.md)). No run had used these options, so no run failed on them.
- **Cause:** three entries had the wrong kind, so `framac_args` rendered a command line Frama-C rejects.
  - `interprocedural-history` and `interprocedural-splits` are flags in Frama-C 34 but were typed `int`. They rendered as `-eva-interprocedural-splits 1`, and Frama-C took `1` as a source file (`[kernel] User Error: source file '1' does not exist`). `interprocedural-history` is also now a deprecated alias of `-eva-interprocedural-partition-path`.
  - `descending-iteration` takes `no`, `full` or `exits` but was typed `bool`. `true` rendered `-eva-descending-iteration` (`requires a string as argument`), and `false` rendered `-eva-no-descending-iteration`, which does not exist.
- **Fixed (commit `438267e`):** in `eva_options.py`:
  - `interprocedural-splits` is now a bool.
  - `interprocedural-history` is replaced by the bool `interprocedural-partition-path`, and the old name is refused with the new one (`RENAMED_OPTIONS`).
  - `descending-iteration` is now an enum of `no`, `full` and `exits`.

  The rendered options run clean in Frama-C 34. Tests are in `tests/test_eva_tuning.py`, and the skill's allowlist text is updated. `hierarchical-convergence` (a hidden flag) works. `all-rounding-modes-constants` works but is deprecated in 34, which prints a warning.

### Symptom: Eva Repair Hints Omit Callees Needing Assigns Clauses or Specifications
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in committed code with tests; the 'uncommitted' note is stale.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit. `run_eva_slice.extract_repair_hints` missed missing-spec warnings when Frama-C emitted `Neither code nor explicit assigns for function ...` or when function names wrapped across newlines.
- **Cause:** `_RE_MISSING_SPEC_RE` in `scripts/run_eva_slice.py` required the literal word `clause` and did not support multi-line continuation layouts.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, on main, `scripts/run_eva_slice.py`):** `extract_repair_hints` now processes [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py) `missing_spec_messages` and `parse_missing_spec`, recognizing all missing-spec variants and multi-line continuation lines while ignoring benign `exits`/`terminates` clauses. Tests: `tests/test_eva_slice.py`.

### Symptom: Eva Error Summary Falls Back to Unrelated Tail Lines on Two-Line Kernel Warnings
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. extract_error_summary now folds messages; committed.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit. Two-line diagnostics such as `[kernel:annot-error] foo.c:42: Warning:\n  unbound logic variable val.` were missed by `_KERNEL_ERR_RE`, causing `extract_error_summary` to fall back to the last 20 lines of raw tail noise.
- **Cause:** `_KERNEL_ERR_RE` used single-line anchors on multi-line Frama-C messages.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, on main, `scripts/run_eva_slice.py`):** `extract_error_summary` folds log messages using [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py) `fold_messages` and matches kernel diagnostics on folded single-line message structures. Tests: `tests/test_eva_slice.py`.

### Symptom: Eva Reachability Is `ok` Although Almost No Statement Was Reached
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fallback gate on statements reached is in committed code.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (no target run). `eva_tuning.parse_eva_reachability` returned `ok` for a log with `20 statements reached (out of 500)` and no `Coverage estimation = N%` line.
- **Cause:** the low-coverage gate ran only when `_COVERAGE_RE` matched that exact line; without it the gate was skipped.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, on main, `scripts/eva_tuning.py`):** without the estimate, the gate uses statements reached over statements total of the analyzed functions. That ratio is an upper bound of Eva's estimate, so it is not recorded as `coverage_estimate_pct`. Tests: `tests/test_regex_hardening.py`.

### Symptom: Loop Unroll Annotation Doubling on Multi-line ACSL Comments with Leading `@`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The regexes named in the fix are in committed code.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit. When attempting to add `/*@ loop unroll N; */` to loops that already carry multi-line ACSL annotations starting with `@` (e.g. `/*@\n  @ loop invariant ... */`), Frama-C aborted with `User Error: syntax error` due to consecutive `/*@` annotations.
- **Cause:** `_LOOP_ANNOTATION_RE` in `scripts/eva_loops.py` used `\s*` after `/*@` or `//@`, which does not match `@`. Consequently, `_existing_annotation_at` returned `None` and inserted a second annotation block before `for`/`while`. In addition, `_has_unroll_annotation` used `"loop unroll" in tokens[i].text`, missing clauses formatted across lines with `@`.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, on main, `scripts/eva_loops.py`):** `_LOOP_ANNOTATION_RE` updated to `(?:/\*@|//@)[\s@]*(?:for\b[^:]*:[\s@]*)?loop\b` and `_has_unroll_annotation` updated to `re.search(r"\bloop[\s@]+unroll\b", tokens[i].text)`. Tests: `tests/test_eva_tuning.py`.

### Symptom: One Eva Shard Runs Hours Longer Than Every Other Shard After an Unsharded `analysis_timeout`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both defects are fixed on main; the 'open / pending merge' wording is stale.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR `run-6` (2026-11-14/15, `libqcbor_a`, 182 dispatch cases, `k = 9` shards in `workspace/reports/eva-shards/libqcbor_a/plan.json`, `predicted_makespan_s: 800.1`). Eight shards (`shard0`..`shard3`, `shard5`..`shard8`) completed in `444 s`–`1,469 s`, whereas `shard4` (22 cases including multi-call sequence `case 171U`) took `6,220.4 s` (7.8x predicted makespan) on the 18-call `QCBORDecode_*` sequence in `case 171U`.
- **Cause:** two compounding pipeline defects in [`../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py`](../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py) and [`../../scripts/eva_shards.py`](../../scripts/eva_shards.py):
  1. **Fallback runs clobber the timed-out base `flamegraph.txt`:** when the 5-domain base run times out at `7,200 s`, `run_eva_slice.py` preserves `eva.log` via `_keep_timed_out_log` (`eva.timed_out.log`), but has no `_keep_timed_out_flamegraph` — both `900 s` `cvalue`-only fallback runs (`cvalue_fast`, `cvalue_imprecise_alloc`) pass `flamegraph=True` and overwrite `workspace/eva/<mod>/flamegraph.txt`. When `pipeline_make.py gen` plans shards from `workspace/reports/eva/<mod>.json` (`status: "analysis_timeout"`), `_shard_basis` reads the 900 s `cvalue_imprecise_alloc` flamegraph (`precision=0, slevel=10`) instead of the 7,200 s 5-domain flamegraph, severely distorting relative case costs for relational/octagon-heavy sequence cases.
  2. **Uniform owner split in `eva_shards.case_costs()` ignores call multiplicity:** `case_callees()` returns a `set[str]` of direct callees per case and `case_costs()` divides each function's inclusive flamegraph weight equally (`t / len(owners)`). When `case 104U` calls `QCBORDecode_Private_GetInt64ConvertInMapSZ` once on a fresh context while sequence `case 171U` calls `QCBORDecode_Private_GetInt64ConvertInMapSZ` 3 times and `QCBORDecode_Private_GetDoubleConvert` 6 times on an accumulated imprecise decode context, `case 171U` is billed only half of each function's weight (`41,606` vs `55,943` for single-call `case 88U`) and is packed alongside 21 other cases instead of isolated into its own shard.
- **Fix:** **Fixed and merged to main (commit `800e9d2`, from branch `sup/QCBOR-eva-flows-bloat-and-caps`):** [`../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py`](../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py) (`_keep_timed_out_flamegraph` / `_restore_timed_out_flamegraph`) saves a non-empty timed-out 5-domain `flamegraph.txt` to `flamegraph.timed_out.txt` before running `cvalue` fallback tiers (`flamegraph=False`) and restores it afterwards so sharding always plans from the 5-domain profile; [`../../scripts/eva_shards.py`](../../scripts/eva_shards.py) (`case_callee_counts`, `case_costs`) weights shared direct callee inclusive samples by static per-case call multiplicity (`t * mult[k] / sum(mult[o] for o in owners)`). Covered by [`../../tests/test_eva_shards.py`](../../tests/test_eva_shards.py) and `tests/test_large_eva_report.py`.
- **Update (c-ares run-1, 2026-11-16):** `800e9d2` is now in `phase1-remediation`. On run-1, `gen` planned dnsrec's shards from the 5-domain timed-out flamegraph.

### Symptom: A Merged Eva Shard Report Is `analysis_error` "shardN is not the same analysis as shard0" Although Every Shard Is `ok`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged with a regression test.
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, 2026-11-16, Phase 2, module `libcares_a__parsers`. `workspace/reports/eva/libcares_a__parsers.json` has `"status": "analysis_error"` and `"reason": "shard2 is not the same analysis as shard0"`, while all 16 `workspace/reports/eva-shards/libcares_a__parsers/shard*.json` are `"status": "ok"`. dnsrec had the same mix (only shards 0 and 3 retried) and would have failed the same way had its shard 2 not timed out first.
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py`](../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py) retries a run that aborted on `assigns:missing` (a recursive call without an ACSL assigns clause) with `-eva-warn-key assigns:missing=warning`, in the base run and in the timeout-fallback tiers, and appends `-eva-warn-key assigns:missing=warning (recursive calls use kernel-inferred assigns)` to `reduced_checks`. Only shards whose cases reach the recursion retry (parsers shards 0, 1 and 7-15), so `reduced_checks` differs across shards, and [`../../scripts/eva_shards.py`](../../scripts/eva_shards.py) `identity_problem` compared the whole list and refused the merge. About 5,000 s of finished shards were thrown away. The difference is not a difference in analysis: a shard that finished `ok` without the downgrade never raised `assigns:missing`, which aborts by default (shards 2-6 have no `assigns:missing` line in `eva.log`), so the downgrade would have changed nothing for it.
- **Fix:** fixed and merged into `phase1-remediation` (2026-11-16), from branch `sup/c-ares-eva-shard-reduced-union` (`8d83691`), verified by the supervisor (`tests/test_eva_shards.py`: 31 passed, 2 skipped).
  - `identity_problem` drops only `RECURSION_REDUCED_CHECK` before comparing; any other differing reduced check is still refused.
  - `merge()` sets `reduced_checks` to the union over the shards, so the ledger keeps the recursion caveat, and records `auto_recursion_fallback` and `auto_recursion_fallback_shards`.
  - Tests: `test_a_shard_that_retried_on_missing_recursion_assigns_merges_with_one_that_did_not` (failed before the fix), `test_shards_differing_in_another_reduced_check_are_still_refused`, `test_the_recursion_reduced_check_is_the_one_run_eva_slice_records`. The rule is in [`../guides/eva-sharding.md`](../guides/eva-sharding.md) (Identity).
  - Recovering a run whose merge failed this way (or that runs an older checkout): rerun only the merge (`eva-<mod>`) with the fixed script; the shard reports stay valid. Replaying run-1's parsers shards gives `ok` (2 Invalid, 2878 Unknown, 1879 Valid, 9530 dead).

### Symptom: A Sharded Eva Report's `input_digest` Cannot Be Recomputed From the Sources
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Status says fixed (5b6d566, 09ed776, 0231e0e), all on main with a test.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15 (post-run review). In `workspace/reports/eva/libqcbor_a.json` the nine shard reports all carry `input_digest` `6f1fe1f9...` and the merged report carries `3a48c56e...`.
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug (fixed, see Status below):** the shard merge in [`../../scripts/eva_shards.py`](../../scripts/eva_shards.py) sets the merged report's `input_digest` to the SHA-256 of the joined shard digests, while `_input_digest` in [`../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py`](../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py) hashes the dependency manifest of the sources. Any freshness check that recomputes `input_digest` from the sources would report every sharded module as stale. This blocks the fix first proposed for the stale-evidence `refresh` bug in [`./wp-and-guardrail.md`](./wp-and-guardrail.md).
- **Fix (superseded by the Status below):** at the time none; proposed: copy the shard digest into the merged report when all shards agree, and stamp a separate `harness_digest` (`phase0_gates.harness_digest`) in the Eva, merged-shard and fuzz reports for `wp_scheduler.py refresh` to compare.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`5b6d566`). `eva_shards.merge` copies the shards' common `input_digest` and keeps the joined hash only when the shards differ. `run_eva_slice.py` (also on a cache hit), the shard merge and `eva_tuning.py finish` stamp a top-level `harness_digest` = `phase0_gates.harness_digest(run_dir, mod, ignore_exempt=True)`; the merge refuses shards from different harnesses (`analysis_error`). `wp_scheduler.py refresh` compares that field instead of recomputing `input_digest` (`09ed776`). Test: `tests/test_eva_harness_digest.py`.
- **Follow-up (2026-11-15, `0231e0e`):** a merge of shards that record no `harness_digest`, and a `finish` on a base trial that records none, write `phase0_gates.derived_harness_digest`: the current digest when every input is newer than the harness source, else `"unknown"` (see [`./wp-and-guardrail.md`](./wp-and-guardrail.md), the `repair:<mod>` entry).

### Symptom: With MetAcsl Loaded, Eva Reports No Row for the Generated Checks and Other Rows Disappear (193 Rows Instead of 215), or `flows.log` Shows `[eva] Analyzing a complete application starting at eva_main`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in package B and in the code.
- **Seen on:** Step 0 of package B of [`../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md`](../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md), 2026-11-18, fixture `tests/fixtures/metacsl_eva/`, `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 34.0~beta, MetAcsl 0.12~beta). With `-meta ... -then-last` before `-rte` and the later stages on `-then`: `counts valid 7` against 29 without MetAcsl, 22 non-meta rows gone, no meta row. With every later stage on `-then-last` the rows matched but the flows step ran Eva again on the untranslated project.
- **Cause:** MetAcsl writes its annotations into a copy of the project. `-then` goes back to the original one, and a saved session keeps the original as its current project.
- **Fix (fixed in package B, commit `7504fdd`, [`../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py`](../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py)):** the MetAcsl stage is `-meta -meta-checks -meta-warn-key unknown-func=inactive -then-last -set-project-as-default`, placed before `-rte`. Checked on the fixture: 215 non-meta rows with and without the stage, none changed, flows and non-termination output identical. Not a target-side issue.

### Symptom: After Turning MetAcsl On, Most of a Module Is Dead for Eva (`2 functions analyzed (out of 9): 22% coverage`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by -meta-checks in the MetAcsl stage.
- **Seen on:** the same Step 0, fixture built with `-DHARNESS_DIRECT` (the harness calls a function whose `\precond` property it violates).
- **Cause:** MetAcsl's default translation emits `assert` and `requires`, which stop Eva's propagation at the first violated instance, one the harness induces included. 25 non-meta rows went from Valid to Dead.
- **Fix (fixed in package B, commit `7504fdd`):** the stage passes `-meta-checks`, which generates non-blocking `check`s: `9 functions analyzed (out of 9): 100%`.

### Symptom: An Eva Report Says `security_props.error: "Frama-C failed with the security properties loaded; this is the run without them"`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fallback and skipped list are implemented.
- **Seen on:** the same Step 0, a property naming a global whose defining file was not in the module's parse: `[meta] User Error: Error during the typing of a HILARE!`, `[kernel] Plug-in meta aborted: invalid user input.`, `status=analysis_error rc=1`.
- **Cause:** MetAcsl types every property against the parsed files and aborts on a name it cannot resolve.
- **Fix (fixed in package B, commit `7504fdd`):** a property's `sources` are added to the parse; a property whose source is not under the work tree is left out and listed under `security_props.skipped`; any remaining failure keeps the log as `workspace/eva/<mod>/eva.meta_failed.log` and repeats the run without the properties, so the module keeps its RTE result and its L4 to L6 units stay planned for WP. The fallback is tested with a fake runner, not re-run in the image through the final code.

---

## From [`./wp-and-guardrail.md`](./wp-and-guardrail.md): Deductive Verification (`WP`), Guardrail & Prover Swarm Issues

### Symptom: Provers End `stalled_after_k_retries` After One WP Call; the Next Call Is Refused With "Prover budget exhausted ... elapsed=...s/900s"
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both fixes are on main.
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, Phase 3, 2026-11-16.
  - `prove-print_help_info_ahost`: its first WP call at 19:57:35Z took about 30 s. The second call was refused at 20:12:55Z, before Frama-C started.
  - `prove-ares_getopt` (19:57:00 to 20:13:21) and `negotiate-ares_malloc` show the same pattern.
  - The scheduler's `auto` slots dispatched 15 agents on a 32-CPU pool (`resources.recommend()`, `duty = 4.0`), and `wp` grants are elastic.
  - `state/resource-history/wp/` has no WP completion from 20:03:00 to 20:12:51. Then five refused calls end together, each using about 0.4 CPU-s and 35 MiB.
  - The run's parent then capped the scheduler with `--slots 6`.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug.
  - [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) checked `max_wall_clock_s` (in `.prover_budget.json`, 900 s for `standard`) as `now - first_wp_at`.
  - The script runs inside a container that `resources.py exec` starts only after the host broker grants the `wp` lease. So the time later calls spent queued for that lease counted as prover time.
  - The call that finally got its lease was refused before Frama-C started, and the lease it waited for was wasted.
  - The broker recorded only a `waited` bool, never a duration.
- **Fix:** fixed and merged into `phase1-remediation` (2026-11-16), from branch `sup/c-ares-prover-budget-lease-wait` (`db0a0f4`, `698f2d3`), verified by the supervisor.
  - [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py): the broker measures `Lease.wait_s`, and `exec` passes `FRAMAC_LEASE_ID` and `FRAMAC_LEASE_WAITED_S` into the container.
  - `run_wp_function.py` accumulates `lease_wait_s` in `.prover_budget.json` and subtracts it from the elapsed time and from the WP wall timeout for the remaining time. It credits each lease once, never the first call's (which came before `first_wp_at`), and never more than the time since `first_wp_at`.
  - Without the two variables, it behaves as before.
  - [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py): the brief's budget line says what the wall budget counts.
  - Tests: [`../../tests/test_prover_budget_lease_wait.py`](../../tests/test_prover_budget_lease_wait.py) (12 tests, all failing before the fix), and `tests/test_resources.py::test_exec_dry_run_builds_the_full_engine_command`.
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** all three remaining gaps are closed across [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) and [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py):
  - `prepare_agent` now renders the "WP Verifiability & Iteration Budget" section for every `role == "prover"` brief (including `core_provable` on `standard`), omitting empty blocker/note bullets.
  - `cmd_exec` and `cmd_run` in [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py) now idempotently credit non-`wp` broker lease waits (for example `guardrail` and `replay`) and in-container `resources.py run` sub-lease waits into `.prover_budget.json` (`_credit_prover_budget_lease_wait`, keyed by `lease_wait_credited` so `run_wp_function.py` never double-counts, and never crediting waits before `first_wp_at`).
  - Before acquiring a `wp` lease in `cmd_exec` / `cmd_run`, `_prover_budget_already_exhausted` checks `.prover_budget.json` (unless `--ignore-prover-budget` is passed) and downgrades already-exhausted `run_wp_function.py` calls to ungated `light` so `run_wp_function.py` writes its normal `budget_exhausted: true` report immediately without waiting on or wasting a `wp` lease.
  - Tests: [`../../tests/test_prover_budget_lease_wait.py`](../../tests/test_prover_budget_lease_wait.py), [`../../tests/test_resources.py`](../../tests/test_resources.py), [`../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`](../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py).

### Symptom: Missing Assigns Clauses Silently Considered Sound by WP Log Scanner
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main (uncommitted at the time, now committed).
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit. Frama-C warnings of the form `Neither code nor explicit assigns for function ...` or `Neither code nor explicit exits, assigns and terminates...` were not caught by `scan_wp_log_soundness`, allowing ungrounded proofs relying on invented empty assigns clauses to pass as sound.
- **Cause:** `_TIER_B_PATTERNS` only matched the exact string `Neither code nor specification for function`.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/run_wp_function.py`):** Wired [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py) `parse_missing_spec` and `fold_messages` into `scan_wp_log_soundness`, `apply_verdict_limits`, and `derive_wp_diagnostic_hints`. Any missing-spec message whose clauses are not exclusively benign (`exits`/`terminates`) is flagged as Tier B silent unsoundness and its callee is recorded in limits. Tests: `tests/test_run_wp_function.py`.

### Symptom: Two-Line `Missing assigns clause` Warning Defeated Macro-Loop Unsoundness Filter
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit. Realistic two-line Frama-C warning outputs (`[wp] macro_test.c:3: Warning:\n  Missing assigns clause ...`) split the file/line location from the warning text, causing `_MISSING_ASSIGNS_LINE_RE` to fail matching and resulting in `unsound_proof` for macro loops.
- **Cause:** `_TIER_B_RE` matched single lines without message folding, capturing only line 2 without line 1's source location.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/run_wp_function.py`, `tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`):** `scan_wp_log_soundness` folds multi-line Frama-C log messages into single-line messages via [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py) `fold_messages` prior to pattern matching. Updated fixture in `tests/test_workspace_overlay.py:305` to realistic two-line format. Tests: `tests/test_run_wp_function.py`, `tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`.

### Symptom: Substring Overlap in File Names Matched Unrelated Diagnostics
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit. `_kernel_error_line` in [`../../scripts/loop_seeds.py`](../../scripts/loop_seeds.py) and `_MISSING_ASSIGNS_LINE_RE` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) matched file names that were substrings of other files (e.g. `sum.c` within `not_sum.c:9`). In addition, `_kernel_error_line` previously used `(?<![\w./-])`, which incorrectly rejected valid error lines on path-qualified file names (e.g. `dir/sum.c:9`, `./sum.c:9`).
- **Cause:** Unanchored file name regex patterns in `run_wp_function.py`, and overly restrictive negative lookbehind including `'/'` against basename queries in `loop_seeds.py`.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/loop_seeds.py`](../../scripts/loop_seeds.py), [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)):** Used negative lookbehind `(?<![\w./-])` before full file path matches in `run_wp_function.py`, and `(?<![\w.-])` in `loop_seeds.py` (omitting `/` so directory-prefixed paths are accepted while continuing to reject prefix/suffix word overlaps such as `not_sum.c` or `prefix_sum.c`). Tests: [`../../tests/test_loop_seeds.py`](../../tests/test_loop_seeds.py), [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py), [`../../tests/test_challenger_m3_stress.py`](../../tests/test_challenger_m3_stress.py).

### Symptom: Truncated WP Log With CSV Report Produces False `all_proven` Verdict
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit. If WP crashed, hit OOM or was truncated after generating `report.csv` but before printing `[wp] Proved goals:`, `run_wp_on_function` reported `status: "all_proven"`.
- **Cause:** `all_valid` evaluated `(vc_total == 0 or vc_proved == vc_total)`, where `vc_total` defaulted to 0 when the summary line was absent. This also undermined the degenerated-goal waiver backstop.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/run_wp_function.py`):** When `csv_path.exists()` is true, `all_valid` strictly requires `vc_proved_valid = (proved_m is not None and vc_total > 0 and vc_proved == vc_total)`. A missing `Proved goals:` line or a count of 0 proved goals falls through to `unproven_goals_remain`. Tests: `tests/test_run_wp_function.py`.

### Symptom: Mock WP Test Runners Failing With `AssertionError: assert 'unproven_goals_remain' == 'all_proven'`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Test-mock fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M3 & M5). Tests using simulated Frama-C runners in `tests/test_tool_sizing.py` and `tests/test_promote_and_verifier.py` failed with `status: unproven_goals_remain` instead of `all_proven`.
- **Cause:** Feature A3 ([`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)) instituted a strict fail-closed verdict gate requiring explicit `[wp] Proved goals:    k / n` in stdout logs when a report CSV is present. Mock runners (`FakeFramaC`, `fake_run`) generated valid CSV reports but emitted empty or incomplete stdout logs missing the `Proved goals` line, correctly triggering the fail-closed guard.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../tests/test_tool_sizing.py`](../../tests/test_tool_sizing.py), [`../../tests/test_promote_and_verifier.py`](../../tests/test_promote_and_verifier.py)):** updated `FakeFramaC` default log output to include `[wp] Proved goals:    1 / 1\n` and added the line to retry branch mocks in `test_bitwise_retry_reuses_the_cache_dir_and_sizing` and `test_wp_unsigned_downcast_and_bitwise_retry`, aligning test mocks with real Frama-C log behavior. Tests: [`../../tests/test_tool_sizing.py`](../../tests/test_tool_sizing.py), [`../../tests/test_promote_and_verifier.py`](../../tests/test_promote_and_verifier.py).

### Symptom: Contradictory WP Proofs Misclassified as Consistent Under Non-Typed Prover Memory Models
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit. Vacuity smoke test logs under `-wp-model Bytes`, `-wp-model Hoare`, or `-wp-model Caveat` proving `\false` were declared `consistent` by `run_wp_function.classify_vacuity_log`.
- **Cause:** `_DOOMED_REQUIRES_RE` and `_DOOMED_DEAD_RE` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) hardcoded the literal prefix `typed_`. When running under other memory models, goal names carry prefixes such as `bytes_` or `hoare_`, resulting in 0 regex matches and silently declaring contradictory contracts consistent. In addition, `classify_vacuity_log` did not validate smoke test ratio bounds (`k > n`, `n <= 0`, `k < 0`) and returned `consistent` whenever unexplained smoke test failures remained (`(n - k) > len(dead)`).
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)):** Dropped the `typed_` prefix requirement from `_DOOMED_REQUIRES_RE` and `_DOOMED_DEAD_RE`, expanded dead goal matching to include `_dead_local_init_`, added `_SMOKE_COUNT_RE` parsing `k / n`, added ratio bounds validation (`n <= 0` or `k > n` or `k < 0`), checked for unexplained smoke test failures (`(n - k) > len(dead)`), and failed closed to `status: "inconclusive"`. Tests: [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py), [`../../tests/test_challenger_m3_stress.py`](../../tests/test_challenger_m3_stress.py), [`../../tests/test_verdict_limits.py`](../../tests/test_verdict_limits.py).

### Symptom: Unproved `pointer_value` Goals Are Counted `proven_under_hyp` After One `(Degenerated)` Line in the WP Log
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit, by reading `run_wp_on_function` (no target run, no test: the loop is inside the function that runs Frama-C).
- **Cause:** `_FPV_DEGEN_RE` captures the degenerated goal names, but only their count was used: with one or more, every Unknown or Invalid row whose property contained `pointer_value` or `\valid_function` moved to `proven_under_hyp`, a data-pointer `\object_pointer` alarm included.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/run_wp_function.py`):** a row is moved only when its property contains `\valid_function` and not `\object_pointer`, and at most one row per degenerated goal. The report CSV has no goal name, so rows cannot be matched to the log by name.
