### Symptom: A Second Function-Pointer Call Leaves `\null == fp || \valid_function(fp)` Unproven (`(Degenerated, 2 warnings)`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main and the named test exists.
- **Seen on:** `tests/fixtures/security_props/forward/l5_forwarding.c`, function `send_msg` (two calls through `ctx->backend`), package A of [`../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md`](../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md), 2026-11-18, `framac-analysis:latest`. WP log: `[wp] [Timeout] typed_send_msg_assert_rte_pointer_value_4 (Qed 5ms) (Alt-Ergo) (Degenerated, 2 warnings)`.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. `_FPV_DEGEN_RE` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) matched the literal `(Degenerated)` only, so a degenerated goal that also carries warnings was not listed in `assumed_function_pointer_goals` and its report row stayed unproven. A function with two pointer calls (libmodbus's `send_msg`) could not end `all_proven`, and its `l5_sanitized` unit could not be credited.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-18 on branch `metacsl-integ`):** the pattern accepts `(Degenerated, N warnings)`. Test: `test_degenerated_pointer_value_goal_with_warnings_is_listed` in [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py). Checked on the log lines above, not by a new image run of the fixture.

### Symptom: A Loop Invariant Goal Is Counted Under `l2_termination`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main and the named test exists.
- **Seen:** package D2 of [`../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md`](../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md), 2026-11-18, in a unit test: `goal_obligation_class({"kind": "precondition", "annotation": "L4_invariants: len <= cap"})` returned `l2_termination`. Not looked for in the reports of earlier runs.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. `goal_obligation_class` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) tested `"variant" in low`, which the word "invariant" satisfies. Every goal whose kind or text holds "invariant" went to the termination unit instead of `rte`: an unproven loop invariant was reported against termination, and proven invariants counted as termination goals. What this did to the credit of `l2_termination` units in past runs was not measured.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-18 on branch `metacsl-integ`):** the test is `variant` not preceded by `in`. Test: `test_a_loop_invariant_is_no_termination_goal` in [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py). No existing test depended on the old behaviour.

### Symptom: WP Verdict Gate Drops `Considered valid` / `Inconsistent` / Unknown CSV Rows, Credits Unproven Global Lemmas/Axioms/Non-Well-Founded Recursive Logic, Loses Memory-Model Hypotheses, Misclassifies 0-Smoke-Goal Leaves and Why3 Anomalies
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. All six defects are fixed in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-20, by code audit and Tier 1 Step 0 against all 21 `sysprog21/frama-c-mcp` fixtures (`4b935a0`, 32 targets + 4 confirmation probes) in `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 34.0~beta, Alt-Ergo 2.4.3, Z3 4.13.3, CVC5 1.1.2) for [`../plans/archive/external-projects-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/archive/external-projects-improvement-plan.md).
- **Cause:** six concrete pipeline defects in [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py), [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py), [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py), and [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py):
  1. **Unproven global `lemma`s credited under `-wp-fct`:** on Frama-C 34, `-wp-fct <fn>` never schedules global `lemma`s and `-report-csv` emits 0 lemma rows (even whole-file `-wp` emitted 0 lemma rows in CSV), yet solvers assume every global `lemma` in scope as an axiom. `run_wp_function.py`'s CSV lemma check was dead code on Frama-C 34; removing the redundant `requires \true;` from `lemma-unproved.c` returned `all_proven`, `soundness.ok=True`, `credited=True` on a false `\result == 42`.
  2. **Global `axiom` / `axiomatic` / `admit` invisible to `run_wp_function.py`:** `-report-csv` emits 0 rows for global axioms (`Axiom 'bogus'` appears only in `-report` stdout alongside ~35 Frama-C libc axioms), so `run_wp_function.py` alone returned `wp_only_cred=True` on `axiom-licensed.c :: main` (leaving baseline target `axiom`s uncovered by `verify_acsl_only`).
  3. **Non-well-founded recursive `logic` credited:** `recursive-logic-unsound.c :: f` (`logic integer bad(integer n) = bad(n) + 1;`) proved false `ensures bad(x) >= 0 ==> \result == 998;` in 4 ms with zero warnings (`credited=True`).
  4. **CSV row drops, `Partially proven` visibility, and `memory-model-hypothesis` propagation:** the `-report-csv` loop in `run_wp_function.py` had no branch for `STATUS_ASSUMED`, `STATUS_INCONSISTENT`, or `STATUS_OTHER`; `assumption-unproved.c :: assumption_carrier` had `Partially proven` rows counted in `proven_count` without surfacing `partially_proven_count`; and `memory-model-hypothesis.c :: two` emitted `requires \separated(p, &g);` under `-wp-check-memory-model` without propagating it to `limits` or `ledger.py` (whose line 3346 read a dead key `soundness.get("memory_model_hypotheses")`).
  5. **Vacuity classification (`seta` vs. unrecognized `(Doomed)`):** clean 0-smoke-goal leaf controls (`indirect-call-annotated.c :: seta`, `rc=0`, `Proved goals: 4 / 4`, no `Smoke Tests:` line) were misclassified `"inconclusive"`, while unrecognized `(Doomed)` / `unexplained_fails > 0` smoke failures returned `"inconclusive"` and were accepted by `apply_verdict_limits` and `wp_limits_problem`.
  6. **Why3 anomaly & `[wp] [Failure]` on Frama-C 34:** on `pointer-cast-anomaly.c :: nextblk` under no-retry `Typed` and `Typed+nocast`, all 3 provers crashed with `[Why3 Error] anomaly: Invalid_argument("missing free variable")` and `[wp] [Failure]` (missed by `_FAILED_GOAL_RE`), misclassified as `unproven_goals_remain` with a misleading `requires \valid(p)` hint.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20):**
  1. [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py): added `find_top_level_lemmas`, `find_target_unsound_axioms_or_admits`, and `find_unsound_recursive_logic` (Tarjan SCC + base-case guard + strict descent/advance on a guarded parameter, skipping `#if defined(__FRAMAC_EACSL__)` / `#if 0` blocks), wired into `find_unsound_acsl_keywords` and `verify_acsl_only`.
  2. [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py): added `collect_target_sources_in_scope` + `scan_target_sources_soundness` (excluding `share/libc`, `/usr/`, and `templates/fc_stubs/`) rejecting global lemmas (`soundness["unproven_global_lemmas"]`, named in `derive_wp_diagnostic_hints`), target axioms/admits, and non-well-founded recursive logic; handled `STATUS_INCONSISTENT`/`STATUS_ASSUMED`/`else` fail-closed in the CSV loop and recorded `partially_proven_count`; extracted `checked_memory_model_hypotheses` into `limits["memory_model_hypotheses"]`; classified clean 0-smoke-goal leaves as `consistent` (`smoke_goals: 0`) while recording `unknown_doomed`/`unexplained_fails` and rejecting them in `apply_verdict_limits`; recognized `[wp] [Failure]` and `[Why3 Error] anomaly:` (`wp_aborted` + dedicated diagnostic hint when not resolved by `Bytes` retry).
  3. [`../../scripts/framac_common.py`](../../scripts/framac_common.py): `wp_limits_problem` refuses reports whose `vacuity` has non-empty `unknown_doomed` or `unexplained_fails > 0` while preserving plain timeout/no-result `"inconclusive"` and `normalize_status("Partially proven") == STATUS_VALID`.
  4. [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py) & [`../../schemas/coverage-ledger.schema.json`](../../schemas/coverage-ledger.schema.json): fixed `cmd_ingest_wp` to read `checked_memory_model_functions` alongside legacy `memory_model_hypotheses`, and added `kind="memory_model_hypothesis"` assumptions whenever `limits.memory_model_hypotheses` is non-empty.
  5. **Verified:** 40 new unit tests in [`../../tests/test_acsl_ast.py`](../../tests/test_acsl_ast.py), [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py), [`../../tests/test_verdict_limits.py`](../../tests/test_verdict_limits.py), [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py), and [`../../tests/test_ledger.py`](../../tests/test_ledger.py), plus post-fix re-run of the trap fixtures and all controls in `framac-analysis:latest` (all soundness traps rejected, all controls credited with `seta` `consistent`, full suite `7842 passed`).

### Symptom: A Guardrail Replay Excuses a Crash Inside a Checked Function (`crash outside the checked functions`) When the Function Lives in a `.y`, `.l` or `.inc` File
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (no target run). `crash_outside_checked_functions` returned an excuse for a stack whose frame #0 was `parse_attr /w/src/attr_tables.inc:41:9` with `functions=["parse_attr"]`.
- **Cause:** it read the stack with `_STACK_FRAME_RE`, which wants a `.c` or `.h` file and a line. A frame in another file, with a space in its path or with no line information was skipped, and a skipped frame counted as "not a checked function".
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`):** the excuse reads the function of every frame with `_STACK_FRAME_FN_RE`, whatever follows the name. Tests: `tests/test_regex_hardening.py`. `_STACK_FRAME_RE` was decoupled to extract functions with or without `.c`/`.h` file and line, and `_FN_RE`, `_ERR_RE`, `_OLD_RE`, and `_UBSAN_RE` accept non-whitespace path locations with any extension.

### Symptom: A Prover's Merge Is Refused With `agent modified content outside assigned function '<fn>'` After It Added a Ghost Lemma Function
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix be4a909 and the later hint fix are on main.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (found 2026-11-09 while reviewing [`../plans/archive/formal-discovery-plan.md`](../plans/archive/formal-discovery-plan.md); reproduced on `acsl_ast.splice_prover_changes` with a `/*@ ghost /@ requires ...; decreases n; ... @/ void lem(int n) {...} */` block placed directly above the prover's function; fixed by Tier 3 in `be4a909`, [`../plans/archive/formal-discovery/tier-3-rule3-ghost-lemma.md`](../plans/archive/formal-discovery/tier-3-rule3-ghost-lemma.md)). Runs made before the fix still show the old message.
- **Cause:** [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) Step B item 3 ("Inductive Lemmas Without Coq") told the prover to replace a global `lemma` with a recursive `/*@ ghost ... */` C function carrying `decreases` and `ensures`. Such a function can only live at file scope, and `acsl_ast._compute_span_start` deliberately leaves top-level ACSL declarations (`ghost` included, via `_TOP_DECL_KWS`) outside the function span, so `splice_prover_changes` raised `ScopeViolationError` for exactly the construct the brief asked for. `verify_acsl_only` passes (the block is pure ACSL), so the prover saw no warning until `merge-agent`. A prover could reach `all_proven` in its scratch (WP under `-wp-fct` takes the ghost function's contract as a hypothesis and never proves the ghost function itself), then lose the whole attempt at merge. A second defect hid the declaration's name: `_parse_acsl_inner_segments` cut a ghost function at the first `;` of its `/@ ... @/` contract and `_extract_decl_name` returned the first parameter (`n`) instead of `lem`.
- **Fix:** (1) the brief's rule now forbids file-scope ghost functions as well as global `lemma`s and tells the prover to stop with `stalled_after_k_retries` and `proof_gap: {"predicate": ..., "reason": "inductive lemma required: ..."}`; (2) [`../../scripts/prover_candidates.py`](../../scripts/prover_candidates.py) `note_oracle` diffs the top-level declarations of `scratch/work` against `scratch/base` after every oracle run and reports `scope_violation_pending: ["ghost lem", ...]` in the first `candidate` block, with `next_synthesis_focus: "remove_top_level_acsl_declarations"`, `next_action: "continue"` even when `all_proven`, and the candidate never kept as best; (3) [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py) `splice_prover_changes` names the added declarations (`agent added top-level ACSL declaration(s) outside '<fn>': ghost lem; ...`), and the segmenter treats a ghost function's `/@ ... @/` contract as part of the declaration; (4) [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py) tags such a `proof_gap` with `details.gap_kind: "inductive_lemma_required"` and [`../../scripts/generate_report.py`](../../scripts/generate_report.py) counts those functions under "Open units: prover-limited vs spec-limited". Provers still cannot contribute lemmas: that is Tier 7 of the plan.
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** the leftover `unproven lemma` hint in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) `derive_wp_diagnostic_hints` no longer recommends a recursive `/*@ ghost ... */` C function; it names any unproven lemma(s) from `unproven_goals`, states that WP assumes unproven lemmas without proof and that merge refuses file-scope ghost functions, and directs the prover to remove the lemma or stop with `stalled_after_k_retries` and `proof_gap` (`inductive lemma required: ...`). Test: `test_wp_hints_unproven_lemma_forbids_ghost_function_and_points_to_stop_rule` in [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py).

### Symptom: A Variant Hint Tells the Prover to Add `if (consumed == 0) return -1;`, Which the Pure-ACSL Check Rejects
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-20, by reading the brief and the hint code while comparing them with external ACSL guidance. No target run, so no prover is known to have followed it.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in a brief and a hint. Rule 1 of [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) says "Never alter executable C tokens", and `workspace_overlay.py check-acsl-only` rejects any such change. The same brief's "Failure-kind repair strategy" (Step B item 2) and the `loop_variant` / `decreases` branch of the repair strategy in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) both end with `add a progress guard (e.g. 'if (consumed == 0) return -1;')`. A progress guard is C code.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20):** reworded both [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) (`_loop_goal_strategies`) and [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) (Step B item 2, mirrors synced) to pure-ACSL remedies: fix the measure, add the missing progress fact via a `loop invariant`, `requires`, or callee `ensures` (`strengthening_requests`), or stop and report a `proof_gap` / termination finding when the loop can fail to progress. Tests: `test_wp_hints_variant_strategy_never_recommends_c_code` in [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py), `test_prover_brief_variant_repair_strategy_never_suggests_editing_c_code` in [`../../tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py`](../../tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py).

### Symptom: Rule 5 of the Prover Brief Says `Memory model hypotheses for function` Fails the Run, but the Default Run Does Not Fail on It
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix a0e591f is on main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-20, by reading. No target run.
- **Cause:** brief out of date with the code. [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) runs `-wp-check-memory-model` by default, which turns the hypotheses into proof goals, and adds `Memory model hypotheses for function(s) ... unchecked (-wp-no-check-memory-model)` to `tier_b_unsound` only when the check is switched off with `--no-check-memory-model`. With the default flags the message alone does not produce `unsound_proof`; an unmet hypothesis shows as an unproven goal. Rule 5 of [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) lists the message among those that make the run fail with `wp_aborted` or `unsound_proof`.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, `a0e591f`):** updated Rule 5 of [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) (mirrors synced) to state that `-wp-check-memory-model` is on by default (turning hypotheses into proof goals) and that the message causes `unsound_proof` only under `--no-check-memory-model`. Also fixed the unreachable `checked_memory_model_functions` hint in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) (`derive_wp_diagnostic_hints`): extracted `mm_sep_hint` / `has_mm_hyp` so the `\separated` hint is emitted on `status == "unproven_goals_remain"` too when `soundness["checked_memory_model_functions"]` is non-empty (deduplicated, never on `all_proven`). Tests: `test_prover_brief_rule5_matches_check_memory_model_default` in [`../../tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py`](../../tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py), `test_wp_hints_checked_memory_model_functions_emitted_on_unproven_goals_remain` in [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py).

### Symptom: Prover Brief Recommends `euclid.c (axiomatic gcd)` and Syntax Hint Recommends `<==>`, Contradicting Pipeline Rules
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix a0e591f is on main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-20, by reading [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) and [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) while challenging Tier 0 of [`../plans/archive/external-projects-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/archive/external-projects-improvement-plan.md). No target run confirmed hitting them yet.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug (two brief/hint contradictions against existing mechanical gates and strict rules):
  1. "Worked contract patterns" in [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) told provers to copy the shape of `euclid.c (axiomatic gcd + ...)`, even though [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py) (`_UNSOUND_ACSL_RE`) mechanically rejects `axiomatic` blocks and top-level declarations are refused at merge.
  2. The `_BOOL_PRED_ERR_RE` syntax-error hint in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) (`derive_wp_diagnostic_hints`) told provers `use '==>' or '<==>' instead of '? :' when combining predicates`, directly contradicting `prover_brief.md` Strict Rule 4 (`never p1 <==> p2`; write `(p1 ==> p2) && (p2 ==> p1)` to avoid the Frama-C 35 E-ACSL `Piff` crash).
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, `a0e591f`):** updated `prover_brief.md` "Worked contract patterns" (mirrors synced) to keep only `euclid.c`'s `loop variant \abs(b)` / `\at(..., Pre)` shape with an explicit ban on copying `axiomatic` or top-level declarations, and updated `_BOOL_PRED_ERR_RE` in `run_wp_function.py` to recommend `==>` or `(p1 ==> p2) && (p2 ==> p1)` instead of `<==>`. Tests: `test_wp_hints_bool_pred_syntax_hint_recommends_implications_not_iff` in [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py), `test_prover_brief_worked_patterns_never_recommends_axiomatic` in [`../../tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py`](../../tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py).

### Symptom: Pointer-Walk `ptr_anchor` / `end_anchor` Idiom Fails on `strlen` Loops and Stride > 1 Pointers on Frama-C 34
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-20, Tier 3a Step 0(a) of [`../plans/archive/external-projects-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/archive/external-projects-improvement-plan.md) in `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 34.0~beta, Alt-Ergo 2.4.3 / Z3 4.13.3 / CVC5 1.1.2).
- **Cause:** pipeline brief and hint bug. [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) Step C and [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) (`_PTR_DIFF_EQ_RE`) recommended `loop invariant ptr_anchor: p == \at(p, Pre) + (p - \at(p, Pre));`, `assert end_anchor: ...`, and `res_anchor`, contradicting Step B item 1 (ghost index). On Frama-C 34:
  - For `char *` under `Typed`/`Typed+ref`, Qed collapses `p == base + (p - base)` to `p.base == base.base` without substituting `p := shift(base, offset)`, breaking E-matching against `__fc_string_axiomatic.h` (`my_strlen` failed `11/12` after 66.2 s across Alt-Ergo/Z3/CVC5, whereas ghost `idx` proved `13/13` in <= 25 ms under Alt-Ergo) and dropping `end_anchor` as a no-op (whereas `ptr_idx: p == base + idx` lets Qed discharge `p == p_end` in 0 ms).
  - For multi-field `struct *` (`Typed`/`Typed+ref`) and `int *` (`Bytes`), `p == q + (p - q)` fails outright (Timeout/Unknown across all 3 provers, confirming `acsl-skills` row C-11 for stride > 1).
  - Under E-ACSL, `\at(p, LoopEntry)` / `\at(p, Pre)` loop invariants are skipped, whereas ghost `idx` + `/*@ ghost const T *base = p; */` compiles and checks at runtime, and `O(1)` `res_base` + `res_bound` postconditions avoid `\exists` loop blowup.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20):** replaced `ptr_anchor`/`end_anchor`/`res_anchor` in [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) (Step B cross-ref, Step C pointer-walk bullet, kept-asserts list; mirrors synced) and [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) (`_PTR_DIFF_EQ_RE` hint) with the verified ghost-index (`ptr_idx: p == base + idx`) + `O(1)` `res_base`/`res_bound` + `/*@ ghost idx = (size_t)(p - buf); */` idiom. Tests: `test_wp_hints_pointer_walk_recommends_ghost_index_not_ptr_anchor` in [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py), `test_prover_brief_pointer_walk_uses_ghost_index_not_ptr_anchor` in [`../../tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py`](../../tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py), plus updated assertions in [`../../tests/test_function_facts.py`](../../tests/test_function_facts.py), [`../../tests/test_prover_context.py`](../../tests/test_prover_context.py), and [`../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`](../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py).

### Symptom: Block-Scope Ghost Function Prototypes Inside a Function Body Bypass `verify_acsl_only` and `splice_prover_changes`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-20, by code audit of [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py) while planning Tier 4 of [`../plans/archive/external-projects-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/archive/external-projects-improvement-plan.md). Not observed on a target run.
- **Cause:** pipeline soundness bug. `extract_top_level_acsl_declarations` in [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py) only scans `brace_depth == 0`, so a block-scope ghost function prototype or definition placed inside the assigned function body (such as `/*@ ghost /@ ensures \false; @/ extern void fake(void); fake(); */` or `/*@ ghost void helper(int n) { ... } */`) passed both `verify_acsl_only` and `splice_prover_changes`, allowing an unproved ghost contract to be smuggled into WP.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20):** added `find_in_body_ghost_declarations` (and line-shift-insensitive diff helper `_added_in_body_ghost_declarations`) in [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py), wired into `find_unsound_acsl_keywords` and `verify_acsl_only` (rejecting in-body ghost `/@ ... @/` declaration contracts, `extern` declarations, and C function prototypes/definitions while preserving legitimate ghost variable declarations, pointer captures, increments, assignments, control flow, loop/assert `/@ ... @/`, and ghost calls). Tests: `TestInBodyGhostDeclarations` (78+ parametrized and end-to-end splice cases) in [`../../tests/test_acsl_ast.py`](../../tests/test_acsl_ast.py).

### Symptom: A Candidate With `admit`, `axiom` or `requires \false` Passes the Unsound-Construct Check
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (no target run). `find_unsound_acsl_keywords` returned no error for `/*@ requires c != '"'; admit requires \false; ensures d != '"'; */`, and none for `requires (\false);`, `requires 1 == 0;`, `requires \false && x > 0;` or `requires x != x;`.
- **Cause (two defects in `scripts/acsl_ast.py`):** `_STRING_IN_ACSL_RE` did not know character literals and crossed lines, so two `'"'` literals hid the text between them from every check. `_VACUOUS_FALSE_CLAUSE_RE` matched only a bare `\false` or `0` as the whole predicate.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/acsl_ast.py`):** `_scrub_acsl_literals` empties string and character literals in one pass, bounded by the line. `_first_vacuous_clause` cuts each `requires` / `assumes` / `loop invariant` clause with `_scan_clause_end` and asks the new `is_trivially_false_predicate`, the twin of `is_trivial_predicate`. Tests: `tests/test_regex_hardening.py`. The check stays syntactic: a predicate false for a reason it cannot see (`x < 0 && x > 0`) is left to the WP smoke test.

### Symptom: User Variables Named `tmp` or `__retres` Dropped From Loop Guards and Prover Facts
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M1).
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/function_facts.py`](../../scripts/function_facts.py) matched compiler temporaries using a static regex `_TEMP_RE = re.compile(r"\b(?:tmp(?:_\d+)?|__retres)\b")`. When user C code legitimately declared variables named `tmp`, `tmp_1`, or `__retres`, their expressions were stripped from write values, loop guards, loop scalars, return-path conditions, Eva flow locations, and caller conditions, producing incomplete or unprovable prover briefs.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/function_facts.py`](../../scripts/function_facts.py), [`../../scripts/kernel_ast.py`](../../scripts/kernel_ast.py), [`../../scripts/kernel_ast_dump.ml`](../../scripts/kernel_ast_dump.ml)):** [`../../scripts/kernel_ast_dump.ml`](../../scripts/kernel_ast_dump.ml) records exact compiler temporaries using the Cil `v.vtemp` flag in the dumped JSON as `temps`. [`../../scripts/kernel_ast.py`](../../scripts/kernel_ast.py) exposes `temp_names(function)`, and [`../../scripts/function_facts.py`](../../scripts/function_facts.py) constructs an identifier-anchored regex matching only the function's actual kernel temporaries (falling back to `_TEMP_RE` only when reading legacy dumps that lack the flag). Caller conditions are filtered at link time using `_temp_pattern(caller_temps)` (falling back to `_TEMP_RE` when `caller_temps` is None). Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py), [`../../tests/test_function_facts.py`](../../tests/test_function_facts.py), [`../../tests/test_challenger_m1_2_empirical.py`](../../tests/test_challenger_m1_2_empirical.py).

### Symptom: Loop Bounds Misclassified or Non-Entry Expressions Allowed in Frame Skeletons
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M1).
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/frame_skeleton.py`](../../scripts/frame_skeleton.py) used loose regex matching for loop index bounds, failing on integer literal suffixes (`16U`), reversed comparison operators (`n > i`, `n >= i`), and allowing expressions not expressible at function entry (`s->len`, `n * 2`, `n - 1`, `i < n || more`) to be classified as simple bounds. A dead `lastindex == 2` branch turned comparison operators like `>` into bounds (`dst + (0 .. > - 1)`).
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/frame_skeleton.py`](../../scripts/frame_skeleton.py)):** named-group token-aware extraction of loop bounds in `_classify_offset` and balanced bracket matching in `_offset_expr`, normalizing reversed comparisons and strictly classifying non-entry-expressible expressions as `unknown`. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py), [`../../tests/test_frame_skeleton.py`](../../tests/test_frame_skeleton.py).

### Symptom: Syntax Error Line and Kind Parsing Fails on Formatted ACSL Exception Text
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M1).
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/function_slice.py`](../../scripts/function_slice.py) used regex `_SYNTAX_LINE_RE` to parse error locations and kinds from the string representation of `AcslSyntaxError`. Any formatting changes in exception rendering broke location extraction.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/function_slice.py`](../../scripts/function_slice.py), [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py)):** `AcslSyntaxError` now carries structured `line`, `kind`, and `offset` attributes directly on the exception instance, eliminating regex extraction from error strings. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py), [`../../tests/test_function_slice.py`](../../tests/test_function_slice.py).

### Symptom: Multi-Line ACSL Contracts or Comments/Literals Interfering With Contract Analysis
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M1).
- **Cause:** Contract parsers used line-oriented regexes that fail on continuation lines, custom behaviors, or clause keywords occurring inside string literals or comments (`mutation_check._ENSURES_CLAUSE_RE` truncating quantified formulas at internal binder semicolons; mutating `<==>` into malformed `<=>`; `find_named_clause` and `extract_frame_clauses` relying on brittle regexes).
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py), [`../../scripts/mutation_check.py`](../../scripts/mutation_check.py)):** `acsl_ast.iter_clauses` parses structured `(kind, label, predicate, span)` clause tuples, normalizing continuation line `@` margins upfront so continuation labels are extracted, and `acsl_ast.mask_comments_and_literals` blanks out comments and literals with whitespace preserving character positions and byte lengths. Mutants slice character spans directly without string replacement collisions. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py), [`../../tests/test_mutation_check.py`](../../tests/test_mutation_check.py), [`../../tests/test_challenger_m1_empirical.py`](../../tests/test_challenger_m1_empirical.py).

### Symptom: `workspace/reports/kb/evolved.json` and `obstructions.json` Never Appear in a Run
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 5fab01a is on main.
- **Seen:** while implementing formal-discovery Tier 1 (dead-end ledger), 2026-11-09, by reading the code; no target run yet.
- **Cause:** `spec_kb.mine_run` (CLI `spec_kb.py mine --run-dir .`) is the only writer of the evolved KB and of the obstruction store, and no script, Make target, scheduler step or skill called it. Both stores therefore stay empty unless someone runs it by hand, and the "evolved" half of the spec KB has never fed a brief or a repair hint in an unattended run.
- **Workaround in place:** the Tier 1 "Known Dead Ends" section does not depend on mining: `prepare_agent` reads earlier provers' refutations of the same function from their scratch (`prover_candidates.refuted_clauses`), and `spec_kb.obstructions_for` reads callee rejections live from `workspace/reports/strengthen_requests.json`. Only cross-function persistence (the stored items) needs `mine`.
- **Fix:** **Fixed** in `5fab01a` (branch `formal-discovery-tiers`, merged into `phase1-remediation` in `c8dad30`): `wp_scheduler.cmd_complete` now calls `spec_kb.mine_after_complete` after every result it accepts (not on a stale or repeated `complete`), under its own `spec-kb` run lock; `FRAMAC_SPEC_KB=off` mines nothing, and a mining failure is recorded in the command's `errors` without failing it. `mine_run` was not idempotent (each call re-scored every agent, so mining after each task would have counted an agent once per later task): it now records `mined_agents` in `evolved.json` and skips them, and in the scheduler's `finished_only` mode leaves an agent without `result.json` for a later call. Tests: `test_mine_run_counts_each_agent_once`, `test_finished_only_leaves_a_running_agent_for_later`, `test_mine_after_complete_fills_both_stores_unless_switched_off`, `test_complete_mines_the_spec_kb_and_a_mining_failure_is_only_an_error`.

### Symptom: The Negotiate Brief's "Your function's previous prover run" Names the First `prove-<fn>` Agent, Not the Latest Negotiate Attempt
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 5b756f3 is on main.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (found 2026-11-09 by the offline A/B of formal-discovery Tier 2 on a copy of libmnl run 1: in 9 of 17 negotiate dispatches, for example `negotiate-mnl_attr_get_payload-2` to `-5`, the section showed `prove-mnl_attr_get_payload`; fixed in `5b756f3`).
- **Cause:** `wp_scheduler._callee_history` concatenated `_agents_of(state, "prove:<key>")` before `_agents_of(state, <negotiate task id>)`. Each list is newest first, but every prove agent came before every negotiate agent, so a later negotiate attempt was never chosen while the first prove agent had a candidate state.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) `_callee_history` walks `state["agents"]` once, newest first, over both task ids. Test: `test_callee_history_prefers_the_newest_agent_across_prove_and_negotiate`.

### Symptom: The Negotiate Brief Says `best rank ... = none recorded` Although the Previous Prover Had a Best Candidate
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 5b756f3 is on main.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (found 2026-11-09 by the same Tier 2 A/B: 5 of 17 dispatches, for example `negotiate-mnl_attr_put` with best rank `{'failed': 14, 'proved': 11}`; fixed in `5b756f3`).
- **Cause:** `workspace_overlay._negotiation_evidence_section` rendered `best_rank` only when it was a list or tuple, but `prover_candidates` stores it as a dict (`failed`, `proved`, `invalid`, and with Tier 5 `trivial_added`, `effective_proved`). The Tier 2 unit tests used a list fixture, so they did not catch it.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) renders the dict as `best rank: failed 1, proved 17, invalid 0` (lists still render, an empty or missing rank says `none recorded`); the test fixtures now use the dict shape.

### Symptom: Prover Candidate Histories Cannot Be Replayed From a Finished Run Directory
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix e79a815 is on main.
- **Fixed pipeline gap** (found 2026-11-09 by the offline A/B of formal-discovery Tiers 3 and 5 on libmnl run 1; fixed in `e79a815`, O11 in [`../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md`](../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md)). The run keeps each agent's final `<fn>.patch` and the per-candidate WP and guardrail JSON, but no candidate sources: no `scratch/base`, `scratch/work` or `candidates/best/work` survives in any of the 168 agents. The trivial-clause rank (Tier 5) and the early top-level-declaration check (Tier 3) can therefore only be measured on the final patches, not on the intermediate iterations they act on.
- **Cause:** `prover_candidates.note_oracle` kept only the two oracle reports per candidate; the sources lived in `scratch/work` and `candidates/best/work`, which `proof_carry._keep_evidence` and the run export drop as source trees.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/prover_candidates.py`](../../scripts/prover_candidates.py) (`_keep_candidate_patch`, called from `note_oracle`) writes `candidates/c<n>/candidate.patch` once per candidate: the tracked files (`file` and `header` of the budget) as a unified diff against `scratch/base`, empty when the candidate equals the base. Replay with `git apply -p1 candidates/c<n>/candidate.patch` in a copy of the function's base files (the pristine overlay of the run, `workspace/work`, at the agent's dispatch). The candidate's `patch` entry in `state.json` records the file, its size and sha256. A diff above `MAX_CANDIDATE_PATCH_BYTES` (256 KiB) is not written: `candidates/c<n>/candidate.patch.omitted.json` and the `patch` entry (`file: null`, `omitted`) give its size and sha256 instead, so the gap is visible. A write error is recorded in the `patch` entry and retried on the next oracle run; it never stops the candidate from being recorded. Tests: [`../../tests/test_prover_candidate_patches.py`](../../tests/test_prover_candidate_patches.py). Runs recorded before the fix (libmnl run 1) still cannot be replayed.

### Symptom: A Negotiate Request Is Dispatched to an Agent, Then Closed as `not dispatched: callee went through 4 negotiation rounds`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 9048897 and the 2026-11-20 follow-ups are on main; the `Still wrong` note concerns an immutable recorded run (one-off).
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (seen 2026-11-09 in the offline A/B on a copy of libmnl run 1; diagnosed and fixed 2026-11-17 in `9048897`, O5 in [`../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md`](../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md)). `req_0015` was assigned to `negotiate-mnl_attr_get_payload-5` at 16:35:10Z (its scratch holds only `.prover_budget.json`, `wp_invocations: 0`), then closed `rejected_by_callee` at 16:36:40Z with the note `not dispatched: callee went through 4 negotiation rounds without an accepted contract (WP Typed pointer-cast limit on the return expression)`. The callee had 5 negotiate dispatches. Because of the `not dispatched:` prefix, Tier 1 (`spec_kb._request_obstruction`) treats it as a structural rejection and leaves it out of "Known Dead Ends".
- **Seen on:** libmnl run 1 (2026-11-06), `state/wp_schedule.json` task `negotiate:src/attr.c#mnl_attr_get_payload` (`dispatches: 5`, `reopened: 4`, its `history` events), `workspace/reports/strengthen_requests.json` `req_0004` to `req_0015`, agents `negotiate-mnl_attr_get_payload` to `-5`.
- **Cause:** two separate things.
  - **The five dispatches:** run 1's scheduler had no per-callee cap. `MAX_NEGOTIATION_ROUNDS_PER_CALLEE` came with `0d9dd7b` (2026-11-06 18:22Z), after the run's 16:35Z reopen. The rounds were: 1, then a follow-up round for `req_0005`/`req_0006` (reopened by `_resolve_negotiation`), then a requeue of `-2` that never returned (`cmd_requeue`; `req_0007`/`req_0008`/`req_0010` had joined `-2` as follow-ups at 09:05Z to 09:13Z and survived the requeue), then a follow-up round for those three, then a new request (`req_0015`) reopening the terminal task.
  - **The `not dispatched:` note was not written by scheduler code.** No format string of any scheduler version produces it (no callee key, no `(cap 3)`), and `-5` has no `result.json`, so `cmd_complete` cannot have closed it. The run's driver session closed the assigned round by hand: a one-off script (`t_close_neg.py`) called `wp_scheduler._resolve_negotiation` with that hand-written note while the cap was being added to the live scripts. The script is in the driver's session transcript (16:36:33Z), not in the recorded run directory.
  - **What today's scheduler still did:** replayed against `phase1-remediation` at `9ac8d88` ([`../../tests/test_wp_negotiation_cap.py`](../../tests/test_wp_negotiation_cap.py)), the same sequence dispatched 4 rounds. The cap was checked only in `_emit_negotiations`, and only for a terminal task: a follow-up round reopened by `_resolve_negotiation`, a requeue, or an invalid-result or analysis-error retry went past it. The fifth request was already refused correctly there. Separately, the skip-verdict closes in `_sync` and `cmd_next` wrote `not dispatched: callee unit is <verdict>` on the requests of a requeued task, which had already been handed to an agent.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py):
  - The cap is checked before every negotiate dispatch (`cmd_next`). It is also checked when `_resolve_negotiation` would reopen for follow-ups, and in `_emit_negotiations` whatever the task's state. There is no `applied` exemption any more: a callee gets at most `MAX_NEGOTIATION_ROUNDS_PER_CALLEE` dispatches.
  - `_assign_agent` records the agents each request was handed to (`request_agents` on the task); a refunded dispatch (`_prepare_failed`, the unprepared requeue) removes its agent again. `_negotiation_request_note` writes `not dispatched:` only for a request no agent was handed, and `closed after dispatch to <agent>: ...` otherwise. The `_sync` and `cmd_next` skip-verdict closes use it too.
  - `_resolve_negotiation` rewrites a `not dispatched:` note to `closed after dispatch: ...`.
  - `NOT_DISPATCHED` is `spec_kb.STRUCTURAL_REJECTION_PREFIX`, so the reader is unchanged.
  - Consequences: a callee whose negotiations were applied 3 times refuses a fourth request; a third round that ends in an invalid result or an analysis error gets no retry; a request arriving while the third round runs is refused before that round's outcome is known.
  - Tests: [`../../tests/test_wp_negotiation_cap.py`](../../tests/test_wp_negotiation_cap.py). The replay, the requeue at the cap, the skip verdict after a requeue and the hand-closed round fail on `9ac8d88`; the refunded-dispatch test guards the refund.
- **Still wrong:** `req_0015` in run 1 and its A/B copies keeps its `not dispatched:` note, because recorded runs are read-only. An offline A/B replaying that run still leaves it out of "Known Dead Ends".
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** both follow-up gaps are fixed in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py):
  - When `_negotiate_skip_verdict` fires in `cmd_next --prepare`, the dispatch is refunded (`dispatches` decremented and `_forget_request_agent`) and the task closes through `_close_negotiation_unrun` so never-dispatched requests carry the `not dispatched:` prefix (while previously-dispatched requeued requests retain prior agent attribution). Tests: `test_negotiate_claim_refused_after_prepare_refunds_dispatch_and_marks_not_dispatched`, `test_requeued_negotiate_claim_refused_after_prepare_keeps_prior_agent_attribution` in [`../../tests/test_wp_negotiation_cap.py`](../../tests/test_wp_negotiation_cap.py).
  - `_sync(..., persist=False)` (`cmd_status`) gates `_resolve_request` (and ledger parking) behind `persist`, so read-only `status` no longer writes `workspace/reports/strengthen_requests.json` or `coverage-ledger.json`. Test: `test_status_does_not_mutate_request_store_for_skippable_negotiate_task` in [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).

### Symptom: Stale Brief Template Placeholder `{references_dir}` Not Detected
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M2 & M5). Unrendered `{references_dir}` placeholders in agent brief files were not flagged by `wp_scheduler._stale_briefs`.
- **Cause:** `_PLACEHOLDER_RE` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) was hardcoded with a static list of 23 keys that omitted `{references_dir}` added in [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py).
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py), [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py)):** introduced `placeholder_re()` dynamically generating the regex from `BRIEF_RENDER_KEYS` encompassing all template keys. Updated test assertions in `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py:950` to expect `{references_dir}` among detected unrendered placeholders. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py), [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).

### Symptom: Implementation Mutants Fail With `acsl_syntax_error` (`return attr->=nla_len;`, `<==`, `>==`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix d6c3352 is on main.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (found 2026-11-09 by the formal-discovery Tier 4 A/B on libmnl run 1: 6 of 15 implementation mutants; fixed in `d6c3352`).
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/mutation_check.py`](../../scripts/mutation_check.py) `impl_mutants` applies `_REL_OP_FLIP` with `body_text.replace(op, flipped, 1)`, a plain substring replace with no token boundaries, so `>` matches inside `->` and `<` inside `<=`. With the corrected criterion these mutants are `not_applicable` and leave the denominator, but the function then gets no real implementation mutant.
- **Fix:** `mutation_check._c_operators` regroups the single-character `c_token`s of `acsl_ast.tokenize_c_acsl` into C operators by maximal munch (`->`, `>>`, `<=`, ...), skipping comments, strings and ACSL, and `impl_mutants` flips only a whole `<`, `<=`, `>` or `>=`. Test: `test_impl_mutants_flip_only_whole_relational_operators`.

### Symptom: `mutation_check.py --run-dir` Scores Every Mutant `not_applicable` on a Target With Include Paths
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixes d6c3352, 5fab01a, 0490c54 are on main.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (flags fixed in `d6c3352`, the preprocessing-failure status in `5fab01a`; found 2026-11-09 by the Tier 4 A/B on libmnl: every mutant failed with `fatal error: libmnl/libmnl.h: No such file or directory`, reported as `acsl_syntax_error`).
- **Cause:** `run_mutation_check` passes the hard-coded cppflags `"-I."` to `check_function` instead of the module's WP flags. A preprocessing failure is also classified as `acsl_syntax_error`, which is misleading.
- **Fix:** `run_mutation_check` resolves each function's module from `coverage-ledger.json` and uses `pipeline_make.build_plan(run).wp_cppflags_for(module)`, as `wp-sweep` does; each function records `cppflags_source`. A function whose module flags cannot be resolved is listed under `skipped` instead of being checked with wrong flags; a directory with no ledger modules keeps the old `-I.` (`cppflags_source: default ...`). A preprocessing failure is no longer hidden behind `acsl_syntax_error`: `run_wp_function` still reported that status until `0490c54` (see "Parent WP Re-check Reports `acsl_syntax_error` for a Missing Header" below), but every `not_applicable` mutant now carries a `failure` (`preprocessing_failed` when an error is a C preprocessor diagnostic or `failed to run: ... -E`, else the WP status), functions and totals count `preprocessing_failed`, a function whose every mutant failed to preprocess gets an `error` instead of a score, and the report's "Specification quality" section names those functions. Tests: `test_a_preprocessing_failure_is_named_and_leaves_the_function_unscored`, `test_an_acsl_error_is_not_a_preprocessing_failure`, `test_report_lists_functions_whose_mutants_failed_to_preprocess`.

### Symptom: A Proof-Carry Replay Rejected After Its Merge Leaves the Cached Contract in `workspace/work/`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix d6c3352 is on main.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (fixed in `d6c3352`; found 2026-11-09 by the formal-discovery Tier 6 A/B on a copy of libmnl run 1: `mnl_cb_noop` was rejected with `ledger ingest-wp failed`, its contract stayed in `workspace/work/src/callback.c` and its units stayed `planned`).
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/proof_carry.py`](../../scripts/proof_carry.py) `replay` calls `reject()` after `merge_agent_overlay` has already spliced the tree (the `merged without a parent WP report` and `ledger ingest-wp failed` paths). `reject()` only removes the agent; the module docstring promises that anything else rolls back.
- **Fix:** `replay` snapshots the function's file, its header and its canonical `workspace/reports/wp/<fn>.json` before any merge and restores them on every rejection after that point (`_snapshot_files` / `_restore_files`), including a prover merge that fails after the header declarations were merged. A `mark-carried` failure after a successful `ingest-wp` is not rolled back: the units are already credited by the fresh report. Tests: `test_ingest_failure_after_a_merge_rolls_the_tree_back`, `test_failed_prover_merge_also_undoes_the_header_declarations`.

### Symptom: A Rejected Proof-Carry Replay Leaves Only a Short Reason, No Guardrail Report or Logs
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 5fab01a is on main.
- **Fixed pipeline gap** (fixed in `5fab01a`; found in the Tier 6 A/B, 2026-11-09): `reject()` deleted `agents/carry-<n>` with its guardrail JSON and logs; only a reason truncated to 400 characters reached `workspace/reports/proof-carry/summary.json`. Diagnosing the rejections below needed a driver that skipped `_remove_agent`.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/proof_carry.py`](../../scripts/proof_carry.py) `reject()` copies each agent directory, without its source trees (`work`, `base`), to `state/proof-carry/<slug>/rejected/<agent>/` with the full reason in `reason.txt`, before removing the agent; the summary row names it as `evidence`. The two rejections after a merge (no parent WP report, `ingest-wp` failure) now also remove their agent, which they used to leave behind. Test: `test_a_rejected_replay_keeps_its_reports_and_full_reason`.

### Symptom: `wp-sweep` Publishes Proofs That the Replay's Merge Gate Always Rejects
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 5fab01a is on main.
- **Fixed pipeline gap** (fixed in `5fab01a`; found in the Tier 6 A/B, 2026-11-09: `mnl_attr_get_str` was rejected at replay with `frame-preservation check failed: projected frame clause missing or weakened: 'assigns \nothing;'`, because run 1's swept contract predates the frame skeleton).
- **Cause:** publishing re-proved the function but never ran the merge-time frame-preservation check. It was never unsound: a rejected hit credits no unit.
- **Fix:** `proof_carry.publish_proof` runs the merge gate's check (`frame_gate_errors`: the function's projected frame skeleton from its facts, `acsl_ast.check_frame_preserved` over the ACSL of the definition and prototype spans) and does not publish a contract it rejects; the sweep summary lists it under `warnings` (`proof not published to the cache (the replay merge gate would reject it: ...)`). Fail-open when the function has no facts, as at merge. The other merge-time checks are not run before publishing. The scope check passes because the published spans are exactly the function's own. The trust-boundary check depends on the tree at replay time. Test: `test_a_contract_the_frame_gate_rejects_is_not_published`.

### Symptom: Prover, Breaker and Architect Briefs Name `workspace/harnesses/harness_<unit>.c` and `workspace/corpus/<unit>/` When the Unit Is Fuzzed Through Sub-Module Harnesses
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 181f814 is on main.
- **Seen on:** minmea, run-1 (`C:\Users\Florian\OneDrive\Documents\Dev\targets\minmea`, commit `c43c9e7c5ed788122a9d8a5445679e1594f8a030`, standard profile), 2026-11-14, agents `prove-minmea_rescale`, `prove-minmea_tocoord`, `prove-minmea_tofloat`, `arch-libminmea_a` and `breaker-minmea_scan`.
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) `prepare_agent` rendered the ledger unit (`libminmea_a`) as `{module_name}` and the templates build the guardrail, breaker and loop-seed commands as `workspace/harnesses/harness_{module_name}.c` and `workspace/corpus/{module_name}/`. The unit has no harness of its own here: its harness modules are `minmea` and the target-fuzzer module `tf_fuzzer`, so the brief ordered a guardrail on a nonexistent `harness_libminmea_a.c`. The merged sidecar used for `{scratch_module_sources}` also put the target's `.clusterfuzzlite/fuzzer.c` among the prover's sources. The parent re-check already resolved the correct harness through `harness_module_for_function`, so only the agent-facing brief was wrong, and a prover following it could not open its corpus.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-14, commit `181f814`):** [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) `prepare_agent` resolves `harness_module_name` before building the source lists (the sub-module whose scope holds the function for a prover or breaker, the sub-module whose sidecar header matches for an architect, falling back to the unit's first non-`tf_` harness), takes `sources`, `cppflags` and `ldflags` from that sidecar, and exposes the new `{harness_module_name}` placeholder. [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md), [`../../skills/framac-wp/breaker_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/breaker_brief.md) and [`../../skills/framac-wp/header_architect_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/header_architect_brief.md) use it in their harness, corpus and campaign `--module` arguments. Test: `test_prepare_agent_brief_names_the_submodule_harness` in [`../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`](../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py). For this run the already-prepared briefs were re-rendered with `workspace_overlay.py prepare-agent` before the agents started.

### Symptom: The Parent Guardrail Fails to Link With `undefined reference to LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput` After a Merge, Because It Compiled the Target-Fuzzer Source Along With the Unit Harness
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 181f814 is on main.
- **Seen on:** minmea, run-1, 2026-11-14, the `negotiate-hex2int` merge: `parent guardrail returned status='compile_error'`; `state/parent_guard/minmea-059ae1d965/guard_minmea.compile.log` shows `[e-acsl] translation done`, then `/usr/bin/ld: ...libclang_rt.fuzzer-x86_64.a(FuzzerMain.cpp.o): in function 'main': undefined reference to 'LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput'`. The same contract is `all_proven` under WP and `passed_dynamic_oracle` in the agent's own function-scope guardrail.
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) `_run_parent_reverify` selected the right harness module for the function but compiled the `sources` its caller passed, which for a link unit come from the **merged** sidecar: `workspace/work/minmea.c` **and** `workspace/work/.clusterfuzzlite/fuzzer.c` (the unit's `tf_fuzzer` sub-module). The parent command applies `-DLLVMFuzzerTestOneInput=__framac_target_embedded_LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput` to every source, so both entry definitions are renamed, the instrumented object exports only the renamed symbol, and libFuzzer's `main` has no real entry.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-14, commit `181f814`, [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py)):** `_run_parent_reverify` now takes its guardrail sources from the chosen harness module's own sidecar (`mm.sidecars[hmod]["sources"]`) when it has them, so a unit harness never compiles a target-fuzz sub-module's fuzzer. Test: `test_parent_reverify_uses_only_the_answerable_harness_sources` in [`../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`](../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py). The `hex2int` request was left rejected in this run; its units are unaffected (no ledger units for the static helper).

### Symptom: A Prover's 900 s Result Is Rejected With `$.strengthening_requests[0].caller_function: unexpected property` and the Whole Task Is Requeued
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixes 11e8bfa, 288c9b6 (merged 1b90874) and the 2026-11-20 follow-ups are on main.
- **Seen on:** minmea, run-1, 2026-11-14, agent `prove-minmea_parse_rmc`: `wp_scheduler.py step --complete` returned `validation_errors: ["$.strengthening_requests[0].caller_function: unexpected property"]`, `action: requeued_invalid_result`, released both units and dispatched `prove-minmea_parse_rmc-2`. The prover had produced a complete analysis, a patch, a passing guardrail and 19 documented unproven goals; only one extra key in one request made all of it unusable.
- **Cause (corrected by the supervisor's fix subagent, 2026-11-14):** the prover result schema ([`../../schemas/agent-result.schema.json`](../../schemas/agent-result.schema.json)) sets `additionalProperties: false` on `strengthening_requests` entries, and [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) `_load_result` discards a result with any validation error (`_on_invalid_result`: one re-run with a fresh agent, then blocked). The brief did name the allowed keys (`callee`, `header_file`, optional `callee_file`, `needed_clause`, `reason`), but only in the prose of Step C (line 120 of [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md)), far above the result skeleton, which shows `"strengthening_requests": []`; it did not say the list is closed, and no brief made the agent validate its `result.json`. The agent added `caller_function`, which appears nowhere in its brief (it is the field name of the stored negotiation records). The scheduler also gave the re-run nothing: the errors were only in the `complete` output. In this run the second attempt's patch and reports are copies of the first attempt's (`wp_invocations: 0`), so the proof was not redone, but that was done by hand.
- **Fixed (commits `11e8bfa`, `288c9b6`, merged into `phase1-remediation` as `1b90874` on 2026-11-14, not pushed).** The schema stays closed. (1) `prover_brief.md` shows one `strengthening_requests` entry next to the result skeleton and says that any other key, `caller_function` included, invalidates the result. (2) The prover, breaker, architect and crash verifier briefs end with a "Validate before you return" step: `python3 /opt/framac-pipeline/scripts/validate_artifacts.py agent-result agents/<id>/scratch/result.json` must print `"ok": true`. (3) On `requeued_invalid_result`, `wp_scheduler.py` keeps the errors and the discarded agent id on the task (`invalid_result`, echoed as `rejected_result`) and passes them to `workspace_overlay.prepare_agent(rejected_result=...)`; the re-run's brief ends with a `Previous Attempt: Result Rejected` section. The rejected result is still discarded: the re-run takes the previous annotations as a starting point and runs both oracles itself. Reproduced in Docker on a copy (`minmea-repro/prover-schema`): the committed validator of `7369244` prints `$.strengthening_requests[0].caller_function: unexpected property` for the rejected result and `"ok": true` once the key is removed. Tests: `tests/test_brief_result_contract.py` (new file, on the branch only) and `test_rerun_after_an_invalid_result_is_told_the_validation_errors` in [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** both follow-ups are fixed. [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) `prepare-agent` accepts `--rejected-result` / `--rejected-result-json` (inline JSON object, JSON file path, or `@<path>`) and forwards it to `prepare_agent`. [`../../scripts/validate_artifacts.py`](../../scripts/validate_artifacts.py) `agent-result` (`validate_agent_result`) now supports explicit `--expected-agent-id` / `--expected-role` / `--expected-function` / `--expected-file` / `--expected-fingerprint` flags and automatically checks directory `agent_id` plus scheduled task identity (`state/wp_schedule.json`, matching `wp_scheduler._load_result`) when validating `agents/<id>/scratch/result.json`. Tests: `test_prepare_agent_cli_rejected_result_preserves_section_and_validates_input` in [`../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`](../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py); `test_agent_result_task_identity_from_schedule_and_agent_dir`, `test_agent_result_standalone_and_explicit_expected_flags`, and `test_agent_result_in_agent_dir_without_schedule_checks_directory_agent_id` in [`../../tests/test_validators.py`](../../tests/test_validators.py). Whether a live prover runs the validation step was not tried.

### Symptom: One `run_wp_function.py` Call Runs 19 Minutes Inside a Prover Whose Budget Is 900 s, and a Second Call on the Same `--out-dir` Starts Meanwhile
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen on:** minmea, run-1, 2026-11-14 (supervisor, from `docker ps` and `docker top`, read-only). Container `framac-83d2286d91c4` ran `run_wp_function.py --file agents/prove-minmea_check/scratch/work/minmea.c --function minmea_check --out-dir agents/prove-minmea_check/scratch/wp` from about 13:19 to 13:38: the first pass wrote `wp_minmea_check.csv` at 13:30:58, the model-retry pass `wp_minmea_check_retry.csv` at 13:33:38, and Z3 was still on goals (`-T 45`) after that. At 13:29 the same prover started a second container (`framac-e0904f6850c4`) with the same file, function and `--out-dir`, wrapped in `timeout 300`, while the first was still running.
- **Cause:** two limits that do not fit together, and no lock. [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py) gives the `wp` job class `wall_timeout_s=3600`, which `run_wp_function.py` `default_wall_timeout` uses when neither `--wall-timeout` nor `FRAMAC_WP_WALL_TIMEOUT` is set, while the scheduler asks a prover to finalize after 900 s. One WP call can therefore outlast the whole agent budget. Nothing stops two `run_wp_function.py` runs from writing the same `--out-dir`, so the reports a prover reads can come from either run. The effect on the `minmea_check` result was not checked.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)):** both gaps are closed. The WP wall timeout is bounded by the agent's remaining `.prover_budget.json` wall budget (`wall_timeout = min(wall_timeout, rem_s)`), and `_out_dir_lock` now acquires an atomic non-blocking directory lock (`<out_dir>/.wp_out_dir.lock` with heartbeat and dead-PID / stale-age recovery; same-thread re-entrant for nested internal retries/passes). A second concurrent process targeting the same `--out-dir` returns `status: "analysis_error"`, `reason: "out_dir_locked"` (exit 1 on CLI) without modifying existing reports. Tests: `test_out_dir_lock_refuses_concurrent_live_holder_without_clobbering_reports`, `test_out_dir_lock_reclaims_stale_dead_pid_and_expired_remote_lock`, `test_out_dir_lock_allows_nested_retries_and_releases_on_exception` in [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py).

### Symptom: The Merge Rejects `requires valid_read_string(sentence)` With `TrustBoundaryError ... constrains attacker-controlled sentence` Even Though the Harness NUL-Terminates the Input
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 181f814 is on main.
- **Seen on:** minmea, run-1, 2026-11-14, `prove-minmea_checksum`: 10/11 goals proved and `passed_dynamic_oracle`, but `partial_merge` failed with `TrustBoundaryError: trust-boundary check failed (untrusted_surface entry requires): minmea.c: minmea_checksum: requires valid_read_string(sentence); constrains attacker-controlled sentence (an untrusted_surface entry may only state memory shape)`.
- **Cause:** [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py) `entry_requires_violations` treats `valid_read_string` as memory shape only when `nul_terminated` is true. [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) `_untrusted_entry_metas` synthesizes an entry for a trust-boundary function that is not a harness `entry_fn` (`minmea_checksum` is called by the `minmea` harness but the harness names `minmea_sentence_id`); that synthetic entry carried no `null_terminate`, so the NUL-termination the harness always establishes was lost and a pure shape predicate was read as a value constraint.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-14, commit `181f814`, [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py)):** the synthetic entry now inherits `null_terminate` (or `entry_style` `c_string`/`argv`) when any harness of the same unit establishes it. Test: `test_a_synthetic_entry_inherits_the_units_nul_termination` in [`../../tests/test_r3_trust.py`](../../tests/test_r3_trust.py). The 10/11 `minmea_checksum` contract was not re-merged in this run; its units stay blocked on the loop-variant gap below.

### Symptom: `run_wp_function.py` Returns `unproven_goals_remain` With `proven_count == total_goals`, `unproven_count: 0` and an Empty `unproven_goals` When Only Loop-Variant Goals Time Out
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen on:** minmea, copy of run-1 (`minmea-repro/eacsl-strlen`), `minmea_checksum`, 2026-11-14, with `loop variant strlen(\at(sentence, Pre)) - (sentence - \at(sentence, Pre));` and with `loop variant (int)strlen(sentence);` (supervisor's subagent; not rerun by the supervisor).
- **Cause:** pipeline bug (diagnosed in `framac-analysis:latest` on 2026-11-20 and fixed). Frama-C 34 `-report-csv` completely omits both `loop variant` and recursion `decreases` (`Recursion variant`) rows, emitting only `termination clause | Partially proven | \true` (which `framac_common` maps to `STATUS_VALID`). When only a variant VC timed out (`vc_timeouts > 0` or `vc_proved < vc_total`), `unproven_goals` stayed empty, `proven_count == total_goals`, `next_synthesis_focus` was `"complete"`, and no variant hints were emitted.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)):** `_reconcile_unreported_wp_goals` reconciles failed/timed-out VCs from `-wp-report-json` (or WP stdout log fallback) whenever VCs are not cleanly all proved, synthesizing `unproven_goals` entries for omitted `loop variant` / `decreases` properties (and any remaining unreported failed VCs), updating `unproven_count`, `buckets["step_b_loop_invariant"]`, and `by_obligation_class["l2_termination"]`, while keeping clean `all_proven` runs with `Partially proven` under hypotheses untouched; `_goal_failure_strategy` / `_loop_goal_strategies` now also match `"loop variant"` (space). Verified in `framac-analysis:latest` on `walk_bad`, `rec_bad`, and `walk_ok`. Tests: `test_timed_out_loop_variant_is_surfaced_from_report_json`, `test_timed_out_loop_variant_log_fallback_without_report_json`, `test_timed_out_recursion_variant_and_mixed_unproven_variant_are_surfaced`, `test_partially_proven_under_hypotheses_stays_all_proven_when_all_vcs_proved` in [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py).

### Symptom: The Gate Stalls With the Orchestrator Idle, Waiting for Verifiers Whose Briefs Exist but Which Were Never Spawned
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, 2026-11-18, Phase 3 crash verification gate.
  - Between 04:44 and 04:46, `wp_scheduler.py step --prepare` marked four verify tasks running and wrote their `agents/<id>/brief.md`: `verify-modbus_tcp_pi_listen_0c5943fca9`, `verify-free_c2bf36bd0d`, `verify-modbus_mapping_new_start_add_dbff8311fb` and `verify-modbus_write_registers_3b18aaa18e`.
  - The opencode orchestrator never spawned subagents for them. Their `scratch/` stayed empty, and 24 child sessions covered only the other tasks.
  - It then ended its turn at 04:54 to "wait for the remaining verifiers". No file, container or transcript activity followed until the supervisor intervened at 05:08.
- **Cause:** model error made possible by a pipeline gap.
  - The orchestrator read the `step --prepare` output through a truncated terminal tail, and lost part of the brief list.
  - The scheduler detects tasks past their wall budget (the "FINALIZE NOW" hints) only when `step` or `status` runs. An orchestrator that ends its turn while waiting for subagents never calls them again, so a task that is prepared but never dispatched is never noticed.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py), [`../../skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md)):**
  - `cmd_next(..., prepare=True)` and `cmd_step(..., prepare=True)` atomically write the full prepared brief list to `state/last_prepared_briefs.json` and return `"briefs_file": "state/last_prepared_briefs.json"`; [`../../skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md) (mirrors synced) instructs the orchestrator to read `briefs_file` whole and verify `spawned count == prepared count` before ending its turn.
  - Added `dispatch_lost` detection (`DISPATCH_LOST_GRACE_S = 600`, `_agent_has_activity`, ignoring `prepare_agent`'s own `base`/`work`/`context`/unused `.prover_budget.json`): `cmd_status` and `_over_budget_tasks` surface prepared tasks with zero post-prepare activity past the grace period as `dispatch_lost` (offering spawn or requeue, never `FINALIZE NOW`), and `cmd_requeue` allows requeueing them on `--stale-minutes` and refunds `dispatches`.
  - Tests: `test_next_and_step_prepare_write_last_prepared_briefs_file`, `test_dispatch_lost_detection_ignores_prepare_files_and_refunds_dispatch_on_requeue` in [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).

### Symptom: `step --prepare` Hands Out No Work (`"available": 0`) While Tasks Stay `running` for Hours With No `agents/<id>/` Directory
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, 2026-11-18, Phase 3. At 12:02 local, 7 of the 15 agent slots were held by tasks with no live agent, and every `step` listed them under `over_budget_tasks` with `FINALIZE NOW: inject 'FINALIZE NOW — write agents/<agent-id>/scratch/result.json immediately' or run wp_scheduler.py requeue --agent-id <id>`.
  - No agent directory at all: `prove-modbus_disable_quirks`, `prove-modbus_get_byte_timeout`, `prove-modbus_get_indication_timeout` (assigned by `_step78` to `_step80`), `prove-modbus_set_debug`, `prove-modbus_set_error_recovery` and `prove-modbus_set_indication_timeout` (assigned by `_step97`).
  - `breaker-modbus_set_float_abcd` is a separate case: its brief exists, but nothing was written after it for 163 minutes. That matches the prepared-but-never-spawned entry above; the transcript was not checked for a spawn.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap, triggered by the run's operators.
  - `cmd_next` in `scripts/wp_scheduler.py` calls `_assign_agent` and saves the schedule before it looks at `prepare`. A `step` without `--prepare` therefore moves ready tasks to `running` and reports them in `next.tasks`, with an empty `briefs` list and no overlay.
  - A later `step --prepare` prepares only the tasks it picks itself, so these tasks are never prepared. They count against the slot limit until someone requeues them.
  - The over-budget hint does not tell the two cases apart: it offers `FINALIZE NOW` for an agent that never existed.
  - Steps 78 to 80 ran without `--prepare` because the supervisor told the session to (a hold on new agents during the guardrail fix). Step 97 left the flag out on the session's own initiative.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py)):** `_over_budget_tasks` marks running tasks missing `agents/<id>/brief.md` with `never_prepared: True`, `reason: "never prepared (missing brief.md)"`, and `action: "wp_scheduler.py requeue --agent-id <id>"` (never `FINALIZE NOW`); `cmd_status` surfaces `never_prepared` tasks; and `cmd_next(..., prepare=True)` / `cmd_step(..., prepare=True)` now retroactively self-heal existing unprepared `running` tasks (even when `available == 0`), preparing their overlays/briefs, adding them to `out["briefs"]` and `state/last_prepared_briefs.json`, and tagging them `retroactively_prepared: True`. Tests: `test_over_budget_and_status_distinguish_never_prepared_tasks_without_brief`, `test_next_and_step_prepare_retroactively_heal_unprepared_running_tasks` in [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).

### Symptom: One `step --complete` Runs for More Than 20 Minutes While Every Other Finished Agent Waits to Be Completed
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, 2026-11-18, Phase 3. `wp_scheduler.py step --complete prove-modbus_tcp_pi_listen-2 prove-modbus_tcp_pi_connect breaker-modbus_rtu_set_serial_mode --slots auto --prepare` started at 12:12 local. At 12:36 it was still in the parent WP re-check of `_modbus_tcp_pi_connect`: a first pass ended at 12:21, and a `-wp-model Bytes` pass with three provers was still producing goals. The schedule had not changed since 12:12, and all 13 running agents had a `scratch/result.json` waiting. The step ended at 12:47 after 35 minutes, including a counter-example pass (`wp__modbus_tcp_pi_connect_cex.*`) that finished at 12:47.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap. `_run_parent_reverify` in `scripts/workspace_overlay.py` calls `subprocess.run` on `run_wp_function.py` with `--ignore-prover-budget` and no timeout, and the re-check runs inside the `step --complete` call. A stalled prover's partial merge therefore delays every other completion for as long as its WP passes take. This was not a hang: the provers were active and the goal count was rising.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py)):** `_run_parent_reverify` now enforces a configurable wall timeout (`DEFAULT_PARENT_WP_WALL_TIMEOUT_S = 600`, overridable via `FRAMAC_PARENT_WP_WALL_TIMEOUT` or disabled with `0`/`off`), passing `--wall-timeout <N>` to `run_wp_function.py` plus a `N + 60`s `subprocess.run` timeout backstop. On `TimeoutExpired`, partial `wp_json` is removed and the merge rolls back cleanly with a retryable refusal message. Tests: `test_parent_wp_wall_timeout_env_parsing`, `test_parent_reverify_passes_wall_timeout_and_subprocess_backstop`, `test_parent_reverify_handles_wp_timeout_expired_cleanly` in [`../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`](../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py). (Moving partial-merge re-checks asynchronously into `merge-pending` remains an optional future optimization.)

### Symptom: A Breaker Returns `clean` Although Its Campaign Found E-ACSL Alarms in Other Functions
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in main (the entry says not merged, but the commits are on main).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, 2026-11-18, Phase 3. `breaker-modbus_tcp_connect` returned `status: clean`, and its `analysis` says of the module-wide build that the crashes it found "are in other functions": `modbus-rtu.c` lines 1053, 1126, 1243 and 1348, and `modbus_tcp_pi_listen` line 841. Its `scratch` holds five `crash-*` inputs of 1 to 4 bytes. The orchestrator kept these in its notes and raised no candidates.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap. The breaker result has one `crash_artifact` and one `failing_predicate`, both about the assigned function, and no field for an alarm elsewhere, so the observation can reach the crash gate only through free text.
  - The four `modbus-rtu.c` lines are all contract clauses that read `\old(ctx->...)` or `\at(ctx->backend->backend_type, Pre)`, in functions whose contract permits a NULL `ctx`.
  - `breaker-modbus_rtu_set_serial_mode` diagnosed line 1053 as E-ACSL snapshotting the `\old` term at function entry without the clause's NULL guard, and `verify-modbus_rtu_set_serial_mode_2820aa2803` returned `rejected` for the same reason. A standalone probe reproduces that behaviour on `34.0~beta` and `35.0~~dev`: see the upstream draft [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-old-term-under-guard.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-eacsl-old-term-under-guard.md). The other three lines fit that pattern, but their libmodbus inputs were not replayed.  - `modbus_rtu_get_serial_mode`, `modbus_rtu_set_custom_rts` and `modbus_rtu_set_rts_delay` are already `done merged`, so their guardrails did not replay these inputs.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `integ/brief-gaps` (`bc56b9e`, `2f1f636`), not merged into `phase1-remediation` and not tried on a live run. The breaker result has an optional `other_function_crashes` list, and `ledger.py` `cmd_ingest_agent_hypothesis` turns each entry into an unverified candidate (`_ingest_breaker_crash`). The location comes from the campaign report in the breaker's scratch, matched by the input's sha256 and placed as `cmd_ingest_fuzz` places it; without a record the breaker's fields are used and the candidate is only proposed as `needs_validation`. The input is promoted as the reproducer, so the parent replays it before any verdict; it is not added to the corpus. The brief's stop rule now says to report failures elsewhere. Tests: `tests/test_breaker_brief_gaps.py`.

### Symptom: `wp_scheduler.py` Dispatches `repair:<mod>` After the First Verifier Batch While Sibling `verify` Tasks of `<mod>` Are Still Open
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is merged into main.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15. `libqcbor_a` had 28 `verify` tasks and `max_repair_attempts: 2` (with 1 attempt already used during Phase 1 `reach-dynamic`). After Batch 1 (`9/28` verifiers) rejected two `DecodeNesting_LevelUpCurrent` candidates as `harness_induced`, `wp_scheduler.py` immediately returned `repair:libqcbor_a` in `parent_tasks` while 19 sibling `verify:*` tasks on `libqcbor_a` were still `pending`, causing the orchestrator to spend the module's final repair attempt (`2/2`, `exhausted: true`) and start a ~40-minute re-fuzz before Batches 2-3 inspected the remaining `QCBORDecode_Private_*` / `QCBOREncode_Private_*` candidates.
- **Cause:** `_ensure_repair` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) created `repair:<module>` with empty `deps: []`, even though line 2882 (`task["reasons"].append(reason)`) was written to accumulate multiple `harness_induced` reasons.
- **Fix:** **Fixed** (merged into `main`; originally commit `a17aa9e` on `sup/QCBOR-eva-flows-bloat-and-caps`): `_repair_verify_deps` populates `repair:<mod>["deps"]` with all `verify:*` tasks targeting `<mod>` (`_ensure_repair`, `_compute_deps`, and `_View.ready`), holding `repair:<mod>` in `pending` and accumulating every `harness_induced` reason until all open `verify:*` tasks of `<mod>` reach `TERMINAL` (single-verifier modules remain ready immediately upon verifier completion). Covered by `tests/test_large_eva_report.py`.

### Symptom: `wp_scheduler.py refresh --module <mod>` Closes `repair:<mod>` Although the Repair Skipped `eva-<mod>`, Leaving the Module's Eva Report From the Pre-Repair Harness
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed by 09ed776, 4ed4057, 5b6d566, 0231e0e, all on main; the `Fix: none yet` bullet is superseded by the Status bullets.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15 (external supervisor, transcript `msg_1dcc20e36001FNG2ZxrGf6Sg1l`). Two Crash Verifiers rejected `DecodeNesting_LevelUpCurrent` candidates as `harness_induced`, so the scheduler opened `repair:libqcbor_a`. Its parent actions end with `pipeline_make.py run module-libqcbor_a`, which runs `fuzz-libqcbor_a` and `eva-libqcbor_a`, then ingest and facts. The run agent repaired dispatch case `129` in `harness_libqcbor_a.c` and then ran only `pipeline_make.py run build fuzz coverage reach-dynamic`. It skipped Eva on purpose because the 9-shard run takes about 2 h.
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug (fixed, see Status below):** `cmd_refresh` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) finishes `repair:<mod>` whenever no follow-up reasons are pending. It never checks that the module's Eva report (or the fuzz report) was produced from the current harness digest. The repaired case still feeds `eva_main`, so the Eva alarms, the facts and the WP queue keep resting on the unrepaired harness, and nothing flags it.
- **Fix:** none yet. `wp_scheduler.py` holds uncommitted hot patches from the same run, so no fixer was dispatched. Proposed fix: make `refresh` recompute the `input_digest` that [`../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py`](../../scripts/run_eva_slice.py) records in `workspace/reports/eva/<mod>.json` (sources and every header they include). It should do the same for the fuzz report's harness. When either digest is stale, `refresh` should refuse to close the repair and name the target that still has to run. It has not been checked whether the sharded merge keeps `input_digest`.
