- **Post-run review (2026-11-15):** the file times in run-6 confirm the stale evidence: `harness_libqcbor_a.c` was last written at 02:12:44, the Eva report at 01:47:10 and the facts at 01:49:41 (both older), the fuzz report at 02:54:39. Recomputing `input_digest` cannot work for a sharded module (see [`./eva.md`](./eva.md), "A Sharded Eva Report's `input_digest` Cannot Be Recomputed From the Sources"), the fuzz report records no harness digest, and the facts JSON records no Eva digest. Revised fix: stamp `harness_digest` (`phase0_gates.harness_digest`, the value the reach gate already uses) into the Eva report, the merged shard report and the fuzz report, and have `cmd_refresh` compare each against the current harness before it closes the repair.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`09ed776`, `4ed4057` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py); `5b6d566` stamps the Eva side). `cmd_refresh` compares `phase0_gates.harness_digest(..., ignore_exempt=True)` with the `harness_digest` field of the fuzz and Eva reports of every harness of the module (`_stale_repair_evidence`; a report kind the harness does not run is skipped). A report without the field is accepted only when it is newer than the harness `.c`. While fuzz or Eva is stale the repair stays open and `refresh` returns `repair: "stale_evidence"` with `stale_evidence`, `targets` and `actions`, exit code 1. Facts older than the Eva report are named in `targets` but do not hold the repair, because `facts-<mod>` is failure-tolerant. On run-6's files the refresh would have refused with `eva-libqcbor_a, facts-libqcbor_a` (checked by reading the mtimes, not run). Tests: `tests/test_wp_scheduler_run6.py::test_refresh_*`.
- **Follow-up (2026-11-15, `0231e0e`):** two writers defeated the "newer than the harness" rule for runs started with an older pipeline. `eva_tuning.py finish` stamped the current digest when the base trial recorded none, whatever the trial's age, and `eva_shards.py merge` of shards without the field wrote a report without one and with a new mtime; a decision or merge made again after a repair, from runs that predate it, then closed the repair. Both now call `phase0_gates.derived_harness_digest`: the current digest only when every input report is newer than the harness source, else the marker `"unknown"`, which equals no digest, so `refresh` answers `stale_evidence` and names `eva-<mod>`. Through Make the gap did not open (`eva-<mod>` re-runs its shards and base run, which stamp the field); it did for `eva_shards.py merge` or `eva_tuning.py finish` run by hand. Tests: `tests/test_eva_harness_digest.py`, `tests/test_wp_scheduler_run6.py::test_refresh_rejects_a_recent_report_rebuilt_from_runs_of_an_unknown_harness`, `::test_refresh_accepts_the_identity_a_fuzz_campaign_stamps_and_rejects_it_after_another_edit`.

### Symptom: Two Budget-Length WP Runs on `load_descriptor_values` Write No Report (5039 VCs, 52 Completed, All `Timeout`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The `former open-marker gap` is fixed on main: killed runs now carry partial verdicts and the prover wall budget is scaled for large functions.
- **Seen on:** nanopb run-1, 2026-11-21, `prove-load_descriptor_values` and its retry `prove-load_descriptor_values-2`. First attempt: `wp_load_descriptor_values.json` reported `analysis_error` after the 900 s budget (5039 VCs scheduled, 52 completed, all `Timeout`, no goal proven, no report written, `restore-best` found no candidate). Retry: a single `run_wp_function.py` run on a minimal contract was still running with no report after about 13 minutes and was stopped on the finalize order.
- **Cause:** not diagnosed. The function is the nanopb iterator initialiser (many field reads through the descriptor); WP scheduled 5039 verification conditions. The step writes no partial report or progress line while it runs, so a timeout leaves no evidence to read.
- **Fixed (was open):** commit `a0e591f`: `run_wp_function.parse_partial_wp_log_verdicts` attaches `partial_verdicts` (goals scheduled, proved or timed out so far) to the `analysis_error` report of a killed run, and `workspace_overlay.scale_prover_wall_budget_s` scales the prover wall budget for large functions. Test: `test_parse_partial_wp_log_verdicts_framac34_and_goal_colon_formats` in `tests/test_run_wp_function.py`.

### Symptom: `pipeline_make.py run wp-sweep` Reports `WP output directory ... is already locked by another active run` and Turns a Proved, Merged Unit `blocked`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 7cf7e5d is on main and its regression tests exist.
- **Seen on:** nanopb run-1, 2026-11-21, final `pipeline_make.py run wp-sweep` (15 jobs). `pb_ostream_from_buffer` (proved and merged, `covered`) came back `rc=1`, no report: `WP output directory 'workspace/reports/wp-sweep/work/pb_encode.c' is already locked by another active run (pid=21, ..., function=safe_read_bool, ...)`, `reason: out_dir_locked`. The sweep summary was `incomplete`, `make: *** [wp-sweep] Error 1`, and `ledger.py ingest-wp --sweep` set the unit to `blocked` with `workspace/reports/wp-sweep/missing/pb_encode.c__pb_ostream_from_buffer.json` ("wp-sweep re-proof wrote no report (rc=1)"). A second sweep no longer listed the unit (the sweep list holds only `covered` units), so a plain rerun could not repair the ledger.
- **Cause:** a regression between two fixes. `pipeline_make.py` `sweep` uses one `--out-dir` per file (`workspace/reports/wp-sweep/work/<file slug>`) and runs the file's functions concurrently, while the 2026-11-20 `_out_dir_lock` in `run_wp_function.py` (see the entry above) refuses a second live run on the same directory. Any target with two sweep-listed functions in one source file loses all but one of them in a parallel sweep.
- **Fix:** fixed on branch `sup/nanopb-wp-sweep-outdir` (commit `080a20a`), merged to main in `7cf7e5d` on 2026-11-21. `pipeline_make.py` `sweep()` now gives each job its own `--out-dir`, `workspace/reports/wp-sweep/work/<file slug>/<function>`; the file slug still separates same-named statics of different files, and the lock in `run_wp_function.py` is unchanged. Published proofs and `ledger.py ingest-wp --sweep` read the `--out-json` report, whose path is unchanged. Regression tests: `tests/test_wp_sweep_outdir.py::test_sweep_gives_each_function_its_own_out_dir` and `::test_parallel_sweep_survives_the_out_dir_lock` (a fake runner emulating the lock; both failed on `6877117`). Confirmed by the run supervisor first in `pipeline_make.py` `sweep` (the `--out-dir` of every task was `{SWEEP_DIR}/work/{slug}`, and the tasks run in a `ThreadPoolExecutor`). Workaround for a run made before the fix, with no pipeline change (as nanopb run-1 did): re-prove the lost unit alone and serially. A `blocked` unit is no longer in the sweep list, so the run used a run-dir script, `repair/resweep_one.py`. It restricts `pipeline_make.sweep_list` to that function and calls `pipeline_make.main(["sweep", "--jobs", "1"])`, through the wrapper with `-Class wp` (see the next entry). Then re-ingest the real report with `ledger.py ingest-wp workspace/reports/wp-sweep/<file>__<function>.json`. The unit went back to `covered` (7 goals proved under the Bytes model).

### Symptom: A Prover Contract Written on a Header's `static inline` Definition Is Rejected as `agent modified content outside assigned function` or `projected frame clause missing or weakened`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Header declaration spans fixed in c40bd9c and d4db9a5; the run-6 hot patch was not kept.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15, the first header provers (`UsefulInputBuf_BytesUnconsumed`, `UsefulInputBuf_GetError`, `UsefulInputBuf_Init`, `UsefulOutBuf_RoomLeft`, `UsefulOutBuf_Reset`, `UsefulOutBuf_IsBufferNULL`). Each proved its goals (`all_proven`) but the merge requeued it: `inc/qcbor/UsefulBuf.h: agent modified content outside assigned function` and, for the frame skeletons, `projected frame clause missing or weakened: 'assigns \\nothing;'` / `'assigns pMe->data_len \\from \\nothing;'`.
- **Cause:** `UsefulBuf.h` declares every API function twice: a `static size_t f(...)` prototype early in the header and the `static inline` definition much later. `acsl_ast.locate_function_span(..., is_header=True)` returns the **prototype** span, and both the scope check in `acsl_ast.splice_prover_changes` and the frame check in `workspace_overlay.py` (the `frame_skel` block) looked only at that span. A contract placed immediately before the definition - valid ACSL for the same function - therefore sat outside the checked span, so the patch looked like an out-of-scope edit and the frame skeleton looked absent.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-15, uncommitted):** [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py) `splice_prover_changes` now accepts the definition span when the prototype span does not bracket the agent's edits, and accepts the **union** of both declarations when a prover writes the same contract on the prototype and on the `static inline` definition (it strips both spans from base and agent and splices both when the remainders match); [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) reads the ACSL of **both** spans for the frame-preservation check. [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) now forbids `requires \aligned(...)` (WP's `Typed` model reports `\aligned` as a skipped guard, so no caller can discharge it and every call site of the function fails its precondition VC); mirrors re-synced. Verified by calling `splice_prover_changes` on the failed agents' scratch trees (accepted) and by `tests/test_acsl_ast.py`, `tests/test_acsl_only_and_merge.py`, `tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`, `tests/test_sync_skill_mirrors.py` (111 passed, 4 skipped).
- **Recurs after the fix (external supervisor, 2026-11-15 04:22):** **Pipeline bug (fixed in `c40bd9c`, see Status below).** In the batch merge fired at 04:20, `UsefulInputBuf_BytesUnconsumed-2`, `UsefulInputBuf_Init-2` and `UsefulOutBuf_RoomLeft-2` merged. `UsefulOutBuf_Reset-2` and `UsefulOutBuf_IsBufferNULL-2` were blocked again (`merge_failed`) with the same `inc/qcbor/UsefulBuf.h: agent modified content outside assigned function '...'`.
  - The prover's scratch diff (`agents/prove-usefuloutbuf_reset-2/scratch/base` vs `work`, +24 lines) shows the same contract written **on both declarations**: once before the prototype at line 1062, whose parameter is named `pUOutBuf`, and once before the `static inline` definition at line 2335, whose parameter is named `pMe`.
  - The hot-patched `splice_prover_changes` accepts edits that fit inside *one* span (prototype or definition), never the two together, so a contract on both declarations is still out of scope.
  - The parameter-name mismatch is the likely reason the prover duplicated the contract. The projected frame clause names `pMe` (`assigns pMe->data_len \from \nothing`), which cannot match the prototype's `pUOutBuf`.
  - The re-dispatch prompt ("Contracts may be written on either the prototype or the static inline definition") does not say "only one of them".
  - Suggested fix: accept edits confined to the union of the prototype and definition spans, rejecting any edit elsewhere. For a header, project frame clauses against the parameter names of the declaration that carries the contract. Alternatively, tell provers to annotate only the definition when the names differ.
  - `UsefulInputBuf_GetError-2` was blocked in the same batch for a different reason: `parent WP check on merged tree returned status='unproven_goals_remain'`. This is not a batch-merge effect: its parent log shows the counter-example `(CVC5) (Cached) (Model)` `Unknown` described in the entry "The Merge Refuses a Fully Proven Contract..." below.
- **Post-run review of the hot patch (2026-11-15, after run-6 ended): pipeline bug (fixed in `c40bd9c`, see Status below; the hot patch was not kept).** The run agent's second version (dual-span branch, still uncommitted) was probed in a scratch worktree against clean `4ad9820`, with synthetic headers and with the six real scratch trees (`prove-usefuloutbuf_reset`, `-reset-2`, `-isbuffernull`, `-isbuffernull-2`, `prove-usefulinputbuf_geterror-2`, `prove-usefuloutbuf_roomleft-2`). All six are refused on clean HEAD and accepted with the patch, so it does remove the symptom. It must not be committed as is:
  - **It opens a trust-boundary bypass.** `entry_trust_boundary_errors` in [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) reads a header function's contract with `function_contract_text(..., is_header=True)`, which is the prototype span only. A prover that writes `requires magic_bound: pMe->len <= 64;` on the `static inline` **definition** of an `untrusted_surface` entry is now spliced (definition path or dual path) and the check returns no error, because the prototype contract is unchanged. Before the patch the same edit was refused as out of scope. `function_param_names` also returns the prototype's formals, so a constraint written with the definition's names (`pMe` vs `pUOutBuf`) would be missed even if the span were read. Shown on a synthetic entry. Run-6 itself was not affected: none of the 9 `UsefulBuf.h` functions whose contract was merged is a trust-boundary entry (`_untrusted_entry_metas` returns nothing for them).
  - **`contract_only` is ignored for a header definition.** `splice_prover_changes` applies it only when `not is_header`, and the dual-span branch returns before that code. A partial-contract merge of a header function therefore also splices the prover's body annotations (probe: an unproven `//@ assert` in the body is merged; the same call on a `.c` file keeps the base body).
  - **A concurrent change is reported as a scope violation.** When both spans were edited and one span of `workspace/work/` changed since the agent's base, the dual branch falls through to `agent modified content outside assigned function` where the single-span paths say `diverged from agent base snapshot`.
  - **A header that only defines the function is still refused** with `could not locate prototype of function '<fn>'`: the first lookup still requires a prototype. QCBOR declares every `static inline` twice, so run-6 did not hit it.
  - **Other readers still see only one declaration.** `has_function_contract(..., is_header=True)` and `function_contract_text(..., is_header=True)` return nothing for a contract on the definition, and [`../../scripts/proof_carry.py`](../../scripts/proof_carry.py) (lines 191, 339, 481) reads the prototype span only.
  - Frama-C 34 merges the two contracts (`found two contracts ... Merging them`) and takes the **union** of their `assigns` (`location pMe->magic is not present in all assigns clauses`), checked in `framac-analysis:latest`. A looser `assigns` on one declaration therefore silently widens a tight one on the other. The frame check's "some clause lists the location" rule is consistent with that union, so the `workspace_overlay.py` hunk does not create a spurious pass.
  - `mypy` reports 13 `union-attr` errors in `acsl_ast.py` (lines 1020-1094) and there is no regression test.
  - Proposed fix: one helper that returns every declaration span of a function in a file (prototypes and definition), used by the splice, the frame check, the trust-boundary check (each span with its own formals), `has_function_contract` and proof carry; keep `contract_only` for a header definition; add the probe cases as tests.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`c40bd9c`). The run agent's hot patch was not kept. One helper, `acsl_ast.function_declaration_spans()`, returns every declaration of a function (prototypes and the definition, each with its kind), and every reader of a header function's contract uses it:
  - `splice_prover_changes` accepts changes confined to the union of those spans (prototype only, definition only, both, a header with only the definition), refuses any other change, reports a concurrent change of a span as `diverged from agent base snapshot`, and keeps the base body under `contract_only`.
  - `entry_trust_boundary_errors` checks every declaration with that declaration's own formals, so `requires pMe->len <= 64;` on the definition of an untrusted entry is refused (the bypass the review showed).
  - The frame check, `has_function_contract`, `function_contract_text` and [`../../scripts/proof_carry.py`](../../scripts/proof_carry.py) read all declarations. Proof-carry keys of such functions change once (old entries miss, never a false hit).
  - `wp_scheduler.touches_module_header` counts a header that holds only the definition (`d4db9a5`).
  - Checked on the six run-6 scratch trees: four are accepted; the two `UsefulOutBuf_Reset` trees pass the scope check and are refused by the new `\aligned` check. Checked in `framac-analysis:latest`: a contract on the definition only is accepted; contracts on both declarations are merged with the `assigns` union warning.
  - Tests: `tests/test_header_decl_spans.py`, `tests/test_proof_carry_header_decls.py`.

### Symptom: A Wrapper Function Is Projected as `assigns \nothing` Although All Its Writes Happen in a Callee, and Its Callee Has No Contract Yet (`soundness.ok=false: Neither code nor specification for function ...`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both gaps (wrapper frame projection, callee-contract ordering) fixed in a313cb9, fa1040e, 4ed4057, d4db9a5, 1c63cc6, d178996.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15, `UsefulOutBuf_AppendData` (`inc/qcbor/UsefulBuf.h:2528-2534`, calls `UsefulOutBuf_AppendUsefulBuf`, defined in `src/UsefulBuf.c`). The prover proved both RTE goals (termination, exit condition) but the projected frame goal `assigns \nothing` stayed Unknown; `run_wp_function.py` also set `soundness.ok=false` because the callee had neither code nor a contract in the proof unit, so Frama-C generated `Neither code nor specification for function UsefulOutBuf_AppendUsefulBuf, generating default exits, assigns and terminates`.
- **Cause:** two independent gaps. (1) `frame_skeleton` (source `ast`) projects only the function's **direct** writes, so a pure forwarder whose writes all happen in a same-module out-of-line callee is projected as `assigns \nothing`; `workspace_overlay.check_frame_preserved` then demands that clause, and the only way to "prove" it is to specify the callee as writing nothing, which is false. The prover refused. (2) The scheduler dispatched the wrapper before its callee had any contract, so WP trusted a default `assigns \everything` and flagged the unit unsound; the caller cannot become eligible until the callee is proven and merged.
- **Fix:** **Pipeline gap (both parts fixed, see the two Status bullets below).** Suggested: project a wrapper's frame from the union of its callees' write sets when it has no direct writes (or drop the projected clause and rely on the callee's contract), and gate a caller on every same-module callee having a contract before it is dispatched (the skill states this for internal static callees; this callee is a non-static out-of-line function in the same link unit and was not gated). Workaround in run-6: the stalled result is accepted (a breaker is spawned, non-blocking); `UsefulOutBuf_AppendData` is re-queued after `UsefulOutBuf_AppendUsefulBuf` merges, and its `assigns` is then recomputed from the callee contract.
- **Post-run review (2026-11-15): both gaps are wider than one wrapper.**
  - (1) [`../../scripts/frame_skeleton.py`](../../scripts/frame_skeleton.py) (lines 418-419) appends `assigns \nothing;` whenever the function has no direct formal or global write, without looking at the record's `calls`. In run-6's `workspace/reports/facts/libqcbor_a.json`, 567 of 755 functions have a skeleton that is exactly `assigns \nothing`, and 504 of those make at least one call. For 475 of the 504, some transitive callee has a projected direct write or is an indirect or external call, so `\nothing` cannot be assumed true (an upper bound: whether the callee's write lands in caller-visible memory depends on the arguments). `check_frame_preserved` then requires every one of those candidates to list `\nothing`. Proposed fix: emit `\nothing` only when the function has no direct write and no call; with calls and no direct write, emit no assigns entry and set the skeleton `partial` with a note naming the callees.
  - (2) `_compute_deps` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) adds a callee dependency only when the callee has a `prove` task, and `_View` counts `blocked` and `skipped` callees as satisfied. So a caller is released when its callee's task ended without a contract (`UsefulOutBuf_Init` after `UsefulOutBuf_Reset` was blocked `merge_failed`; `UsefulOutBuf_AppendData` after `AppendUsefulBuf` was skipped as a crash candidate) or when the callee never had a task (`IEEE754_DoubleToSmaller`: `DoubleToSingle` and `SingleToHalf` only have candidate units). The callees were not "still pending", as the run report says. On the final run-6 schedule, 11 of the 20 blocked provers had a callee without a contract, and 44 of the 295 pending provers have a callee with no contract and no open task. Parking every such caller is not an option: it would park 168 of the 295 pending tasks transitively. Proposed fix: for a callee without a contract whose task is terminal or absent and which is not a crash candidate, the scheduler opens a `negotiate:<callee>` request itself and the caller waits on it; for a crash-candidate callee, dispatch the caller last and name the contract-less callees in its brief.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation), gap (1), the projection:** **fixed** (`a313cb9`). `frame_skeleton.compute(record, write_free_callees)` projects `assigns \nothing` only when the function writes nothing directly and every call reaches a module function that writes nothing either, transitively (`function_facts.write_free_functions`, a greatest fixpoint by kernel name, so recursion is handled). An indirect call or a callee outside the module blocks it; there is no pure-libc allowlist, so a caller of `strlen` becomes `partial`. Otherwise the skeleton has no assigns entry, `status: partial` and a note naming the callees, and the brief says "no clause is required for this function". `FACTS_SCHEMA` is 4: facts of a resumed run must be regenerated (`facts-<mod>`). Applied to run-6's stored records: 92 functions keep `\nothing`, 475 become `partial`. Test: `tests/test_frame_skeleton_calls.py`.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation), gap (2), callee ordering:** **fixed** (`fa1040e`, `4ed4057`, `d4db9a5`). `wp_scheduler._contract_less_callees` handles a pending prover whose callee has no contract the caller can see and no open task: when a negotiation may run, the scheduler files a request itself (`origin: scheduler`, reason `caller needs a contract`, the callee's frame skeleton as `needed_clause`) and the caller waits on `negotiate:<callee>` under the round caps; otherwise (crash candidate, out of scope, negotiation ended without a contract) the caller is tagged `callees_without_contract`, dispatched after the untagged ready tasks, and its brief gets a "Calls Without a Contract" section. `callee_has_contract` no longer accepts a `.c`-only contract for a caller in another file. On a copy of the run-6 schedule the pending provers released without a contract drop from 9 to 0; 37 wait on a negotiation and 12 are tagged. Tests: `tests/test_wp_scheduler_run6.py`, `tests/test_brief_result_contract.py::test_prover_brief_names_the_callees_that_have_no_contract`. Not run on a live schedule.
- **Follow-up (2026-11-15, `1c63cc6`):** the WP hint for a callee without a contract (`derive_wp_diagnostic_hints`) said "a contract is needed on the prototype" and always sent the prover to `strengthening_requests`, against both the declaration rule and the "Calls Without a Contract" section. It now says "on one of its declarations" and excludes callees the brief lists in that section. The prover, architect, breaker and verifier briefs and the skills describe the new behaviour (`d178996`).

### Symptom: `--wrap-lines` Cannot Waive a `signed_downcast` Goal, so an Implementation-Defined Narrowing Makes `all_proven` Unreachable
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. --waive-signed-downcast added in ac80ab8 (replay 1340d6e, ledger fallback f790d15).
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15, `IEEE754_SingleToDouble` (`src/ieee754.c:282`, `(int)(uSingleBiasedExponent - SINGLE_EXPONENT_BIAS)` where the subtraction is `uint64_t`). The unsigned wrap on the same line is declared with `--wrap-lines` and the function proves 8 of 11 goals, including all four `l2_termination` goals, but two `rte,signed_downcast` goals stay Unknown and `--wrap-lines` skips every goal kind except `unsigned_overflow` (`check_declared_wraps`), so the prover has no flag or annotation that can close them; `run_wp_function.py` always passes `-warn-signed-downcast`.
- **Cause:** the conversion is implementation-defined (the `uint64_t` subtraction wraps and truncating to `int` yields the intended `-127` for biased exponent 0..126), which is why no runtime check fires, but RTE still emits a `signed_downcast` obligation. The pipeline's waiver mechanism covers only unsigned modular arithmetic. The neighbouring expression at `src/ieee754.c:382` (same file) writes `(int32_t)uSingleBiasedExponent - SINGLE_EXPONENT_BIAS` without the wrap, which suggests the target-side fix is a cast reformulation rather than a waiver.
- **Fix:** **Pipeline gap (fixed, see Status below).** Suggested: extend `--wrap-lines` (and `check_declared_wraps`) to accept `signed_downcast` on a declared line with a justification recorded in `unsigned_wraps`/`reduced_checks`, the same way it accepts `unsigned_overflow`; or record such a line as a `needs_validation` transformation rather than leaving the unit unproven. Workaround in run-6: none; the unit is reported with these two goals and the caller/callee frame request separately.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`ac80ab8`, replay `1340d6e`, ledger fallback `f790d15`). New flag `run_wp_function.py --waive-signed-downcast`, accepted only with `--no-warn-unsigned` and `--wrap-lines` / `--wrap-code-lines`: the proof run omits `-warn-signed-downcast` and records the reduced check `-warn-signed-downcast OFF on declared wrap lines`; `check_declared_wraps` re-runs with `-wp-prop=unsigned_overflow,signed_downcast` and fails the function on any such goal that is not fully proved outside the declared lines. `workspace_overlay.infer_prover_wp_flags` replays the flag for the parent check, the sweep and the mutation check. Checked in `framac-analysis:latest` on `(int)(e - 127)`: waived on its line gives `all_proven`; an undeclared line stays unproven. Tests: `tests/test_unsigned_wraps.py`, `tests/test_signed_downcast_waiver_replay.py`.

### Symptom: The Merge Refuses a Fully Proven Contract With `parent WP check on merged tree returned status='unproven_goals_remain'`, the Parent Log Showing `[Unknown] ... (CVC5) (Cached) (Model)`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Counter-example pass no longer decides the merge verdict (0490c54); --cache-dir off fixed in 5a333e2; upstream issue #2811 filed with MR !42.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15 (external supervisor), on two header functions:
  - `negotiate-usefuloutbuf_reset` at 05:30. Its own run proved 11 of 11 goals. The parent log `agents/negotiate-usefuloutbuf_reset/scratch/parent_wp/wp_UsefulOutBuf_Reset.log` shows `[wp] [Unknown] typed_UsefulOutBuf_Reset_ensures_keep_UB_ptr (Qed 2ms) (CVC5) (Cached) (Model)`, `Proved goals: 10 / 11`.
  - `prove-usefulinputbuf_geterror-2` at 04:22, with the same signature on `ensures keep_buffer`.
  - Both goals are postconditions saying a `void *` field keeps its value (`pMe->UB.ptr == \old(pMe->UB.ptr)`).
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug (fixed, see Fix below; the branch is merged):**
  - The parent check in [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) runs `run_wp_function.py --counter-examples`, and `run_wp_on_function` passed `-wp-counter-examples` into the same WP run whose statuses decide the merge. The prover's own run does not use the flag.
  - Under that flag, Frama-C 34 WP builds a **weaker goal**: it loses the frame equality between the memory before and after the call. Seen with `-wp-print` on a minimal fixture:
    - normal goal: `Prove: true.`;
    - counter-example goal: `Prove: Mptr_0[a] = Mptr_1[a].`, with nothing relating `Mptr_0` and `Mptr_1`.
  - Alt-Ergo times out, and Z3 and CVC5 return `Unknown` with a spurious model.
  - The WP cache is **not** involved:
    - a fresh cache dir reproduces it;
    - `(Cached)` appears even on a first run;
    - a plain run on the cache written by the counter-example run still proves.
  - A second defect: `_MODEL_GOAL_RE` did not match Frama-C 34's `(Qed 2ms) (CVC5) (Cached) (Model)` tag order, so `counter_example_goals` was always empty.
  - [`../../scripts/mutation_check.py`](../../scripts/mutation_check.py) always passes `counter_examples=True`, so it undercounted equivalent mutants for the same reason.
  - Effect: a correct contract is blocked or sent back to negotiation, and its callers stay blocked (`UsefulOutBuf_Init`).
  - Upstream draft: [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-wp-counter-examples-drops-frame.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-wp-counter-examples-drops-frame.md) (Issue 19, filed as [pub/frama-c#2811](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/work_items/2811), merge request [!42](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/merge_requests/42)), reproduced on `34.0~beta`.
- **Fix:** **fixed**: branch `sup/QCBOR-wp-counterexample-false-unknown` (`811dbe8`, `bcd3d74`), merged into `phase1-remediation` with F6 as `0490c54` on 2026-11-15 after QCBOR run-6 ended.
  - With `counter_examples=True`, `run_wp_on_function` first proves without the flag, retries included, and takes status, counts, CSV and SARIF from that pass.
  - Only when goals fail does it run a `_cex`-suffixed pass with `-wp-counter-examples` and `-wp-cache none`, with no retries, SARIF, session or budget charge.
  - It keeps models only for goals the proof pass also failed. `counter_example_pass.models_dropped_for_proved_goals` records how many were dropped.
  - `parse_model_goals` accepts Frama-C 34's tags.
  - Tests: `tests/test_run_wp_counter_examples.py` (3 tests, all failing before the fix) and `tests/test_tool_parsing.py::test_wp_counter_example_model_goals_parse`.
  - Reproduced in the analysis image under run tag `sup-fix-cex`: before the fix, `cex_fresh` returned `unproven_goals_remain 7 1`; after it, `all_proven 8 0`.
  - Run-6 itself ran without the fix until its end. Its merged `UsefulOutBuf_Reset` contract lacks the true `keep_UB_ptr` postcondition because of this bug: the second negotiator dropped it to get past the check.
- **Related open gap:** `run_wp_function.py --cache-dir off` does not disable WP's cache. With no `-wp-cache` mode passed, WP still uses its default cache.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation), `--cache-dir off`:** **fixed** (`5a333e2`). `run_wp_on_function` passes `-wp-cache none` whenever the WP cache is not in use, with or without `-wp-session`; before, a run with the cache off and no session passed no mode and WP used its default cache (`[Cache] found:9` in run-6's cast-probe logs), and a run with a session always passed `-wp-cache update`. The sweep and the cast probe (`--cache-dir off`) now really run uncached. Tests: `tests/test_tool_sizing.py::test_wp_cache_dir_off_explicit_and_forced_off`, `::test_wp_session_never_uses_the_default_cache_when_the_cache_is_off`.

### Symptom: Parent WP Re-check Reports `acsl_syntax_error` for a Missing Header (`failed to run: gcc -E`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Preprocess failure reported as analysis_error (5166333 via 0490c54), --cppflags guidance (4ad9820), merge-agent sidecar inputs and reason in rejection (0ad56f2), mutation classification (39151db), dead check removed (9aef74a).
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15, 06:50 (external supervisor).
  - The run agent ran `workspace_overlay.py merge-agent --role prover --agent-id prove-qcbordecode_private_doubleconformance --file src/qcbor_main_decode.c --function QCBORDecode_Private_DoubleConformance --allow-partial-wp` by hand, without `--cppflags` or `--module`.
  - `agents/prove-qcbordecode_private_doubleconformance/scratch/parent_wp/wp_QCBORDecode_Private_DoubleConformance.log` shows `[kernel] User Error: failed to run: gcc -E -C -I. ...` and `qcbor_main_decode.c:36:10: fatal error: qcbor/qcbor_main_decode.h: No such file or directory`.
  - The JSON said `status: acsl_syntax_error`. The merge said only `parent partial WP check rejected (status='acsl_syntax_error', sound_ok=False, assigns_verified=True)`, so the run agent concluded that its proven contract was malformed ACSL.
- **Cause:** `_folded_syntax_errors` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) returned `kernel_parse_errors(log)` for every non-zero kernel exit before WP. `kernel_parse_errors` includes preprocessing messages, so a build-configuration error was reported as an ACSL syntax error, with ACSL repair hints.
- **Fix:** **fixed**: branch `sup/QCBOR-wp-preprocess-error-status` (`5166333`, built on F5's `bcd3d74`), merged into `phase1-remediation` as `0490c54` on 2026-11-15.
  - `_preprocess_failure` recognises the kernel's `User Error: failed to run:` line and reports `analysis_error`:
    - `reason` is `preprocessing_failed: <first cpp error>`;
    - the messages go in `preprocess_errors`;
    - a hint says to pass the module's `--cppflags` or `--module` and to leave the contract unchanged.
  - The scheduler now retries once, then blocks with that reason.
  - [`../../scripts/mutation_check.py`](../../scripts/mutation_check.py) `_failure_kind` still names these mutants `preprocessing_failed`.
  - Tests: `tests/test_tool_sizing.py::test_a_preprocessor_failure_is_an_analysis_error_not_an_acsl_syntax_error` and `::test_an_acsl_annotation_error_still_reports_acsl_syntax_error`, and `tests/test_mutation_check.py::test_a_preprocessing_analysis_error_is_named_and_leaves_the_function_unscored`.
- **Correction (`4ad9820`):** at 07:14 the run agent retried with `merge-agent ... --module libqcbor_a`, and the parent check again preprocessed with `-I.` only (`state/ma2.json`, same `fatal error`). In `merge-agent`, `--module` only resolves the header (`_module_header_flags`); the parent WP check gets the raw `--cppflags`. The F6 hint had offered `--module` as the fix. It now names `--cppflags` with the module sidecar's value, and `tests/test_tool_sizing.py` asserts that.
- **Pipeline bug** (fixed in `0ad56f2`, see Status below; at the time in files with uncommitted run hot patches, so not fixed then):
  - [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) `merge-agent` defaults `--cppflags` to `-I.` and `--module` to None, and `--module` does not supply the module's include paths to the parent WP check. `prepare` and `batch` default `--cppflags` to None. A manual merge call therefore silently loses the module's include paths.
  - The merge rejection message prints only `status`, not the report's `reason`.
- **Also open:**
  - `mutation_check.py`'s COINS duality (line ~478) maps every `analysis_error`, including a preprocessing failure, to `prover_limited`.
  - The dead `check_acsl_syntax()` in `run_wp_function.py` would still mislabel the same log.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** the open bullets are **fixed**. `merge-agent` defaults `--cppflags`, `--sources` and `--ldflags` to None and `workspace_overlay.single_merge_build_inputs` takes them from the module's merged sidecar (explicit flags win); both parent-WP rejection messages append the report's `reason` and its first error line (`0ad56f2`, `tests/test_merge_agent_single_path.py`). `mutation_check.classify_open_unit` maps `preprocessing_failed` and `unsigned_wrap_check_failed` to `unknown` instead of `prover_limited` (`39151db`); the dead `check_acsl_syntax()` is removed (`9aef74a`); the preprocessing hint no longer says that `merge-agent --module` ignores the sidecar flags (`f790d15`).

### Symptom: The Residual Census Reports Every Unproved Goal Under `unproved_without_prover_data`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Per-prover verdicts now parsed from -wp-status and consumed by the census (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** development of the caller-sufficient contracts plan, step 5 (2026-11-17), no target run. Measured in `framac-analysis:latest` on small probe files with `-wp-prover alt-ergo,z3,cvc5`.
- **Cause:** WP 34 merges the provers' verdicts into one entry per unproved goal in `-wp-report-json`. The entry lists one prover and a time of 0, for example only Alt-Ergo with `Timeout` although Z3 and CVC5 also ran. `unproven_goal_costs[].provers` and `verdict_kind` therefore carry the merged result, not each prover's. The plan assumed per-prover verdicts were recorded. On the pipeline's default `--provers alt-ergo,z3,cvc5`, no unproved goal can be attributed to a prover.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/wp_residual_census.py`](../../scripts/wp_residual_census.py) (`prover_verdicts`, commit `d925d29` on `feat/caller-sufficient-contracts`) first fell back to `unproved_without_prover_data` on multi-prover runs (`tests/test_wp_residual_census.py::test_verdicts_by_prover_only_for_single_prover_runs`).
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py), [`../../scripts/wp_residual_census.py`](../../scripts/wp_residual_census.py)):** `run_wp_on_function` now passes `-wp-status` (whenever `not dump_goal`, since `-wp-print` already prints the same `Prover ... returns ...` lines and `-wp-status` suppresses `Valid` dumps), `parse_wp_status_per_prover` parses per-prover verdicts and attaches `per_prover_verdicts` (and `per_prover_time_ms`) to `unproven_goal_costs`, and `wp_residual_census.prover_verdicts` consumes `per_prover_verdicts` on multi-prover runs (falling back to `without_prover_data` only for legacy reports). Verified end-to-end in `framac-analysis:latest` (`unproved_without_prover_data: 0` across `alt-ergo,z3,cvc5`). Tests: `test_parse_wp_status_per_prover_real_framac34_output`, `test_parse_wp_status_per_prover_handles_par_reordering_and_fails_closed` in [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py), `test_verdicts_by_prover_uses_per_prover_verdicts_on_multi_prover_runs` in [`../../tests/test_wp_residual_census.py`](../../tests/test_wp_residual_census.py).

### Symptom: The Contract Refinement Probe Could Not Find a Header Named by a Relative `-I`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. contract_refinement.absolute_path_flags added; branch feat/caller-sufficient-contracts merged.
- **Seen on:** review of the caller-sufficient contracts plan before hand-off (2026-11-17). Found by reading the code, then covered by a container test. No run showed it, because the code had not reached a run.
- **Cause:** `contract_refinement.run_probe` runs Frama-C with its working directory set to the probe's temporary directory. A run's flags name include directories relative to the run directory (`-I. -Iwork/include`), so from the temporary directory the preprocessor would look in the wrong place for a header included with angle brackets. The step-1 integration cases used a header beside the source, which a quoted include finds without `-I`, so they did not exercise it.
- **Fix:** **fixed** (uncommitted when written, then in the commit after `546e226` on `feat/caller-sufficient-contracts`). `contract_refinement.absolute_path_flags` makes the relative paths of `-I`, `-iquote`, `-isystem`, `-idirafter`, `-include` and `-imacros` absolute against the caller's working directory before the run. Tests: `tests/test_contract_refinement.py::test_relative_include_paths_are_made_absolute` (unit) and `::test_real_pipeline_uses_on_a_run_layout` (integration, `framac-analysis:latest`: header under `work/include/lib/`, `-I. -Iwork/include`, real `apply_verdict_limits` and `refinement_check`).

### Symptom: The Frame-Only Probe Is `undecided` With `[kernel:annot-error] contract_probe.c:6: Failure: unexpected token ''`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. contract_refinement._annotated drops an empty annotation; merged with the caller-sufficient contracts branch.
- **Seen on:** libmnl `framac-run-1-results`, 2026-11-17, read-only pass of `contract_refinement.py frame-only` over the 31 `all_proven` functions, on `src/nlmsg.c#mnl_nlmsg_batch_is_empty` (`"status": "undecided"`, `"reason": "the probe did not run to the end (rc=1): ..."`).
- **Cause:** the contract is split: `requires` and `assigns` on the header prototype, the only `ensures` on the definition in the source. `build_probe` writes one declaration per piece, and the frame-only reading strips the `ensures` of the pieces it declares `f__given` with, so the definition's piece became an empty `/*@ */`, which Frama-C refuses.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on `feat/caller-sufficient-contracts`: `contract_refinement._annotated` leaves the annotation comment out when a piece has no clause left. Test: `tests/test_contract_refinement.py::test_frame_only_probe_of_a_split_contract_has_no_empty_annotation`. Checked on the same run: the function is now decided (its `ensures` does not follow from the frame).

### Symptom: `test_real_wp_verdicts[defensive_branch]` Fails in the Analysis Image With Vacuity `inconclusive`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. classify_vacuity_log counts raw per-prover doomed lines (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** `framac-analysis:latest` (image `b33b01633608`), 2026-11-17, while running the integration tests of the caller-sufficient contracts plan in a fresh container.
  - `tests/test_verdict_limits.py:445`: `assert res["limits"]["vacuity"]["status"] == vacuity`, `AssertionError: assert 'inconclusive' == 'consistent'`. The WP status is `all_proven` (4 proved, 0 unproved).
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in `classify_vacuity_log` ([`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)), diagnosed in `framac-analysis:latest` on 2026-11-20. With multiple configured provers (`alt-ergo,z3,cvc5`), Frama-C 34 emits `[wp] [Failed] (Doomed) typed_f_wp_smoke_dead_code_s3 (Qed)` once per prover (3 lines) and adds every occurrence to the `Smoke Tests: k / n` denominator (`2 / 5`, so `n - k = 3`), while `-wp-report-json` lists 1 failed dead-code goal. `classify_vacuity_log` deduplicated `dead` to a set (`len(dead) == 1`) before computing `(n - k) - len(dead) == 2`, falsely marking it `inconclusive`.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py), [`../../tests/test_verdict_limits.py`](../../tests/test_verdict_limits.py)):** `classify_vacuity_log` now matches `n - k` against raw recognised `_DOOMED_DEAD_RE` occurrences while still reporting unique `dead_goals`, and still fails closed on any unrecognised `(Doomed)` smoke goal (`_ANY_DOOMED_RE`). Verified in `framac-analysis:latest`: all 6 `test_real_wp_verdicts` cases pass (`FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1`). Tests: `test_dead_defensive_code_repeated_per_prover_is_consistent`, `test_unrecognised_doomed_smoke_goal_fails_closed`, and `test_real_wp_verdicts` in [`../../tests/test_verdict_limits.py`](../../tests/test_verdict_limits.py).

### Symptom: The Contract Refinement Probe Returns `undecided` With "no declaration with named parameters"
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Token-based declarator rewriting handles K&R and nested declarators (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** development of the caller-sufficient contracts plan, step 1 (2026-11-17), unit tests on synthetic sources, no target run.
- **Cause:** `acsl_ast.contract_declaration_spans` found no declaration for a K&R definition (`int f(a) int a; { ... }`) or for a nested declarator (a function returning a function pointer, `int (*f(int x))(int)`). `contract_refinement.read_function_contract` then had no piece with a parameter list, and `limits.contract_shape` returned `unknown`. A variadic prototype and an unnamed formal are refused on purpose: the probe body cannot forward the arguments.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py), [`../../scripts/contract_refinement.py`](../../scripts/contract_refinement.py)):** consolidated declaration scanning in [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py) (`_iter_declaration_spans`, `_match_declaration_at`, `_find_body_brace_offset`, `declaration_param_names`) and replaced regex head parsing in [`../../scripts/contract_refinement.py`](../../scripts/contract_refinement.py) with token-based declarator rewriting (`_find_function_declarator`, `_unwind_nested_declarator`, `_parse_kr_declarations`, `_top_level_storage_spans`). K&R definitions (including out-of-order, `register`, multi-declarator, default-`int`, struct, and function-pointer parameters) and nested declarators (`int (*f(int x))(int)`, `int (*row(int i))[4]`, `signal`-style callbacks) now synthesize valid ANSI `f__given` / `f__probe` probes and are decided by `compare`, `frame-only`, and `limits.contract_shape`; variadic, unnamed-parameter, and empty-paren declarations stay `undecided` on purpose. Verified in `framac-analysis:latest` (`test_real_probe_kr_definitions`, `test_real_probe_nested_declarators`). Tests: [`../../tests/test_contract_refinement.py`](../../tests/test_contract_refinement.py) and [`../../tests/test_contract_clause_changes.py`](../../tests/test_contract_clause_changes.py).

### Symptom: Mutation Scores of a Function Proved Under `--wp-model Bytes` or With Reduced Checks Are Computed Under Default `Typed`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Mutants replay the proof's model and checks (39151db).
- **Seen on:** code reading after QCBOR run-6, 2026-11-15 (not observed as a wrong score in the run).
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug (fixed, see Status below):** `_default_runner` in [`../../scripts/mutation_check.py`](../../scripts/mutation_check.py) calls `run_wp_on_function(c_file, fn, out_dir, cppflags=..., counter_examples=True)` with the default model and default checks. It never replays the flags the proof used (`infer_prover_wp_flags`), so the mutants of a function proved under `Bytes`, or with declared unsigned wraps, are judged under a configuration in which the original contract does not prove either.
- **Fix:** none yet. Proposed: replay the proof's `wp_model` and `reduced_checks` for the mutants.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`39151db`). `mutation_check.run_mutation_check` replays `workspace_overlay.infer_prover_wp_flags` through `wp_options_from_flags` (model, unsigned, invalid-pointer and signed-downcast checks) and records `wp_flags` / `wp_options` per function. Functions with no recorded flags now run their mutants with `warn_unsigned_downcast=False`, the parent check's default. The mutants no longer request counter-examples (nothing read the models, and each failing mutant paid a second WP pass). Tests: `tests/test_mutation_check.py::test_mutants_replay_the_model_and_checks_the_proof_used` and neighbours.

### Symptom: `breaker:*` Tasks Stay `pending` for Hours While `prove:*` Tasks Keep Being Dispatched
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Breakers get a reserved dispatch slot (5124c6b).
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15 (external supervisor). The first breakers appeared at about 04:40 and there were 12 `pending` by 05:30. None was dispatched, although every `step` handed out new prove, negotiate and verify tasks. The run agent never mentions breakers in its reasoning, and `step` never offers one.
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug (fixed, see Status below):** this is how the scheduler ranks tasks; the model did not skip them.
  - `_ensure_breaker` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) creates a breaker with `deps: []` and height 0, so `_View.ready` accepts it at once.
  - `cmd_next` sorts all ready agent tasks with `_priority_key` = `(shell_leaf, -height, -eva_unknowns, id)` and fills the `recommend().max_agents` slots (5-9 here) from the top.
  - A read-only re-ranking of `state/wp_schedule.json` at 05:30 put the 12 breakers at positions 92-104 of about 107 ready tasks. Every ready prover with height > 0 ranks above them, and so do most height-0 leaves with more Eva unknowns.
  - A breaker therefore runs only after the prove queue has almost drained. That contradicts the skill's "non-blocking Adversarial Breakers on stalled proofs": a stalled proof's crash hunt waits behind about 300 provers. A real crash it could find (for example in `IEEE754_DoubleToInt`, which has 18 Eva unknowns) is also delayed until the end of Phase 3, and may never arrive if the run stops early.
- **Fix:** none yet. `wp_scheduler.py` holds uncommitted hot patches from the same run, so no fixer was dispatched. Suggested fix: rank breakers by their stalled prover's height plus one, or reserve a slot fraction for breakers (for example `max(1, slots // 4)` whenever a breaker is ready). Add a test that a ready breaker is handed out within one `next` call when height > 0 provers are ready too.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`5124c6b`). `cmd_next` reserves `max(1, budget // 4)` slots for ready breakers; unused reserved slots go back to the normal order, and without a ready breaker the order is unchanged. Test: `tests/test_wp_scheduler_run6.py::test_a_ready_breaker_gets_a_reserved_slot_ahead_of_taller_provers`.

### Symptom: `run_wp_function.py --counter-examples` Starts a Full `frama-c -wp-counter-examples` Run Although the Prover Budget Is Exhausted
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Model pass skipped when the prover budget is exhausted; verdict JSON written before the model pass (f3ef60f).
- **Seen on:** post-run review of the F5 fix (`811dbe8`, `bcd3d74`, merged as `0490c54`), 2026-11-15, on the host with a synthetic exhausted `.prover_budget.json`: HEAD launches one run, `40c7ed7` launched none.
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug (introduced by F5; fixed, see Status below):** `_prove_then_collect_models` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) starts the model pass whenever goals remain unproven. It does not check `budget_exhausted`, and the model pass sets `ignore_prover_budget: True`. Only an agent that passes `--counter-examples` without `--ignore-prover-budget` inside `agents/<id>/scratch` is affected; the parent check passes `--ignore-prover-budget`.
- **Fix:** none yet. Proposed: skip the model pass when the verdict pass reports the budget exhausted.
- **Same review, F5 itself is confirmed on the real case:** the `UsefulOutBuf_Reset` parent check was replayed in `framac-analysis:latest` on a copy of `agents/negotiate-usefuloutbuf_reset/scratch/work`. With `40c7ed7` scripts it returns `unproven_goals_remain` (9/10) with the run-6 line `[Unknown] typed_UsefulOutBuf_Reset_ensures_keep_UB_ptr (Qed 2ms) (CVC5) (Cached) (Model)`; with `4ad9820` it returns `all_proven` (10/10). With one postcondition falsified, HEAD reports that goal as unproven with a model and drops the spurious model on `keep_UB_ptr`. Remaining costs: each run with a failing goal pays a second full uncached pass (17 s on this function); the parent check in `workspace_overlay.py` has no wall timeout; the report JSON is written last, so a kill during the model pass loses the verdict.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** the gap is **fixed** (`f3ef60f`). `collect_counter_example_models` skips the model pass when the verdict pass reports `budget_exhausted` (`counter_example_pass.status: skipped`). `main()` writes the verdict JSON (`counter_example_pass.status: pending`) before the model pass starts and rewrites it afterwards, so a kill during the model pass no longer loses the verdict. The CLI now runs the model pass after the declared-wrap and vacuity checks. Tests: `tests/test_run_wp_counter_examples.py::test_no_model_pass_when_the_prover_budget_is_exhausted`, `::test_main_writes_the_verdict_before_the_model_pass`.

### Symptom: An Annotation With a Missing Closing `*/` Is Reported as `analysis_error` / `preprocessing_failed: <file>:1:1: error: unterminated comment`, With the Hint to Leave the Contract Unchanged
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. An unterminated annotation comment stays acsl_syntax_error (2dfadcf).
- **Seen on:** post-run review of the F6 fix (`5166333`, `4ad9820`), 2026-11-15, in `framac-analysis:latest`. Before F6 the same file was `acsl_syntax_error`. Five other annotation-level preprocessing errors (macro arity, `#if` in an annotation, a missing header included from an annotation, an unterminated string in `//@ assert`) keep `acsl_syntax_error`, because Frama-C prints `Can't preprocess annotation` for them.
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug (introduced by F6; fixed, see Status below):** `_PREPROCESS_FAILED_RE` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) matches every `[kernel] ... User Error: failed to run:` line, and an unterminated annotation comment makes the main `gcc -E` fail. The prover gets the build-configuration hint for its own typo, and the scheduler spends one of its two analysis-error retries.
- **Fix:** none yet. Proposed: keep `acsl_syntax_error` when the first preprocessor error is `unterminated comment`.
- **Related, by reading:** a deterministic `preprocessing_failed` is still retried once with a fresh agent (`MAX_ANALYSIS_ERRORS = 2`), and on the recheck path it reopens the caller's prover although the contract is not at fault.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`2dfadcf`). `_preprocess_failure` returns None when the first preprocessor error is `unterminated comment`, so the run is `acsl_syntax_error` with the preprocessor's `file:line` first in `syntax_check.errors` and a hint naming the missing `*/`. A missing header stays `analysis_error` / `preprocessing_failed`. Checked in `framac-analysis:latest` on both cases. A plain C comment without `*/` is classified the same way (a source typo too). Test: `tests/test_tool_sizing.py::test_an_annotation_missing_its_closing_comment_is_an_acsl_syntax_error`.

### Symptom: Every Unsound or Aborted WP Report Carries the Hint "Indirect call through a function pointer without a 'calls' annotation"
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Indirect-call hint only on real indirect-call diagnostics (2026-11-20).

- **Seen on:** run-6 remediation, 2026-11-15, a Docker check of `run_wp_function.py` in `framac-analysis:latest` on a function that writes a local union and has no indirect call.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug (fixed, see Fix). `derive_wp_diagnostic_hints` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) added the hint whenever `soundness.skipped_rte_guards` was non-empty. Frama-C 34 always prints `Skipped RTE guards: ... (\valid_function not supported)`, so that list is never empty.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)):** `derive_wp_diagnostic_hints` now emits the indirect-call hint only when actual indirect-call diagnostics appear (`Missing 'calls'`, `Unknown callee`, `Through function pointer`, `No 'calls' specification`, or `indirect call present without '//@ calls'`), matching `scan_wp_log_soundness`. Tests: `test_wp_hints_indirect_call_not_triggered_by_valid_function_skipped_rte_only` and `test_wp_hints_indirect_call_triggered_by_actual_indirect_call_diagnostics` in [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py).

### Symptom: `wp_scheduler.py status` Changes the Ledger and the Request Store Although Its Sync Is "In Memory Only"
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. cmd_status no longer mutates ledger or request store (2026-11-20).

- **Seen on:** code reading during the run-6 remediation, 2026-11-15.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug (fixed). `cmd_status` ran `_sync` without saving the schedule, but `_sync` itself moved ledger units (`_park_shell_unverifiable_units`, `_park_quick_off_chain`) and resolved requests (`_resolve_request` when a pending negotiate task was skipped). So `status` could resolve a request whose negotiate task stayed `pending` in the saved schedule.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py)):** gated `_park_quick_off_chain`, `_park_shell_unverifiable_units`, and `_close_negotiation_unrun` (`_resolve_request`) behind `persist`, and ensured `_waiting_result_entry` parses `result.json` read-only in memory without write-back, so `cmd_status` (`_sync(persist=False)`) performs no disk mutations. Tests: `test_status_does_not_mutate_ledger_for_quick_off_chain_units`, `test_status_does_not_mutate_ledger_for_shell_unverifiable_wp_units`, `test_status_does_not_mutate_request_store_for_skippable_negotiate_task` in [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).

### Symptom: A False Goal Reads `[wp] [Timeout] typed_fast_unknown_ensures_fast_false (Qed 0.60ms) (Alt-Ergo)` With Three Provers, While CVC5 Alone Answers `[Unknown]`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Per-prover verdicts fixed pipeline-side (-wp-status parsing); the upstream feature request is filed, so the 'Not filed' statements are stale.

- **Seen on:** Tier 1 Step 0 (per-goal solver cost), 2026-11-16, toy probes in `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 34): `ensures \result > 50` on `0 <= x <= 100` (false), and Fermat's n=3 as an `assert` (true, too hard), `-wp-timeout 4`.
- **Cause:** **upstream Frama-C limitation**, not a pipeline bug. WP merges the provers' answers into one result and ranks a Timeout above an Unknown: z3 Timeout plus CVC5 Unknown is printed as one `[Timeout]` line naming one prover. The `-wp-report-json` report has the same single merged entry, with a time of `0` for every unproved goal (Why3 measured 0.01 s and 4.00 s). Under `-wp-cache update` the log also tags a result `(Cached)` on the run that first fills the cache, while the JSON's `cached` count only appears on a real cache hit. Only `-wp-msg-key prover` prints each prover's answer and time (`Why3 result for CVC5 1.1.2: Unknown (unknown + incomplete) (0.01s, 3415 steps)`), but those lines do not name their goal and interleave under `-wp-par`.
- **Fix:** **Pipeline gap, fixed on 2026-11-20 (see the Status below); recorded as first found:** [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) (Tier 1, `2a1ac0e` on branch `worktree-agent-tier1-goal-costs`) reads the JSON report, gives an unproved goal `time_ms: null`, and fires the "too large" hint only when one prover is configured. With the default three provers that hint never fires, and a merged Timeout may hide a quick Unknown. Getting per-prover answers would need either an upstream JSON field or a goal-attributed form of the `prover` messages. Tests: `tests/test_run_wp_function.py::test_goal_cost_hint_timeout_only_with_a_single_prover`.
- **Correction and upstream draft (2026-11-17):** the Cause above is wrong on one point. `-wp-status` also prints every prover's answer, under the name of its goal, so the answers can be had today by parsing that text. The JSON's `"time"` is in seconds where the WP manual says milliseconds, and the manual's definition of `"cached"` does not match the code. Drafted as package 40, a feature request without a patch, [`../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-wp-report-per-prover-results.md`](../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-wp-report-per-prover-results.md). Filed upstream as [#2829](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/work_items/2829), manual-only merge request [!57](https://git.frama-c.com/pub/frama-c/-/merge_requests/57).
- **Status (2026-11-20, pipeline side fixed, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py), [`../../scripts/wp_residual_census.py`](../../scripts/wp_residual_census.py)):** `run_wp_on_function` now passes `-wp-status` (when `not dump_goal`), parses per-prover verdicts into `unproven_goal_costs[].per_prover_verdicts`, and `goal_cost_hints` fires the multi-prover "ran out of time or steps" hint when all configured provers timed/stepped out while suppressing it when any prover returned `Unknown` (verified in `framac-analysis:latest` on `fast_unknown` vs `stepout_all`). Tests: `test_goal_cost_hints_multi_prover_with_per_prover_verdicts`, `test_run_wp_on_function_passes_wp_status_except_dump_goal` in [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py). Upstream package 40 is filed as #2829 (merge request !57).

### Symptom: `wp_residual_census.py` Reports `join: per_function_only` And Every Near-Miss Goal As `unjoined` Although The Reports Carry `unproven_goal_costs`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Census joins rows to costs by file and line (d97ae88).

- **Seen on:** merging Tier 1 (per-goal costs) and Tier 2a (residual census) on branch `integ/proof-papers`, 2026-11-16; reproduced with a probe in `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 34) that writes `-wp-report-csv` and `-wp-report-json` for the same function.
- **Cause:** **pipeline bug** in [`../../scripts/wp_residual_census.py`](../../scripts/wp_residual_census.py). It paired a report row with a goal cost when `unproven_goals[].annotation` equalled `unproven_goal_costs[].property`. The two never match: the CSV row's `property` column is the predicate text (`s + *(a + i) <= 2147483647`), while the JSON report's `property` is WP's property name (`f_assert_rte_signed_overflow_2`). The census and the cost fields were written in parallel against a guessed key, and the census tests used made-up reports where both fields held the same string. The join failed closed, so no wrong verdict was reported, but `would_be_all_proven_if_timeouts_closed` was the only verdict information left.
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `d97ae88` on `integ/proof-papers`. [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) (`goal_costs_from_report`) now copies the JSON report's `file` and `line` into each cost, and the census pairs a row with the costs at the same line and file name whose property name has the row's kind (`join_by_line`: `signed_overflow` against `assert_rte_signed_overflow`, `postcondition` against `ensures`, anchored so `signed_overflow` does not pair with `unsigned_overflow`). A row takes the verdict its costs agree on, `mixed` when they disagree, `unjoined` when there is no candidate or more rows than costs. Reports written before this change have no `line` on their costs and stay `unjoined`. Only RTE assertions, postconditions, loop invariants and user assertions were checked against real WP output; a callee's precondition or a behavior clause may still come out `unjoined`. Tests: `tests/test_wp_residual_census.py::test_join_on_a_real_report_pair` (rows and property names of the probe), `test_kind_matches`, `test_join_by_line_rules`.

### Symptom: The WP Baseline Report of Two Same-Named Functions in Different Files Shares One `workspace/reports/wp/<fn>.json`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Baseline WP report keyed by file and function (e454254) and readers made file-aware (2026-11-20).

- **Seen on:** reproduced on 2026-11-16 with the scheduler simulator of `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py` (`sim.build({"src/a/x.c#helper": ..., "src/b/y.c#helper": ...})`, a fake `run_cmd` writing one report per `--file`, `cmd_baseline(jobs=1)`). Found by code reading of `cmd_baseline` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py), not on a live target run (libmnl run-1 has no such name among its in-scope units); any C `static` helper defined in two files triggers it. The ledger stayed right: `ingest-wp` runs under the lock right after each report and matches units by the report's own `file`.
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug:** `cmd_baseline` keyed its work directory by file and function (`work/<slug>_<fn>`) but its report, `--out-json`, and its "already proven" test by function name only (`workspace/reports/wp/<fn>.json`). Four effects:
  - x.c proven, then y.c unproven: the only `helper.json` left is y.c's unproven report; x.c's proof is gone.
  - x.c unproven, then y.c proven: a second `baseline` listed both tasks under `already_proven_tasks` and ran neither, although `prove:src/a/x.c#helper` was still `pending`.
  - The pending task's `baseline.report` named the shared file, and `workspace_overlay.py prepare-agent` (which reads `reports/wp/<function>.json`) briefed its prover with the other function's goals.
  - With `--jobs` of 2 or more, both tasks deleted and re-read the same `out_json` while running (`_run_single`), so one task could read the other's report and be finished as `merged` on a proof that is not its own (by code reading, then confirmed by a barrier test).
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `fix/wp-baseline-report-key` (`e454254`), merged into `phase1-remediation`. The baseline report is now `workspace/reports/wp/work/<file slug>_<function>.json` (`workspace_overlay.baseline_wp_report_rel`; a path whose slug exceeds 40 characters keeps a hash, `wp_work_slug`), one level below the `*.json` globs of `ledger.py ingest-wp`, `mutation_check.py`, `benchmark.py` and `generate_report.py`. `baseline.report` records it. The name-only `reports/wp/<fn>.json` is copied from it only when neither the queue nor the ledger has the name in another file, the rule `_publish_canonical_wp` already applies to merges, so an unambiguous name behaves as before. The skip test, `load_function_wp_report` (the prepare-agent brief), `_prover_wp_config` and `_caller_report` accept a name-only or baseline report only when its `file` and `function` are the task's own (`wp_report_is_for`). Changed: `scripts/wp_scheduler.py`, `scripts/workspace_overlay.py`, [`../guides/agentic-swarm.md`](../guides/agentic-swarm.md). Tests (fail on the unfixed code, pass now): `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py::test_baseline_same_name_functions_keep_each_files_report`, `::test_baseline_skip_test_reads_only_the_tasks_own_file_report`, `::test_baseline_skip_test_ignores_a_legacy_name_only_report_of_another_file`, `::test_baseline_pending_task_records_and_is_briefed_from_its_own_report`, `::test_baseline_same_name_tasks_running_together_do_not_share_a_report`, `::test_baseline_unambiguous_name_still_publishes_the_name_only_report`.
- **Status (2026-11-20, remaining gap fixed):** [`../../scripts/mutation_check.py`](../../scripts/mutation_check.py) now provides `discover_wp_reports` / `WpReportIndex` scanning both `workspace/reports/wp/*.json` and `workspace/reports/wp/work/*.json` (non-recursive, deduplicated by `(normalized_file, function)` with canonical top-level precedence and ignoring non-report artifacts), and [`../../scripts/benchmark.py`](../../scripts/benchmark.py) and [`../../scripts/generate_report.py`](../../scripts/generate_report.py) consume it so same-named `static` functions in different files are all discovered and kept distinct. Tests in [`../../tests/test_mutation_check.py`](../../tests/test_mutation_check.py), [`../../tests/test_benchmark.py`](../../tests/test_benchmark.py), and [`../../tests/test_report.py`](../../tests/test_report.py). Runs started before the original fix may still hold a legacy name-only report of another file; readers ignore it by its `file` field.

### Symptom: A Guardrail Returns `compile_error` / `eacsl_instrumentation_failed` With a Struct-Comparison Hint While `e-acsl-gcc` Only Says `aborted by Frama-C`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Guardrail class memory raised and silent stage kill classified as eacsl_stage_killed (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** c-ares run-1, 2026-11-16, prover `negotiate-config_defaults` on `src/tools/adig.c#config_defaults`, module `adig` (98 translation units in the guardrail `--sources`), twice on the same unchanged files. Two later runs of the identical command compiled.
  - Report: `"status": "compile_error"`, `"reason": "eacsl_instrumentation_failed"`, `compile.returncode: 1`, `diagnostic_hints: ["E-ACSL encountered an unsupported ACSL construct (such as an uninterpreted 'axiomatic' logic function ...)"]`, no `guard_config_defaults.instrumented.c` written.
  - `guard_config_defaults.compile.log`: stage 1 ends at `[e-acsl] beginning translation.`, then `++ /home/opam/.opam/5.5/bin/frama-c ...`, then `e-acsl-gcc: fatal error: aborted by Frama-C`, with **no** Frama-C message anywhere in `compile.stdout`, `compile.stderr` or the log.
- **Cause:** the stage-1 Frama-C was killed for memory; the container was held to the guardrail class's fixed 2048 MB. [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py) defined `"guardrail": JobClass("guardrail", True, 1, 2, 2048, 0, wall_timeout_s=1800)`. The failure mode was indistinguishable from an annotation error at the report level: [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py) mapped any non-zero stage-1 return code to `eacsl_instrumentation_failed`, and `derive_eacsl_diagnostic_hints` in [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py) matched bare `"with"` against Frama-C's `(with preprocessing)` on every log, emitting the false struct-comparison / unsupported-construct hint.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py), [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py), [`../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../scripts/run_eacsl_fuzz.py)):** raised `CLASSES["guardrail"].mem_base_mb` to `4096` and extended Phase 0 parse-peak memory floor scaling (`first_build_estimate`) to `"guardrail"`; `_detect_stage_kill` classifies silent `aborted by Frama-C` (no Frama-C diagnostic) and SIGKILL exits as `reason: "eacsl_stage_killed"` with `killed` / `killed_suspected`; `derive_eacsl_diagnostic_hints` emits a container memory/OOM hint for `eacsl_stage_killed` and narrows `"with"` to `"\\with"`. Tests: `test_guardrail_memory_floor_is_4096_mib_and_scales_from_phase0_parse` in [`../../tests/test_resources.py`](../../tests/test_resources.py), `test_guardrail_classifies_silent_aborted_by_frama_c_as_eacsl_stage_killed` and `test_compile_eacsl_binary_distinguishes_stage_kill_from_frama_c_error` in [`../../tests/test_guardrail_p1.py`](../../tests/test_guardrail_p1.py), `test_derive_eacsl_diagnostic_hints_for_stage_killed_and_with_preprocessing` in [`../../tests/test_run_eacsl_fuzz.py`](../../tests/test_run_eacsl_fuzz.py).

### Symptom: Crash Verifier, Breaker and Header Architect Briefs Say "Through the Wrapper" But Never Give the Wrapper Invocation; the Parent Writes Its Own Environment Sheet for Subagents
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Shared run-commands block spliced into the four briefs (9639fff, 4e150be, merge ff418c7).
- **Seen on:** mpack run-4, 2026-11-17, start of Phase 3 (supervisor, from the opencode transcript and `agents/verify-framac_target_main_eefc7d1f37/brief.md`). After reading the first verifier brief, the parent searched it for `wrapper|framac-docker|host|PowerShell` and decided: "The briefs assume a container shell, so I'll publish one shared environment sheet for every subagent, then dispatch the first batch."
- **Cause:** [`../../skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md) says `prepare-agent` renders "the host paths the agent needs for the wrapper" into every brief, but only [`../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/prover_brief.md) does (`-TargetDir "{target_dir}" -RunDir "{run_dir}"`). [`crash_verifier_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/crash_verifier_brief.md), [`breaker_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/breaker_brief.md) and [`header_architect_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/header_architect_brief.md) only say "through the wrapper", and the verifier brief calls `/framac-run` the "container working directory". The subagents are opencode subagents on the Windows host, so they cannot tell how to reach the container. This is the same gap that lets breakers start their own containers ("Phase 3 Subagents Start Their Own Containers With `docker run`..." in [`./resources-and-infra.md`](./resources-and-infra.md)).
  - `prepare_agent` in [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) filled `{target_dir}` and `{run_dir}` for every role all along. Three templates never used them, and no brief gave the wrapper's own path; the prover brief had the flags but not the path.
- **Fix:** **Fixed:** `9639fff`, `4e150be` from `sup/mpack-brief-wrapper` (run supervisor S15), merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-17 (merge `ff418c7`).
  - A shared block, `skills/framac-wp/run_commands.md` (on the branch), is spliced into all four briefs at a `<!-- run-commands -->` sentinel. It gives:
    - the `framac-docker.ps1` invocation (`-TargetDir`/`-RunDir`, `-ScriptFile` or `-Command`) and the `framac-docker.sh` invocation (`--target`/`--run-dir`, `--script-file` or `--`);
    - which paths are container paths;
    - a request to put quoted or multi-line commands in an LF script file under the agent's scratch directory.
  - A new `{pipeline_dir}` placeholder is derived from `FRAMAC_REFERENCES_HOST`, and the block counts toward brief staleness.
  - Test: `tests/test_robustness.py::test_brief_gives_concrete_wrapper_invocation_on_the_host`, one case per brief; all 4 failed before the fix.
  - The supervisor reran the brief tests (7 passed), ran `ruff` and `mypy` (clean), checked that the wrapper flags exist, and dry-ran the merge against `phase1-remediation`, S13 and S14 (all clean).
  - Limits:
    - On the `docker-compose.yml` path `FRAMAC_REFERENCES_HOST` is unset, so `{pipeline_dir}` falls back to a container path, as `{references_dir}` already does.
    - `framac-docker.sh` does not strip CRLF from `--script-file` (the ps1 wrapper does).
  - After the merge, `wp_scheduler` reconcile lists run-4's already-rendered briefs as `stale_briefs`; that list is informational. For run-4, the parent's hand-written environment sheet remains the workaround.

### Symptom: A Header Architect Returns `failed` on a `code_fault` in Code Its Header Does Not Declare (a Known Defect in `main`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Architect guardrail scoped to the header's functions (38a2838, merge ff418c7).
- **Seen on:** mpack run-4, 2026-11-17, Phase 3 (supervisor S18). `arch-sax_example__program` proved its contracts for `parse_messagepack` (WP 7/7), but its Check 2 guardrail reported `code_fault` with `functions: null`. The fault was the already-confirmed uninitialized `st_size` read in the example program's `main` (`examples/sax-example.c:186`), which the only input hits before `parse_messagepack` runs. As the brief requires, the architect returned `failed`.
- **Cause:** the guardrail rule for failures outside the checked functions (`failure_outside_checked_functions`, merged as `831caf1`) needs `--functions`. Neither [`../../skills/framac-wp/header_architect_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/header_architect_brief.md) Check 2 nor the parent's architect re-check in `merge_agent_overlay` passed the header's functions, so a known defect anywhere in the harness module counted against the header. Any module with a confirmed defect outside its header's functions could never merge its header contracts. If the architect had returned `passed`, the parent's re-check would have rolled the merge back on the same failure.
- **Fix:** **Fixed:** `38a2838` from `sup/mpack-brief-wrapper` (run supervisor S18), merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-17 (merge `ff418c7`).
  - `workspace_overlay.header_function_names` lists the header's prototypes and inline definitions. An architect adds no declarations, so the list is stable.
  - `prepare_agent` renders a new `{header_functions_args}` placeholder (`--functions a b`, or nothing) into the architect's Check 2 guardrail command.
  - `merge_agent_overlay` (architect) and the batch module guardrail pass the same functions as `reach_functions`.
  - Test: `tests/test_architect_guardrail_scope.py`, 4 cases; all failed before the fix.
  - The supervisor reran the 82 tests of `test_architect_guardrail_scope.py`, `test_agent_result_evidence_files.py` and `test_wp_scheduler.py` (all green), ran `ruff` and `mypy` (clean), and dry-ran the merges with S13, S14, S16 and `phase1-remediation` (all clean).
  - Limit: in run-4, `sax_example__program`'s harness passes the fuzz bytes as the argv path, so it never reaches `parse_messagepack` and the module still needs a harness repair. The fix only stops a known defect in `main` from blocking the header's contracts.

### Symptom: A Negotiation for an Eva-Closed Callee Is Dispatched at Once and Ends `stalled_after_k_retries` on Callees Without a Contract
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Negotiation deps fixed (8a253c4, 4496414, merge ff418c7); the 'not repaired' note concerns a past run's state only.
- **Seen on:** mpack run-4, Phase 3, 2026-11-17 (supervisor S19).
  - In `state/wp_schedule.json`, all 36 `negotiate:` tasks had `deps: []` and no `prove:` task. All 382 `prove` tasks were still pending behind `arch:libmpack_a` and `gate:libmpack_a`.
  - The first negotiations to finish all stalled, and by 17:00 six had: `mpack_expect_i32`, `mpack_expect_i64`, `mpack_expect_str`, `mpack_encode_array16`, `mpack_encode_map32` and `mpack_writer_ensure`. For example:
    - `agents/negotiate-mpack_expect_i32/result.json`: "The function's 7 goals are all Valid, but the run is not sound because neither callee has any contract" (`mpack_read_tag`, `mpack_reader_flag_error`, both with pending `prove:` tasks).
    - `mpack_encode_map32`: the assigns goals timed out because `mpack_store_u8__fc_inline` and `mpack_store_u32__fc_inline` had no assigns clause.
- **Cause:** a bug in `_emit_negotiations` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py).
  - A `negotiate:<callee>` task got a dependency only when `prove:<callee>` existed.
  - The callees that callers ask to strengthen are often closed by Eva, so they have no prove task. Their negotiation was dispatched at once: before the module's architect and gate, and before the prove tasks of the callee's own callees.
  - The negotiator therefore proved the callee against default contracts, and each stalled round counted against the caps (`MAX_NEGOTIATIONS_PER_PAIR = 2`, `MAX_NEGOTIATION_ROUNDS_PER_CALLEE = 3`).
  - A rejected round also leaves the caller's `contract_requests[<callee>]` set. The scheduler then never refiles, and the caller is later dispatched tagged `callees_without_contract`.
- **Fix:** **Fixed:** `8a253c4`, `4496414` from `sup/mpack-brief-wrapper` (run supervisor S19), merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-17 (merge `ff418c7`), while run-4 was paused; the resumed run picks it up with `init --reconcile`. The rounds spent before the fix stay open, see below.
  - `_negotiate_deps` gives a negotiation with no live prove task what that prove task would wait on: the module gate (the fine-grained call-cone gate, as for prove tasks), the module's architects, and the `prove:` task (or open `negotiate:` task) of each of the callee's own callees without a contract, plus the architects of their modules.
  - A callee whose call cone reaches the negotiated function is left out, so cycles cannot deadlock. The fixer checked that both cycle tests deadlock with the guard disabled. A callee left out this way is still seen without a contract, and the negotiate brief does not say so (possible follow-up).
  - Every sync recomputes the deps of pending negotiations, so `init --reconcile` repairs an existing state file. Running and finished negotiations are not touched.
