  - [`../../skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md) item 6 says what a negotiation waits on (mirrors synced).
  - Tests: `tests/test_negotiate_deps.py` (on the branch), 10 cases, 7 of which failed before the fix. `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py::test_prover_waits_for_the_architects_of_its_module_and_of_every_callee_module` asserted the old order and now expects the architect first.
  - The supervisor read the diff, reran 88 tests (green), ran `ruff`, `mypy` and `check_generated_text.py` (clean), and dry-ran the merges with S13, S14, S16 and `phase1-remediation` (all clean). The fixer's full suite: 6069 passed, 223 skipped.
  - **Not repaired by the fix:** rounds that stalled before it still count against the caps, and their callers' `contract_requests` block a refile, so those callees are never negotiated again and their callers are proved without their contracts. Fixing it for a live run takes a hand edit of the run's scheduler state between dispatches: drop the caller's `contract_requests[<callee>]`, decrement `negotiation_counts[pair]`, reset the task's `dispatches`. The supervisor does not write in a run directory, so in run-4 the caps stay as they are. A better long-term fix: a round dispatched before its dependencies existed does not count against the caps.

### Symptom: `wp_scheduler.py requeue` Reopens a Verifier as "never returned" Although Its `scratch/result.json` Is Written, and `--stale-minutes 0` Reassigns Live Agents
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. status reports result_waiting; requeue protects waiting and young running agents (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** mpack run-4, 2026-11-17, Phase 3 (supervisor). Both gates (`gate:libmpack_a`, `gate:sax_example`) waited on 10 verify tasks still `running` after 2–2.5 h. Three of them already had results: `agents/verify-unknown_function_fd03be63af/scratch/result.json` (14:37), `verify-unknown_function_3117fe4adc` (14:32) and `verify-mpack_write_bool_da7350c06e` (14:58). The parent had never run `complete` on them, and had never launched the other seven. At 16:55 it ran `requeue --agent-id` on all ten. The history recorded `reopened ... requeued: agent verify-unknown_function_fd03be63af never returned`, and `next --prepare` assigned `-2` agents. Its own summary script then misread `next`'s output as "tasks: 0", so at 16:58 it ran `requeue --stale-minutes 0`. That reassigned every running task to `-2`/`-3` agents, including live negotiation subagents (`mpack_encode_array32`, `mpack_encode_map16`, …).
- **Cause:**
  - The missed returns and the misread are model errors.
  - Pipeline gap 1: `requeue` does not look for a result the agent already wrote. It discards a finished verdict as "never returned", and the work is redone (about 15 min of agent time per verifier).
  - Pipeline gap 2: `requeue --stale-minutes 0` reassigns every running task with no guard. It does not check the agent's age against its `wall_budget_s`, or whether its scratch directory is still being written. So one confused call duplicates live work.
  - Pipeline gap 3: `status` gives no hint that a running task's result already sits on disk, waiting for `complete`.
  - Contributing factor: `step` nests its dispatch list under `next.tasks`, while `next` returns `tasks` at the top level. The parent's reader assumed the `step` shape and printed 0 for a `next` output that held 10 tasks. It confirmed this at 17:03.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py)):**
  - `cmd_status` now reports running tasks with a valid `agents/<id>/scratch/result.json` on disk under `result_waiting` (with `action: "wp_scheduler.py step --complete <agent_id>"`).
  - `cmd_requeue` (CLI `--force` added) skips running tasks that already have a valid waiting `result.json` (reporting them under `result_waiting`) and, on `--stale-minutes`, skips prepared (`brief.md` present) running agents younger than their `wall_budget_s` (`skipped_active`) unless `--force` is passed.
  - Tests: `test_status_and_requeue_protect_running_task_with_valid_result_waiting`, `test_requeue_stale_minutes_protects_live_agents_younger_than_wall_budget_unless_forced` in [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).
  - Workaround used in run-4: the supervisor nudged the parent to launch the agents it now holds, to complete the three late results if `complete` accepts them, and to stop requeueing.

### Symptom: A Contract Cannot Name `MPACK_BUILD_ALIGNMENT` Because the Prover Build and the Guardrail Build Disagree on `_Alignof` vs `__alignof__`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. C99+ -std= preserved, gnu17 default for E-ACSL and __alignof__ rewrite (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** mpack run-4 (2026-11-17), negotiation `negotiate-mpack_builder_align_build` on `src/mpack/mpack-writer.c#mpack_builder_align_build`. The prover wrote `_Alignof(mpack_build_t)` because the macro it wanted (`MPACK_BUILD_ALIGNMENT`) expands differently in the two builds: `mpack-platform.h:1409-1429` selects `_Alignof` under C11 (the WP build) and `__alignof__` under `-std=c99` (the E-ACSL guardrail build), and the ACSL parser rejects `__alignof__(` with `unexpected token )`. The proof is `all_proven` (9/9, Typed) and the guardrail passed; the contract simply cannot be written with the macro.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap (fixed). `gcc -E` (used by WP/Eva) defaults to GNU17 (`__STDC_VERSION__ = 201710L`), while `e-acsl-gcc` hardcodes `-std=c99` in both stage-1 `FRAMAC_CPP_EXTRA` and stage-2 `CFLAGS`, and `generate_harness.py` stripped every `-std=` flag on link units. In addition, under an explicit `-std=c99` target build, Frama-C's ACSL parser rejects `__alignof__(` inside annotations and inside `-dD` `#define` bodies fed to `-pp-annot`.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/generate_harness.py`](../../scripts/generate_harness.py), [`../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py), [`../../scripts/fc_cpp_wrapper.py`](../../scripts/fc_cpp_wrapper.py)):**
  - `generate_harness._merge_dag_module_cppflags` now preserves C99+ `-std=` flags on link units (dropping only `is_pre_c99_std`).
  - `eacsl_toolchain.build_eacsl_flag_sets` forwards explicit C99+ `-std=` flags or appends `EACSL_DEFAULT_STD = "-std=gnu17"` to both `frontend` and `backend` when none is specified, overriding `e-acsl-gcc`'s `-std=c99`.
  - `fc_cpp_wrapper.rewrite_alignof_text` rewrites `__alignof__`/`__alignof` to `_Alignof` inside ACSL annotations and `-dD` `#define` replacement bodies (leaving already-expanded C code, strings, and comments untouched, gated by `FRAMAC_CPP_REWRITE=off`), covering explicit `-std=c99` targets across both WP/Eva and E-ACSL.
  - Verified in `framac-analysis:latest` (`test_wp_and_eacsl_guardrail_agree_on_alignof_macros_under_default_and_c99`, including negative control with `FRAMAC_CPP_REWRITE=off`). Tests: [`../../tests/test_generate_harness.py`](../../tests/test_generate_harness.py), [`../../tests/test_eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../tests/test_eacsl_toolchain.py), [`../../tests/test_fc_cpp_wrapper.py`](../../tests/test_fc_cpp_wrapper.py).

### Symptom: An `assigns errno` Clause in a Shared Header Aborts the E-ACSL Guardrail With `Failure: unbound logic variable errno`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. errno shim in fc_compat_types.h / errno.h stub (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18), architect `arch-libmodbus_so_5_1_0`. The architect tried `assigns errno;` on entry points that write `errno`; the Dual-Oracle E-ACSL guardrail build aborted in translation units that do not include `<errno.h>` before the shared header (for example `src/modbus-data.c` and the module harness) with `[kernel:annot-error] ... Failure: unbound logic variable errno`.
- **Cause:** pipeline gap (fixed). Neither `__fc_libc/errno.h` (`__fc_errno`) nor `templates/fc_stubs/errno.h` (`__fc_eacsl_errno`) was read in translation units that do not `#include <errno.h>`, so `errno` was unbound in shared-header contracts.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h`](../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h), [`../../templates/fc_stubs/errno.h`](../../templates/fc_stubs/errno.h)):**
  - Without `#include <errno.h>` (so glibc `<features.h>` is never prematurely frozen before target `_GNU_SOURCE`/`_POSIX_C_SOURCE` lines), force-included `fc_compat_types.h` now defines weak `int __fc_eacsl_errno __attribute__((weak));` + `#define errno __fc_eacsl_errno` under `__FRAMAC_EACSL__`, and declares `__FC_EXTERN_FOR_MACRO(errno) _Thread_local int __fc_errno;` + `#define errno __fc_errno` (when `errno` is not already defined) under `__fc_libc` (WP/Eva).
  - `templates/fc_stubs/errno.h` runs `#undef errno` before `#include_next <errno.h>` under `__FRAMAC_EACSL__` (preventing redefinition warnings), guards `__fc_eacsl_errno` with `__FC_EACSL_ERRNO_DEFINED`, and re-applies `#define errno __fc_eacsl_errno`.
  - Verified in `framac-analysis:latest` across WP (`all_proven`, `assigns_vcs_proven=True`), Eva (0 alarms, default and opt-out macro modes), 3-TU E-ACSL guardrail with `errno_shim` and `-fno-common`, and `_GNU_SOURCE` / `memrchr` preservation. Tests: `test_fc_compat_types_and_errno_stub_map_errno_without_including_errno_h` and Docker test `test_shared_header_assigns_errno_works_in_wp_eva_eacsl_guardrail_and_preserves_gnu_source` in [`../../tests/test_fc_compat_contracts.py`](../../tests/test_fc_compat_contracts.py).

### Symptom: A Correct `assigns \nothing` Frame on a `tcflush` Call Stays Unknown, While the Only WP-Provable Spelling (`Frama_C_entropy_source`) Aborts the E-ACSL Guardrail With `unbound logic variable`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Weak definitions of Frama_C_entropy_source and the libc model objects for the E-ACSL host-header branch (1ea83a1, f89ce15, 9250359).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18), prover `prove-modbus_rtu_flush` on `src/modbus-rtu.c#_modbus_rtu_flush`. All RTE goals were proven; the `l3_frame` goal `typed_modbus_rtu_flush_assigns_normal_part1` stayed Unknown for the semantically correct `assigns \nothing`.
- **Cause:** Pipeline gap, **fixed** (see Fix). Frama-C's libc model declares `tcflush` as `assigns Frama_C_entropy_source` (`share/libc/termios.h:207`); the marker is a nondeterminism gadget, not process memory. Naming it is the only WP-provable spelling, but the E-ACSL guardrail aborts with `unbound logic variable Frama_C_entropy_source` because `templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h` declares it only in its Frama-C-libc branch, not in the `__FRAMAC_EACSL__` host-header branch. `assigns \everything` would be vacuous and would poison every caller's frame.
- **Fix:** fixed by branch `sup/libmodbus-libc-model-ghosts` (1ea83a1, f89ce15), merged live into phase1-remediation as 9250359 during run-1 Phase 3 (07:21). The `__FRAMAC_EACSL__` branch of [`../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h`](../../templates/fc_stubs/fc_compat_types.h) now gives weak C definitions of `Frama_C_entropy_source`, `__fc_fds`, `__fc_sockfds`, `__fc_socket_counter` and `__fc_fds_state`, plus the `__FC_MAX_OPEN_FILES` and `__FC_MAX_OPEN_SOCKETS` macros, all with `__fc_libc`'s types. They are definitions and not `extern` declarations because full memory tracking registers every global: an `extern` failed the link with ``undefined reference to `Frama_C_entropy_source'``. They are weak so that several translation units merge. Regression tests: `tests/test_fc_compat_contracts.py::test_eacsl_host_headers_define_the_libc_model_objects_weakly` and `::test_contracts_naming_libc_model_objects_prove_under_wp_and_build_under_eacsl`. Under E-ACSL, only `assigns` and `\from` uses mean anything: glibc never writes these objects, so a clause E-ACSL executes reads 0. Before the merge, run-1 kept `assigns \nothing` and treated the `l3_frame` unit as spec-limited.

### Symptom: WP Proves `assigns __fc_sockfds[s]` (or `__fc_fds[s]`) for a `setsockopt`/`send`/`recv`/`close` Call, but the E-ACSL Guardrail Rejects the Clause With `unbound logic variable __fc_sockfds` (or `__fc_fds`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Same change as the tcflush entry (9250359).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18), `negotiate-modbus_tcp_set_ipv4_options` on `src/modbus-tcp.c#_modbus_tcp_set_ipv4_options`, and the provers of `_connect`, `_modbus_tcp_close`, `_modbus_tcp_flush`, `_modbus_tcp_recv`. WP proved the frame clause (for example 8/8 or 11/11 goals, `Bytes` memory model), but the guardrail failed with `eacsl_instrumentation_failed` / `modbus-tcp.c:<n>: unbound logic variable __fc_sockfds` (or `__fc_fds`). The only spelling the guardrail accepts, `assigns \nothing` or a buffer-only frame, leaves the frame VC unproven or times out on the 1024-cell model tables.
- **Cause:** Pipeline gap, **fixed** (see Fix). `run_eacsl_fuzz.py guardrail` compiles with `-no-frama-c-stdlib` against the host glibc headers, where Frama-C's libc model arrays `__fc_sockfds` / `__fc_fds` (the locations the modelled socket and `close` calls assign) do not exist. WP sees them through `__fc_libc` and needs them in the frame. Same class as the `Frama_C_entropy_source` entry above: a WP-sound `assigns` clause names a Frama-C libc-model object the guardrail build cannot parse.
- **Fix:** fixed by the same change as the entry above (9250359, merged live during run-1 Phase 3). The fixer reproduced it in the analysis image through `run_eacsl_fuzz.py guardrail`. Before the change, the build failed with `eacsl_instrumentation_failed` and `unbound logic variable __fc_sockfds`. After it, the result is `passed_dynamic_oracle` and every function is reached, including a two-source build. WP (`Bytes`) still proves the frames. Workaround used in run-1 before the merge: keep the strongest guardrail-passing frame (buffer-only or `assigns \nothing`), record the `l3_frame` unit as blocked/spec-limited, and note that all RTE and termination goals of those functions are proven; the WP proof of the clause itself is sound under `Bytes` (itself an experimental-model assumption) and the clause is not false.

### Symptom: A Prover Deletes Its Function's `assigns` Clause, Reports `all_proven` and the Merge Accepts It
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Removal of assigns clauses refused, frame_summary.assigns_clause, ledger credit gated (8664419, 4fc63e1, 20ffa06, 0135bed, c4d0055 plus 2026-11-20 follow-ups).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18), `prove-modbus_write_register`, `done:merged` at 17:34. Its patch removes `assigns *ctx \from reg_addr, value;` and `ensures status: \result >= -1;` from `src/modbus.h` and adds only `ensures null_status: ctx == \null ==> \result == -1;`. The result says the callee `write_single` has no contract WP can use, so the frame is "left unspecified".
- **Cause:** the merge's frame check (`_frame_refusal` in `scripts/workspace_overlay.py`) only protects clauses listed in the projected frame skeleton, so a clause outside the skeleton can be deleted. The WP report of the merged contract still says `assigns_vcs_proven: true` and `missing_assigns_warning: false`, although the contract has no `assigns` clause.
- **Effect:** no wrong proof was found. The ledger credits only the function's `rte` unit, and a caller sees WP's default specification, which is weaker. `all_proven` and `done:merged` overstate the result: the merged contract says nothing about what the function writes, and bounds `\result` only when `ctx` is NULL. Not explained yet: which goal WP counts as the one proven `l3_frame` goal of this function. (Explained on 2026-11-18: it was not a frame goal, see the correction in the Status below.)
- **Fix:** fixed, see the Status below. Pipeline bug as recorded in run-1, with this suggestion for run-2: refuse a merge that removes an existing `assigns` clause unless a negotiation closed it, or report the function as `partial`. Report `assigns_vcs_proven` as false when the contract has no `assigns` clause. No workaround was applied in run-1.
- **Status (2026-11-18):** **fixed** in `8664419`, `4fc63e1`, `20ffa06`, `0135bed` and `c4d0055`, on branch `integ/vb-wave1`, not yet merged into `phase1-remediation`.
  - **Correction of the Effect above:** the question it left open is answered. WP proved no frame goal for `modbus_write_register`. The one "proven `l3_frame` goal" was a precondition of the callee, WP's memory-model hypothesis `precondition of write_single: \separated(ctx + (..), &__fc_errno)`, which `goal_obligation_class` in `scripts/run_wp_function.py` classed as `l3_frame` because its predicate contains `\separated`. The rule of `ledger.py ingest-wp` credited an `l3_frame` unit from that class count alone, without asking whether the contract has an `assigns` clause, so it would have credited such a unit. Run-1's ledger was not rechecked for this entry: the Effect above says it credits only the function's `rte` unit.
  - `scripts/run_wp_function.py` (`frame_clause_state`, `8664419`): the report says whether the contract has a frame, from WP's own `[wp:pedantic-assigns] ... No 'assigns' specification for function` warning and the `-report` output. `frame_summary` carries `assigns_clause` (`present`, `absent`, `behaviors_only`, `unknown`), `assigns_clause_basis` and `assigns_behaviors`. For `absent` and `behaviors_only`, `assigns_vcs_proven` is false and `missing_assigns_warning` is true; `unknown` keeps the old value. The status is unchanged and a hint says what is missing. Test: `tests/test_wp_frame_clause.py`.
  - `scripts/acsl_ast.py` (`compare_function_contracts`, `4fc63e1`) compares a function's contract before and after a candidate, per behavior. `scripts/workspace_overlay.py` (`contract_change`, `_contract_refusal`, `_no_frame_refusal`, `20ffa06`) uses it in the prover merge and in the dry run: a result that leaves a behavior without the `assigns` clause it had is refused, and so is a full proof whose report says `absent` or `behaviors_only` when the replaced contract had an `assigns` clause. A tightened or rewritten clause is not a removal. A removed `ensures`, behavior or `complete`/`disjoint` clause still merges and is listed in the merge result (`contract_changes`); `wp_scheduler.py` keeps it on the task as `merge_notes`, and REPORT.md section 5 lists it (`6166906`, `90d11ff`). So the deleted `ensures status: \result >= -1;` of run-1 would now be reported, not refused. Tests: `tests/test_contract_clause_changes.py`, `tests/test_merge_contract_gate.py`, `tests/test_scheduler_merge_notes.py`.
  - `_merged_contract_acsl` in `scripts/workspace_overlay.py` and `frame_gate_errors` in `scripts/proof_carry.py` (`0135bed`) read only the contract in front of a declaration (`acsl_ast.declaration_contract_text`), so a statement contract in the body no longer satisfies a projected clause. Test: `tests/test_frame_check_contract_only.py`.
  - `scripts/ledger.py` (`wp_frame_problem`, `c4d0055`): an `l3_frame` unit is credited only when `frame_summary.assigns_clause` is `present` and `assigns_vcs_proven` is true; otherwise the evidence is `wp_no_frame_clause` or `wp_frame_not_shown` and the unit keeps its status. `goal_obligation_class` no longer classes a `precondition`, `precondition of <callee>` or `postcondition` row as `l3_frame` for a `\separated` in its predicate (1,684 rows of libmodbus run-1's report CSVs and the fixtures move to `rte`, per the commit). Test: `tests/test_ledger_frame_credit.py`.
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** architect merges are now checked for removed `assigns` clauses in [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) (`_architect_contract_changes` across single, batch, and dry-run merges). Tests in [`../../tests/test_merge_contract_gate.py`](../../tests/test_merge_contract_gate.py).
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** `_acsl_text_of_span` in [`../../scripts/mutation_check.py`](../../scripts/mutation_check.py) now delegates to `acsl_ast.declaration_contract_text` (excluding body annotations, loop invariants, asserts, and statement contracts), and `_replace_contract_in_span` scopes replacements strictly to the pre-body header so multi-block/indented contracts never strip body annotations. Tests: `test_acsl_text_of_span_ignores_body_annotations`, `test_check_function_keeps_body_annotations_intact_across_spec_mutants`, `test_check_function_multi_block_indented_contract_keeps_body_annotations`, and `test_acsl_text_of_span_contract_shapes` in [`../../tests/test_mutation_check.py`](../../tests/test_mutation_check.py).
- **Status (2026-11-19):** the merge gate was replayed, read-only, over the 53 prover results libmodbus run-1 merged (all 53 reconstructed; each reconstructed contract equals what the next merge of the function started from, or the final tree). Three would now be refused, each for a contract without a frame: `modbus_write_register` (the deleted clause) and the partial merges of `modbus_tcp_listen` and `modbus_rtu_set_serial_mode` (no `assigns` clause, so `assigns_verified_for_partial_merge` is false). `_modbus_tcp_build_request_basis` is refused only against the skeleton recorded in run-1; the skeleton `scripts/frame_skeleton.py` now generates accepts its contract (`tests/test_frame_skeleton_expressible.py::test_the_provers_contract_for_the_tcp_request_passes_the_merge_check`). All 26 `l3_frame` units run-1 credited keep their credit when `frame_summary` is recomputed from the recorded WP logs. A WP report written before this fix has no `frame_summary` and credits no `l3_frame` unit, so such a report must be produced again.
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** all three false-positive frame-refusal cases are fixed in [`../../scripts/acsl_ast.py`](../../scripts/acsl_ast.py) (`contract_has_whole_frame`) and [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) (`contract_change_errors`, `_has_whole_frame`): replacing a default `assigns` with `complete behaviors` that each have `assigns` now merges (`("guarded_all", SEAM_WAS_DEFAULT)` -> `"merged"`), and named-behavior-only baselines no longer trip `_no_frame_refusal` (`("guarded_some", SEAM_WAS_SAME_FRAME)` and `("guarded_gap", SEAM_WAS_SAME_FRAME)` -> `"merged_no_frame"`, with `l3_frame` credit still withheld). Updated `SEAM_MERGE_CASES` in [`../../tests/test_verdict_fix_seams.py`](../../tests/test_verdict_fix_seams.py) and added tests in [`../../tests/test_contract_clause_changes.py`](../../tests/test_contract_clause_changes.py) and [`../../tests/test_merge_contract_gate.py`](../../tests/test_merge_contract_gate.py).

### Symptom: A Second WP Run at the Same `--timeout` Replays Cached `[Timeout]` Verdicts and Is Still Killed Before Any Report
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Wall budget scaling for large functions and salvage of partial WP log verdicts (2026-11-20); the original cache diagnosis was already corrected in the entry.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18), `negotiate-modbus_receive_msg`. Invocation 1 ran with `--provers alt-ergo --timeout 2` at `wp_par 1` (it shared the wrapper call with the guardrail) and recorded every goal it reached as `[Timeout]`; invocation 2 (requested 400 s, capped to 351 s at `wp_par 8`) "replayed 316 of those cached Timeout verdicts" from `state/cache/wp` and ran out of wall clock on the rest, so the candidate ended with `analysis_error` twice and no verdict. The same annotation digest was also killed in `prove-modbus_receive_msg`'s own 900 s run.
- **Cause:** the WP cache is not at fault, and the first diagnosis recorded here ("the cache key ignores the prover and the timeout") was wrong.
  - `Cache.ml` in the run's image (`framac-analysis:latest`, 34.0~beta) hashes the prover into the entry (`digest prover goal`). `promote` reuses a cached `Timeout` only when the requested timeout is not longer than the recorded prover time. A longer timeout runs the prover again.
  - Both invocations used `--provers alt-ergo --timeout 2` (`agents/negotiate-modbus_receive_msg/scratch/wp_run.sh`), so replaying the 316 verdicts was correct.
  - The kill comes from the wall time left to the call: the first invocation used up most of the agent's 900 s, so the second was capped at 351 s. The 316 goals it logged carry about 247 s of Qed simplification. WP prints no report when it is killed, so `run_wp_function.py` returns `analysis_error` with `total_goals: 0`.
  - `negotiate-modbus_receive_msg-2` shows that one call fits when it gets the time: with `--timeout 10`, `--wall-timeout 600`, its own cache directory and no guardrail beside it, WP reported in about 560 s (54 proven, 1 under hypotheses, 61 unproven of 116 properties).
  - Not measured: whether the first run's timeouts came from sharing the CPU with the guardrail (`run_both.sh`). If they did, those verdicts are replayed at the same timeout until a run asks for a longer one.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py), [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)):**
  - `workspace_overlay.scale_prover_wall_budget_s` scales `max_wall_clock_s` in `.prover_budget.json` for large functions above goal/VC thresholds (`total_goals > 40` or `vc_total > 80`, including `partial_verdicts.scheduled_vcs` from killed prior runs, with an Eva-unknowns/LOC/loops fallback when no WP report exists), capped at `quick: 600`s / `standard: 2400`s / `deep: 3600`s while leaving small/medium functions, `shell_unverifiable_wp`, and explicit `FRAMAC_PROVER_WALL_BUDGET_S` untouched; records `budget_base_s` and `budget_scaling_reason` in `.prover_budget.json` and the prover brief (`wp_scheduler._task_wall_budget_s` inherits it automatically).
  - `run_wp_function.parse_partial_wp_log_verdicts` salvages `[wp] N goals scheduled` and incremental per-goal verdicts (`[Timeout]`, `[Stepout]`, `[Unknown]`, `[Failed]`, `[Valid]`/`[Proved]`, `(Cached)`) from stdout/stderr when WP is killed before writing `-report-csv`, attaching `partial_verdicts` (`incomplete: true`) to the `analysis_error` report (keeping `total_goals: 0` so killed runs are never credited) and emitting a progress/remedy hint in `derive_wp_diagnostic_hints` (including raising `--timeout` when `cached_timed_out_vcs > 0`). Verified in `framac-analysis:latest` (Frama-C 34 flushes scheduled/timeout lines to pipes before `TimeoutExpired` without needing `stdbuf`).
  - Tests: [`../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py`](../../tests/test_workspace_overlay.py), [`../../tests/test_prover_budget_lease_wait.py`](../../tests/test_prover_budget_lease_wait.py), [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py), [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py). Workaround in run-1: give the agent's single WP call the wall time and do not run the guardrail beside it; raising `--timeout` is enough to bypass cached timeouts.

### Symptom: `guardrail_error` When a Sampled Corpus Input Blocks in `select`, Losing the Verdict for an Unrelated Function
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Hanging replay inputs dropped (a4618f1, 2d45df3, db6a9e4, merge fd69371) and stdio guards extended (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18), in `prove-modbus_get_float_abcd`, `prove-modbus_set_float_abcd` and `negotiate-modbus_tcp_check_integrity-2`, and in every guardrail report written after 09:14. The contract compiled and was accepted, but the replay aborted with `guardrail_error`: libFuzzer hit a per-input timeout inside `LLVMFuzzerTestOneInput -> modbus_receive -> _modbus_tcp_receive -> _modbus_receive_msg -> _modbus_tcp_select -> select`, `returncode` 70, and `reach.functions.<target>` was 0, so the annotations were neither challenged nor refuted. WP was `all_proven` in the same agent run.
- **Also seen as:** four merges reopened with `merge failed: parent guardrail returned status='guardrail_error'` (`_modbus_rtu_is_connected`, `_modbus_tcp_is_connected`, `_modbus_tcp_close`, `_modbus_tcp_select`).
- **Cause:** pipeline bug, triggered by a harness-induced hang. `breaker-modbus_tcp_select` (status `clean`) listed `seed-ce4942acc3eaa255` and `seed-b5d3116add13facb`, and `ledger.py` promoted them into `workspace/corpus/libmodbus_so_5_1_0/` at 09:09:13. Harness case 66 (first byte `0x42`) passes a closed descriptor above the fd table to `modbus_set_socket`, Linux `select()` ignores it, and `modbus_receive` waits with no indication timeout until libFuzzer's 30 s limit. Every guardrail replays every promoted seed, and a libFuzzer timeout was never dropped as an excluded input, so no guardrail could return a verdict. This is not a libmodbus memory-safety finding.
- **Second failure behind the hangs:** `seed-5795f4eb2aa48e87` makes `modbus_close` close descriptor 2, libFuzzer's stderr, and the next input exits 77 with no report. The exact cause of that exit is not identified.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/libmodbus-guardrail-hang` (`a4618f1`, `2d45df3`, `db6a9e4`), merged live into phase1-remediation as `fd69371`.
  - `run_eacsl_fuzz.py` drops a hanging input (`excluded_inputs`, `kind: timeout`) and replays the rest under one wall budget. A pass that dropped a hang is not cached. If the hang cannot be dropped, the status stays `guardrail_error` with `replay_hang`.
  - `promote_artifact.py` refuses a seed that matches the agent's own `timeout-` artifact.
  - The generated `close()` shim keeps descriptors 0 to 2 open, and its text is part of the E-ACSL build cache key.
  - `wp_scheduler.py requeue --guardrail-hung` puts proved results back in the merge queue and reopens the affected tasks at no dispatch cost. It is idempotent.
  - Tests: `tests/test_guardrail_hang.py`, `tests/test_requeue_guardrail_hung.py`.
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed, [`../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../scripts/eacsl_toolchain.py), [`../../scripts/fuzz_build.py`](../../scripts/fuzz_build.py), [`../../scripts/promote_artifact.py`](../../scripts/promote_artifact.py)):**
  - Extended `STDIO_GUARD_FUNCTIONS` to `("close", "dup2", "fclose")` in `generate_errno_shim_source`: `dup2(a0, a1)` refuses redirecting stdout/stderr (`1`/`2`) when `a0 != a1` with `EBADF` (preserving `dup2(..., 0)` for `--entry-style stdin` harnesses and `dup2(n, n)`), and `fclose(a0)` flushes and returns `0` without closing `stdin`/`stdout`/`stderr` (preserving `errno` and leaving `NULL`/bad-pointer checks intact). `_eacsl_cache_material` now hashes all `ERRNO_SHIM_FUNCTIONS` shims into the build cache key. Verified in `framac-analysis:latest` (`test_stdio_guards_keep_stderr_open_after_close_dup2_and_fclose_in_the_analysis_image`, `test_stdin_harness_under_the_eacsl_fuzz_build`).
  - `promote_artifact.py` `promote_seeds` (`--binary`, `--replay-timeout`, default `5.0`s) now screens candidate seeds against the module's fuzz binary under `sandbox_exec.run_sandboxed` with `-runs=1 -timeout=0` (preventing libFuzzer's `-timeout=N` `SIGALRM` from interrupting blocking `select()` calls with `EINTR` before libFuzzer reports a timeout), refusing hanging seeds that left no `timeout-` artifact (`refused_hanging`) while allowing clean and crashing seeds through. Verified in `framac-analysis:latest` on `select()` and spin-loop hangs (`test_promote_seeds_pre_promotion_screen_refuses_hanging_seed_in_the_analysis_image`).
  - Tests: [`../../tests/test_guardrail_hang.py`](../../tests/test_guardrail_hang.py), [`../../tests/test_eacsl_toolchain.py`](../../tests/test_eacsl_toolchain.py), [`../../tests/test_promote_and_verifier.py`](../../tests/test_promote_and_verifier.py). (Run-1's recorded corpus is read-only and still holds its two seeds; harness case 66 is a target-run harness detail.)

### Symptom: Merge Refuses a Proven Contract With `could not locate definition of function '<name>'` After a Function That Contains Preprocessor Branches
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. _conditional_delims added (15e08a5, c270863, merge f24f382) and extended to c_definitions, cache, build_capture (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18) after the memory-refusal recovery. `_modbus_tcp_select` (negotiation, blocked) and `modbus_rtu_get_rts` / `modbus_rtu_get_serial_mode` / `modbus_rtu_get_rts_delay` (prove, requeued) were all refused at merge time with `src/<file>: could not locate definition of function '<name>'`, although each agent's patch applies with `git apply --check -p1` and reproduces the proven work file byte-for-byte. The same recovery had just merged 15 other contracts, so the merge path itself was healthy.
- **Cause:** the top-level scanners of `scripts/acsl_ast.py` counted delimiters across both arms of a preprocessor conditional. libmodbus opens one block in both arms and closes it once after `#endif` (`src/modbus-tcp.c:379-388` and `707-711`, `src/modbus-rtu.c:761-765`), so the depth never returned to 0 and every later function of the file was invisible: 12 of 25 scheduled functions in `src/modbus-rtu.c`, 14 of 25 in `src/modbus-tcp.c`. `splice_prover_changes` then refused the merge, and two failures blocked the task or rejected the negotiation. It reproduces on the unannotated base tree, so it is independent of the contract.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/libmodbus-locate-conditional` (`15e08a5`, `c270863`), merged live into phase1-remediation as `f24f382`. `_conditional_delims` reads one configuration of the conditionals and every scanner ignores what an unread arm leaves unmatched; balanced arms behave as before. `wp_scheduler.py requeue --locate-failed` restores the damaged tasks after a dry-run splice of each result; run it only on a checkout that has the fix. Tests: `tests/test_acsl_ast_conditionals.py`, `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py::test_requeue_locate_failed_*`, `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py::test_splice_refusal_*`.
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** `acsl_ast._conditional_delims` tracking is now applied in [`../../scripts/c_definitions.py`](../../scripts/c_definitions.py) (`extract_functions_from_code`), [`../../scripts/cache.py`](../../scripts/cache.py) (`_identify_unrelated_acsl_tokens`), and [`../../scripts/build_capture.py`](../../scripts/build_capture.py) (`declared_names`), so unbalanced `#if`/`#else` brace arms no longer blind subsequent definitions while balanced conditionals preserve identical line ranges. Tests in [`../../tests/test_c_definitions.py`](../../tests/test_c_definitions.py), [`../../tests/test_cache.py`](../../tests/test_cache.py), and [`../../tests/test_build_capture.py`](../../tests/test_build_capture.py).

### Symptom: A Negotiation That Changed Nothing Is Refused With `projected frame clause missing or weakened`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Frame check reads the merged tree (eb225df, 6719f9d), requeue --frame-refused (68b40d7), no-op negotiation handling (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, Phase 3, 2026-11-18: `merge failed: frame-preservation check failed:   src/modbus-tcp.c: projected frame clause missing or weakened: 'requires \separated(rsp + (0 .. 7), sft);' (no \separated clause over roots ['rsp', 'sft'])` on `_modbus_tcp_build_response_basis`, `_modbus_rtu_build_request_basis`, `_modbus_rtu_build_response_basis`, `_modbus_rtu_pre_check_confirmation` and `_modbus_tcp_check_integrity` (agents `negotiate-<name>-3`). Each result was `all_proven` with an empty diff, and the contract on the merged tree held the reported clause.
- **Cause:** `_merge_agent_overlay_locked` built the frame-check text only from the files the agent changed. An unchanged result gave empty text, and an agent that changed only the header hid a contract in the unchanged source. Functions with an empty skeleton (`_modbus_tcp_send`, `modbus_get_float_cdab`) passed, which masked the defect.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/libmodbus-noop-frame` (`eb225df`), merged live into phase1-remediation as `6719f9d`. `_frame_contract_files` and `_merged_contract_acsl` in `scripts/workspace_overlay.py` read the contract from the source, the declaring header and every changed file, using the spliced text for changed files and the current tree for the others. `check_frame_preserved` is unchanged, and a tree lacking a projected clause is still refused. Tests: `tests/test_frame_preservation.py`.
- **Recovery:** `wp_scheduler.py requeue --frame-refused` (branch `sup/libmodbus-frame-recovery`, `68b40d7`, merged live into phase1-remediation) re-runs the merge's own frame check on the current tree. A result that passes goes back to the merge queue with its units and requests, at no dispatch cost. A result still refused, a negotiation whose requests have moved on, a prover answered for by a negotiation and a task running or queued are listed in `left`. In run-1 it was needed for `negotiate:src/modbus-rtu.c#_modbus_rtu_pre_check_confirmation` (agent `-4`, requests `req_0037` and `req_0038`), whose merge had started before the fix landed. Tests: `tests/test_requeue_frame_refused.py`, `tests/test_frame_preservation.py::test_the_dry_run_of_a_requeue_gives_the_verdict_of_the_merge`.
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** both follow-ups are fixed in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py):
  - `_hold_callers` records `held_negotiations` alongside `extra_deps`, and `_contract_less_callees` preserves open `held_negotiations` in `extra_deps` across `_sync` until the negotiation reaches `TERMINAL`.
  - When a `negotiate` task merges with `not merged.get("modified_files")`, `_resolve_noop_negotiation` compares each request's `needed_clause` (parsed via `acsl_ast.iter_clauses` / `extract_frame_clauses`, stripping `[INCOMPLETE: ...]`) against the function's merged contract in `workspace/work/`: satisfied requests remain `verified_and_applied`, while missing clauses are resolved `rejected_by_callee` with a `no-op merge` note and recorded in `merge_notes.noop_missing_clauses`.
  - Tests: `test_sync_and_next_preserve_hold_callers_extra_deps_when_callee_is_merged_and_filed`, `test_noop_negotiation_lacking_needed_clause_marks_request_rejected_with_merge_note` in [`../../tests/test_requeue_frame_refused.py`](../../tests/test_requeue_frame_refused.py), `test_missing_needed_clause_matching_semantics`, `test_noop_negotiation_mixed_requests_applies_present_and_rejects_missing` in [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).

### Symptom: The Frame Check Accepts a Projected Location That Appears Only in an ACSL Comment
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. iter_clauses reads the kernel's view of comments (b4ad5ba, 9fe106f) and the four other readers were routed through it (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1, Phase 3, 2026-11-18: `negotiate-modbus_tcp_build_request_basis-2` merged `assigns t_id_frame: ((modbus_tcp_t *)ctx->backend_data)->t_id // , ctx_tcp->t_id ,` followed by `\from \nothing;`. The parsed locations were `['((modbus_tcp_t *)ctx->backend_data)->t_id //', 'ctx_tcp->t_id', '']`.
- **Cause:** `extract_frame_clauses` in `scripts/acsl_ast.py` does not strip `//` comments inside an annotation, so commented text counts as a clause location.
- **Fix:** fixed, see the Status below. Pipeline bug as recorded in run-1: strip ACSL comments before matching. Fix the local-alias projection first, or the `_modbus_tcp_build_request_basis` contract will fail every later merge. In run-1 the real clause is present and the parent WP re-check passed, so the proof itself is not affected.
- **Status (2026-11-18):** **fixed** in `b4ad5ba` and `9fe106f` (`scripts/acsl_ast.py`), on branch `integ/vb-wave1`, not yet merged into `phase1-remediation`. The local-alias projection was changed in the same wave (`3805f72`, see "A Correct Contract Is Refused Because the Projected Frame Clause Names a Local Variable" in [`./wp-and-guardrail.md`](./wp-and-guardrail.md)).
  - **Correction of the Cause above:** the hole was wider than `extract_frame_clauses`. `iter_clauses` found a clause on masked text but cut its label and predicate from the raw text, so comment text reached every check built on it, and it worked both ways: a `//` comment or an `@` could also hide a `\false` precondition, a trivial postcondition or a value constraint of a trust-boundary entry.
  - `_kernel_views` gives every clause reader the kernel's reading, captured from `framac-analysis:latest` (34.0~beta): `//` to the end of the line is a comment inside `/*@ */` and `//@`, a nested `//@` is a comment outside ghost code, `@` is white space anywhere, and `//` in a literal opens nothing. `iter_clauses`, and so `extract_frame_clauses`, `check_frame_preserved`, `find_named_clause`, `entry_requires_violations`, `contract_shape` and the vacuous-clause check, read that view. `verify_acsl_only` refuses a new annotation with a line ending in a backslash.
  - `9fe106f` (`_is_doxygen_comment`): a comment whose annotation opener (block or line form) is directly followed by `{`, `}` or `*` is skipped by Frama-C's lexer as doxygen, so it is no annotation: `mask_comments_and_literals` blanks it, `has_function_contract` skips it and `verify_acsl_only` refuses a new one. This reading comes from the lexer source (`clexer.mll`), not from a run in the image.
  - Test: `tests/test_acsl_annotation_comments.py` (fixtures `tests/fixtures/acsl_kernel_comments/`).
- **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** all four remaining readers are now routed through `acsl_ast.iter_clauses` / `acsl_ast._kernel_views` (ignoring `//` comments, nested `//@`, `@` whitespace, and doxygen comments): `trivial_added_clauses` in [`../../scripts/prover_candidates.py`](../../scripts/prover_candidates.py), `block_state` in [`../../scripts/stub_gen.py`](../../scripts/stub_gen.py), [`../../scripts/benchmark_briefs.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_briefs.py), and [`../../scripts/loop_seeds.py`](../../scripts/loop_seeds.py). Tests in [`../../tests/test_prover_candidates.py`](../../tests/test_prover_candidates.py), [`../../tests/test_stub_gen.py`](../../tests/test_stub_gen.py), [`../../tests/test_benchmark_briefs.py`](../../tests/test_benchmark_briefs.py), and [`../../tests/test_loop_seeds.py`](../../tests/test_loop_seeds.py).

### Symptom: No Prove Task Merges: `parent WP check on merged tree returned status='analysis_error': insufficient_memory`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. step infers the verify class, merges are not started below the memory minimum, requeue --memory-refused (62a54cf, 10b1fac, merge 0dbfddd, plus 2026-11-20 follow-up).
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18), Phase 3. 0 prove tasks merged between 06:00 and 08:37 local time. 12 provers ended `blocked`/`analysis_error`, 9 negotiations ended `rejected`, and 25 strengthening requests ended `rejected_by_callee` with the note `parent WP re-check did not complete (insufficient_memory)`. The parent report said `this job may use 1024 MiB, but WP needs at least 1768 MiB (768 MiB for Frama-C plus the fixed 1000 MiB -wp-memlimit of one prover)`. The orchestrator's "done" counts hid it: they held verifiers, breakers and negotiations only.
- **Cause:** three defects in a chain.
  - `resources.py` inferred the `verify` class for `wp_scheduler.py complete` and `merge-pending` but not for `step`, which runs the same merges, so `step` fell through to `light` (1024 MiB). The orchestrator also passed `-Class light` explicitly on its `step` and `complete` calls, and `skills/framac-wp/SKILL.md` did not say which class they need.
  - `run_wp_function.py` refuses to start WP below `WP_MIN_MEMORY_MB`. Nothing ran, so the refusal is not a verdict on the proof.
  - `wp_scheduler.py` treated the refusal as a transient analysis error: one retry in the same undersized container, then units parked, the task blocked and the negotiation resolved as rejected. The false rejections also reached the known dead ends in `workspace/reports/kb/obstructions.json`, which later briefs quote.
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `sup/libmodbus-merge-memory` (`62a54cf`, `10b1fac`), merged live into phase1-remediation as `0dbfddd`.
  - `step` now infers `verify`, and the skill says never to run the three commands with `-Class light`.
  - `merge-pending` and `step` start no merge or re-check in a container below the minimum, and `complete` leaves prover and architect results uncompleted. A refusal that still comes back keeps the task and the re-check queued with retries, units and requests untouched. The command exits 1 with a top-level `merge_deferred` that gives the memory numbers and the instruction to rerun with `-Class verify`.
  - Recovery for a damaged run: `wp_scheduler.py requeue --memory-refused`. It returns refused provers and negotiations to the merge queue, gives the units back to the agent, reopens the requests and removes the false dead ends. It is idempotent and leaves tasks blocked for any other reason alone.
  - A negotiation whose callee proof was refused too cannot be re-merged, because after the prove merge its base has diverged (`function '<f>' in workspace/work/ diverged from agent base snapshot`). The recovery reopens it to run again on the merged contract (`negotiations_rerun`), without counting the voided round against the cap.
  - A refused proof whose function has since received an applied negotiation stays blocked and is listed under `left`: the negotiator's proved contract is already merged.
  - **Status (2026-11-20, fixed):** `cmd_requeue_memory_refused` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) now retries eligible memory-refused partial merges (`_partial_merge_job_for_refused`, recorded under `partial_merges_retried`, idempotent and respecting `_memory_too_small()`), and calls `clear_memory_refused_evidence` in [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py) (also CLI `clear-memory-refused-evidence`) to prune non-discharging `insufficient_memory` `wp_analysis_error` attempts from restored units (`evidence_cleaned`). Tests: `test_clear_memory_refused_evidence_prunes_only_insufficient_memory_wp_errors` in [`../../tests/test_ledger_p1.py`](../../tests/test_ledger_p1.py); `test_requeue_memory_refused_cleans_memory_refused_wp_evidence_and_preserves_other_evidence`, `test_requeue_memory_refused_retries_eligible_partial_merge_and_is_idempotent`, `test_requeue_memory_refused_skips_partial_merge_superseded_by_applied_negotiation` in [`../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`](../../tests/test_wp_scheduler.py).
  - Regression tests: `tests/test_resources.py::test_infer_class_scheduler_commands_that_merge_run_in_the_verify_class` and the `memory_refus*`, `undersized_container` and `stale_refusal` tests in `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py`.

### Symptom: Brief Review Finds a Breaker Field Nothing Reads, a Verifier Brief That Still Names `harness_{module_name}`, and a Stub Author Brief Without the Wrapper Invocation
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The three former open-marker gaps and the typo were fixed on integ/brief-gaps (merge 5daee01), which is an ancestor of main; the entry's 'not merged' wording is stale.
- **Seen on:** a read of the five brief templates against their renderers and consumers, 2026-11-18 (no pipeline run). Nothing here was observed failing in a run.
- **Cause:** four gaps in the briefs, each read from the template and the code.
  - **Fixed (was open):** `suggested_requires` is required in the breaker result ([`../../schemas/agent-result.schema.json`](../../schemas/agent-result.schema.json)) and asked for by [`../../skills/framac-wp/breaker_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/breaker_brief.md), but no script under `scripts/` reads it (`git grep suggested_requires` finds only the schema, the brief and test fixtures). The breaker is also not given the function's `{reachability}`, so on an untrusted entry it can suggest a value precondition that the prover brief's Rule 3 forbids.
  - **Fixed (was open):** [`../../skills/framac-wp/crash_verifier_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/crash_verifier_brief.md) builds its paths and commands from `harness_{module_name}`. Commit `181f814` moved the prover, breaker and architect briefs to `{harness_module_name}`, and `prepare_agent` in [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) resolves that name only for those three roles. Whether a verifier is ever dispatched with a link unit as its module (a prover or breaker hypothesis on a unit fuzzed through sub-module harnesses) was not checked.
  - **Fixed (was open):** [`../../skills/framac-slice/stub_author_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-slice/stub_author_brief.md) says "Run, from `/framac-run`" and has no `<!-- run-commands -->` sentinel; `render_brief` in [`../../scripts/stub_gen.py`](../../scripts/stub_gen.py) substitutes five placeholders and no host path. It is the gap fixed for the four Phase 3 briefs in `9639fff`, in the one brief that fix did not cover.
  - Smaller: `prover_brief.md` line 5 says "to return_only a single JSON result". In the verifier brief, the WP counter-example bullet for `origin: "eva"` candidates sits inside Case A (candidates with reproducer inputs), and the Memory Leaks section separates Case A from Case B. Its Strict Rule 2 allows `confirmed` only for "an E-ACSL failure through the module harness", while the same brief confirms LeakSanitizer leaks and the pipeline raises sanitizer-only candidates (see "A Crash Verifier Replays a Sanitizer-Only Defect on the E-ACSL Binary" in [`./ledger-and-verifier.md`](./ledger-and-verifier.md), still open).
- **Fix:** **fixed** on branch `integ/brief-gaps` (merge `5daee01` of three agent branches cut from `5cbf8c2`), merged into `main` since (`5daee01` is an ancestor of `main`); not tried on a live run. On that branch the suite gives 6473 passed and 1 failed; the failure is the docs link test on two files that are untracked at `5cbf8c2`.
  - `suggested_requires` is removed from the schema, the brief, the validator and the fixtures (`bc56b9e`). The breaker brief now states `{reachability}`. A brief rendered before the change still asks for the key, so that agent's result is rejected once. Routing a hint to the next prover needs `prepare_agent` and was not done.
  - `prepare_agent` resolves `{harness_module_name}` for the verifier from the candidate's location, as `wp_scheduler._verify_harness_module` does, and the verifier brief uses it (`761e839`). A verify task can carry a link unit: a hypothesis candidate takes the task's module. Test: `test_verifier_brief_names_the_submodule_harness_of_a_link_unit_candidate` in `tests/test_verifier_brief_gaps.py`. (`ledger.py scaffold-verifier` has since also been updated on `main` to resolve `module` via `_candidate_harness_module`.)
  - `stub_gen.py` `render_brief` splices the run-commands block and fills the host paths as `prepare_agent` does; the author's id is `stub-<header name>` or `--agent-id` (`24c99a5`, `d5e5e58`). Test: `test_stub_brief_gives_the_wrapper_invocation_with_host_paths` in `tests/test_prover_stub_brief_gaps.py`.
  - The typo is fixed. In the verifier brief the counter-example bullet moved into Case B, the leak section follows Case B, and Strict Rule 2 accepts a failure the parent's replay reproduces on the build of the engine that sees it (E-ACSL, ASan, UBSan, LeakSanitizer).

### Symptom: `loop invariant` Goals Are Counted in Class `l2_termination`, and So Is a Postcondition Whose Label Contains `decreases`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. goal_obligation_class matches termination goals on the report kind (2026-11-18 / 2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** the verdict-bug fixes of 2026-11-18 (branch `integ/vb-wave1`), while reading the report CSVs of libmodbus run-1: 189 `loop invariant` rows and 133 postcondition rows with a "decreases" label are in class `l2_termination`. The counts come from that session's digest and were not recounted for this entry; the cause was read in the code.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. `goal_obligation_class` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) classed a goal by substring on its kind and annotation text: `if "variant" in low or "decreases" in low: return "l2_termination"`. "loop invariant" contains "variant", and a clause label such as `decreases_...` contains "decreases". The test ran before the frame and RTE classes, so these rows never reached them.
- **Effect:** `by_obligation_class` over-counted `l2_termination` and under-counted `rte`. A proven loop invariant could then credit a termination unit, see "An `l2_termination` Unit Is Credited `wp_qed_simplified`" in [`./ledger-and-verifier.md`](./ledger-and-verifier.md). No wrong credit was traced to a specific unit of run-1.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-18 for `loop invariant`, fixed 2026-11-20 for `decreases` labels, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)):** `goal_obligation_class` now matches termination goals on the report `kind` (`_TERMINATION_KIND_RE`: `^(?:(?:loop[\s_]+)?variant|decreases)\b`), using `_TERMINATION_CLAUSE_RE` on the annotation only for unlabeled / synthetic `assertion` items, so `postcondition` / `precondition` rows labelled `decreases_*` classify as `rte`. Test: `test_goal_obligation_class_termination_matches_kind_not_predicate_label` in [`../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py`](../../tests/test_run_wp_function.py).

### Symptom: The Hint `Frama-C WP does not support dynamic allocation predicates` Appears for a Function That Includes `stdlib.h` Without Allocating
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Allocation hint only on actual allocation goals or function-scoped warnings (2026-11-20).
- **Seen on:** the verdict-bug fixes of 2026-11-18 (branch `integ/vb-wave1`); reported in that session's digest, which names no target, function or log line.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. On `unproven_goals_remain`, the diagnostic hints of [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) added the allocation hint when an unproven goal was an allocation goal, or when `_WP_ALLOC_UNSUPPORTED_RE` matched anywhere in the WP log. That pattern held the bare words `freeable`, `allocates` and `frees`, which a log can contain for the libc contracts of `stdlib.h` although the function under proof neither allocates nor frees.
- **Effect:** the prover was told that its unproven goals "cannot be discharged by WP" and to stop, for goals that have nothing to do with allocation. (`next_synthesis_focus` was confirmed unaffected: `all_unproven_are_alloc` inspects only `unproven_goals`.)
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-20, [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py)):** added `_classify_skipped_spec_messages` (with `_is_libc_header`, `_warn_file_matches_target`, `_is_alloc_warning_for_function`) to fold and classify `not yet supported (skipped)` / `Ignoring` messages per message for the target function/file only, ignoring libc headers and unrelated log text; `unproven_goals_remain` now emits the allocation stop-now hint only on actual `alloc_goals` or function-scoped `alloc_skipped` warnings, and unrelated `stdlib.h` text no longer masks other ignored-spec hints. Tests: `test_wp_hints_no_spurious_dynamic_allocation_hint_from_stdlib_log`, `test_wp_hints_ignored_non_alloc_spec_not_masked_by_stdlib_mentions`, and `test_wp_hints_dynamic_allocation_hint_for_actual_alloc_goal_or_fn_warning` in [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py).

### Symptom: A Merge Refused for Its Frame Leaves an `all_proven` Report That Credits the Function's `rte` Unit
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. _on_merge_failed ingests a failed merge's report only when it is not all_proven (fae863d).
- **Seen on:** `tests/test_verdict_fix_seams.py` on branch `integ/vb-wave1`, 2026-11-19; found by feeding the merge's real output into `ledger.py ingest-wp`, not on a target run.
- **Cause:** a merge refused for its frame keeps the parent WP report, which says `all_proven` for a contract that was rolled back. When the task was given up after the second refusal, `_on_merge_failed` in [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) ingested that report, so the function's `rte` unit became covered by a proof of text the shared tree does not hold.
- **Fix:** `_on_merge_failed` ingests the report of a failed merge only when it is not `all_proven`.
- **Status (2026-11-19):** fixed in commit `fae863d` on branch `integ/vb-wave1`, not yet merged into `phase1-remediation`. Tests: `test_a_merge_refused_for_its_frame_credits_nothing` and the control `test_a_merge_refused_for_unproven_goals_still_records_them` in `tests/test_verdict_fix_seams.py`.

---

## From [`./security-properties.md`](./security-properties.md): Security Properties (Layers 4–6) & MetAcsl Issues

### Symptom: MetAcsl Property Generation Finds No Parameter Types for Functions Whose Model Signature Carries an `__attribute__`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged with tests.
- **Seen on:** sudo run-1 (2026-11-21). All 238 `sudo_dso_public` functions have model signatures like `unsigned long __attribute__((__visibility__("default")))(char *,unsigned char *,unsigned long)`. Found while fixing the seed recorder's attribute regex (see [`./harnesses-and-seeds.md`](./harnesses-and-seeds.md), "`seed_harvest.py record` Returns `no_calls_recorded` ...").
- **Cause:** `security_props._split_params` started at the signature's first `(`, which is the attribute's own, and returned `[]`. So `_Model.param_types()` and `arg_refs()` were None for every attributed function, and `pointer_writers` read a pointer return type as a pointer parameter.
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `scripts/security_props.py` on branch `sup/sudo-seed-harvest` (`6dfe3c0`), merged live into `main` as `63caeeb` during sudo run-1. New `_params_open()` returns the `(` matching the last `)`; `_split_params` and `pointer_writers` use it. Tests: `tests/test_seed_harvest_sudo.py::test_security_props_reads_the_parameters_of_an_attributed_signature` and `::test_security_props_pointer_result_behind_an_attribute_is_no_pointer_parameter`. Run-1 generated its security properties before the merge, so they still carry the defect.

### Symptom: `run_wp_function.py` Ends `wp_aborted` With `Cannot find field bytes_written in type struct pb_istream_s` on Every Function That Takes a `stream` Parameter (`L3_writers_ostream_*`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The former open-marker bug marker is superseded by the second Fix bullet (merged 2444bd7).
- **Seen on:** nanopb run-1, 2026-11-21, `prove-decode_pointer_field` (module `libprotobuf_nanopb_a`, `pb_decode.c`). `agents/prove-decode_pointer_field/scratch/wp_decode_pointer_field.json`: `status: wp_aborted`, `next_synthesis_focus: tier_a_abort_restructure_required`, kernel user errors `Cannot find field bytes_written in type struct pb_istream_s` and `Cannot find field max_size in type struct pb_istream_s`. With `--security-props none` the same file proves all 8 own goals (`rte` x7, `l3_frame` x1, soundness ok), so the contract is fine.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in `scripts/security_props.py` (found by the driver from `workspace/reports/security_props.json` and the prover's report). The threat model names two assets of the output stream, `stream->bytes_written` and `stream->max_size`. The generator turns each into a meta-property whose guards read `\formal(stream)->bytes_written` and `\formal(ostream)->bytes_written`, and it decides where the property applies by the *parameter name* (the note says: "checked only in functions with a parameter named stream or ostream"). nanopb names the parameter of its decoders `stream` too, but that one is a `pb_istream_t`, which has neither field, so MetAcsl's `\formal(stream)->bytes_written` is a type error and Frama-C aborts. `L3_writers_ostream_bytes_written` and `L3_writers_ostream_max_size` list 45 to 47 instances in `security_props.json` (functions of `pb_decode.c` and `pb_encode.c`, and five of `pb_common.c`).
  - **Confirmed to abort (default properties, seen in the provers' reports):** `decode_pointer_field`, `buf_read` and `pb_readbyte`. The 29 pending or running prove tasks whose formals include a `pb_istream_s` (every `pb_decode.c` decoder with a `stream`) have the same exposure but were not each run.
  - **Not affected:** functions whose `stream` is a `pb_ostream_t`, and `pb_common.c` functions with no `stream` formal (`advance_iterator` ran 38 goals with the default properties, `pb_release` and `pb_ostream_from_buffer` proved).
- **Fix:** **Fixed (was open; see the next bullet):** nothing had been changed in the pipeline when this was first recorded. The generator should match the parameter's *type* to the asset's struct (`pb_ostream_t` for `stream->bytes_written`), not its name, and leave a function whose `stream` has another type out of the property's `\targets` and its instances. Until then `run_wp_function.py` with its default security properties cannot prove such a function; the effect on the parent's re-check of a merged proof was not observed (no such proof merged). Workaround used in this run (the driver's spawn instruction, not a pipeline feature): provers run once with `--security-props none` as a diagnostic, report `blocked` with the abort in `evidence`, and the `l3_isolation` units of such functions stay uncovered. The run's `incomplete` reason must name this bug.
- **Fix (fixed: branch `sup/nanopb-l3-asset-param-type`, commits `570965d` and `bb6840b`, merged to main in `2444bd7` on 2026-11-21; dispatched by the run supervisor):** a run made before the merge (nanopb run-1) keeps its old `security_props.json` until Phase 0 regenerates it.
  - `scripts/security_props.py` reads parameter types from the model `signature` (`_Model.param_types`, `_norm_type`, which also drops the parse counter of anonymous struct names). It takes the asset's type from the declared writers, readers or gate, else from a listed parameter name with one type everywhere. Every function whose parameters of those names all have another type leaves `\targets` and `instances` and is recorded in the new optional `param_type` field (`schemas/security_props.schema.json`). A function that may still hold the asset goes under `unchecked` (never credited). A parameter of unknown type keeps the guard, and an undecidable type keeps today's behaviour with a warning.
  - The same rule covers `L3_readers_*`, `L4_auth_state_*`, `L4_auth_required_*` (protected functions, with a warning) and monotonic formal invariants. `L4_call_*`, L5 `entry` and L6 properties use their own function's parameters and were not affected.
  - Tests: `tests/test_security_props_asset_param_type.py` (15, 12 fail on the old code). In Docker, nanopb's three sources with the regenerated meta file went from 406 `Cannot find field ... struct pb_istream_s` errors to 0; the two asset properties drop from 47 and 49 to 20 and 22 instances (type `struct pb_ostream_s *`).

### Symptom: `L5_sanitized_send_msg` Is False at the Call Although No Uninitialized Byte Is Ever Sent (`\initialized(&req[0 .. req_length - 1])`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Forwarding sinks implemented and merged; the remaining limits are design limits and the undeclared-backend gap is tracked in the next entry.
- **Seen on:** libmodbus run-1 (2026-11-18). `breaker-modbus_mask_write_register` reported the property false at the `send_msg` call with `req_length == 14`, and `verify-modbus_mask_write_register_2a7ed14cd2` closed the candidate `src/modbus.c#modbus_mask_write_register::l5_sanitized::2a7ed14cd2` as `rejected`. The same property raised candidates on `modbus_write_bits`, `modbus_write_registers` and `modbus_write_and_read_registers`, and their verifiers rejected them for the same reason. The three verifications held `modbus_write_bit` and `modbus_write_register` back: both were dispatched 11 s after the last verifier closed, 23 minutes after their callee's negotiation.
- **Cause:** the property is evaluated at the call of the wrapper `send_msg`, one step before the buffer is complete. On TCP, `_modbus_tcp_build_request_basis` leaves the MBAP length bytes `req[4]` and `req[5]` unset. `send_msg` first calls `ctx->backend->send_msg_pre(msg, msg_length)` (`src/modbus.c:174`), and `_modbus_tcp_send_msg_pre` writes both bytes (`src/modbus-tcp.c:170-171`) before anything is transmitted. The target is correct: the generated property demands more than the sink needs. Pipeline bug: [`../../scripts/security_props.py`](../../scripts/security_props.py) put the obligation of every declared sink on its callers, whether the sink transmits or only forwards. Rebuilt as [`../../tests/fixtures/security_props/forward/l5_forwarding.c`](../../tests/fixtures/security_props/forward/l5_forwarding.c): before the fix, WP on the wrapper's caller left `L5_sanitized_send_msg: meta: \initialized(&req[0 .. 12 - 1])` Unknown on the correct program. Not measured: how many of the run-1 caller stalls this goal explains on its own.
- **Fixed (package A of [`../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md`](../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md), commit `14f017e` on `metacsl/a-l5-placement`):** a bytes sink (every kind but `log`, `terminal` and `buffer`) that the target defines and that reaches another declared bytes sink in the Phase 0 model's call graph (direct and indirect edges) is a forwarding sink (`_Gen._forwarding`). Its property has no instance and no MetAcsl text, and names the innermost sinks under `forwarded_to` ([`../../schemas/security_props.schema.json`](../../schemas/security_props.schema.json)); the bytes are checked at the calls of those sinks, so the wrapper's callers get no `l5_sanitized` unit. Regression tests in [`../../tests/test_security_props.py`](../../tests/test_security_props.py): `test_a_forwarding_sink_is_checked_at_the_innermost_sink` and its neighbours, and in the image `test_forwarding_sink_acceptance_in_the_image` (the caller proves with no L5 goal; `L5_sanitized_tcp_send` proves in `send_msg` after the hook, and stays Unknown when `send_msg` skips the hook).
  - **Why it is sound:** bytes leave the target only through the declared sinks. On every path the model finds from the wrapper, the first declared sink is checked at its own call, after whatever the wrapper or its callees completed. An uninitialized byte handed to the wrapper is either written before that call or reported there.
  - **Limits:** (1) the threat model must list every function the bytes leave through: a wrapper that also transmits by itself through an undeclared function on a direct call (`write`) is not seen. (2) The reach is the model's: a pointer call resolved by type that never happens at run time still counts as a path to a sink. (3) Text sinks keep their placement (a wrapper does not sanitize, its callers must). (4) Two sinks that only reach each other keep one property between them.
  - **A backend that is not declared keeps the old placement.** See the next entry: with `_modbus_tcp_send` declared and the RTU backend's send undeclared, `L5_sanitized_send_msg` stays at the callers of `send_msg` and a warning names the function to declare. Not verified on libmodbus itself: the target is not in this repository and no target run was made for this fix.
  - **Two fixes of this bug met at the merge (2026-11-19).** `fix/vb-l5-sink` fixed it with the threat-model field `checked_at`, and package A by inferring forwarding sinks. Merged rule, in `_Gen.output_sinks` of [`../../scripts/security_props.py`](../../scripts/security_props.py): a sink declared with `checked_at` never forwards. Its property is the `check` before each hand-off call, or stays at the wrapper's callers with a warning when the claim is refused. Forwarding applies to every other wrapper. Tests: `test_a_wrapper_sink_is_checked_at_its_hand_off_not_at_its_callers` and `test_without_checked_at_nothing_is_relocated_and_the_sink_forwards` in [`../../tests/test_security_props_wrapper.py`](../../tests/test_security_props_wrapper.py).

### Symptom: A Unit Is Credited `wp_meta_simplified` Although the File That Defines Its Sink Was Not Parsed (`\targets({tcp_send}), \context(\precond)`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in da63ab1 with a test.
- **Seen on:** the merge of `integ/vb-wave1` and `metacsl-integ` (2026-11-19), by reading the two changes together; `tests/test_wp_code_checks.py::test_a_name_is_seen_in_code_only` showed the empty `call_targets`. No target run showed it.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug. `call_targets` in [`../../scripts/run_wp_function.py`](../../scripts/run_wp_function.py) recorded only properties written `\called == f`, and `ledger.py` refuses the no-goal credit from that record (`wp_meta_not_evaluated`). Package A states the property of a sink called through a pointer as its precondition, which has no `\called`. When the sink's file is not parsed, MetAcsl generates nothing, no target is recorded, and the run looks decided statically.
- **Fixed (`da63ab1`):** `call_targets` also reads `\targets({f}), \context(\precond)` with one target. Test: `test_a_precondition_on_a_sink_the_parse_lacks_is_not_decided_statically`.

### Symptom: `test_the_callers_goal_is_false_before_and_gone_after_in_the_image` Fails With `assert [] == ['prog.c#request_rtu', 'prog.c#request_tcp']`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Test defect fixed in 60c3488; the limit it records is documented in the forwarding entries.
- **Seen on:** `tests/test_security_props_wrapper.py` with `FRAMAC_DOCKER_TESTS=1` on the merge `23e74e6` (2026-11-19). The test passes on `integ/vb-wave1` (`c74ae27`) and fails the same way on `metacsl-integ` (`22ae459`) with the test copied in. A test defect, not a pipeline defect.
- **Cause:** the "before" half declared the wrapper `send_frame` and its inner sinks `tcp_send` and `rtu_send` without `checked_at`, and expected `L5_sanitized_send_frame` at the wrapper's callers. Since `metacsl-integ` that declaration is a forwarding sink (`instances: []`, `forwarded_to: ["rtu_send", "tcp_send"]`), which the merge `da63ab1` kept. The merge updated the unit test for these sinks but not the in-image test.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-19, `60c3488`):** the "before" half declares the wrapper alone. The new `test_a_forwarding_wrapper_without_calls_gets_no_credit_in_the_image` covers the three-sink declaration.
- **Limit that test records:** without a `calls` annotation at the call through the pointer, WP generates no L5 goal in the forwarding wrapper, on a correct program and on one that leaves a byte unset. The run is flagged unsound and the ledger records `wp_no_credited_goals`, so a violation gets no credit, but WP does not report it as an L5 failure either. Declare `checked_at` to get a named check at the hand-off.

### Symptom: A Report Row of an In-Code `check` Has No Name (`"kind": "user check", "property": "\separated(&(buf[0]), &key)"`), and Two Checks Share One Line
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both generator and ledger sides are fixed with tests.
- **Seen on:** the same test (2026-11-18). The CSV of `-report-csv` gives a generated check its file, line and predicate, not `L6_no_leak_key`; the textual report does print `Check 'L4_auth_state_login' ("/target/prog.c", line 90)`. One initializer can carry the checks of several properties (`L5_sanitized_net_send` and `L6_no_leak_key` at the same call).
- **Cause:** Frama-C's CSV report omits the names of code annotations (the MetAcsl assertions have the same problem, solved by their line in the meta file, `name_meta_goal`). `ledger.py` maps an in-code check by `(file, line)` (`_code_checks`), which held one check per line until package E.
- **Fixed on the generator's side (commit `d7405e8`):** each `code_checks` entry of an initializer check carries `index`, its 0-based position among the generated checks of that line in source order (the checks before the declaration by property name, then the ones after it), and `line` is the line the check is written on. The rows of one line come in that order in the CSV (checked in the image on both builds of the fixture).
- **Fixed (package G, commit `6f7f100`):** `run_wp_function.py` did not put the property's name on such a goal, so it was filed under class `rte`, and `ledger.py` keyed in-code checks by `(file, line)` (the second check of a line replaced the first). `resolve_security_meta` now returns the function's `code_checks`; `code_check_names` matches the check rows of a line (kind holding `check`, same file and function) to its checks in `index` order, and `name_code_check` prefixes `<property>: `, so the goal is credited to the property's class. A line whose rows and checks differ in number, or whose indexes repeat, stays unnamed and is listed under `security_props.code_checks_unnamed`. Verified in the image: on line 64 of the fixture, row 0 is the `\initialized` check (`L5_sanitized_net_send`) and row 1 the `\separated` one (`L6_no_leak_key`). `ledger.py::_code_checks` is keyed by `(file, line, index)` (a missing index is 0) and an Eva row of a line is filed under every check of that line. A retry pass parses its CSV through the same loop. Tests: `test_the_checks_of_one_line_are_named_by_their_index`, `test_a_line_whose_check_rows_do_not_match_its_checks_stays_unnamed`, `test_a_check_without_an_index_is_the_only_one_of_its_line`, `test_in_code_checks_are_keyed_by_file_line_and_index`, `test_an_eva_row_of_a_line_with_two_checks_is_filed_under_both`.

### Symptom: `threat model /framac-scratch/tree/THREAT_MODEL.md, which the model recorded, is missing` in an Image Test That Runs `security_props.py run`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Test-setup detail, not a pipeline defect; the test already mounts the target.
- **Seen on:** the first version of `test_initializer_calls_end_to_end_in_the_image` (package G, 2026-11-19), which mounted the run directory but not the target.
- **Cause:** a test detail, not a pipeline defect: the Phase 0 model records the threat model under the target's mount (`/framac-scratch/tree`), and `security_props.py run` reads it from there.
- **Fix:** the test mounts the target at `/framac-scratch/tree:ro`, as the pipeline does.

### Symptom: `Declaration of send does not match previous declaration from FRAMAC_SHARE/libc/sys/socket.h` When a Fixture Declares Its Own `send`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Fixture naming issue, already resolved (fixture uses net_send).
- **Seen on:** the first version of the initializer fixture (package E, 2026-11-18): the Phase 0 parse of a one-file target that declared `extern int send(int fd, const char *buf, unsigned long n);`.
- **Cause:** not a pipeline defect. The pipeline's parse force-includes Frama-C's libc declarations, and the fixture's prototype differed from the libc one.
- **Fix:** the fixture's sink is called `net_send`. A test program must not reuse a libc name with another prototype.

### Symptom: A Variant's Correct Program Ends `unsound_proof` Although Its Named Goal Is Valid
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Fixture defect already fixed; the unsound_proof gate behaves as designed.
- **Seen on:** `test_kind_variant_acceptance_in_the_image[l4_call_global-satisfied]` and `[l4_call_formal-satisfied]` (package D1, 2026-11-18).
- **Cause:** not a pipeline defect: the fixtures declared the callee (`use_dev`, `write_reg`) without a contract, and `run_wp_function.py` flags a call of a prototype with a generated default contract as unsound, as it should.
- **Fix:** the fixtures give the declared callee `/*@ assigns \nothing; */`.

### Symptom: A Gate That Sets the Auth State Before Its Check Passes Both L4 Properties (`auth_ok = 1; if (pw != 42) return -1;`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. M7 implemented: success field and on_failure check.
- **Seen on:** `tests/fixtures/security_props/gate/l4_gate_failure.c` built with `-DVIOLATE` (M7 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), 2026-11-10).
- **Cause:** `L4_auth_state_<gate>` exempts the gate from the write check and `L4_auth_required_<gate>` only reads the state at the protected calls, so nothing constrains what the gate writes on its failure path. The natural fix, a MetAcsl `\postcond` on the gate (`\result != success ==> state == \old(state)`), is refused by MetAcsl: `Error during the typing of a HILARE! ... Why: \result meaningless` (checked in the image).
- **Fixed (M7, worktree commit of M7):** auth gates take an optional `success` ([`../../schemas/threat_model.schema.json`](../../schemas/threat_model.schema.json)). [`../../scripts/security_props.py`](../../scripts/security_props.py) generates `L4_auth_state_<gate>_on_failure` (template variant `on_failure` of [`../../templates/metacsl/l4_auth_state.acsl`](../../templates/metacsl/l4_auth_state.acsl)) as an in-code `check` before each `return` of the gate, with the returned expression in place of `\result` and `\at(state, Pre)` for the state on entry; the M6 code-check machinery writes it (`code_checks`). In the image WP proves it on the correct gate and not on the violating one, and E-ACSL reports the violation and goes on. A `return` whose expression the check cannot repeat (a call, an assignment) or a `return;` is skipped and reported in the warnings.

### Symptom: An `L5_sanitized_<sink>` Goal on a String Literal Never Proves (`log_line("admin command")`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. M2 implemented: literals decided from the model, credited statically.
- **Seen on:** the `tests/fixtures/security_props` session library (`session_admin`), and the known limit in [`../guides/security-properties.md`](../guides/security-properties.md): `L5_no_crlf` on a literal argument stays unproven with Alt-Ergo, Z3 and CVC5 at 10 s.
- **Cause:** `L5_no_crlf` quantifies over every index before the terminating NUL; the provers do not unroll that quantifier over the literal's bytes. A correct caller stayed `blocked`.
- **Fixed (M2 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), worktree commit of M2):** the Phase 0 dump records the string literals each direct call passes (`scripts/phase0_dump.ml`, dump schema 4; since Frama-C 32 a literal is a global the argument points to, `Ast_info.is_string_literal`), the model keeps them as `call_sites` (`scripts/phase0_model.py`, feature `call_sites`). `scripts/security_props.py` decides a caller whose every call of a `log`/`terminal` sink passes literals to its text parameters: satisfied when no literal holds CR or LF before its first NUL, else violated. That caller is no instance (no meta goal), the property lists it under `decided`, and `ledger.py seed` records the verdict: `covered` by evidence of phase `static` (`decided_syntactically`) or `blocked` (`decided_violated`), which no WP result overrides. A caller that also passes other text keeps its goal, and the literal calls in it still do not prove (a note on the property says so). A model without call sites (dump before schema 4) decides nothing and `security_props.json` says so in a warning. Option 2 of the plan (restate the predicate over libc's `strlen`) was not needed.

### Symptom: Two Predicates for "No CR or LF in the String": `L5_no_crlf` in the MetAcsl Preamble and the Vocabulary's Names
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. S8 fix merged; the preamble now reuses the vocabulary text under a guard.
- **Seen on:** plan step S8 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), MetAcsl half (2026-11-10). The MetAcsl preamble defined a private `L5_no_crlf{L}(char *s)` (an unbounded quantifier guarded by "no NUL before i"), while `templates/acsl/acsl_vocabulary.h` has `no_crlf_or_nul(buf, len)` for buffers and, on the libc track's S8 branch, `no_crlf_string(s)` bounded by `strlen`. A prover saw two names for one notion, and only the vocabulary's can be executed by E-ACSL.
