- **Cause:** the meta file `run_wp_function.py` writes is its own translation unit and includes nothing, so the preamble defined what it needed itself.
- **Fixed (MetAcsl half, worktree commit of S8):** [`../../templates/metacsl/preamble.acsl`](../../templates/metacsl/preamble.acsl) defines no name of its own: the meta file includes `__fc_string_axiomatic.h` (for `strlen`; `security_props.meta_file_text` writes the directive, so the templates stay pure ACSL) and the preamble repeats the vocabulary's `no_crlf_string` text word for word, and [`../../templates/metacsl/l5_sanitized.acsl`](../../templates/metacsl/l5_sanitized.acsl) names `no_crlf_string(\called_arg(p))`. Checked in the image: the meta file and a target that includes the vocabulary parse together (Frama-C merges identical definitions), and the property proves on a caller that requires the predicate. `test_preamble_repeats_the_vocabulary_text` fails if the two texts drift apart (it compares with the vocabulary once the libc track's definition is there) or if a template defines a predicate. E-ACSL never runs the MetAcsl properties; it executes `no_crlf_string` through `strlen` where a contract names it. A string literal still does not prove under `no_crlf_string` (the M2 decision from the model stays). `L5_no_crlf` remains only as the name hand-written contract clauses use (`ensures L5_no_crlf: no_crlf_or_nul(out, \result)`), which is a clause name, not a predicate.

### Symptom: Every MetAcsl WP Run Stops on `predicate no_crlf_string is already declared with the same profile`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The 'uncommitted' fix is in committed code.
- **Seen on:** `tests/test_security_props.py::test_wipe_acceptance_in_the_image` and every `test_template_variant_acceptance_in_the_image` case (32 tests), on an analysis image rebuilt from the current templates (`framac-analysis:fc34`, 2026-11-13). The WP report of the function is `status: acsl_syntax_error`, with `[kernel:annot-error] workspace/wp/security_meta_<fn>.c:3: Failure: predicate no_crlf_string is already declared with the same profile. Ignoring global annotation`, and has no `security_props` key.
- **Cause:** a pipeline bug, not a Frama-C change. Since S8 (`733235c`), `templates/acsl/acsl_vocabulary.h` defines `no_crlf_string`, and `fc_compat_types.h`, which `augment_framac_cppflags` force-includes into every Frama-C unit, includes that vocabulary. The meta file also repeats the predicate through the preamble, so its own unit declared it twice. Frama-C merges identical definitions across files but refuses a second one in the same file, on 35.0~~dev and on 34.0~beta alike (a two-line file shows it on both). The tests passed on the old `framac-analysis:latest` only because that image was built before S8: its vocabulary had no `no_crlf_string`. The entry above ("checked in the image: ... Frama-C merges identical definitions") checked two separate files, never the force-included header.
- **Fixed (on main):** [`../../scripts/security_props.py`](../../scripts/security_props.py) `meta_file_text` puts the preamble under `#ifndef ACSL_VOCABULARY_H` (`META_PREAMBLE_GUARD`), so it applies only to a run without the vocabulary; the comment in [`../../templates/metacsl/preamble.acsl`](../../templates/metacsl/preamble.acsl) says so. Test: `test_meta_file_lines_and_function_match` checks the guard; the 32 acceptance tests pass in the image again.

---

## From [`./ledger-and-verifier.md`](./ledger-and-verifier.md): Ledger, Crash Verifier & Artifact Validation Issues

### Symptom: `wp_scheduler.py` Rejects Verifier Results with `$.record.linked_units: fewer than 1 items`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both the original defect and its regression are fixed on main with tests.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (commit `8936217`, 2026-11-10; the commit names no target or run).
- **Cause:** `schemas/findings.schema.json` enforced `minItems: 1` on `linked_units` for every record. A crash or leak candidate located in a libc stub (`FRAMAC_SHARE/libc/stdlib.h#free`) or in generated harness code (`harness_<module>.c#unknown_function`) has no target unit in `coverage-ledger.json`, so `scripts/ledger.py scaffold-verifier` wrote `linked_units: []`, the schema check failed, and the scheduler discarded the verifier's result as invalid.
- **Fix:** [`../../schemas/findings.schema.json`](../../schemas/findings.schema.json) keeps `minItems: 1` (definition `linked_units_nonempty`) on `confirmed` records only; a `rejected` record may have empty `linked_units` (`8936217`), and so may a `needs_validation` record (2026-11-10).
- **Regression of `8936217`, fixed 2026-11-10** (found by the O1 regression tests, [`../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md`](../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md)): `8936217` made a non-`confirmed` scaffold take only the candidate's own `linked_units`. A `needs_validation` result for a candidate with none (an Eva Invalid in a libc stub, an unparsed crash in `harness_<mod>.c`) then had `linked_units: []`, which the schema still refused for `needs_validation`: `wp_scheduler` requeued it as `invalid_result`, then blocked the task, and the candidate stayed unverified. That held even when the verifier resolved the location to a target function with `--file/--function`, which linked that function's units before `8936217`. Now a non-`confirmed` scaffold falls back to the resolved function's units that may carry evidence (not `deferred`, `out_of_scope` or `not_applicable`), and `needs_validation` may link nothing. Tests: `tests/test_ledger_p1.py` (`test_rejected_record_without_target_unit_validates_and_ingests`, `test_scaffold_needs_validation_for_unlinked_candidate_is_ingestible`, `test_scaffold_rejected_links_only_the_candidate_units`).

### Symptom: `ingest-verifier refused the result: deferred unit must not hide evidence (use 'blocked' for partial work)`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is on main with its regression test.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (commit `8936217`, 2026-11-10; the commit names no target or run).
- **Cause:** when `cmd_scaffold_verifier` scaffolded a `rejected` result for a function with `deferred` units (for example functions kept out of WP dispatch by `shell_unverifiable_wp` or a bounded profile), it linked every unit of the function that was not out of scope. `cmd_ingest_verifier` then added the rejection's non-discharging evidence to each of them, and `validate_artifacts.py` refused the ledger: a `deferred` unit cannot carry evidence.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py) `cmd_scaffold_verifier` links every unit of the function only for a `confirmed` result; `rejected` and `needs_validation` results keep the candidate's own `linked_units`. As a safety net, `cmd_ingest_verifier` turns a unit in `NO_EVIDENCE_STATES` (`deferred`, `out_of_scope`, `not_applicable`) that still receives rejection evidence into `blocked`. As committed in `8936217` it set the rejection as `unresolved` only when that list was empty, which a valid ledger never has for such a unit, so the blocked unit kept its deferral reason (`tier3_not_scheduled: ...`). Since 2026-11-10 the rejection comes first in `unresolved`, followed by the old reason. Test: `test_rejection_evidence_on_a_deferred_unit_blocks_it` ([`../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md`](../plans/open-topics-remediation-plan.md), O1).

### Symptom: `refusing to write invalid ledger: $[index].unresolved[N]: duplicate item`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is on main with its regression test.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (commit `8936217`, 2026-11-10; the commit names no target or run).
- **Cause:** when several unproven goals of a function render to the same text (same kind, line and annotation, for example two pointer accesses on one source line), `cmd_ingest_wp` in `scripts/ledger.py` wrote each of them to `u["unresolved"]`, which `coverage-ledger.schema.json` declares `uniqueItems: true`.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py) `cmd_ingest_wp` deduplicates `unresolved` in order (`dict.fromkeys`), on both the unproven-goal and the error path. Test: `tests/test_ledger.py::test_ingest_wp_deduplicates_identical_unresolved_entries`.

### Symptom: A Rejected Candidate's Units Are `candidate` Again After a Re-Fuzz, But No Verifier Is Dispatched
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The entry says uncommitted on a branch, but the named code and test are now on main.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (branch `papers-generalization`, uncommitted, 2026-11-10). Found by reading the code while building the reopening of weak rejections; reproduced in `tests/test_rejection_reopen.py::test_flag_off_restores_the_earlier_behaviour`, not observed on a target run.
- **Cause:** `cmd_ingest_fuzz` in `scripts/ledger.py` called `mark_candidate` on the linked units of every fuzz failure, also when the candidate was already `verified` with a `rejected` record. After a `harness_induced` rejection and a harness repair, a recurring crash left the units `candidate` while `candidates.json` stayed `verified`, so nothing asked for a second verdict.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py) `tool_evidence_on_verified_candidate`: a replayed reproducer on a rejected candidate now reopens it or is recorded as contradicting evidence, and the units are not marked `candidate` otherwise. `FRAMAC_REJECTION_REOPEN=off` keeps the earlier behaviour, on purpose.

### Symptom: A Reopened Candidate (`status: unverified`, With a `reopened` Entry) Gets No New Verifier Task
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The entry says uncommitted on a branch, but the code and test are now on main.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (branch `papers-generalization`, uncommitted, 2026-11-10). Read from the code, covered by `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py::test_a_candidate_the_ledger_reopened_gets_its_verify_task_back_once`; not exercised on a target run.
- **Cause:** `_sync` in `scripts/wp_scheduler.py` created `verify:<fingerprint>` only when no task with that id existed. A candidate reopened by `ledger.py` already has a terminal `verify:` task from its first verdict, so the scheduler left it.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) `_sync` reopens a terminal verify task once per ledger reopening (`candidate_reopens` on the task against the candidate's `reopened` entries), so a verify task that ended blocked for another reason is not dispatched again.

### Symptom: Every `ledger.py` Write Refuses the libmnl Run 1 Ledger (`callgraph_source: "regex"`, `l6_secret`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** one-off, no longer needed. Stale artifact of one old run, not a pipeline defect; the schema deliberately refuses it and the advice is to re-run Phase 0.
- **Not a pipeline defect: stale artifact** (seen 2026-11-09 in the Tier 6 A/B). 182 units of `targets/libmnl/framac-run-1-results/coverage-ledger.json` use `callgraph_source: "regex"` (no longer allowed since 3ca823b) and 14 the obligation class `l6_secret`, so `ingest-wp`, `audit-hypotheses` and `mark-carried` all refuse it. The A/B remapped the labels in its run copies only (no verdict changes). Re-run Phase 0 rather than reuse that ledger.

### Symptom: Malformed `function_id` With Spaces Accepted by Schemas (`see a.c#f`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The schema pattern is tightened on main (in a later form) and the named test file exists.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M2). Schemas for build capture, Phase 0 model, and security properties accepted function IDs with space-separated file paths.
- **Cause:** `definitions.function_id.pattern` in `schemas/build_capture.schema.json`, `schemas/phase0_model.schema.json`, and `schemas/security_props.schema.json` was `^[^#\s][^#]*#[A-Za-z_$][A-Za-z0-9_$.@]*$`. The unanchored `[^#]*` permitted whitespace characters in filenames without slash directory separators.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, schemas):** pattern updated to `^(?:[^#\s]+|[^#\s][^#]*\/[^#/\\s]+)#[A-Za-z_$][A-Za-z0-9_$.@]*$`, which rejects unanchored whitespace in bare filenames while allowing legitimate spaces in directory paths containing slashes. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py).

### Symptom: Two Crash Verifiers Give Opposite Verdicts on the Same Decision Point, and the Parent Has No Command to Reconcile Them (`ledger.py: error: argument cmd: invalid choice: 'reopen'`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Status says fixed by sink-conflict reconciliation; commits are on main with tests.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15 (external supervisor; run transcript from 03:20). `src/qcbor_main_encode.c#QCBOREncode_EncodeHead::index_bound` has two fingerprints at the same line, both about `uMinLen` > 8 reading past `aIterate[4]`.
  - `ce76fb1dc0` was ingested `confirmed` (medium): the function is public and takes any `uint8_t`.
  - `02209a353f` was ingested `rejected` (`harness_induced`): only the harness passes `uMinLen` > 8.
  - The scheduler raised no flag. The parent noticed by itself, tried `ledger.py reopen` (`invalid choice`), then dispatched an off-schedule subagent `reverify-encodehead-ce76` with a parent-written brief citing the sibling's rejection evidence. It planned to ingest that result with `ledger.py ingest-verifier` directly, outside `wp_scheduler.py`. The re-verifier returned `rejected` at 03:33.
  - `ledger.py ingest-verifier agents/reverify-encodehead-ce76/scratch/result.json` refused it: `"cannot merge conflicting verdicts for src/qcbor_main_encode.c#QCBOREncode_EncodeHead::index_bound::ce76fb1dc0: confirmed vs rejected"`.
  - The parent then backed up `findings.json` to `state/findings.json.bak` and deleted the finding by hand.
  - `validate_artifacts.py run-dir .` caught it: `candidates.json: ...ce76fb1dc0 marked verified but resolved fingerprint(s) [...] have no findings.json record`. The parent restored the backup (validation `ok`, 20 findings), so the run's state is as the pipeline wrote it. The validator worked as intended.
  - The run therefore keeps `EncodeHead` as a confirmed medium finding, while the parent's own assessment is "rejected". The parent says it will mention this in its final summary.
- **Cause:** **Pipeline gap (fixed, see Status below).** Verifiers are dispatched per fingerprint. Nothing in `wp_scheduler.py` `complete` or `ledger.py ingest-verifier` compares a new verdict with earlier verdicts that share its `decision_point` (or root cause), and there is no command for a deliberate second opinion.
  - Reopening exists only when new tool evidence reproduces a rejected candidate (`tool_evidence_on_verified_candidate`, entry above).
  - The parent's workaround breaks two properties the gate relies on. The re-verifier was not independent: its brief argued the sibling's case. And its verdict bypassed the scheduler's bookkeeping.
  - For context, the run-5 review judged this same defect a real public-API hardening bug (upstream draft 07).
  - The run-6 findings assessment ([`../runs/2026-11-15-qcbor-run6-findings-assessment.md`](../runs/2026-11-15-qcbor-run6-findings-assessment.md) §3.5) reproduced it again on `dev` `9bd0845` and `19c3367`.
    - The `harness_induced` rejection of `02209a353f` is wrong. It repeats run-5 #26: the verifier read the in-tree callers' `uMinLen` values as the contract of a function the public header exports.
    - Two verdicts on one decision point therefore disagree, and the gap is also in the verifier's reasoning, not only in reconciliation. `crash_verifier_brief.md` should say that an exported function's contract is its header documentation, not its callers' usage.
- **Fix:** none yet. `ledger.py` and `wp_scheduler.py` hold uncommitted hot patches from this run, so no fixer was dispatched. Suggested:
  - `complete`/`ingest-verifier` should flag a verdict that contradicts an ingested verdict with the same `decision_point`, and open a `reconcile:<decision_point>` task.
  - That task gets a fresh verifier whose brief carries both records, without the parent's framing.
  - `ledger.py` should keep both verdicts and record the reconciled one, instead of letting the last ingest win.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`87bb1b6`, `5073fa8`, `f790d15`, `d4db9a5`), without a new command.
  - `ledger.py` (`_sink_key`, `sink_conflicts`, `_reconcile_sink_conflicts`, run at the end of `cmd_ingest_verifier`): a positive verdict (`confirmed`, `needs_validation`) and a rejection of any class but `duplicate` at one sink (file, function, baseline line, kind; not the decision point, which the rejected run-6 record did not have) are recorded on both records as `conflicts_with` and returned as `sink_conflicts`. A verifier cannot supply `conflicts_with` itself.
  - The rejected candidate is reopened once, with the evidence source `sibling_verdict` and the sibling's reproducer. That source skips the `harness_unchanged` suppression. A `checked` rejection is kept against a `needs_validation` sibling and re-verified against a `confirmed` one (`override_checked`): the run-6 pair (`ce76fb1dc0` confirmed, `02209a353f` rejected as `harness_induced` with basis `checked`) would otherwise only have been flagged. A second rejection is not reopened again (`already_reverified`); nothing picks a side.
  - `wp_scheduler._complete_verifier` returns `sink_conflicts`; REPORT.md, FINDINGS-DETAIL.md and NEEDS-VALIDATION.md list the pair and what happened to the rejection.
  - Replay on a copy of run-6's `findings.json` and `candidates.json`: exactly that pair is flagged.
  - Tests: `tests/test_ledger_sink_conflicts.py`, `tests/test_wp_scheduler.py::test_verifier_completion_reports_opposite_verdicts_at_one_sink`.
  - Not verified: how the `reopened` entry reaches the next verifier beyond the brief telling it to read the candidate's `reopened` entries.

### Symptom: A Stray `crash-<sha1>` File Appears in the Run-Dir Root During Crash Verification
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed on the pipeline side (the actor was never identified, which is not actionable).
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15 (external supervisor). `run-6/crash-fa138ae356d35c8d77083c406a5be73745643aae` (1 byte) was written at 03:30:24.
  - It is identical to `workspace/crashes/libqcbor_a/crash-fa138ae356d35c8d77083c406a5be73745643aae`, the `QCBORDecode_GetTEpochDate` reproducer (sha256 `2a0ab732...`).
  - It was written during the Batch 3 window (03:27-03:35), when `verify-qcbordecode_gettepochdate_745871f887` replayed that input.
- **Cause (probable, not confirmed):** libFuzzer writes `crash-<sha1 of input>` into the current directory when it runs an input without `-artifact_prefix`. A verifier probably ran `fuzz_libqcbor_a.e-acsl <input>` directly from `/framac-run` instead of through `run_eacsl_fuzz.py replay`. The verifier's own transcript was not inspected, because its child session is not in `opencode session list`.
- **Effect:** harmless here (a duplicate of a recorded input). But a verifier writing outside its scratch dir breaks the run-dir layout, and a new crash written there would never be ingested.
- **Fix:** **Pipeline gap (fixed on the pipeline side, see Status below):** none at that time. Suggested:
  - The crash-verifier brief should say that replays go through `run_eacsl_fuzz.py replay`, or that a direct run passes `-artifact_prefix=<scratch>/`.
  - `validate_artifacts.py` could flag unexpected files in the run-dir root.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed on the pipeline side**; the actor was not identified. `run_eacsl_fuzz.replay_artifact` and `replay_for_coverage` pass `-artifact_prefix=<scratch>/replay_artifacts/`, so no pipeline replay can write into the working directory (`e4f2f22`, `tests/test_fuzz_artifact_prefix.py`). `validate_artifacts.py run-dir` fails on `crash-*`, `leak-*`, `timeout-*`, `oom-*` and `slow-unit-*` files in the run-dir root (`2fa53b6`, `tests/test_validate_stray_fuzzer_artifacts.py`); it is an error of the CLI command only, not of `validate_run_dir()`, so `make report` and `pipeline_make.py ingest` stop visibly while the run stays readable. On run-6 it reports exactly `crash-fa138ae356d35c8d77083c406a5be73745643aae`. A verifier that runs a fuzz binary by hand from the run directory still writes there; the crash verifier brief says to replay through `run_eacsl_fuzz.py replay`.

### Symptom: A Candidate Merged by Site Replays Its Reproducers on One Engine, and `fingerprint_matches` / the Minimize Interest Test Read a Detection of the Other Engine as Not Reproduced
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in 586d28d, which is on main.
- **Seen on:** code review of S4 (site grouping, `phase1-remediation`, 2026-11-17). No run yet; the cause is in the code, not a log.
- **Cause:** a candidate now holds the detections of every engine at its site under its first key (`observations`, D1a). `crash_replay.replay_candidates` replays all the candidate's reproducers on one engine (`_candidate_engine`: the first tool output's engine), and compares the report with the candidate key only (`fingerprint_matches`). `crash_replay.minimize_candidates` passes the key as the interest test's fingerprint. A reproducer that the other engine found therefore reads as not reproduced, and a minimized reproducer is judged against the wrong fingerprint.
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `586d28d` (`scripts/crash_replay.py`; test commit `e0d8b03` makes the test prove the fix). Each reproducer replays on the engine of the observation whose `reproducer` names it, and `fingerprint_matches` accepts the key or any observation's fingerprint. `minimize_candidates` does the same, and the cvise interest test takes a comma-separated set of fingerprints. A candidate without observations, and a forced `--engine`, behave as before. Other engines build as `cross_<engine>_<harness>`; the candidate's own engine keeps the `make build` path.

### Symptom: A Verifier Verdict Is Lost (`requeued_ingest_error`) Because It Cites a Repository Doc as `evidence[].tool_output`, Although Its Own `agent-result` Check Passed
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged; the remaining oversized-file gap is carried by the 1 MiB promotion entry, which stays.
- **Seen on:** mpack run-4, 2026-11-17 at about 15:10, `verify-mpack_write_false_2e1ac57099`, whose verdict was a correct `contract_overconstraint` rejection.
  - `wp_scheduler.py step --complete` answered `action: requeued_ingest_error` with `refusing to write invalid ledger/candidates/findings: findings: $[14].evidence[7].tool_output: referenced file does not exist under run dir: docs/references/rte/frama-c-rte-manual.md`.
  - A fresh verifier must redo the task.
  - The run's model recorded it in the entry about the 1 MiB promotion limit; the supervisor gave it its own fix (S17).
- **Cause:** two gaps together.
  - The verifier brief points to the manuals through `{references_dir}` but never said that a `tool_output` must be a file in the run directory.
  - `validate_artifacts.py agent-result` checked only the schema and the path syntax, not that cited files exist or fit the promotion limits. So the agent's pre-return check passed, and the parent's ingest refused the whole result: the existence check when `findings.json` is written, or `promote_file`'s size limits for a scratch file above 1 MiB.
- **Fix:** **Fixed:** `4dfaeea` from `sup/mpack-brief-wrapper` (run supervisor S17), merged into `phase1-remediation` on 2026-11-17 (merge `ff418c7`).
  - The pre-read checks of `promote_file` are now `promote_artifact.check_promotable`. `validate_agent_result(data, run_dir)` runs them on a crash verifier's scratch evidence, reproducers and `fix_patch`; any other cited file must exist under the run directory.
  - `agent-result` gains `--run-dir`, defaulting to the run directory of an `agents/<id>/scratch/result.json`, so the command the briefs already give now runs these checks. The ingest itself is unchanged.
  - The verifier, prover and header architect briefs say that manuals and repository docs are cited in prose, and give the size limits.
  - Tests: `tests/test_agent_result_evidence_files.py` (a repository doc, a missing file, an oversized file refused; valid files pass); 3 of 4 failed before the fix.
  - The supervisor reran the related tests (11 and 40 passed), ran `ruff` and `mypy` (clean), and dry-ran the merges (clean). Run on run-4's lost result, the branch's `agent-result` check now refuses it with the ingest's error.
  - Still open: an oversized evidence file is still refused at ingest rather than skipped (see the 1 MiB promotion limit entry above), but the agent now sees the error before it returns.
- **Retry (2026-11-17, `verify-mpack_write_false_2e1ac57099-2`):** the workaround holds without any pipeline change. The retry re-ran the verification from scratch and kept every `evidence[].tool_output` inside its own scratch directory: a verbatim extract of the Eva report's `invalid_alarms` array (3 KB, instead of the 1.1 MB report), a fact sheet quoting the E-ACSL instrumented source (398,209 lines, 20.7 MB) with its search counts and the instrumented body, the sandboxed replay and coverage reports, the standalone RTE/Eva/sanitizer logs, and the case-38 seed. The largest cited file is 91 KB. `validate_artifacts.py agent-result` printed `"ok": true` and the verdict (a `contract_overconstraint` rejection of the same candidate) is ingestible. A source manual or repository file is now cited in prose only, never as `tool_output`.

### Symptom: `validate_artifacts.py` Fails With `$.api_cases[255].selector: above maximum 254` on `identity16` `--api` Harnesses
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix ec4a477 is on main with tests (the entry still says not yet merged).
- **Seen on:** nginx 1.31.9 run-3 (2026-11-19). `validate_artifacts.py run-dir .` reported 100 errors (the validator's cap) of the form `workspace/harnesses/harness_nginx__ngx_event_openssl.json: $.api_cases[255].selector: above maximum 254`, one per case from 255 up, on an `--api` harness of 498 cases whose sidecar says `dispatch_selector: identity16`. The run's artifact check failed, which contributed to the run ending `incomplete`.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in [`../../schemas/harness_sidecar.schema.json`](../../schemas/harness_sidecar.schema.json). Every `api_cases[*].selector` kept `"maximum": 254` from the time `--api` stopped at 255 cases. `a60a5a2` (S6b, D7) gave the generator a two-byte selector above 256 cases and raised `api_harness.MAX_CASES` to `DISPATCH_MAX_CASES_WIDE - 1` (4095), but did not touch the schema, and no test validated a sidecar of more than 255 cases. The harness itself was correct.
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `ec4a477`, now on `main`.
  - The five case kinds take their selector from one `case_selector` definition bounded by the generator: `0..api_harness.MAX_CASES - 1` (4094). `dispatch_case_count` and `dispatch_cases` are bounded by `generate_harness.DISPATCH_MAX_CASES_WIDE` (4096).
  - The one-byte bound depends on `dispatch_selector`, and minischema has no `if`/`then` to tie a bound to it. So [`../../scripts/validate_artifacts.py`](../../scripts/validate_artifacts.py) `validate_sidecar` checks it: a sidecar without `dispatch_selector: identity16` (`identity`, or the older modulo selector) with more than `ONE_BYTE_SELECTOR_CASES` (256) cases is refused, naming `identity16`.
  - No other tool assumed 255: `seed_harvest.py` and `fuzz_assets.py` already write and read selectors with the sidecar's selector width.
  - Tests: [`../../tests/test_sidecar_selector_bound.py`](../../tests/test_sidecar_selector_bound.py) (a 498-case `identity16` sidecar validates; the largest `--api` harness validates and one more case is refused; a one-byte selector above 256 cases is refused; the schema bounds are the generator's constants). `test_an_identity16_api_sidecar_above_255_cases_validates` failed on the old schema with `$.api_cases[255].selector: above maximum 254` (all five failed on the old schema and validator). `tests/test_challenger_m4_adversarial.py::TestB8SidecarSchemaAndDispatchMetadata::test_sidecar_schema_valid_dispatch_case_counts` accepted a `dispatch_case_count` of 1000000; it now accepts up to 4096 and refuses 4097.
  - `test_the_generator_records_no_schema_warning_above_255_cases` covers the producer side: `generate_harness.write_sidecar` checks the sidecar against the schema and records the problems in `warnings` (R5), and a rewrite drops a stale one.
  - Effect on a run already past Phase 0: the fixed schema alone does not clear it. Run-3's sidecar carries the generator's own warning (`schema: ['$.api_cases[255].selector: above maximum 254', ...]`), and `run-dir` fails on a recorded warning as on a live schema error, so the fixed validator still reports one error on it (checked read-only on run-3's file). Regenerate the module's harness, or rerun on a fresh run. With `ec4a477` alone the harness C file comes out the same and only the sidecar loses its warning; with this branch's `ea22f05` too, nginx__ngx_event_openssl regenerates with 720 cases instead of 498, so the C file, the case count and the harness digest change. **Not verified** on a real run directory: only the `write_sidecar` test and the read-only validation of run-3's file back this.

### Symptom: An Initializer Check That Eva Leaves Unknown Does Not Stop an Eva Cover of Its MetAcsl Unit
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix 6f7f100 is on main with named tests.
- **Seen on:** review of `ledger.py` during package G of [`../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md`](../plans/metacsl-exploitation-plan.md) (2026-11-19), no run: `_ingest_eva_meta` judged an L3-L6 MetAcsl unit from the rows of its MetAcsl instances only. The in-code check written at an initializer call (see "A Call Placed in an Initializer" in [`./security-properties.md`](./security-properties.md)) is an ordinary `check` row, filed by `_ingest_code_checks`, and an Unknown one did not enter the unit's Eva verdict, so the unit could be covered `eva_meta_valid` from its statement calls while the initializer call stayed open. A property Eva did not load (`not_for_engine`) has no row and gives `none`, which is never credited.
- **Cause:** a pipeline gap: package D2 wrote the Eva verdict before package E placed in-code checks.
- **Fixed (package G, commit `6f7f100` on `metacsl/g-wiring`):** `_ingest_eva_meta` in [`../../scripts/ledger.py`](../../scripts/ledger.py) takes the code rows and their lines (`_open_code_checks`): an Unknown, Invalid or Dead in-code check of the unit's property in its function counts as `unknown` (`per["in-code checks"]`). A unit whose property `security_props.json` lists as not checked in the function is not covered either (`_eva_meta_cover_barred`). Tests in [`../../tests/test_metacsl_wiring.py`](../../tests/test_metacsl_wiring.py): `test_an_open_initializer_check_under_eva_keeps_the_unit_from_an_eva_cover`, `test_a_property_eva_did_not_load_keeps_its_unit_from_an_eva_cover`, `test_eva_does_not_cover_a_unit_with_an_unchecked_site`. **Not verified** in an Eva run in the image.

### Symptom: Crash Verifiers Give Opposite Verdicts to Twenty Candidates With One Root Cause: 17 `confirmed`, 1 `duplicate`, 2 `rejected` as Not a Defect
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged into main (c5ec307); the listed limits are design notes, not open bugs.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR v2.0-beta-1 run-2 (`run-2-d574bb040623`), 2026-11-24, Crash Verification Gate, 00:05-00:26. The scheduler dispatched up to 14 Crash Verifiers at once for the E-ACSL `initialization` candidates the judge found in the map accessors, all with the fingerprint suffix `c132f24cbe` (for example `src/qcbor_number_decode.c#QCBORDecode_GetTBigNumberInMapN::initialization::c132f24cbe`). Every verifier found the same facts: `QCBORDecode_Private_GetItemInMapNoCheck` (`src/qcbor_spiffy_decode.c:489-491`) returns early on a sticky `uLastError` before it writes `*puOffset`, so the caller's local `size_t uOffset` is copied by value uninitialized, and the callee returns on its own `uLastError` check before reading it. The verdicts split:
  - 17 `confirmed` (low severity), for example "Uninitialized uOffset passed by value after a sticky decode error in QCBORDecode_GetTBigNumberInMapN (src/qcbor_number_decode.c:2151)", in `GetTBigFloat*`, `GetTBigNumber*`, `GetTDecimalFraction*`, `EnterBstrWrappedFromMap{N,SZ}`, `GetTEpochDateInMapN` and `QCBORDecode_Private_GetItemInMap`.
  - 1 `rejected` / `duplicate` (`GetTBigFloatBigMantissaInMapN`), naming the `GetTBigNumberInMapN` record.
  - 1 `rejected` / `other` (`GetTBigNumberRawInMapSZ`): "Not a defect with demonstrated effect".
  - 1 `rejected` / `disproved_by_source` (`GetTDecimalFractionBigMantissaInMapN`): "the alarm is literally true ... But the value is never consumed on that path".
- **Cause:** two pipeline gaps, diagnosed from the verifiers' `agents/verify-*/scratch/result.json` and [`../../skills/framac-wp/crash_verifier_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/crash_verifier_brief.md):
  - The brief has no rule for an alarm that is true but has no effect: an indeterminate value copied by value and never consumed. Its classes cover a failure that does not happen (`disproved_by_source`, `dead_code`, `toolchain_artifact`) or that the harness causes; nothing says whether a true copy-only `\initialized` failure is `confirmed` (and at what severity) or rejected. In C11 the copy is not undefined behaviour here (the object's address is taken, so 6.3.2.1p2 does not apply, and `size_t` has no trap representation), which is why some verifiers rejected it. So independent verifiers decide it differently.
  - `duplicate` is decided by timing. The brief allows it only when "another verified record already covers the same root cause", but the scheduler sends candidates with the same decision point to parallel verifiers, which cannot see each other's verdicts. Only the verifier that ran after a sibling's verdict was ingested could use `duplicate`. The report will therefore list about 17 confirmed findings for one missing initialization.
- **Fix:** **fixed** in `e3385a5` and `ab03b09` on branch `sup/QCBOR-verifier-root-cause` (from `main` `7495bed`), merged into `main` in `c5ec307` on 2026-11-24 after the run ended. Verified by the supervisor: diff read, `tests/test_verify_root_cause_siblings.py`, `tests/test_large_eva_report.py` and `tests/test_wp_scheduler*.py` rerun on the branch (152 passed), `ruff check` clean, `git merge-tree` against `main` clean. The fixer reports `pytest -n auto` 8810 passed; `mypy` has 9 errors, all in `scripts/api_harness.py`, which the branch does not change and which `main` already has.
  - [`../../skills/framac-wp/crash_verifier_brief.md`](../../skills/framac-wp/crash_verifier_brief.md), new section "True Alarm Without Effect": a true alarm whose value is never consumed on the reproducer's path is `confirmed` with `severity: "low"`, a `severity_rationale` starting `No effect:`, and its decision point at the callee's early `return` that skips the write; `disproved_by_source`, `toolchain_artifact` and `other` are ruled out by name. Strict Rule 3's `low` anchor names the case.
  - [`../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py`](../../scripts/wp_scheduler.py) `_hold_verify_siblings`, applied in `_ready` after the breaker's probe filter (breaker on or off): `verify:*` tasks of one module sharing `fingerprint.root_cause_sibling_key` (property kind and predicate hash) go one first; the rest wait while it runs and are released once any member is terminal (a verdict, or blocked without one; `superseded_by_repair` excluded). Groups of one are unaffected.
  - [`../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py`](../../scripts/workspace_overlay.py) `_verified_siblings_section`: a released sibling's brief lists the verified sibling records (fingerprint, verdict, class, severity, `decision_point`, root cause) and says to return `rejected`/`duplicate` with `refutation_basis: "located"` for the same decision point, and to verify independently otherwise. The scheduler passes `sibling_fingerprints`, so candidates recorded without a `module` match too.
  - Tests: `tests/test_verify_root_cause_siblings.py` (9 tests; 6 failed before the fix). `tests/test_large_eva_report.py::test_repair_waits_for_all_open_verifiers_of_module_and_accumulates_reasons` now gives its three Eva alarms distinct predicates, since equal ones are now siblings.
  - Limits: one level deep (a representative with an unusual decision point releases the rest in parallel, with its record shown); a representative blocked without a verdict releases the rest with nothing to show; grouping by predicate hash also serializes unrelated functions sharing a common predicate (one verifier duration per group). The report folding by `decision_point` was not done (`generate_report.shared_decision_point_lines` keys on exact `(file, line)`, and run-2 verifiers recorded the same early return as lines 489 and 490).
  - Run-2 itself is unchanged: its findings still hold about 17 confirmed records for one missing write.

---

## From [`./resources-and-infra.md`](./resources-and-infra.md): Resources, Caches, Make & Host Tooling

### Symptom: Static Command Classification Fails Open on Unknown Make Targets
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fail-closed allowlist is in the code; the entry just says uncommitted.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M2). `resources.is_known_static_command` returned `True` for arbitrary target arguments such as `pipeline_make.py run unknown_target` or commands without the pipeline directory prefix.
- **Cause:** `is_known_static_command` in [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py) used a denylist against `_DYNAMIC_MAKE_TARGETS` and optional prefix matching `(?:/opt/framac-pipeline/scripts/)?`, allowing untrusted or unrecognized target commands to pass as static analysis.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py)):** required strict `^/opt/framac-pipeline/scripts/` prefix for pipeline scripts, added `build-check` subcommand, and switched Make target validation to fail-closed allowlist `all(_is_static_make_target(t) for t in targets)` checking against `STAGE_OF_TARGET` and `_STATIC_MAKE_PREFIXES`. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py).

### Symptom: `loop_seeds.py` Under the Wrapper Reports `status: partial` With `analysis_error: insufficient_memory`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. loop_seeds.py maps to the guardrail class on main; the entry says uncommitted.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (branch `papers-generalization`, uncommitted, 2026-11-10). Found by reading `_CLASS_PATTERNS` while building the tool; never reproduced under the wrapper (the container runs used plain `docker run`). Only reachable with `FRAMAC_LOOP_SEEDS=on`.
- **Cause:** `resources.infer_class` had no pattern for `loop_seeds.py`, so the command got the `light` class (1 CPU, 1024 MB), below what its WP rounds need.
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py) maps `loop_seeds.py` to the `guardrail` class. Test: `tests/test_resources.py::test_infer_class_run_eacsl_fuzz_subcommand_precedes_build_module_name`.

### Symptom: Pasting Proven Loop Seeds Makes a Previously Proved Goal Time Out (Nested Loop)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. no_harm check exists on main; the entry says uncommitted.
- **Fixed pipeline bug** (branch `papers-generalization`, uncommitted, 2026-11-10). Seen on a toy benchmark of `scripts/loop_seeds.py` in `framac-analysis:latest`, no target run. Only reachable with `FRAMAC_LOOP_SEEDS=on` (default off).
- **Seen on:** function `fill` (two nested loops writing `m[r * cols + c]`): 12 of 13 goals proved before seeding, 15 of 17 after, and `\valid(m + (size_t)((size_t)(r * cols) + c))` went from proved to unproven at a 10 s goal timeout.
- **Cause:** the seeds are proven, so the goal is not false: the solver misses a nonlinear goal once more hypotheses are in context. Measured by running WP on `fill` with seed subsets: no seed 12/13; `c <= cols` alone on the inner loop loses the goal; `r <= rows` alone on the outer loop loses it and an assigns goal; `r < rows` alone on the inner loop keeps everything and is fast. The tool only checked that each seed proves, never what pasting them does to the function's other goals. (The first report of a duplicate `r <= rows` was wrong: it was the outer loop's seed and its restatement in the inner loop, redundant next to the inner `r < rows`.)
- **Fix:** [`../../scripts/loop_seeds.py`](../../scripts/loop_seeds.py) `no_harm`: after Houdini, one full WP run of the function without seeds and one with the proven seeds; if a goal is lost, it leaves out one seed of the loop the goal sits in (at most `MAX_NO_HARM_RUNS` seeded runs) and otherwise withholds every seed, printed as "Proven, but withheld". A check that cannot run withholds nothing and the report says the seeds were not checked. The restated outer bound is no longer generated when the strict outer guard is. On the rerun `fill` is back to 12/13 with all three seeds withheld; the other ten functions are unchanged.
- **Cost, and a limit:** the check adds two full WP runs per function with seeds: on the eleven toy functions the tool's total went from 45 s to 483 s. Leave-one-out did not find the one helpful subset for `fill` (`r < rows` alone), so the fallback loses that hint.

### Symptom: A Cached Result Is Restored After a `global invariant` or `volatile` Annotation Changed
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Both annotations are in the pattern on main.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (no target run).
- **Cause:** `_GLOBAL_ACSL_DECL_RE` in `scripts/cache.py` decides which annotations next to another function stay in a function's digest. It did not list `global invariant` or `volatile`, so they were dropped from the digest and editing them did not change the key.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, `scripts/cache.py`):** both are in the pattern, so a function next to such an annotation gets a new key and its older entries are no longer hit. Tests: `tests/test_regex_hardening.py`.

### Symptom: Editing One Stub Header Re-runs Every Cached Eva Run and E-ACSL Build of the Run
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Marked fixed; per-module stub keying is in cache.py.
- **Seen on:** K2 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), from reading `scripts/cache.py` (2026-11-10); not measured on a run.
- **Cause:** every cache key hashed all of `scripts/` and `templates/` (`cache.scripts_digest`), and after S2 the whole stub overlay (`workspace/stubs`) too. A contract added to one stub header therefore invalidated the results of modules that never include it.
- **Fix (fixed):** `cache.make_key` keys stub headers per module. A key whose material has a dependency manifest (`deps`: Eva, facts and E-ACSL/sanitizer builds, through `cache.memoize`) leaves `templates/fc_stubs/` and the overlay out of its global digests: the manifest (`gcc -M`/`clang -M` over the effective flags, which put the overlay and `templates/fc_stubs` on the include path) already names, and hashes, the stub headers that module's preprocessing read. Keys without a manifest (`slice-index`, `proofs`, `guardrail`) keep the digest of all of `templates/` and of the whole overlay. Everything else under `templates/` (the harness template, the E-ACSL runtime, `pipeline.mk`) still invalidates every key.
- **Limit:** the manifest is the include set `gcc -M` sees with Frama-C's defines; a stub header Frama-C reaches but `gcc -M` does not (none known) would not enter the key. If you suspect a stale result, compare with `FRAMAC_CACHE=off`.

### Symptom: `make help` Does Not List a Target `pipeline_make.py run` Accepts, or `facts-<mod>` and `module-<mod>` Compute Facts Differently
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix is in the template with the named test.
- **Seen on:** K3 of [`../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md`](../plans/templates-and-stubs-improvement-plan.md), from reading `templates/pipeline.mk`; no run failed on it.
- **Cause:** `help` omitted `eva-tune-base` (staged by `pipeline_make.STAGE_OF_TARGET`, in `.PHONY`), and `module-%` repeated the `facts-%` recipe line for line, so an option added to one would silently miss the other.
- **Fix (fixed):** `templates/pipeline.mk` has one `define FACTS_RECIPE` that both rules call (`$(call FACTS_RECIPE,$*)`; its body is tab-indented like any recipe, so `test_pipeline_makefile_builds_only_through_the_pipeline_scripts` still reads it, and GNU make keeps the `-` prefix after expansion, checked in `framac-analysis:latest`), and `help` lists `eva-tune-base`; `stubs` and `stub-check` are in `.PHONY`, `help` and the stage order (S2, S3). `tests/test_pipeline_make.py::test_pipeline_mk_lists_every_staged_target_and_shares_the_facts_recipe` checks that every target in `STAGE_OF_TARGET` is in `help`, in `.PHONY` and has a rule.

### Symptom: `benchmark.py run --phase0-only --compare <baseline>` Exits 1 With Regressions for Stages That Never Ran
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in 8ef9c98, on main.
- **Seen on:** CryptoLib, run `cl-p0-20261123` against `phase0-r1` (2026-11-23). Phase 0 passed (`phase0: rc=0`), yet the comparison printed `REGRESSION (2)` (`[target] cryptolib: status ok -> partial: eva core: no report; ...` and `[units] cryptolib: covered units lost: ...`), `IMPROVEMENT: functions with an Unknown 14 -> 0`, and `WARNING: eva candidates 4 -> 0`, and exited 1.
- **Cause:** `run_target` skips every stage after Phase 0 under `--phase0-only`, but `collect_target_metrics` still recorded missing Eva reports as problems (`status: "partial"`), the run file did not record `selection.phase0_only`, and `benchmark_compare.py` compared empty post-Phase-0 fields against the baseline's full-run values.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline gap (`8ef9c98`)** in [`../../scripts/benchmark.py`](../../scripts/benchmark.py) and [`../../scripts/benchmark_compare.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_compare.py):
  - `cmd_run` records `selection["phase0_only"] = bool(args.phase0_only)`, and `collect_target_metrics(..., phase0_only=True)` omits missing-Eva-report problems so a clean Phase 0 run keeps `status: "ok"`.
  - `compare_runs` detects `selection.phase0_only` on either side, compares `target`, `dag`, `api_reach`, and Phase 0 `stage_time` rows (`capture`, `model`, `seeds`, `phase0`), and emits `not_compared` for post-Phase-0 areas (`eva`, `functions_with_unknown`, `prover_tasks`, `units`, `candidates`, and one-sided total `time`).
  - **Regression tests:** [`../../tests/test_benchmark.py`](../../tests/test_benchmark.py) and [`../../tests/test_benchmark_compare.py`](../../tests/test_benchmark_compare.py).

### Symptom: Benchmark Host Path Assertion Bypassed by Dictionary Keys, UNC Paths, or Flagging Relative Paths
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The reworked _HOST_PATH_RE is in benchmark.py; the entry says uncommitted.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M2 Iteration 2). `benchmark.assert_no_host_paths` did not inspect dictionary keys, ignored Windows UNC (`\\server\share\...`, `\\wsl$\...`), forward-slash UNC (`//server/share`), and driverless root paths (`\Users\...`), while falsely raising `BenchmarkError` on relative paths starting with `./` or `../`.
- **Cause:** `_HOST_PATH_RE` in [`../../scripts/benchmark.py`](../../scripts/benchmark.py) used negative lookbehind `(?<![\w/:])` which excluded `.`, lacked UNC/network patterns, and `assert_no_host_paths` only traversed dictionary values, skipping keys.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/benchmark.py`](../../scripts/benchmark.py)):** updated `_HOST_PATH_RE` with lookbehind `(?<![.\w/:])`, added explicit container traversal detection `/framac-run/\.\.`, added UNC and driverless backslash branches, added dictionary key validation `assert_no_host_paths(key, f"{where}.(key)")`, and updated `scrub_host_paths` to scrub dictionary keys. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_infra.py).

### Symptom: Technical Terms and Single-Letter Identifiers at Page Bottom Falsely Dropped as Page Numbers
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. The constrained Roman-numeral regex is in extract_references.py.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M5). In `scripts/extract_references.py`, words such as `mix`, `dim`, `c`, `C`, `MIX`, `I`, `X`, `d`, `cc` or malformed repetitions `lxxxx`, `iiii`, `vv` occurring on the last line of a page were stripped as page numbers.
- **Cause:** `_PAGE_NUMBER_RE` used `^\s*(?:\d+|[ivxlcdm]+)\s*$` with `re.IGNORECASE`. The unbounded character class `[ivxlcdm]+` with case insensitivity matched any English word composed of those letters or single capital letters/acronyms.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/extract_references.py`](../../scripts/extract_references.py)):** constrained Roman numeral matching to valid lowercase Roman numeral sequences from 1 to 89: `^\s*(?:\d+|(?=[ivxl])(?:xc|xl|l?x{0,3})(?:ix|iv|v?i{0,3}))\s*$`, removing `re.IGNORECASE`. Valid lowercase page numerals (`iv`, `xii`, `xxxix`, `xliv`, `lxxxviii`) match and are dropped, while ordinary words and uppercase identifiers are preserved. Tests: [`../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py`](../../tests/test_regex_hardening_stubs.py).

### Symptom: Circular Import Hazards or Divergent Implementations of Memory and Dependency Parsers
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. scripts/patterns.py exists and is imported by the scripts named.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M5). Modules across different layers (`cache.py`, `resources.py`, `build_capture.py`, `ledger.py`) had duplicate definitions of Makefile dependency parsers (`parse_make_deps`), memory size parsers (`parse_mem`, `_parse_size`), fuzzer definitions (`_FUZZER_INIT_DEF_RE`, `_FUZZER_ENTRY_DEF_RE`), and kernel rename regexes (`KERNEL_RENAME_RE`).
- **Cause:** lack of a common foundational patterns module led each script to define its own regexes and parser helpers, risking subtle divergences (e.g. `cache._parse_size` returning bytes vs `resources.parse_mem` returning MiB). Importing helpers between scripts would create circular dependency cycles between low-level infrastructure modules.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted, [`../../scripts/patterns.py`](../../scripts/patterns.py)):** created `scripts/patterns.py` as a pure standard-library module with zero internal pipeline imports. Exported canonical compiled regexes (`C_IDENT_RE`, `SLUG_RE`, `LOCATION_RE`, `MODULE_RE`, `CALL_SITE_RE`, `KERNEL_RENAME_RE`, `FUZZER_INIT_DEF_RE`, `FUZZER_ENTRY_DEF_RE`, `DOMAIN_RE`, `ASSIGNS_RE`, `TREE_TAIL_RE`, `WORK_PREFIX_RE`) and unified parsers `parse_mem(..., as_bytes=...)` and `parse_make_deps(text, cwd=...)`. Downstream scripts imported these canonical implementations. Tests: [`../../tests/test_cache.py`](../../tests/test_cache.py), [`../../tests/test_resources_api.py`](../../tests/test_resources_api.py), [`../../tests/test_build_capture.py`](../../tests/test_build_capture.py).

### Symptom: `pipeline_make.py run stubs` and `pipeline_make.py run seed` Fail With `workspace/build/Makefile not found: run \`pipeline_make.py gen\` first, from the run directory` in Phase 0
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed and merged with tests.
- **Fixed:** supervisor branch `sup/QCBOR-phase0-make-bootstrap` (commit `4dcb52f`), merged into `phase1-remediation` as `6590815` on 2026-11-15 after `QCBOR` `run-6` ended.
- **Seen on:**
  - `minmea`, `run-1`, 2026-11-14 (`standard` profile), immediately after `security_props.py run`: `run stubs` returned `{"ok": false, "error": "workspace/build/Makefile not found: run \`pipeline_make.py gen\` first, from the run directory"}`; the same happened to `run seed`, which then had to be retried after `gen` (its `ledger.py record-stub-contracts` step reported `recorded: 0, skipped_reason: "no stub report at workspace/reports/stubs.json: run \`stubs\` first"`).
  - `QCBOR`, `run-6`, 2026-11-14 (`deep` profile), Phase 0 Step 3: `python3 /opt/framac-pipeline/scripts/pipeline_make.py run stubs` failed with `{"ok": false, "error": "workspace/build/Makefile not found: run \`pipeline_make.py gen\` first, from the run directory"}`, forcing the session to invoke `stub_gen.py run` directly.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in [`../../scripts/pipeline_make.py`](../../scripts/pipeline_make.py) `run_targets`: it refused unconditionally when `workspace/build/Makefile` was missing before inspecting `targets`, even though `run_targets` calls `_generate(run_dir, templates_dir, private_dir=priv_dir)` at the start of every stage to write `workspace/build/Makefile` and `workspace/build/modules.mk`. [`../../skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md`](../../skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md) Steps 1-5 instruct running Phase 0 targets (`slice-init`, `dag`, `stubs`, `stub-check`, `security-props`, `reach-gate`, `seed`) before Step 5's `pipeline_make.py gen`, making those Phase 0 Make targets unreachable in the documented order.
- **Fix (merged as `6590815` from branch `sup/QCBOR-phase0-make-bootstrap`, commit `4dcb52f`, [`../../scripts/pipeline_make.py`](../../scripts/pipeline_make.py)):** `run_targets` now validates `targets` against `_stage(t)` first and requires a pre-existing `workspace/build/Makefile` only when at least one requested target belongs to Phase 1 or later (`_stage(t) > STAGE_OF_TARGET["seed"]`). Phase 0 invocations (`slice-init` through `seed`, stages 0-3) proceed into the stage loop and bootstrap `workspace/build/{Makefile,modules.mk}` via `_generate`, whereas Phase 1+ or mixed Phase 0 + Phase 1+ calls without a prior `gen` still fail with exit code `2`. Tests: [`../../tests/test_pipeline_make.py`](../../tests/test_pipeline_make.py) (`test_phase0_targets_bootstrap_makefile_without_gen`, `test_phase1_and_mixed_targets_still_require_gen`).

### Symptom: `AssertionError: ... is a stale copy of skills/...: SKILL.md differ` on Windows Checkouts
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** stale (what it describes no longer exists). Superseded by the entry at line 259: the symlinks were dropped, the mirrors are plain copies and test_framac_skills_mirror_is_in_sync no longer exists.
- **Seen:** 2026-11-11, regex audit (Milestone M5). Tests `test_framac_skills_mirror_is_in_sync` and `test_brief_templates_synced_and_render_cleanly` failed on Windows checkouts where symlinks are plain directories.
- **Cause:** in git tracking, `.agents/skills/` and `.claude/skills/` are symlinks (`120000`). On Windows checkouts without native symlink support, git creates physical directories. When files under `skills/` are updated or new brief templates added (`skills/framac-slice/stub_author_brief.md`), the physical copies become stale.
- **Fix (fixed 2026-11-11, uncommitted):** synchronized all skill files from `skills/` to `.agents/skills/` and `.claude/skills/` via binary copy (`write_bytes`), maintaining byte-exact equivalence and LF line endings without CRLF translation. Tests: [`../../tests/test_extract_references.py`](../../tests/test_extract_references.py), [`../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py`](../../tests/test_diagnostic_hints.py).

### Symptom: `test_budget_is_capped_by_env_and_free_disk` Fails Under `-n` With `assert ... == int((0.25 * <free>))`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Test race fixed in tests/test_cache.py, not a pipeline defect.
- **Fixed test race, not a pipeline defect** (seen 2026-11-09 in a full `-n 6` run on branch `formal-discovery-tiers` while an analysis was running; passes alone).
- **Cause:** the test read `shutil.disk_usage(tmp_path).free` once and `Store.budget_bytes()` read it again; other writers (parallel workers, a live run) changed the free space in between.
- **Fix:** [`../../tests/test_cache.py`](../../tests/test_cache.py) pins `cache.shutil.disk_usage` to one measurement.

### Symptom: After a Merge, `test_framac_skills_mirror_is_in_sync` and `test_brief_templates_synced_and_render_cleanly` Fail in the Main Checkout Only
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in fdb60e7: plain copies synced by tools/sync_skill_mirrors.py with a check test.
- **Seen on:** 2026-11-14, merging three fix branches into `phase1-remediation` (`1b90874`): `.agents\skills\framac-wp is a stale copy of skills/framac-wp: SKILL.md, breaker_brief.md, crash_verifier_brief.md, header_architect_brief.md, prover_brief.md differ; refresh it from skills/framac-wp`. The same commit passes in a fresh worktree.
- **Cause:** in this Windows checkout `.agents/skills/*` and `.claude/skills/*` are directory copies (git shows the tracked entries as deleted), so a merge that changes `skills/` does not update them. Not a pipeline defect; a checkout-side issue that worktree test runs never see.
- **Fix (2026-11-14, commit `fdb60e7`):** the symlinks are given up. `skills/` is the source of truth and both mirrors are plain tracked copies. [`../../tools/sync_skill_mirrors.py`](../../tools/sync_skill_mirrors.py) copies `skills/` into both mirrors, and `--check` lists every difference; the pre-commit hook `check-skill-mirrors`, a CI step and [`../../tests/test_sync_skill_mirrors.py`](../../tests/test_sync_skill_mirrors.py) run the check. The old `test_framac_skills_mirror_is_in_sync` is removed. After a merge that changes `skills/`, run `python tools/sync_skill_mirrors.py`. The pre-commit hook only runs where `pre-commit` is installed (`pre-commit install`); it was not on the machine where this was written.

### Symptom: `pipeline_make.py run stubs` Fails With `workspace/build/Makefile not found: run `pipeline_make.py gen` first`
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fix and the later guard fix are both on main with tests.
- **Seen on:** QCBOR, run-6, 2026-11-15, at Phase 0 Step 3 (external stub overlay), before the ledger seed.
- **Cause:** [`../../skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md`](../../skills/framac-slice/SKILL.md) Step 3 says to run `pipeline_make.py run stubs` (and `stub-check`) once per run, after the model, but the Makefile is generated in Step 5 (`pipeline_make.py gen`). `pipeline_make.py run` always dispatches through that Makefile.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline gap (`4dcb52f`, on `phase1-remediation`; see the post-run review and Status below):** `pipeline_make.run_targets` bootstraps `workspace/build/Makefile` for Phase 0 targets, test `tests/test_pipeline_make.py::test_phase0_targets_bootstrap_makefile_without_gen`. Workaround used in run-6, before the fix: call `python3 /opt/framac-pipeline/scripts/stub_gen.py run` directly (QCBOR had no external headers to stub: `{"headers": 0}`), and `stub_check.py run` if a header exists. A cleaner pipeline fix is to generate the Makefile before the stub step (it needs only the DAG, the model and the harness sidecars, none of which the stub step changes) or to let `run stubs` fall back to the direct script.
- **Post-run review of F1 (`4dcb52f`, 2026-11-15):** confirmed in `framac-analysis:latest` on an empty run directory: base exits 2 with `workspace/build/Makefile not found`, HEAD bootstraps `workspace/build/` and runs the `stubs` recipe. One low-severity gap remains open: the guard that refuses Phase 1+ targets before `gen` fires only while the Makefile is missing, so after any Phase 0 target has bootstrapped it, `pipeline_make.py run build` without `gen` returns `rc=0` with "no runnable module". Harmless today because `gen` only regenerates and prints a summary.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation), the guard gap from the post-run review:** **fixed** (`27c7815`). `run_targets` refuses Phase 1+ targets when `build_plan` has no DAG module or no harness sidecar for one (`_phase1_inputs_problem`), whatever the state of the Makefile, and names `pipeline_make.py gen`. A run that has the DAG and the sidecars no longer needs the Makefile to exist. Test: `tests/test_pipeline_make.py::test_phase1_targets_still_require_gen_after_a_phase0_target_bootstrapped_the_makefile`.

### Symptom: `tests/test_pipeline_make.py::test_make_dry_run` Fails Wherever GNU make Exists (`ValueError: '--' is not in list`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Status says fixed in da541d3, which is on main.
- **Seen on:** `4ad9820` and both held-branch merge trees, with the suite run inside `framac-analysis:latest`, 2026-11-15 (post-run review of QCBOR run-6). Exact text: `tests/test_pipeline_make.py:1207: ValueError: '--' is not in list`, with `argv = ['/repo/scripts/phase0_gates.py', 'check', '--module', 'cfg', '--need', 'reach']`.
- **Cause:** **Pipeline bug (test; fixed, see Status below):** the test took the first `ARGV` line printed by `make eva-cfg`. The `eva-%` recipe in [`../../templates/pipeline.mk`](../../templates/pipeline.mk) now runs the gate command first, and that command has no `--`. The Windows host skips the test (`GNU make not available`), so the local suite stays green. CI has an `ubuntu-latest` job, so it should fail there; CI was not run to confirm.
- **Fix:** none yet. Proposed: pick the `ARGV` line that contains `run_eva_slice.py`.
- **Status (2026-11-15, run-6 remediation):** **fixed** (`da541d3`). The test takes the `ARGV` line that contains `run_eva_slice.py`. Verified in `framac-analysis:latest`: the old test fails with the `ValueError`, the fixed one passes. The image has GNU make but no pytest; the test was run with the host's pure-Python pytest mounted read-only.

### Symptom: The `check_generated_text` PostToolUse Hook Reports Allow-Listed Fixtures of Another Worktree (`.claude/worktrees/<name>/tests/test_check_generated_text.py:134: [acsl-corrupted] ...`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in ea2f9c3 with tests.

- **Seen on:** run-6 remediation, 2026-11-15. Three sessions working in worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` got the report after every shell command that named a test file, for example `.claude/worktrees/run6-integ/tests/test_acsl_only_and_merge.py:167: [acsl-corrupted] string evaluates to 0x0c + 'alse'` after `cd <r6-merge-gate>; python -m pytest tests/test_acsl_only_and_merge.py`, while `python tools/check_generated_text.py` in that worktree exits 0.
- **Cause:** two defects in the hook mode of [`../../tools/check_generated_text.py`](../../tools/check_generated_text.py). `_paths_in_command` resolved a relative file name against the session's directory (`payload["cwd"]`), not the directory the command changed to, so the session worktree's copy of the file was checked. And `run_hook` matched every file against the allowlist by its path from the main checkout (`.claude/worktrees/<name>/tests/x.py`), which no entry matches, so intended fixtures were reported.
- **Fix:** **fixed** (`ea2f9c3`). `_paths_in_command` follows `cd`, `Set-Location`, `sl`, `pushd` and `chdir`; `run_hook` checks each file against the allowlist by the path inside its own checkout (`_checkout_root`: the nearest ancestor with a `.git` entry) and still reports where the file is. Tests: `tests/test_check_generated_text.py::TestHook::test_a_relative_name_follows_the_directory_the_command_changed_to`, `::test_a_file_in_a_worktree_matches_the_allowlist_by_its_own_checkout_path`. The hook that runs is the main checkout's copy, so the fix takes effect once it is merged there.

### Symptom: `tests/test_docs_structure.py::test_markdown_links_are_explicit_relative_and_resolve` Fails in a Clean Worktree of `phase1-remediation` (16 Links Such As `docs/troubleshooting/wp-and-guardrail.md:332 -> ../upstream/frama-c/frama-c-wp-counter-examples-drops-frame.md`)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in d1bba51 (drafts committed), verified on 2026-11-18; I did not rerun the test.

- **Seen on:** a worktree cut from `phase1-remediation` at `a07c7ef`, 2026-11-16, full `python -m pytest tests -n auto` (5328 passed, 1 failed).
- **Cause:** committed documents (`docs/troubleshooting/harnesses-and-seeds.md`, `wp-and-guardrail.md`, `docs/runs/2026-11-14-qcbor-run6-supervision.md`, ...) link to upstream drafts under `docs/upstream/frama-c/` that exist on disk in the main checkout but are not committed (`git ls-files docs/upstream/frama-c` lists none of them), so every clean worktree of the branch fails the check.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline bug (`d1bba51`, on `phase1-remediation`; repository content, not code):** the linked drafts are committed (for example `frama-c-wp-counter-examples-drops-frame.md` in `d1bba51`). Checked on 2026-11-18: `python -m pytest tests/test_docs_structure.py` passes (4 passed) on a clean `git archive` export of `ec7c8c7`. The same failure comes back whenever a committed document links to a draft that is still untracked, so commit a draft together with the document that links to it, or drop the link until then.

### Symptom: Eva Shards Run One at a Time: Every `libqcbor_a:shardN` Sub-Lease Asks for the Whole Module's Memory (`mem_floor_mb` 13372 of a 17408 MiB Pool)
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in 03523de, on main, with regression tests.

- **Seen on:** QCBOR v2.0-beta-1 run-2, 2026-11-23. After the unsharded base Eva hit its 7200 s cap and both fallback tiers timed out, `gen` wrote an 8-shard plan (`reports/eva-shards/libqcbor_a/plan.json`, `predicted_makespan_s` 904.5, i.e. all 8 in parallel). `pipeline_make.py run eva` then ran in a `phase-static` container with 15 CPUs and 17,408 MiB (`FRAMAC_JOB_CPUS=15`, `FRAMAC_JOB_MEM_MB=17408`). Inside it, `/tmp/framac-broker/leases/` held one `granted` lease (`libqcbor_a:shard1`, `mem_mb` 13372) and seven `waiting` ones, each with `demand.mem_floor_mb: 13372`. Only one `frama-c` process ran; the other seven `resources.py run --class eva --key libqcbor_a:shardN` wrappers slept. So the shards run in sequence, about 8 x 900 s instead of about 900 s.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py). `cmd_run` sizes each sub-lease from `history_estimate(run_dir, "eva", key)`. A shard key `libqcbor_a:shardN` has no exact history, so Tier 3 (module prefix) matches the **unsharded** module records `libqcbor_a`, including the shared cross-run store (`/framac-cache/resource-history/eva/`): QCBOR run-1's whole-harness Eva peaked at 10,697 MiB (2026-11-22T23:24), and 1.25 x 10,697 = 13,372 MiB. A shard analyses a fraction of the harness cases (here 23-33 of 244), and even the timed-out run-2 whole-harness run peaked at only 4,780 MiB. But each shard inherits the whole module's worst peak, so only `floor(17408 / 13372) = 1` fits in the pool. The shard plan predicts its makespan as if every shard ran at once, and nothing checks the plan against the sub-lease memory the shards will ask for.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline bug (`03523de`)** in [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py), [`../../scripts/eva_shards.py`](../../scripts/eva_shards.py), [`../../scripts/pipeline_make.py`](../../scripts/pipeline_make.py), and [`../../templates/pipeline.mk`](../../templates/pipeline.mk):
  - `write_history` / `_enrich_eva_record` in [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py) now stamps `harness_digest` (from `phase0_gates.harness_digest(..., ignore_exempt=True)`) on `eva` history records and `shard_plan_digest` (matching `eva_shards.plan_digest`) on `<module>:shard<N>` records.
  - `history_estimate` / `_eva_history_estimate` prefers records matching the module's current `harness_digest` (and local untagged records written after the current harness over legacy untagged shared-cache records), falling back to the newest prior-harness group rather than the maximum across all runs when the harness changed.
  - For `<module>:shard<N>` keys, exact Tier 1 matches require a compatible `shard_plan_digest`, and unsharded module fallback scales by the shard's share from `workspace/reports/eva-shards/<module>/plan.json` (`predicted_shard_s[i] / sum(predicted_shard_s)`, falling back to `len(shards[i]) / case_count` for legacy plans; returns `None` without a plan so a shard never inherits an unscaled whole-module peak). Non-shard Eva keys (`<module>:<rung>`) also exclude `<module>:shard<N>` records from Tier 3 fallback so shard peaks cannot mask unsharded peaks.
  - `eva_shards.estimate_shard_concurrency` / `memory_concurrency_note` and `pipeline_make._plan_shards` / `run_targets` check estimated per-shard sub-lease memory against the effective `phase-static` pool and emit an `Eva sharding: memory concurrency:` warning (and print it to `stderr` / the stage log when starting an Eva stage) whenever memory forces serial or wave execution, while keeping `k` fixed and hardware-independent.
  - **Regression tests:** [`../../tests/test_eva_shard_memory.py`](../../tests/test_eva_shard_memory.py) (12 tests, including an end-to-end 8-shard container-broker concurrency test reproducing QCBOR run-2's 15-CPU / 17,408 MiB pool with run-1's 10,697 MiB shared-cache peak).
  - **Workaround (for already-running containers on older pipeline code):** clear or move the module's `eva` records from `state/resource-history/eva/` and the shared `resource-history/eva/` before rerunning; do not do this under a live run.

### Symptom: After `03523de`, Eva Shard Leases Are Too Small: 2048 MiB Leases, 2.9 GB Shards, Container Near Its Memory Limit
- **Archived 2026-11-26:** fixed on `main`. Fixed in e0f67c7, on main, with tests.

- **Seen on:** QCBOR v2.0-beta-1 run-2, 2026-11-23, 15:53, after the Eva step was restarted on `03523de` (see "Eva Shards Run One at a Time" above). The `phase-static` container (`framac-e5884054ec11`, `memory.max` 18,253,611,008 bytes) granted shard 1 5264 MiB and shards 2-7 their 747 MiB scaled estimate floored at the 2048 MiB class base, admitting 6 at once. 65-95 min in, five shard `frama-c` processes were at 2.8-2.9 GB RSS each and still growing. `memory.current` was 15.36 GB, `memory.peak` 18.14 GB (0.11 GB under the limit) and `oom_kill` still 0. Shard 3's `frama-c` had just ended, and its flow folding (`.tmp_fold_bucket_*`) was still running.
- **Cause:** pipeline bug in the `03523de` estimate (`history_estimate` / `_eva_history_estimate` in [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py)). It scales the module's unsharded peak by the shard's share of the predicted time (1/8 here), as if memory were proportional to the cases. An Eva shard still parses and annotates the whole program and keeps the whole state, so a large fixed part does not shrink with the case count. The leases (sum 15,504 MiB) undercount the real use (about 14.4 GB of `frama-c` alone at this point), so when a shard finishes the broker can admit the next one on a 2048 MiB demand that it will exceed. The likely outcome is an OOM kill and `resources.py`'s single retry with double memory, which starts that shard over.
- **Fix:** **Fixed pipeline bug (`e0f67c7`)** in [`../../scripts/resources.py`](../../scripts/resources.py) and [`../../templates/pipeline.mk`](../../templates/pipeline.mk):
  - **Fixed-overhead floor (`EVA_SHARD_MEM_FLOOR_SHARE = 0.6`):** unsharded fallback in `_eva_history_estimate` now scales by `max(EVA_SHARD_MEM_FLOOR_SHARE, share)` (including when no `plan.json` exists or a shard has 0 predicted share) instead of pure linear `1/k`, retaining the fixed program parse/AST/domain footprint (e.g. QCBOR run-2's 4,780 MiB unsharded peak gives 3,585 MiB/shard -> 4 concurrent shards in 17,408 MiB instead of 8 at 2,048 MiB).
  - **Same-plan peer sibling shard sizing + waiting-ticket refresh:** `_eva_history_estimate` adds Tier 2 for completed (`rc == 0`) or OOM-killed peer shards sharing the current `shard_plan_digest` (within 2x of the target shard's predicted share, so skewed outliers do not cross-contaminate, and local legacy pre-`03523de` shard records newer than `plan.json` are included). Once shard 1 completes at 4,211 MiB (5,264 MiB with 1.25x), shards 2-7 immediately size to 5,264 MiB (3 concurrent in 17,408 MiB). `cmd_run` now records history before releasing its lease (`Broker.is_single`), and `Broker._acquire` refreshes `history_fn` outside `broker.lock` whenever the granted lease set changes so queued shards pick up newly measured sibling peaks before admission.
  - **Live cgroup working-set admission:** added `cgroup_current_mem_mb` (`max(0, memory.current - inactive_file)` on cgroup v2, `max(0, usage_in_bytes - total_inactive_file)` on cgroup v1, subtracting reclaimable page cache) and wired it into `cmd_run` -> `Broker(live_mem_fn=...)` -> `plan_grant(..., live_mem_mb=...)`. When at least one gated lease is active, admission accounts for `max(leased_mem, live_mem_mb)` so overrunning jobs cannot admit another shard into an almost-full container; when no gated lease is running, `live_mem_mb` never blocks the sole next job.
  - **Regression tests:** [`../../tests/test_eva_shard_memory.py`](../../tests/test_eva_shard_memory.py) (19 tests covering the 0.6 floor, same-plan peer siblings, skewed-plan isolation, `cgroup_current_mem_mb` working-set subtraction, live-memory `plan_grant` blocking + zero-deadlock invariant, and 3 end-to-end multi-process broker tests reproducing QCBOR run-2 cold start, post-shard1 restart, and live-cgroup/sibling-refresh gating).
